## Cluster `aki_general` (topics 45, 47, 48, 49, 98, 99, 100) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** AKI survivors face increased risk of death, dialysis and CKD after discharge. Guidelines vary in methodological quality and need to address diverse populations; lower-intensity CRRT dose did not raise ICU mortality in meta-analysis.

**Teaching points**
- AKI survivors to discharge have increased risk of death, dialysis (RR 2.48) and CKD compared with controls. [PMID 41247233]
- A systematic review of AKI clinical practice guidelines assessed methodological quality and called for recommendations addressing diverse populations such as ageing populations. [PMID 40776908]
- Lower dose-intensity CRRT was not associated with increased ICU mortality compared with standard dose, based on very low certainty evidence. [PMID 41896891]
- A Chinese guideline covers renal replacement therapy in critically ill adults with AKI, including prescribed dose and weaning strategies. [PMID 42203644, 42859779]
- The pooled prevalence of AKI in preterm neonates is 17.8%, varying from 15.2% in Africa to 20.1% in Europe. [PMID 41489685]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal CRRT dose intensity.

**MCQ angles**
- Long-term risk after AKI.
- KDIGO staging (not in excerpts).

**Case hooks**
- Patient discharged after AKI needing follow-up of kidney function.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** KDIGO definition and staging, causes, fluid and nephrotoxin management, and RRT indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Needs the KDIGO AKI guideline.

**Sources**
- PMID 40776908: Clinical practice guidelines for acute kidney injury: a systematic review of the methodological quality (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41247233: Long-Term Outcomes After Acute Kidney Injury During Hospitalization: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Matched Controls Studies (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `contrast_aki` (topics 46, 453, 454) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Contrast-associated AKI risk is raised by hypertension, and the potential need for dialysis should be explained, especially after intra-arterial contrast. Biomarkers such as NGAL, cystatin-C and KIM-1 may predict it; RenalGuard-guided hydration may be safer than conventional hydration.

**Teaching points**
- The risk of dialysis should be communicated to patients undergoing procedures requiring contrast, especially intra-arterial exposure. [PMID 35573008]
- Hypertension is an independent risk factor for contrast-associated AKI and can be used for risk stratification. [PMID 34492656]
- RenalGuard is more secure than conventional hydration for preventing contrast-associated AKI, though its role in TAVR is questionable. [PMID 33662483]
- NGAL had the highest diagnostic performance for contrast-induced nephropathy, followed by cystatin-C and urinary KIM-1. [PMID 38561791]
- Pre-angiography CRP and some haematological indices are associated with contrast-induced nephropathy, and NLR shows potential as a cost-effective predictor. [PMID 33719591, 38488664]
- SGLT2 inhibitors may help prevent contrast-induced nephropathy in diabetic patients undergoing coronary angiography or PCI, but clinical trials are needed. [PMID 38179307]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal prophylaxis dose and type.

**MCQ angles**
- Risk factors and biomarkers.
- Hydration strategies.

**Case hooks**
- Diabetic patient with CKD scheduled for coronary angiography.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Definition, prevention by isotonic hydration, withholding nephrotoxins and contrast volume limits are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33662483: RenalGuard system and conventional hydration for preventing contrast-associated acute kidney injury in patients undergoing cardiac interventional procedures: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 33719591: Inflammatory Indicators and Hematological Indices in Contrast-Induced Nephropathy Among Patients Receiving Coronary Intervention: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34492656: Hypertension as a Risk Factor for Contrast-Associated Acute Kidney Injury: A Meta-Analysis Including 2,830,338 Patients (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35573008: The Incidence of Contrast-Induced Nephropathy and the Need of Dialysis in Patients Receiving Angiography: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 38179307: SGLT-2 inhibitors and prevention of contrast-induced nephropathy in patients with diabetes undergoing coronary angiography and percutaneous coronary interventions: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38488664: Prognostic Role of Neutrophil to Lymphocyte Ratio in Contrast-Induced Nephropathy: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 38561791: Evaluating biomarkers for contrast-induced nephropathy following coronary interventions: an umbrella review on meta-analyses (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `alal` (topics 50) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Mixed phenotype acute leukaemia is diagnosed by flow cytometric immunophenotyping per WHO criteria. ALL-type therapy gives higher initial remission and equal long-term survival to intensive AML therapy, and allogeneic transplant may be used as consolidation.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found ALL-based therapy for MPAL is associated with higher initial remission rates and at least equivalent long-term survival to more intensive AML therapy. [PMID 29550836]
- A review of MPAL diagnosis and treatment supports ALL-based regimens, targeted therapy by genetics and immunophenotype, and allogeneic transplant as consolidation in some patients. [PMID 36009521]
- The CAP/ASH guideline covers the initial diagnostic workup of acute leukaemia, noting molecular testing is changing rapidly. [PMID 28225303]
- AIEOP-BFM consensus guidelines standardise flow cytometric immunophenotyping of paediatric acute lymphoblastic leukaemia, including MPAL. [PMID 28187514]
- French recommendations on acute leukaemia of ambiguous lineage aim for future inclusion in the ALLTogether protocol and molecular exploration for targeted therapies. [PMID 39492072]
- Blinatumomab with donor lymphocyte infusion produced minimal residual disease negativity in a case of B/myeloid MPAL. [PMID 30786841]

**Uncertainties**
- No uniform approach; prospective trials needed.

**MCQ angles**
- MPAL treated with ALL-type regimens.

**Case hooks**
- Leukaemia blasts co-expressing myeloid and B-lymphoid markers.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** WHO versus EGIL criteria, cytogenetic risk, Philadelphia-positive disease and transplant indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28187514: AIEOP-BFM consensus guidelines 2016 for flow cytometric immunophenotyping of Pediatric acute lymphoblastic leukemia (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 28225303: Initial Diagnostic Workup of Acute Leukemia: Guideline From the College of American Pathologists and the American Society of Hematology (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 29550836: Therapy for children and adults with mixed phenotype acute leukemia: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 30786841: Concomitant use of blinatumomab and donor lymphocyte infusion for mixed-phenotype acute leukemia: a case report with literature review (systematic review, 2019)
- PMID 36009521: Mixed-Phenotype Acute Leukemia: Clinical Diagnosis and Therapeutic Strategies (review, 2022)

## Cluster `limb_ischaemia` (topics 51) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2024 ACC/AHA PAD guideline sets the framework for lower-extremity disease. Acute limb ischaemia is revascularised by endovascular or surgical techniques with hybrid approaches, and long-term antithrombotic choice (clopidogrel, or aspirin plus low-dose rivaroxaban after intervention) follows.

**Teaching points**
- The 2024 multisociety guideline for lower extremity PAD updates earlier recommendations with new evidence and adds recommendations for comprehensive care. [PMID 38752899, 38743805]
- A meta-analysis of endovascular and surgical revascularisation in acute limb ischaemia notes hybrid approaches, ultrasound-accelerated catheter-directed thrombolysis and rheolytic thrombectomy, and calls for carefully designed trials with standardised outcome reporting. [PMID 31353270]
- Clopidogrel may be the antithrombotic of choice for most symptomatic PAD patients, with aspirin plus low-dose rivaroxaban considered long-term after peripheral vascular intervention. [PMID 35997941]
- Supervised exercise therapy is a class IA recommendation for symptomatic PAD with dramatic walking improvement but is vastly underused. [PMID 36114638]
- A non-negligible proportion of hospitalised COVID-19 patients had arterial thrombotic events, similar to influenza or non-COVID viral pneumonia. [PMID 35793687]
- Recommendations exist for registry data collection for revascularisation in acute limb ischaemia. [PMID 31128987]

**Uncertainties**
- Best revascularisation strategy for acute limb ischaemia.

**MCQ angles**
- Hybrid revascularisation options.
- Antithrombotic therapy after peripheral intervention.

**Case hooks**
- Sudden cold painful leg in a patient with atrial fibrillation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Rutherford classification, immediate heparin, fasciotomy and reperfusion injury are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 31128987: Editor's Choice - Recommendations for Registry Data Collection for Revascularisations of Acute Limb Ischaemia: A Delphi Consensus from the International Consortium of Vascular Registries (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 31353270: A systematic review and meta-analysis of endovascular and surgical revascularization techniques in acute limb ischemia (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 35793687: Arterial Thrombotic Events in Hospitalized COVID-19 Patients: A Short Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 35997941: Antithrombotic Therapy for Symptomatic Peripheral Arterial Disease: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)

## Cluster `alf` (topics 52, 53, 54, 55) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Multidisciplinary ICU guidelines for acute and acute-on-chronic liver failure exist but are mostly based on low-quality or indirect evidence. Nutrition guidance, drug-induced liver injury guidelines and paediatric consensus complement them.

**Teaching points**
- International experts formulated recommendations on adult acute and acute-on-chronic liver failure in the ICU (neurology, pulmonary and haemodynamic aspects), acknowledging that most rest on low-quality and indirect evidence. [PMID 37052436]
- ESPEN guidelines give practical guidance on assessing nutritional status and nutritional care in liver disease. [PMID 33213977, 30712783, 35014850]
- The Chinese guideline on drug-induced liver injury covers DILI in chronic liver disease, hepatitis reactivation and common culprits such as herbal supplements, anti-tuberculosis and anti-neoplastic drugs. [PMID 38402364, 37248976]
- Indian consensus recommendations address diagnosis and management of paediatric acute liver failure. [PMID 39212863]

**Uncertainties**
- Most ICU recommendations are low-quality evidence.

**MCQ angles**
- Drug-induced liver injury as a cause.
- Multidisciplinary ICU support.

**Case hooks**
- Young patient with paracetamol-like hepatitis and rising INR.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** King's College criteria, N-acetylcysteine, encephalopathy and cerebral oedema management and transplant referral thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30712783: ESPEN guideline on clinical nutrition in liver disease (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 33213977: ESPEN practical guideline: Clinical nutrition in liver disease (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 35014850: [ESPEN Practical Guideline: clinical nutrition in liver disease] (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37052436: Guidelines for the Management of Adult Acute and Acute-on-Chronic Liver Failure in the ICU: Neurology, Peri-Transplant Medicine, Infectious Disease, and Gastroenterology Considerations (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37248976: [Chinese guideline for diagnosis and management of drug-induced liver injury (2023 version)] (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38402364: Chinese guideline for the diagnosis and treatment of drug-induced liver injury: an update (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39212863: Diagnosis and management of pediatric acute liver failure: consensus recommendations of the Indian Society of Pediatric Gastroenterology, Hepatology, and Nutrition (ISPGHAN) (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `all_adult` (topics 56) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Adult ALL care is guided by ELN 2024 recommendations for diagnosis and prognostic assessment, and ASH 2026 guidelines for frontline and relapsed disease. Transplant in first remission is not routine but may suit higher-risk or poor responders, and targeted agents are increasingly used.

**Teaching points**
- ELN 2024 recommendations cover diagnosis, prognostic factors and assessment of adult ALL as guidance for initial management. [PMID 38295337]
- The ASH 2026 frontline guideline says transplant is not routinely recommended in first remission but may be indicated for higher-risk or poorly responding patients, and targeted agents are increasingly supported. [PMID 41670627]
- The ASH 2026 relapsed/refractory guideline addresses T-cell ALL, CNS relapse and allogeneic transplant consolidation, with attention to the AYA population. [PMID 41670624]
- A Chinese guideline standardises diagnosis and treatment of adult ALL. [PMID 38964915]
- A consensus definition of B-cell recovery after CD19 CAR T-cell therapy aims to standardise post-CAR T surveillance. [PMID 41367923]
- A practice guideline covers preparation for CAR T-cell therapy in children and young adults with B-ALL. [PMID 38488312]
- Higher VEGF-A expression is associated with poorer clinical outcomes in ALL. [PMID 40944395]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal use of targeted agents in frontline therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Transplant in first remission is risk-adapted.
- CAR T in relapsed B-ALL.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with B-ALL and Philadelphia chromosome.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Induction regimens (paediatric-inspired), blinatumomab and inotuzumab use, MRD thresholds and CNS prophylaxis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38295337: Diagnosis, prognostic factors, and assessment of ALL in adults: 2024 ELN recommendations from a European expert panel (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38488312: Practice guideline: Preparation for CAR T-cell therapy in children and young adults with B-acute lymphoblastic leukaemia (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38964915: [Chinese guideline for diagnosis and treatment of adult acute lymphoblastic leukemia (2024)] (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40944395: Update Prognostic Potency of Vascular Endothelial Growth Factor-A in the Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia Landscape: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41367923: Harmonization on defining B-cell recovery post CD19-CAR T-cell therapy in B-cell acute lymphoblastic leukemia: An international consensus statement (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41670624: American Society of Hematology 2026 guidelines for management of relapsed/refractory acute lymphoblastic leukemia in adolescents and young adults (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41670627: American Society of Hematology 2026 guidelines for frontline management of acute lymphoblastic leukemia in adolescents and young adults (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `ms_relapse` (topics 57) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Oral and intravenous steroids show no clear efficacy difference for acute MS relapse in adults, though oral steroids cause more insomnia. Relapse recovery is often incomplete, with relapse severity the strongest predictor.

**Teaching points**
- In adults with acute MS relapse, there were no clear differences in efficacy and overall tolerability between oral and intravenous steroids, with insomnia associated with the oral route (RR 1.25). [PMID 28492970]
- Relapse recovery is often incomplete, and relapse severity is the strongest and most consistent predictor of incomplete recovery. [PMID 40398064]
- Low or medium-dose vitamin D supplementation significantly reduced annualised relapse rate, whereas high-dose supplementation had no significant effect. [PMID 41551317]
- Relapse rates fall during pregnancy, and assisted reproductive technology does not independently increase relapse risk. [PMID 32521483, 42854666]
- COVID-19 infection does not appear to influence MS relapse rate, and vaccination benefits outweigh the small relapse risk. [PMID 37979408, 36029752]

**Uncertainties**
- Plasma exchange for steroid-refractory relapse is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Oral versus intravenous steroids.
- Predictors of incomplete recovery.

**Case hooks**
- MS patient with new weakness and optic neuritis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** High-dose methylprednisolone regimens, plasma exchange criteria and infection exclusion are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28492970: Oral and intravenous steroids for multiple sclerosis relapse: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 32521483: Change in pregnancy-associated multiple sclerosis relapse rates over time: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 36029752: Multiple sclerosis relapse after COVID-19 vaccination: A case report-based systematic review (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 37979408: The effect of COVID-19 on Multiple Sclerosis relapse: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40398064: Multiple sclerosis relapse incomplete recovery and associated factors - a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41551317: Vitamin D deficiency and multiple sclerosis relapse: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 42854666: Challenging historical associations and reassuring women: an updated meta-analysis of multiple sclerosis relapse risk following assisted reproductive technology (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `aml` (topics 58, 59) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** For older adults with AML, ASH 2025 guidelines favour treatment over supportive care, venetoclax-based regimens over monotherapy and FLT3 inhibitors with induction. MRD assessment is harmonised by ELN-DAVID, and DDX41 and TP53 mutations carry distinct prognoses.

**Teaching points**
- ASH 2025 guidelines for older adults recommend treatment over best supportive care, venetoclax-based regimens over monotherapies, and FLT3 inhibitors in induction and post-remission therapy. [PMID 41321225]
- The 2025 ELN-DAVID MRD update formulated 56 recommendations, 53 with at least 90% consensus, to harmonise MRD assessment. [PMID 41397238]
- An international expert panel issued consensus recommendations on diagnosis and management of paediatric AML. [PMID 41105890]
- TP53 mutations strongly predict poorer overall and relapse-free survival in adult AML. [PMID 42199193]
- DDX41 mutation is associated with significantly good overall survival in myeloid neoplasms. [PMID 40257479]
- G-CSF priming with intensive induction is not universally effective in AML. [PMID 38755516]
- Elevated serum ferritin before treatment is associated with poor AML outcomes. [PMID 39205594]

**Uncertainties**
- Which patients benefit from G-CSF priming.

**MCQ angles**
- Venetoclax with hypomethylating agent in unfit patients.
- Prognostic genes (TP53, DDX41).

**Case hooks**
- Older adult unfit for intensive chemotherapy with newly diagnosed AML.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Induction regimens (7+3), cytogenetic risk groups, transplant indications and differentiation syndrome are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38755516: Effectiveness and safety of granulocyte colony-stimulating factor priming regimen for acute myeloid leukemia: A systematic review and meta-analysis of the Clinical Practice Guideline for the use of G-CSF 2022 from the Japan Society of Clinical Oncology (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39205594: Impact of Serum Ferritin and Iron Overload on Acute Myeloid Leukemia Outcomes: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40257479: Clinicopathological and prognostic significance of DDX41 mutation in myeloid neoplasms: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41105890: Diagnosis and management of AML in pediatric patients: consensus recommendations from an international expert panel (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41321225: American Society of Hematology 2025 guidelines for treating newly diagnosed acute myeloid leukemia in older adults (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41397238: 2025 update on MRD in acute myeloid leukemia: a consensus document from the ELN-DAVID MRD Working Party (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42199193: Prognosis of Acute Myeloid Leukemia Based on TP53 Mutation Among Adult Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `myocarditis` (topics 60, 61) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cardiac magnetic resonance is the key non-invasive test for acute myocarditis: mapping techniques match or exceed the Lake Louise criteria, and late gadolinium enhancement is an independent prognostic marker. Genetic variants are common, and steroid use in COVID-19 myocarditis is individualised.

**Teaching points**
- Novel CMR mapping techniques provide high diagnostic accuracy in acute myocarditis and are promising successors of classic Lake Louise elements, with only T1 mapping surpassing them. [PMID 29454761]
- Extracellular volume and native T1 and T2 mapping give diagnostic performance comparable to Lake Louise criteria, and native T1 had significantly better sensitivity. [PMID 30012826]
- Late gadolinium enhancement on baseline CMR is an independent prognostic marker of adverse cardiac outcomes in acute myocarditis. [PMID 33441003]
- Pathogenic variants in cardiomyopathy genes were found in a large proportion of acute myocarditis patients (58% of adults and 71% of children in the cited analysis), varying with age and presentation. [PMID 38573261]
- A European and US expert consensus describes the role of histology in defining myocarditis subtypes with prognostic and therapeutic implications. [PMID 33176455]
- In COVID-19 myocarditis outcomes with steroid therapy appeared favourable, but treatment should be individualised until larger studies exist. [PMID 32847728]
- Temporary mechanical circulatory support can bridge myocarditis patients in cardiogenic shock to durable LVAD. [PMID 28472406]

**Uncertainties**
- Immunosuppression indications beyond biopsy-proven subtypes.

**MCQ angles**
- CMR mapping versus Lake Louise.
- LGE as a prognostic marker.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with chest pain, raised troponin and normal coronaries.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Dallas criteria, endomyocardial biopsy indications, activity restriction and immune checkpoint-inhibitor myocarditis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28472406: Short-term mechanical circulatory support as a bridge to durable left ventricular assist device implantation in refractory cardiogenic shock: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29454761: Diagnostic Accuracy of Cardiovascular Magnetic Resonance in Acute Myocarditis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 30012826: Diagnostic Performance of Extracellular Volume, Native T1, and T2 Mapping Versus Lake Louise Criteria by Cardiac Magnetic Resonance for Detection of Acute Myocarditis: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 32847728: Systematic Review of COVID-19 Related Myocarditis: Insights on Management and Outcome (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 33176455: Management of Acute Myocarditis and Chronic Inflammatory Cardiomyopathy: An Expert Consensus Document (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 33441003: Prognostic Impact of Late Gadolinium Enhancement by Cardiovascular Magnetic Resonance in Myocarditis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 38573261: Prevalence of Pathogenic Variants in Cardiomyopathy-Associated Genes in Acute Myocarditis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `pancreatitis` (topics 62, 64, 65, 66) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Lactated Ringer's solution may reduce progression to moderate-to-severe acute pancreatitis compared with saline. CRP is a useful severity marker, early nutrition is recommended when oral intake is insufficient, and hypertriglyceridaemia-induced pancreatitis has a poorer prognosis.

**Teaching points**
- Lactated Ringer's solution versus saline resuscitation reduced progression to moderate-to-severe acute pancreatitis, with no notable differences in other complications. [PMID 40085761]
- CRP has significant value as a biomarker for acute pancreatitis severity, but history, signs and imaging should also be considered. [PMID 39417604]
- A clinical nutrition guideline recommends nutrition within 72 hours of admission in all patients in whom oral intake does not cover 60% of protein-calorie requirements. [PMID 40035369]
- Hypertriglyceridaemia-induced acute pancreatitis is more frequent in Eastern countries, has poorer prognosis than other causes and may warrant aggressive management. [PMID 40550999]
- Ulinastatin, alone or combined, was associated with improved outcomes and reduced mortality in severe acute pancreatitis. [PMID 40890662]
- The Ibero-Latin American guideline (iLATAM-AP) covers medical, endoscopic and surgical approaches to acute pancreatitis. [PMID 42216617]
- Postoperative hyperamylasaemia and post-pancreatectomy acute pancreatitis definitions stratify risk of complications. [PMID 40435914]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of ulinastatin outside trials.

**MCQ angles**
- Lactated Ringer's versus saline.
- Early enteral nutrition.

**Case hooks**
- Gallstone pancreatitis patient requiring fluid and nutrition plan.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, severity scores, ERCP and cholecystectomy timing, and necrosis step-up approach are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39417604: Diagnostic value of CRP for predicting the severity of acute pancreatitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40035369: Clinical nutrition in gastrointestinal diseases: an up-to-date clinical practice guideline (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40085761: Lactated Ringer's solution versus saline fluid resuscitation for reducing progression to moderate-to-severe acute pancreatitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40435914: Clinical implications of postoperative hyperamylasemia and postpancreatectomy acute pancreatitis after pancreatectomy: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40550999: Global burden and characterization of hypertriglyceridemia-induced acute pancreatitis: results from a systematic review and a multi-center cohort study (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40890662: Ulinastatin for the treatment of severe acute pancreatitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 42216617: AEG-AESPANC-OPGE-SIED-SPG Ibero-Latin American Guidelines on Acute Pancreatitis (iLATAM-AP) (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `aom` (topics 63) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Antibiotic prescribing for acute otitis media often departs from guidelines in agent and duration, and bundled interventions improve guideline-concordant durations. Tympanostomy tube guidance recommends against tubes after a single short episode of effusion.

**Teaching points**
- Non-guideline-concordant antibiotic treatment of acute otitis media in children is common, including broad-spectrum agents (amoxicillin-clavulanate 12%, azithromycin 7%) and longer than recommended durations. [PMID 36269007]
- A bundled intervention improved guideline-concordant antibiotic durations for AOM more than an EHR-only intervention. [PMID 34274307]
- An Italian intersociety consensus addresses antibiotic treatment of acute and recurrent otitis media in children. [PMID 39980042]
- A review of European paediatric AOM guidelines found variable quality and limited reference to local resistance patterns, favouring a core guideline adapted by country. [PMID 32371515]
- The tympanostomy tube guideline update says not to insert tubes for a single episode of otitis media with effusion under 3 months, and to reassess chronic effusion every 3 to 6 months. [PMID 35138954, 35138976]
- The prevalence of multi-drug-resistant Haemophilus influenzae is increasing worldwide. [PMID 38225571]

**Uncertainties**
- Local resistance patterns determine first-line choice.

**MCQ angles**
- Antibiotic duration in children.
- When to place tympanostomy tubes.

**Case hooks**
- Toddler with fever, ear pain and a bulging tympanic membrane.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Watchful waiting criteria, amoxicillin dosing, diagnostic otoscopy signs and complications (mastoiditis) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32371515: Clinical practice guidelines for acute otitis media in children: a systematic review and appraisal of European national guidelines (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 34274307: Increasing Guideline-Concordant Durations of Antibiotic Therapy for Acute Otitis Media (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 35138954: Clinical Practice Guideline: Tympanostomy Tubes in Children (Update) (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 35138976: Executive Summary of Clinical Practice Guideline on Tympanostomy Tubes in Children (Update) (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36269007: Utilization of nonguideline concordant antibiotic treatment following acute otitis media in children in the United States (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38225571: First global report about the prevalence of multi-drug resistant Haemophilus influenzae: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39980042: Antibiotic treatment of acute and recurrent otitis media in children: an Italian intersociety Consensus (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `apl` (topics 69) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** APL is a highly curable but urgent leukaemia: prompt recognition avoids early death. ATRA plus arsenic trioxide may be preferred to ATRA plus chemotherapy, particularly in low-to-intermediate risk disease, and FLT3 mutations and obesity predict worse outcomes.

**Teaching points**
- Brazilian consensus guidelines describe APL as highly curable through differentiation therapy, yet a serious and urgent condition needing prompt recognition and management to avoid early mortality. [PMID 38890097]
- A meta-analysis indicated ATRA plus arsenic trioxide may be preferred to ATRA plus chemotherapy, especially in low-to-intermediate risk APL, with larger trials needed in high-risk disease. [PMID 27391027]
- International recommendations address treatment of APL in older patients, highlighting consensus, gaps and research opportunities. [PMID 32273246]
- FLT3-ITD and FLT3-D835 mutations are associated with higher presenting white cell count and poorer prognosis in APL. [PMID 31492033]
- High BMI predicts adverse outcomes in APL, with a higher risk of differentiation syndrome (HR 1.53). [PMID 29269861]
- Japanese DIC guidelines address anticoagulants in DIC, weakly recommending against heparin and serine protease inhibitors beyond selected options. [PMID 39674834]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal therapy in high-risk APL.

**MCQ angles**
- Haemorrhage and DIC at presentation.
- ATRA plus ATO.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with pancytopenia, bleeding and coagulopathy with promyelocytes on film.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Differentiation syndrome recognition and treatment, immediate ATRA on suspicion, transfusion targets and the PML-RARA diagnostic test are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27391027: All-Trans Retinoic Acid plus Arsenic Trioxide versus All-Trans Retinoic Acid plus Chemotherapy for Newly Diagnosed Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 29269861: Influence of body mass index on incidence and prognosis of acute myeloid leukemia and acute promyelocytic leukemia: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31492033: The Impact of <i>Flt3</i> Gene Mutations in Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 32273246: Treatment of acute promyelocytic leukemia in older patients: recommendations of an International Society of Geriatric Oncology (SIOG) task force (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 38890097: Diagnosis and management of acute promyelocytic leukemia: Brazilian consensus guidelines 2024 on behalf of the Brazilian Association of Hematology, Hemotherapy and Cellular Therapy (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39674834: Clinical practice guidelines for management of disseminated intravascular coagulation in Japan 2024. Part 2: hematologic malignancy (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `acute_psychosis` (topics 70) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** After two antipsychotics fail, a trial of clozapine is supported. Metformin can prevent antipsychotic-induced weight gain, shorter duration of untreated psychosis relates to outcomes, and sex-sensitive prescribing guidance exists for females.

**Teaching points**
- Findings support a trial of clozapine after two other antipsychotics prove ineffective. [PMID 38493023]
- A consensus-validated guideline recommends metformin for preventing antipsychotic-induced weight gain, including co-commencement. [PMID 39657713]
- A guideline on the first antipsychotic for females in first-episode psychosis supports safer, sex-sensitive prescribing. [PMID 41793755]
- The US VA/DoD guideline synopsis gives pharmacological and somatic treatment recommendations for first-episode psychosis and schizophrenia, with many decision points lacking data. [PMID 40580548]
- Longer duration of untreated psychosis correlates with poorer outcomes, though whether it causes them needs study and a consensus definition of long DUP is needed. [PMID 39580760]
- Treatment strategies for antipsychotic-induced akathisia rest on low to very low confidence evidence. [PMID 38869177]
- An appraisal of early intervention in psychosis guidelines found a need for more methodologically robust and implementation-oriented guidance. [PMID 42318093]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for add-on agents against akathisia is weak.

**MCQ angles**
- Clozapine after two failed antipsychotics.
- Metformin for antipsychotic weight gain.

**Case hooks**
- First-episode psychosis in a young woman choosing an antipsychotic.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Acute behavioural disturbance, rapid tranquillisation, risk assessment and legal framework are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38493023: Response rates to sequential trials of antipsychotic medications according to algorithms or treatment guidelines in psychotic disorders. A systematic review and meta-analysis (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38869177: Comparative Efficacy and Acceptability of Treatment Strategies for Antipsychotic-Induced Akathisia: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 39580760: "Short" Versus "Long" Duration of Untreated Psychosis in People with First-Episode Psychosis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Baseline Status and Follow-Up Outcomes (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39657713: Metformin for the Prevention of Antipsychotic-Induced Weight Gain: Guideline Development and Consensus Validation (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40580548: Pharmacological and Somatic Treatments for First-Episode Psychosis and Schizophrenia: Synopsis of the US Department of Veterans Affairs and US Department of Defense Clinical Practice Guidelines (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41793755: Clinical Practice Guideline on the Choice of First Antipsychotic Medicine for Females Experiencing a First-Episode of Psychosis (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42318093: Clinical practice guidelines and quality standards for early intervention in psychosis: an AGREE II appraisal and systematic review of service components (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `pulm_oedema` (topics 71) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence concerns respiratory support in acute hypoxaemic failure rather than cardiogenic pulmonary oedema specifically: high-flow nasal oxygen probably reduces intubation versus standard oxygen, helmet NIV may reduce intubation, and HFNC may be inferior to NIV in hypercapnic COPD exacerbations.

**Teaching points**
- In acute hypoxaemic respiratory failure, high-flow nasal cannula probably reduces the risk of invasive mechanical ventilation and may be as comfortable as standard oxygen. [PMID 37085046]
- Compared with facemask NIV, helmet NIV may reduce mortality and intubation, while its effect versus high-flow nasal cannula remains uncertain. [PMID 34413155]
- In AECOPD with hypercapnic failure, HFNC was associated with more treatment failure and switch to NIV, which may mask HFNC inferiority. [PMID 40360910]
- The ACP suggests high-flow nasal oxygen rather than conventional oxygen for hospitalised adults with postextubation hypoxaemic respiratory failure (conditional recommendation, low certainty). [PMID 33900796]
- The AARC guideline suggests considering high-flow oxygen immediately after extubation to avoid reintubation and humidification for flows above 4 L/min. [PMID 34728574]
- Sugammadex reversal was associated with fewer postoperative pulmonary complications than neostigmine. [PMID 37081384]

**Uncertainties**
- Direct trials in acute cardiogenic pulmonary oedema are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Helmet NIV and intubation.
- HFNC versus NIV in hypercapnic failure.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with flash pulmonary oedema and hypoxaemia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Cardiogenic pulmonary oedema management (CPAP/NIV evidence, nitrates, diuretics, the morphine controversy) is not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

**Sources**
- PMID 33900796: Appropriate Use of High-Flow Nasal Oxygen in Hospitalized Patients for Initial or Postextubation Management of Acute Respiratory Failure: A Clinical Guideline From the American College of Physicians (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34413155: Helmet noninvasive ventilation compared to facemask noninvasive ventilation and high-flow nasal cannula in acute respiratory failure: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 34728574: AARC Clinical Practice Guideline: Management of Adult Patients With Oxygen in the Acute Care Setting (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37081384: Postoperative pulmonary complications after sugammadex reversal of neuromuscular blockade: a systematic review and meta-analysis with trial sequential analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37085046: Noninvasive Oxygenation Strategies in Adult Patients With Acute Hypoxemic Respiratory Failure: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 40360910: High flow nasal therapy versus noninvasive ventilation for AECOPD with acute hypercapnic respiratory failure: a meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `resp_failure` (topics 73) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Non-invasive support is central in acute respiratory failure: NIV works especially in hypercapnic failure and cardiogenic pulmonary oedema, high-flow nasal oxygen suits hypoxaemic and post-extubation failure, and awake proning helps in COVID-19. Preoxygenation technique prolongs safe apnoea time.

**Teaching points**
- Guidelines on non-invasive mechanical ventilation in acute respiratory failure support its use in hypercapnic respiratory failure and pulmonary oedema, including in the prehospital setting, and in paediatric ICU patients with monitoring. [PMID 37832578]
- The ACP suggests high-flow nasal oxygen rather than conventional oxygen for hospitalised adults with postextubation acute hypoxaemic respiratory failure (conditional recommendation, low certainty). [PMID 33900796]
- A rapid practice guideline recommends a trial of awake prone positioning in COVID-19 acute hypoxaemic respiratory failure, with monitoring by staff experienced in recognising deterioration. [PMID 36691710]
- A network meta-analysis found head-up position likely the most effective technique to prolong safe apnoea time during preoxygenation. [PMID 38599916]
- A 2026 guideline covers acute respiratory insufficiency treated with invasive ventilation and extracorporeal support, conveying where certainty of evidence is limited. [PMID 41379760]
- The AARC patient-ventilator assessment guideline gives a strong, high-certainty recommendation on measures to decrease ventilator-associated pneumonia risk. [PMID 39048148]
- The ESICM guideline on fluid therapy in critically ill adults gives eleven recommendations on resuscitation fluid choice. [PMID 38771364]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of HFNC versus NIV in hypercapnic failure.

**MCQ angles**
- NIV in hypercapnic failure and pulmonary oedema.
- Awake proning in COVID-19.

**Case hooks**
- COPD patient with respiratory acidosis on arrival.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Type 1 versus type 2 failure definitions, oxygen targets, intubation criteria and ventilator settings are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33900796: Appropriate Use of High-Flow Nasal Oxygen in Hospitalized Patients for Initial or Postextubation Management of Acute Respiratory Failure: A Clinical Guideline From the American College of Physicians (guideline, 2021)

## Cluster `renal_rejection` (topics 76) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Antibody-mediated rejection treatment is largely expert opinion; plasmapheresis and IVIG are the standard of care for acute AMR while the role of bortezomib and complement inhibitors is unclear. Post-transplant DSA assessment and ABO-incompatible transplantation have defined consensus and outcome data.

**Teaching points**
- The 2019 expert consensus on AMR after kidney transplantation notes recommendations are largely expert opinion and that powered trials of plausible agents are urgently needed. [PMID 31895348]
- Plasmapheresis and IVIG have become standard of care for acute AMR despite uncertain evidence, while the efficacy of bortezomib and complement inhibitors remains unclear. [PMID 29315141]
- Clinical recommendations address posttransplant assessment of anti-HLA donor-specific antibodies and identify gaps in current knowledge. [PMID 36695614]
- ABO-incompatible kidney transplant recipients have good outcomes, albeit inferior to centre-matched ABO-compatible controls, with increased bleeding. [PMID 30012630]
- ISHLT consensus defines antibody-mediated rejection of the lung, which may be clinical or subclinical. [PMID 27044531]
- An ISHLT consensus conference on emerging antibody and AMR science lays a platform for future studies. [PMID 40498433]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal AMR therapy beyond plasmapheresis and IVIG.

**MCQ angles**
- AMR treatment: plasmapheresis and IVIG.
- Donor-specific antibody monitoring.

**Case hooks**
- Kidney transplant recipient with rising creatinine and new DSA.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** T-cell mediated rejection treatment (pulse steroids, ATG), Banff classification and biopsy indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27044531: Antibody-mediated rejection of the lung: A consensus report of the International Society for Heart and Lung Transplantation (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 29315141: The Treatment of Antibody-Mediated Rejection in Kidney Transplantation: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 30012630: ABO-Incompatible Kidney Transplant Outcomes: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 31895348: Recommended Treatment for Antibody-mediated Rejection After Kidney Transplantation: The 2019 Expert Consensus From the Transplantion Society Working Group (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 36695614: Clinical recommendations for posttransplant assessment of anti-HLA (Human Leukocyte Antigen) donor-specific antibodies: A Sensitization in Transplantation: Assessment of Risk consensus document (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 40498433: Summary of the International Society for Heart and Lung Transplantation consensus conference on emerging understanding of antibodies and antibody-mediated rejection in heart transplantation (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `diverticulitis` (topics 77) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** EAES/SAGES and WSES guidelines guide acute diverticulitis management; colorectal cancer prevalence is 1.9% after acute diverticulitis and higher after complicated disease. Abscesses are treated non-operatively first, with surgery reserved for failure.

**Teaching points**
- The joint EAES and SAGES consensus conference provides evidence-based recommendations for acute diverticulitis management, including after resolution of non-complicated disease. [PMID 31250244]
- WSES guidelines address management of acute left-sided colonic diverticulitis in the emergency setting. [PMID 27478494]
- The Fiesole Consensus report gives global guidelines on diverticular disease and highlights research priorities including selective antimicrobial and surgical strategies. [PMID 41443984]
- The pooled prevalence of colorectal cancer after acute diverticulitis is 1.9%, significantly higher after complicated than uncomplicated diverticulitis. [PMID 30056181]
- For diverticular abscess, surgery should be reserved for critically ill patients failing non-operative treatment, with trials needed for different abscess sizes. [PMID 27741423]
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria address imaging of left lower quadrant pain with suspected diverticulitis. [PMID 31054740]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of selective antibiotic use in uncomplicated disease.

**MCQ angles**
- Colonoscopy after complicated diverticulitis.
- Non-operative management of abscess.

**Case hooks**
- Left iliac fossa pain and fever in a 60-year-old.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Antibiotic-free management, CT classification (Hinchey), percutaneous drainage size thresholds and elective surgery indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27478494: WSES Guidelines for the management of acute left sided colonic diverticulitis in the emergency setting (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 27741423: Treatment of patients with acute colonic diverticulitis complicated by abscess formation: A systematic review (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 30056181: Risk of Colorectal Cancer in Patients With Acute Diverticulitis: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis of Observational Studies (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 31054740: ACR Appropriateness Criteria<sup>®</sup> Left Lower Quadrant Pain-Suspected Diverticulitis (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 31250244: EAES and SAGES 2018 consensus conference on acute diverticulitis management: evidence-based recommendations for clinical practice (guideline, 2019)

## Cluster `adhd` (topics 78) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The Australian evidence-based ADHD guideline (2023) supports implementation of evidence-based treatment. Pharmacological treatment improves quality of life, stimulants carry increased occurrence of psychosis and bipolar disorder needing monitoring, and deprescribing stimulants is considered with substance misuse, contraindications or diagnostic inaccuracy.

**Teaching points**
- The Australian evidence-based clinical practice guideline for ADHD aims to increase delivery of evidence-based treatment and improve outcomes. [PMID 37254562]
- Pharmacological treatment of ADHD improves overall quality of life, not only symptoms, in a meta-analysis. [PMID 38823477]
- Psychosis and bipolar disorder occur more often in people with ADHD exposed to stimulants, so clinicians should inform patients and monitor. [PMID 40900605]
- An ASCP task force consensus identifies reasons to consider deprescribing stimulants in adult ADHD, including substance misuse, comorbid contraindications and diagnostic inaccuracy. [PMID 42160906]
- ADHD in pregnancy and postpartum can be managed with preconception counselling and perinatal planning. [PMID 38432409]
- Asian regional ADHD guidelines for medication in children differ from Western guidelines. [PMID 38059346]
- A quality appraisal of ADHD guidelines helps providers select guidelines. [PMID 40589651]
- Predictors of adult psychiatric outcomes of childhood ADHD were pooled in a meta-analysis. [PMID 40287009]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term outcome predictors.

**MCQ angles**
- Stimulant monitoring for psychosis.
- ADHD in pregnancy.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with ADHD on stimulant who develops psychotic symptoms.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** First-line drug choice by age, non-stimulant options and behavioural therapy details are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37254562: The Australian evidence-based clinical practice guideline for attention deficit hyperactivity disorder (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38059346: Practical clinical guidelines and pharmacological treatment for attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder in Asia (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38432409: Attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder in pregnancy and the postpartum period (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38823477: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis: Effects of Pharmacological Treatment for Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder on Quality of Life (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40287009: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis: Predictors of Adult Psychiatric Outcomes of Childhood Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 40589651: Quality appraisal of clinical practice guidelines for attention deficit hyperactivity disorder: a systematic review using the appraisal of guidelines for research and evaluation (AGREE II) instrument (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40900605: Occurrence of Psychosis and Bipolar Disorder in Individuals With Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder Treated With Stimulants: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 42160906: The American Society of Clinical Psychopharmacology task force consensus statement on the deprescribing of stimulant medications in adults with ADHD&lt;sup&gt;✰&lt;/sup&gt; (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `sbo` (topics 79) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Adhesive small bowel obstruction is managed non-operatively where possible: water-soluble contrast guidelines reduce readmission, CT predicts which cases resolve, and a laparoscopic approach is feasible in selected patients. Mesenteric defect closure after gastric bypass cuts later obstruction.

**Teaching points**
- Implementing a water-soluble contrast guideline for adhesive small bowel obstruction was feasible; rates of immediate operation increased and readmission rates decreased. [PMID 36104043]
- Predictors including CT findings identify which small bowel obstructions resolve without operation, and multivariable models for management should incorporate them. [PMID 37938387]
- Laparoscopic versus open adhesiolysis for adhesive obstruction needs better patient selection for the laparoscopic approach. [PMID 32195994]
- Closing mesenteric defects during laparoscopic Roux-en-Y gastric bypass reduces internal hernia and reoperation for small bowel obstruction (2% versus 10%). [PMID 37162714]
- A Chinese expert consensus covers diagnosis and treatment of small bowel obstruction. [PMID 37217345]
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria cover imaging of suspected small-bowel obstruction. [PMID 32370974]

**Uncertainties**
- Selection for laparoscopic adhesiolysis.

**MCQ angles**
- Water-soluble contrast as diagnostic and therapeutic aid.
- Internal hernia after gastric bypass.

**Case hooks**
- Previous laparotomy, vomiting and distension with no flatus.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Timing of surgery, signs of strangulation, nasogastric decompression and fluid resuscitation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32195994: Laparoscopic versus open approach for adhesive small bowel obstruction, a systematic review and meta-analysis of short term outcomes (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 32370974: ACR Appropriateness Criteria® Suspected Small-Bowel Obstruction (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 36104043: Water-soluble contrast in the management of adhesive small-bowel obstruction: a Canadian centre's experience with guideline development and implementation (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37162714: Mesenteric Defect Closure and the Rate of Internal Hernia in Laparoscopic Roux-en-Y Gastric Bypass: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37217345: [Chinese expert consensus on the diagnosis and treatment of small bowel obstruction (2023 edition)] (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37938387: Prognostic factors to identify resolution of small bowel obstruction without need for operative management: systematic review (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `adrenal_insuff` (topics 80, 83, 84) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Glucocorticoid-induced adrenal insufficiency is covered by a joint ESE/Endocrine Society guideline, and paediatric emergency and perioperative guidance exists. In septic shock, adding fludrocortisone to hydrocortisone may help but evidence is not definitive.

**Teaching points**
- The first joint ESE/Endocrine Society guideline gives guidance on diagnosis and therapy of glucocorticoid-induced adrenal insufficiency in patients on chronic glucocorticoid therapy. [PMID 38724043, 38714321]
- British Society for Paediatric Endocrinology guidance sets out management of intercurrent illness and procedures to allow timely recognition and treatment of adrenal insufficiency and crisis in children. [PMID 37045585]
- Fludrocortisone added to hydrocortisone in adult septic shock may benefit patients, but findings are not definitive given limited certainty and wide NNT range. [PMID 38156911]
- Timing of hydrocortisone in neonates with shock is supported only by very low certainty evidence. [PMID 40144277]
- PD-1 inhibitors carry risk of endocrine immune-related adverse events, so awareness of risk features is needed. [PMID 38756658]
- After trans-sphenoidal surgery, routine postoperative cortisol testing is recommended in Cushing disease. [PMID 40815128]

**Uncertainties**
- Relative adrenal insufficiency in critical illness is not clearly covered.

**MCQ angles**
- Glucocorticoid-induced adrenal insufficiency.
- Sick-day rules in children.

**Case hooks**
- Patient on long-term prednisolone presenting with vomiting and hypotension.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Emergency hydrocortisone dose (100 mg IV), triggers, ACTH stimulation testing and stress-dose rules are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37045585: Emergency and perioperative management of adrenal insufficiency in children and young people: British Society for Paediatric Endocrinology and Diabetes consensus guidance (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38156911: Do We Need to Administer Fludrocortisone in Addition to Hydrocortisone in Adult Patients With Septic Shock? An Updated Systematic Review With Bayesian Network Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials and an Observational Study With Target Trial Emulation (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38714321: European Society of Endocrinology and Endocrine Society Joint Clinical Guideline: Diagnosis and therapy of glucocorticoid-induced adrenal insufficiency (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38724043: European Society of Endocrinology and Endocrine Society Joint Clinical Guideline: Diagnosis and Therapy of Glucocorticoid-induced Adrenal Insufficiency (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38756658: The risk of endocrine immune-related adverse events induced by PD-1 inhibitors in cancer patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40144277: Timing of hydrocortisone therapy in neonates with shock: a systematic review, meta-analysis, and clinical practice guideline (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40815128: Congress of Neurological Surgeons Systematic Review and Evidence-Based Guidelines for the Role of Medical Perioperative Management for Patients With Functioning Pituitary Adenomas (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `adrenal_incid` (topics 81, 82) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The ESE guideline and national guidelines direct adrenal incidentaloma work-up (imaging, hormonal function, malignancy risk), though guidelines are discrepant and referral is poor. Mild autonomous cortisol secretion is linked to comorbidities, but causality is unproven.

**Teaching points**
- The ESE guideline includes recommendations for patients who do not undergo surgery, for bilateral incidentalomas, extra-adrenal malignancy with adrenal masses, and young and elderly patients. [PMID 27390021]
- The CUA guideline provides contemporary clinical, radiographic and endocrine assessment for evaluation, management and follow-up of incidentally discovered adrenal masses. [PMID 37556768]
- Guidelines for adrenal incidentaloma management are discrepant, and best practice lies at their convergence. [PMID 32856984]
- Mild autonomous cortisol secretion is associated with comorbidities, but small randomised trials prevent strong conclusions on causality or the effect of treatment. [PMID 37801655]
- Outpatient referral for adrenal incidentalomas is low, and radiology report language significantly influenced referral rates. [PMID 34243951]
- The 2008 Endocrine Society recommendations on whom to screen for Cushing syndrome remain valid in 2022. [PMID 35730067]
- A Delphi process proposed 20 critical recommendations from an initial 47 for adrenal incidentaloma management. [PMID 28685511]

**Uncertainties**
- Management of mild autonomous cortisol secretion.

**MCQ angles**
- Biochemical work-up of incidentalomas.
- Guideline discrepancies.

**Case hooks**
- Incidental 3 cm adrenal mass on CT for abdominal pain.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Hounsfield unit thresholds, size cut-offs, dexamethasone suppression and phaeochromocytoma exclusion are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27390021: Management of adrenal incidentalomas: European Society of Endocrinology Clinical Practice Guideline in collaboration with the European Network for the Study of Adrenal Tumors (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 28685511: Clinical Guidelines for the Management of Adrenal Incidentaloma (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 32856984: Discrepancies in the Recommended Management of Adrenal Incidentalomas by Various Guidelines (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34243951: Radiology report language positively influences adrenal incidentaloma guideline adherence (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 35730067: Whom Should We Screen for Cushing Syndrome? The Endocrine Society Practice Guideline Recommendations 2008 Revisited (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37556768: Diagnosis, Management, and Follow-Up of the Incidentally Discovered Adrenal Mass: CUA Guideline Endorsed by the AUA (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37801655: Comorbidities in mild autonomous cortisol secretion and the effect of treatment: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `acc` (topics 85) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Adrenocortical carcinoma management rests on surgery, perioperative recommendations from ESES/ENSAT and mitotane, with cortisol excess linked to worse survival. Adrenal biopsy should be limited and only after excluding catecholamine-secreting tumours.

**Teaching points**
- ESES/ENSAT recommendations provide evidence-based advice on perioperative care for patients undergoing surgery for adrenocortical carcinoma, with prospective studies urgently needed. [PMID 28199015]
- Cortisol-secreting adrenocortical carcinoma is associated with worse overall survival, whether as an aggressive subtype or a prognostic marker. [PMID 30325179]
- Adrenal biopsy should be done only if findings are likely to alter management and after biochemical exclusion of catecholamine-producing tumours. [PMID 27257146]
- An international consensus provides guidance on mitotane treatment in paediatric adrenocortical tumours. [PMID 38552173]
- Minimally invasive approaches to adrenalectomy are safe, chosen according to surgeon training and experience. [PMID 27431446]
- The ESE adrenal incidentaloma guideline addresses adrenal masses in patients with extra-adrenal malignancy. [PMID 27390021]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of adjuvant mitotane in adults is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Exclude phaeochromocytoma before biopsy.
- Cortisol excess and prognosis.

**Case hooks**
- Large heterogeneous adrenal mass with virilisation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** ENSAT staging, Weiss score, Ki-67, mitotane dosing and monitoring and chemotherapy regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27257146: DIAGNOSIS OF ENDOCRINE DISEASE: The diagnostic performance of adrenal biopsy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 27390021: Management of adrenal incidentalomas: European Society of Endocrinology Clinical Practice Guideline in collaboration with the European Network for the Study of Adrenal Tumors (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 27431446: International Consultation on Urological Diseases and European Association of Urology International Consultation on Minimally Invasive Surgery in Urology: laparoscopic and robotic adrenalectomy (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 28199015: European Society of Endocrine Surgeons (ESES) and European Network for the Study of Adrenal Tumours (ENSAT) recommendations for the surgical management of adrenocortical carcinoma (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 30325179: Influence of hormonal functional status on survival in adrenocortical carcinoma: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 38552173: International consensus on mitotane treatment in pediatric patients with adrenal cortical tumors: indications, therapy, and management of adverse effects (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `achd_eisenmenger` (topics 86) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Adult congenital heart disease care follows the 2020 ESC guidelines and the AHA/ACC guideline, which differ in places. Transition from paediatric care, genetic counselling and standardised echocardiography are core components.

**Teaching points**
- The 2020 ESC ACHD guidelines revise or add recommendations for management, percutaneous interventions and pulmonary arterial hypertension, and for the first time address coronary anomalies. [PMID 33258988]
- AHA/ACC and ESC guidelines for adult congenital heart disease agree in key areas but differ in ways that affect patient management. [PMID 34736567]
- A global consensus states adolescents with congenital heart disease should be transferred to adult care providers capable of managing their needs, with systems ensuring continuity. [PMID 34198319]
- A consensus document covers genetic counselling and testing in ACHD, including indications and timing. [PMID 31184212]
- International consensus gives echocardiography protocols for data acquisition and reporting for major adult congenital lesions. [PMID 30017529, 36253815]
- Expert consensus provides diagnostic and treatment algorithms for pulmonary hypertension in children with congenital heart disease. [PMID 27053697]

**Uncertainties**
- Eisenmenger-specific therapy is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Transition to adult care.
- Genetic counselling in ACHD.

**Case hooks**
- Cyanosed adult with an unrepaired large ventricular septal defect.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Eisenmenger pathophysiology and management (avoid venesection, pregnancy contraindication, PAH therapy), and risk stratification are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

The European Paediatric Pulmonary Vascular Disease Network, endorsed by ISHLT and DGPK (guideline, 2016)

- PMID 33258988: [2020 ESC guidelines on treatment of adult congenital heart disease (ACHD)] (guideline, 2021)

## Cluster `aosd` (topics 87, 88, 89) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** EULAR/PReS issued the first consensus recommendations for Still's disease in children and adults. IL-1 and IL-6 blockade are the key biologic options, with TNF inhibitors considered when joint disease predominates; earlier IL-1 treatment may respond better.

**Teaching points**
- The EULAR/PReS recommendations are the first consensus for diagnosis and management of Still's disease across children and adults. [PMID 39317417]
- A Delphi consensus recommends IL-1 inhibition in AOSD refractory to conventional therapy, with data from systemic JIA suggesting better response to earlier treatment. [PMID 31829244]
- An expert consensus treat-to-target statement says another IL-1 inhibitor can follow IL-1 failure and TNF inhibitors may be considered when joint involvement is prominent. [PMID 37482365]
- IL-6 inhibition is effective for several inflammatory diseases with a safety profile broadly comparable to other biologics. [PMID 36260501, 35953263]
- A Delphi panel reached consensus on diagnosis and therapy recommendations for AOSD that can underpin a treatment guideline. [PMID 37669122]
- A Japanese evidence-based guideline for adult Still's disease provides 26 clinical questions with recommendations. [PMID 29651907]
- A systematic review assessed safety and efficacy of IL-1-targeted biologics (anakinra, canakinumab, rilonacept) in immune-mediated disorders. [PMID 35874710]

**Uncertainties**
- Sequencing of IL-1 and IL-6 inhibitors.

**MCQ angles**
- IL-1 blockade in refractory AOSD.
- Macrophage activation syndrome risk (not in excerpts).

**Case hooks**
- Quotidian fever, salmon rash, arthralgia and very high ferritin.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Yamaguchi criteria, ferritin and glycosylated ferritin, steroid use and macrophage activation syndrome are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29651907: Evidence-based clinical practice guideline for adult Still's disease (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 31829244: Management of adult-onset Still's disease with interleukin-1 inhibitors: evidence- and consensus-based statements by a panel of Italian experts (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 35874710: Systematic Review of Safety and Efficacy of IL-1-Targeted Biologics in Treating Immune-Mediated Disorders (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 35953263: Consensus statement on blocking interleukin-6 receptor and interleukin-6 in inflammatory conditions: an update (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 36260501: A systematic literature review informing the consensus statement on efficacy and safety of pharmacological treatment with interleukin-6 pathway inhibition with biological DMARDs in immune-mediated inflammatory diseases (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37482365: Expert consensus on the treatment of patients with adult-onset still's disease with the goal of achieving an early and long-term remission (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37669122: Management of adult-onset Still's disease: evidence- and consensus-based recommendations by experts (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39317417: EULAR/PReS recommendations for the diagnosis and management of Still's disease, comprising systemic juvenile idiopathic arthritis and adult-onset Still's disease (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `septic_shock_steroids` (topics 90, 465) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence centres on vasopressors rather than steroids: vasopressin or analogues added to catecholamines are suggested in distributive shock, and a network meta-analysis found norepinephrine, terlipressin and vasopressin associated with lower 28-day mortality than dopamine. Corticosteroid trial data were not retrieved.

**Teaching points**
- The Canadian Critical Care Society guideline suggests using vasopressin or analogues in addition to catecholamines rather than catecholamines alone in distributive shock (conditional recommendation, low certainty). [PMID 31797234]
- A network meta-analysis found dopamine associated with higher 28-day mortality in septic shock than norepinephrine, terlipressin and vasopressin. [PMID 37548686]
- Vasopressin and glucocorticoids improved return of spontaneous circulation compared with placebo in in-hospital cardiac arrest, with larger trials needed for longer-term outcomes. [PMID 34990764]
- Global expert consensus statements address management of adult sepsis in resource-limited settings. [PMID 39714613]
- A consensus blood transcriptomic framework aligns sepsis subgroupings to assist precision medicine and trial design. [PMID 41028542]

**Uncertainties**
- Corticosteroid trials in septic shock are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Vasopressin as an add-on to norepinephrine.
- Norepinephrine over dopamine.

**Case hooks**
- Septic shock on rising norepinephrine dose.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Hydrocortisone dose and indications, ADRENAL and APROCCHSS trial results and fludrocortisone use are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

**Sources**
- PMID 31797234: Canadian Critical Care Society clinical practice guideline: The use of vasopressin and vasopressin analogues in critically ill adults with distributive shock (guideline, 2020)

## Cluster `melanoma` (topics 91) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** First-line advanced melanoma options include anti-PD-1, nivolumab plus ipilimumab and relatlimab plus nivolumab, which has similar efficacy with a trend to better safety. TIL therapy is a second-line option after anti-PD-1 failure, and combination immunotherapy carries immune-related adverse events.

**Teaching points**
- In a network meta-analysis, relatlimab plus nivolumab showed similar PFS and response rate to ipilimumab plus nivolumab with a trend towards a better safety profile. [PMID 37196485]
- Anti-PD-1 monotherapy and nivolumab plus ipilimumab improved response rate and prolonged PFS in advanced melanoma, with generally manageable adverse events. [PMID 28658143]
- TIL cell therapy should be considered a treatment of choice in second line for metastatic melanoma patients failing anti-PD-(L)1 therapy. [PMID 39053767, 31566658]
- Combination ipilimumab and nivolumab carries more immune-related adverse events, with clinicians aware of gastrointestinal, skin, hyperglycaemic, thyroid, hepatic and musculoskeletal irAEs. [PMID 32117745, 32211869]
- BMI should not currently be recommended as a predictor of survival in melanoma on immune checkpoint inhibitors. [PMID 36871633]
- A consensus on cutaneous melanoma detection and prognosis found no consensus on a defined role for gene expression profile testing. [PMID 36920356]

**Uncertainties**
- Best first-line combination for each patient.

**MCQ angles**
- Dual checkpoint blockade toxicity.
- TIL therapy after anti-PD-1 failure.

**Case hooks**
- BRAF wild-type metastatic melanoma starting first-line immunotherapy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** BRAF/MEK inhibitors, adjuvant therapy, brain metastases and irAE steroid management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28658143: Efficacy and safety of anti-PD-1 and anti-PD-1 combined with anti-CTLA-4 immunotherapy to advanced melanoma: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31566658: Efficacy of adoptive therapy with tumor-infiltrating lymphocytes and recombinant interleukin-2 in advanced cutaneous melanoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 32117745: Potential Immune-Related Adverse Events Associated With Monotherapy and Combination Therapy of Ipilimumab, Nivolumab, and Pembrolizumab for Advanced Melanoma: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 32211869: Immune Checkpoint Inhibitors and Immune-Related Adverse Events in Patients With Advanced Melanoma: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 36871633: Obesity and immune-checkpoint inhibitors in advanced melanoma: A meta-analysis of survival outcomes from clinical studies (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 36920356: Early Detection and Prognostic Assessment of Cutaneous Melanoma: Consensus on Optimal Practice and the Role of Gene Expression Profile Testing (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37196485: Activity and safety of first-line treatments for advanced melanoma: A network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39053767: Efficacy of TIL therapy in advanced cutaneous melanoma in the current immuno-oncology era: updated systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `urothelial` (topics 92) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** For metastatic urothelial carcinoma, enfortumab vedotin with or without pembrolizumab is a key regimen and requires skin toxicity monitoring. Updated EAU, SEOM and AFU guidelines cover staging and perioperative chemo-immunotherapy.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of enfortumab vedotin with or without pembrolizumab in metastatic urothelial carcinoma found clinical benefit also in later lines and stressed personalised approaches. [PMID 40067303]
- Enfortumab vedotin skin toxicities are mostly manageable, but patients should be closely monitored to prevent serious cutaneous adverse events. [PMID 40592676]
- The 2025 SEOM-SOGUG guideline summarises evidence and therapies for urothelial cancer across all stages. [PMID 40960583]
- The AFU 2025 update includes perioperative combination chemotherapy and immunotherapy. [PMID 41110705]
- The 2026 EAU guidelines update diagnosis, treatment and follow-up for non-muscle-invasive bladder cancer with a focus on quality of life. [PMID 42457459]
- The ASTRO guideline gives evidence-based recommendations on radiation therapy for bladder cancer. [PMID 42744092]
- Time-of-day immunotherapy administration was associated with improved outcomes in advanced cancers, warranting prospective evaluation. [PMID 42084869]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal timing of immunotherapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Enfortumab vedotin skin toxicity.
- Perioperative chemo-immunotherapy.

**Case hooks**
- Cisplatin-ineligible metastatic bladder cancer.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** First-line trial results (EV-302), maintenance avelumab, FGFR inhibitors and platinum eligibility criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40067303: Enfortumab Vedotin With or Without Pembrolizumab in Metastatic Urothelial Carcinoma: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40592676: Enfortumab vedotin-related skin toxicities in patients with urothelial carcinoma: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40960583: SEOM-SOGUG clinical guideline for urothelial cancer (2025) (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41110705: French recommendations of the AFU Oncology Committee for Bladder Cancer: Update 2025 (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `air_embolism` (topics 94) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Air or gas embolism is treated with 100% oxygen and early hyperbaric oxygen: delay lowers the probability of a favourable outcome. Iatrogenic causes include percutaneous lung biopsy, with identifiable risk factors.

**Teaching points**
- Hyperbaric treatment recommendations for air or gas embolism advise high-concentration oxygen followed by hyperbaric oxygen, and animal studies have not shown benefit from induced hypocapnia in cerebral air embolism. [PMID 40249721, 31683367]
- Increased time to hyperbaric oxygen is associated with a lower probability of favourable outcome in iatrogenic cerebral arterial gas embolism, so early initiation is vital. [PMID 37434172]
- Risk factors for air embolism after CT-guided transthoracic needle biopsy include subsolid lesion, lower lobe location, pneumothorax or haemorrhage and lesions above the left atrium. [PMID 36994842]
- After percutaneous transthoracic needle biopsy, sequelae and death in symptomatic air embolism were associated with aspiration biopsy and coronary or intracranial locations. [PMID 33051730]
- Both decompression sickness and arterial gas embolism are potential mechanisms of decompression illness in breath-hold divers. [PMID 36966520]
- The 2025 AHA special circumstances guideline requires modified life support in specific conditions. [PMID 41122889]

**Uncertainties**
- Benefit of adjunctive therapies is based on limited evidence.

**MCQ angles**
- Hyperbaric oxygen for cerebral air embolism.
- Iatrogenic risk factors.

**Case hooks**
- Neurological deterioration after central line removal.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Left lateral decubitus versus Trendelenburg positioning, venous versus arterial embolism and aspiration of right-sided air are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 31683367: Hyperbaric treatment of air or gas embolism: current recommendations (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 33051730: Incidence, risk factors, and prognostic indicators of symptomatic air embolism after percutaneous transthoracic lung biopsy: a systematic review and pooled analysis (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 36966520: The risk of decompression illness in breath-hold divers: a systematic review (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 36994842: Risk factors for air embolism following computed tomography-guided percutaneous transthoracic needle biopsy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37434172: Early hyperbaric oxygen therapy is associated with favorable outcome in patients with iatrogenic cerebral arterial gas embolism: systematic review and individual patient data meta-analysis of observational studies (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 40249721: Hyperbaric Treatment of Air or Gas Embolism: Current Recommendations (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41122889: Part 10: Adult and Pediatric Special Circumstances of Resuscitation: 2025 American Heart Association Guidelines for Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiovascular Care (guideline, 2025)
