## Cluster `airway` (topics 95, 96) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The DAS 2025 guidelines for unanticipated difficult intubation emphasise maximising the chance of first-pass success. Video laryngoscopy and bougies help in rapid sequence intubation, and obesity and thoracic anaesthesia have their own consensus documents.

**Teaching points**
- The Difficult Airway Society 2025 guidelines stress maximising the chances of success rather than only managing failure, to improve outcomes. [PMID 41203471]
- Predictive and protective factors for failing first-pass prehospital rapid sequence intubation include bougies and training as opportunities to improve first-pass success. [PMID 38508943]
- Best-practice recommendations address airway management in patients living with obesity. [PMID 40468902]
- An EACTAIC consensus consolidates practice in airway management for thoracic anaesthesia. [PMID 41353026]
- Point-of-care ultrasound of the upper airway was associated with improved first-pass success in percutaneous tracheostomy. [PMID 40891437]
- A Spanish multisociety guideline using GRADE and Delphi methods covers airway management, and an evidence-based guideline covers prehospital airway management. [PMID 38340790, 38133523]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence quality for many airway recommendations is low and consensus-based.

**MCQ angles**
- First-pass success is the goal.
- Obesity airway planning.

**Case hooks**
- Unanticipated difficult intubation in a critically ill patient.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Plan A-D sequence, preoxygenation, induction agents, cricoid pressure and front-of-neck access are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38133523: Evidence-Based Guideline for Prehospital Airway Management (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38340790: Spanish Society of Anesthesiology, Reanimation and Pain Therapy (SEDAR), Spanish Society of Emergency and Emergency Medicine (SEMES) and Spanish Society of Otolaryngology, Head and Neck Surgery (SEORL-CCC) Guideline for difficult airway management. Part II (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38508943: Predictive and protective factors for failing first pass intubation in prehospital rapid sequence intubation: an aetiology and risk systematic review with meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `airway_malignancy` (topics 97) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Malignant central airway obstruction can often be palliated by therapeutic bronchoscopy, which improves symptoms, quality of life and survival. Ablation, cryotherapy and stenting are options, but stents carry high adverse event rates and suit selected patients.

**Teaching points**
- Symptoms of malignant central airway obstruction can often be successfully palliated, with individual risk-benefit assessment forming the basis of management. [PMID 29214067]
- Therapeutic bronchoscopy has been shown to improve symptoms, quality of life and survival in malignant central airway obstruction. [PMID 38090036]
- Airway stenting is associated with high rates of adverse events and should be used only when curative open surgery is not feasible or contraindicated. [PMID 26874192]
- WABIP guidelines on airway stenting were developed by systematic evidence review with a modified Delphi process. [PMID 38812262]
- Bronchoscopic ablation options include laser, electrosurgery, photodynamic therapy, cryotherapy, radiofrequency, microwave and thermal vapour ablation. [PMID 31194922]
- Bronchoscopic cryotherapy has limited controlled data but promising future directions, including synergy with radiation. [PMID 27268274]
- A pilot study of microwave ablation found it a novel technique for tumour destruction while maintaining the airway axis. [PMID 35316812]

**Uncertainties**
- Controlled trial data for bronchoscopic ablation are limited.

**MCQ angles**
- Therapeutic bronchoscopy for palliation.
- Stent complications.

**Case hooks**
- Lung cancer patient with stridor and a tracheal tumour.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Emergency airway control, rigid bronchoscopy, radiotherapy and steroids are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 26874192: Central Airway Obstruction: Benign Strictures, Tracheobronchomalacia, and Malignancy-related Obstruction (review, 2016)
- PMID 27268274: Bronchoscopic Cryotherapy. Clinical Applications of the Cryoprobe, Cryospray, and Cryoadhesion (review, 2016)
- PMID 29214067: Malignant central airway obstruction (review, 2017)
- PMID 31194922: Bronchoscopic Ablative Therapies for Malignant Central Airway Obstruction and Peripheral Lung Tumors (review, 2019)
- PMID 35316812: Microwave Ablation for Malignant Central Airway Obstruction: A Pilot Study (review, 2022)
- PMID 38090036: Overview of malignant central airway obstruction (review, 2023)
- PMID 38812262: World Association for Bronchology and Interventional Pulmonology (WABIP) guidelines on airway stenting for malignant central airway obstruction (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `aki_recovery` (topics 101) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** AKI after cardiac surgery carries more than five-fold odds of later CKD, and any hospital AKI raises long-term risk of death, dialysis and CKD. Immune checkpoint inhibitor AKI recovers with steroids, but rechallenge risks recurrence.

**Teaching points**
- AKI after cardiac surgery was associated with more than five-fold odds of developing CKD (pooled OR 5.67), with new-onset CKD in almost one in four patients. [PMID 38879369]
- Survivors of hospital AKI have increased risk of death, dialysis and CKD compared with controls. [PMID 41247233]
- In ICI-associated AKI, steroid treatment promotes kidney recovery, adding intravenous steroids offers no additional benefit, and rechallenge raises recurrence risk. [PMID 41026215]
- A consensus addresses CRRT and fluid balance in AKI during ECMO, with knowledge gaps and research priorities. [PMID 42113209]

**Uncertainties**
- Follow-up strategy after AKI.

**MCQ angles**
- AKI as a CKD risk factor.
- Recurrent AKI on ICI rechallenge.

**Case hooks**
- Patient 3 months after cardiac surgery AKI with persistent eGFR decline.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Follow-up schedules, nephrology referral criteria and recovery biomarkers are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38879369: The Impact of Acute Kidney Injury on Chronic Kidney Disease After Cardiac Surgery: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 41026215: Immune checkpoint inhibitors-associated acute kidney injury: a systematic review and meta-analysis of incidence, kidney recovery, and recurrent risk (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41247233: Long-Term Outcomes After Acute Kidney Injury During Hospitalization: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Matched Controls Studies (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 42113209: Extracorporeal membrane oxygenation, acute kidney injury, fluid balance, and continuous renal replacement therapy: Acute Disease Quality Initiative (ADQI) and Extracorporeal Life Support Organization (ELSO) joint consensus conference (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `alcoholic_hepatitis` (topics 102, 103) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Corticosteroids reduce risk of death within 28 days in severe alcoholic hepatitis but not over six months, which is why new therapies are needed. Selected non-responders may be considered for liver transplantation after structured selection.

**Teaching points**
- Corticosteroid use reduced risk of death within 28 days in severe alcoholic hepatitis but not in the following 6 months. [PMID 29738698]
- The ACG guideline notes early liver transplantation may be considered in selected patients with alcoholic hepatitis who do not respond to medical therapy, with unmet need for effective, safer therapies. [PMID 29336434]
- The Dallas consensus on liver transplantation for alcohol-associated hepatitis says transparent selection, objective data collection and oversight of listing practices are crucial. [PMID 31743578]
- The 2024 ACG guideline on alcohol-associated liver disease uses GRADE to evaluate recommendations. [PMID 38174913]
- In alcohol-related liver disease, FibroScan assessment of fibrosis should account for AST and bilirubin with adjusted stiffness cutoffs. [PMID 29983372]
- The ACG 2025 guideline addresses malnutrition and nutritional recommendations in liver disease. [PMID 40314389]

**Uncertainties**
- Medium-term benefit of steroids.

**MCQ angles**
- Steroids short-term mortality benefit only.
- Transplant selection.

**Case hooks**
- Jaundiced heavy drinker with fever and Maddrey score above 32.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, Maddrey and Lille scores, infection screening and pentoxifylline are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29336434: ACG Clinical Guideline: Alcoholic Liver Disease (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 29738698: Corticosteroids Reduce Risk of Death Within 28 Days for Patients With Severe Alcoholic Hepatitis, Compared With Pentoxifylline or Placebo-a Meta-analysis of Individual Data From Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 29983372: Non-invasive diagnosis of liver fibrosis in patients with alcohol-related liver disease by transient elastography: an individual patient data meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 31743578: Meeting Report: The Dallas Consensus Conference on Liver Transplantation for Alcohol Associated Hepatitis (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 38174913: ACG Clinical Guideline: Alcohol-Associated Liver Disease (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40314389: ACG Clinical Guideline: Malnutrition and Nutritional Recommendations in Liver Disease (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `aud` (topics 104, 105, 106) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Canadian guidance covers screening and management of high-risk drinking and AUD, including pharmacotherapy. Extended-release naltrexone improves retention versus oral naltrexone, and Wernicke encephalopathy extends beyond alcohol misuse and needs prompt thiamine.

**Teaching points**
- The Canadian guideline for high-risk drinking and alcohol use disorder gives a framework for addressing unmet treatment needs, and a 2026 update supports universal screening. [PMID 37844924, 42082413]
- Patients receiving extended-release naltrexone stay in treatment longer than those on oral naltrexone, though hospitalisation and ED visit rates did not differ. [PMID 40660643]
- Wernicke encephalopathy occurs beyond alcohol misuse, with wide clinical variability, underscoring early recognition and prompt thiamine replacement, particularly in non-alcohol-related cases. [PMID 40911513]
- Psychosocial treatment options for adolescents and young adults with AUD are inconclusive and newer third-wave therapies need study. [PMID 39114525]
- International guidelines leave clinicians without evidence-based pathways for AUD treatment in pregnancy. [PMID 42249628]
- A systematic review of serotonergic psychedelics and MDMA for mental disorders calls for studies addressing expectancy and functional unblinding. [PMID 41205366]

**Uncertainties**
- Pharmacotherapy evidence in pregnancy.

**MCQ angles**
- Naltrexone formulation and retention.
- Thiamine in Wernicke encephalopathy.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with alcohol use disorder and confusion, ataxia and ophthalmoplegia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Acamprosate and disulfiram use, brief intervention, AUDIT screening and thiamine dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37844924: Canadian guideline for the clinical management of high-risk drinking and alcohol use disorder (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39114525: Psychosocial treatment options for adolescents and young adults with alcohol use disorder: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40660643: Extended-release naltrexone versus oral naltrexone for substance use disorders: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40911513: Alcohol-Related and Non-Alcohol-Related Wernicke Encephalopathy: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Epidemiology and Clinical Features (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41205366: Efficacy, all-cause discontinuation, and safety of serotonergic psychedelics and MDMA to treat mental disorders: A living systematic review with meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

- PMID 42249628: A Systematic Review of International Guidelines for Addressing Alcohol Use Disorder in Pregnancy (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `alcohol_withdrawal` (topics 107, 108) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Alcohol withdrawal risk can be predicted with symptom-sign tools, and ASAM guidance covers management. Perioperative cessation interventions and pregnancy-specific advice exist; benzodiazepine regimens are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**Teaching points**
- Assessment tools combining symptoms and signs are useful for identifying hospitalised patients at risk of severe alcohol withdrawal syndrome, though most were not fully validated. [PMID 30167704]
- The ASAM executive summary of the clinical practice guideline on alcohol withdrawal management includes recommendations where the panel reached consensus and identifies research gaps. [PMID 32909985]
- The WFSBP/IAWMH guidelines advise that medications for maintaining abstinence should not be used during pregnancy and that foetal alcohol spectrum disorders be sought at birth. [PMID 30632868]
- Perioperative alcohol cessation interventions, including pharmacological strategies for withdrawal symptoms, were reviewed for postoperative complications. [PMID 30408162]
- Alcohol use disorder is associated with an abnormal circulating cytokine profile. [PMID 32805393]
- The ACG guideline on alcoholic liver disease notes unmet need for effective therapies. [PMID 29336434]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal withdrawal regimen is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Risk prediction for severe withdrawal.
- Perioperative alcohol cessation.

**Case hooks**
- Admitted patient 48 hours after last drink with tremor and agitation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** CIWA-Ar scoring, benzodiazepine choice and dosing (symptom-triggered versus fixed), thiamine and delirium tremens management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29336434: ACG Clinical Guideline: Alcoholic Liver Disease (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 30167704: Will This Hospitalized Patient Develop Severe Alcohol Withdrawal Syndrome?: The Rational Clinical Examination Systematic Review (meta-analysis, 2018)

## Cluster `abpa` (topics 109) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The revised ISHAM-ABPA guidelines set diagnosis, classification and treatment-response criteria. Biologics (omalizumab, dupilumab, mepolizumab) show benefit in adults, whereas the paediatric guideline conditionally advises against routine biologic therapy.

**Teaching points**
- The revised ISHAM-ABPA working group guidelines give consensus on diagnosing, classifying and treating allergic bronchopulmonary aspergillosis, with an objective multidimensional criterion for treatment response. [PMID 38423624]
- Omalizumab reduced exacerbations and oral corticosteroid use and improved lung function and asthma control in ABPA, and was well tolerated. [PMID 36581073]
- Omalizumab, dupilumab and mepolizumab showed clinical benefit in ABPA, with larger randomised trials needed. [PMID 38898129]
- The evidence-based paediatric ABPA guideline gives a conditional recommendation (very low certainty) against biologic therapy such as omalizumab or dupilumab. [PMID 37264275]
- The EQUAL ABPA score quantifies guideline adherence in ABPA management. [PMID 39462638]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal antifungal and steroid sequencing is not in the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Biologics for ABPA.
- Treatment response criteria.

**Case hooks**
- Asthma patient with brown mucus plugs, high IgE and central bronchiectasis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria (IgE, Aspergillus IgE/IgG), steroid and itraconazole dosing and CT findings are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `allergic_rhinitis` (topics 110) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The ARIA-EAACI 2024-2025 revision gives guidance on intranasal, oral and ocular treatments, weighing patient values and affordability. Montelukast plus levocetirizine outperforms monotherapy in combined allergic rhinitis and asthma, and remission is defined with a 12-month minimum.

**Teaching points**
- ARIA-EAACI Part I guides choice of intranasal treatment and Part II oral and ocular treatments, with decisions considering clinical variability, patient values and affordability. [PMID 41324154, 41877472]
- Montelukast combined with levocetirizine is more effective than monotherapy for nasal symptoms in allergic rhinitis with asthma, with good safety. [PMID 39394937]
- A EUFOREA consensus proposes 12 months as the minimum period to define remission in global airway disease. [PMID 42304188]
- Vitamin D supplementation may alleviate allergic rhinitis symptoms, with effects differing by sex and concomitant medication. [PMID 40005471]
- Obstructive sleep apnoea is associated with asthma (especially moderate to severe) and allergic rhinitis. [PMID 40774162]
- Risk factors for food allergy in infants include early allergic conditions, delayed allergen introduction, genetics and antibiotic exposure. [PMID 41661638]

**Uncertainties**
- Immunotherapy evidence is not in the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Intranasal steroid as mainstay (not in excerpts).
- Link with asthma and OSA.

**Case hooks**
- Seasonal sneezing and itch with coexisting asthma.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Intranasal corticosteroid versus antihistamine ranking, allergen immunotherapy (SCIT/SLIT) and diagnostic testing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39394937: Efficacy and safety of montelukast-levocetirizine combination therapy in combined allergic rhinitis and asthma syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40005471: Vitamin D Supplementation and Allergic Rhinitis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `allo_sct` (topics 111) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Allogeneic transplant practice is guided by NMDP/CIBMTR donor selection, EBMT HLA matching and ATG recommendations, and NIH chronic GVHD consensus. Disease-specific guidance exists for severe aplastic anaemia and advanced cutaneous T-cell lymphoma.

**Teaching points**
- The NMDP/CIBMTR guidelines give donor selection algorithms and prioritisation in the era of novel GVHD prophylaxis. [PMID 40619101]
- EBMT recommendations cover HLA matching in contemporary HCT and monitoring HLA loss in post-transplant relapse. [PMID 42230962]
- EBMT consensus supports context-specific use of rabbit ATG and ATLG in allo-HCT for haematological malignancies. [PMID 41298854]
- The 2025 NIH chronic GVHD consensus conference update reports modest progress on most initiatives. [PMID 40409691]
- A proposal refines skin chronic GVHD response assessment, including a functional impact scale from 0 to 4. [PMID 41539488]
- Evidence-based guidance addresses allo-HCT for severe aplastic anaemia: donor choice, conditioning, GVHD prophylaxis and immunosuppression. [PMID 39307421]
- EBMT recommendations address selection, timing and conduct of allogeneic transplantation in advanced mycosis fungoides and Sezary syndrome. [PMID 41034407]

**Uncertainties**
- Many questions in donor selection remain open.

**MCQ angles**
- Donor selection with novel GVHD prophylaxis.
- Chronic GVHD assessment.

**Case hooks**
- High-risk AML in first remission being considered for transplant.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Indications, conditioning intensity, acute GVHD grading and infection prophylaxis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39307421: Allogeneic Hematopoietic Cell Transplantation for the Treatment of Severe Aplastic Anemia: Evidence-Based Guidelines From the American Society for Transplantation and Cellular Therapy (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40409691: NIH Chronic Graft-Versus-Host Disease Consensus Conference 2025 Update (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40619101: Allogeneic Hematopoietic Cell Donor Selection: Contemporary Guidelines from the NMDP/CIBMTR (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41034407: Allogeneic hematopoietic cell transplant in cutaneous T-cell lymphomas: recommendations from the EBMT PH&amp;G Committee (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41298854: Usage of rabbit anti-thymocyte / anti-T-lymphocyte globulins (ATG / ATLG) for hematological malignancies in allogeneic hematopoietic cell transplantation: Best practice recommendations from the EBMT Practice Harmonisation and Guidelines Committee (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41539488: Toward Better Response Assessment of Cutaneous Chronic Graft-Versus-Host Disease: A Report from the National Institutes of Health Consensus Project Task Force (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42230962: HLA matching in contemporary haematopoietic cell transplantation: Recommendations from the EBMT Practice Harmonisation and Guidelines Committee (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `aatd` (topics 112) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency guidance covers targeted testing, augmentation therapy for severe deficiency with emphysema, and monitoring of lung disease. Guidelines show limited concordance internationally.

**Teaching points**
- The Canadian Thoracic Society statement defines augmentation therapy for patients with FEV1 below 80% predicted and emphysema, documented deficiency genotypes and severely reduced A1AT level (below 11 micromol/L) on optimal COPD therapy. [PMID 39880301]
- An analysis of international AATD guidelines found limited concordance and suggested a broadly representative international expert group to harmonise them. [PMID 31564856]
- A European expert survey provides recommendations for assessment and monitoring of lung disease in severe AATD. [PMID 39160517]
- A multi-society consensus provides a unified framework for diagnosing, staging and studying AATD liver disease. [PMID 41390004]
- Pulmonary rehabilitation for AATD-related COPD may differ from usual COPD, and optimal intensity and educational needs need research. [PMID 37813825]
- Abnormal liver chemistries imply hepatocellular disease or biliary obstruction, and liver biopsy may be considered when other tests fail to establish a diagnosis. [PMID 27995906]
- A Portuguese consensus document addresses management of alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency. [PMID 30473034]

**Uncertainties**
- Harmonisation of international guidelines.

**MCQ angles**
- Who qualifies for augmentation therapy.
- AATD liver disease.

**Case hooks**
- Young non-smoker with basal emphysema.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Targeted testing triggers, phenotype interpretation (PiZZ), liver disease surveillance and smoking avoidance are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27995906: ACG Clinical Guideline: Evaluation of Abnormal Liver Chemistries (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 30473034: Portuguese consensus document for the management of alpha-1-antitrypsin deficiency (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 31564856: An analysis of the degree of concordance among international guidelines regarding alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 37813825: Pulmonary Rehabilitation for Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease Patients with Underlying Alpha-1 Antitrypsin Deficiency: A Systematic Review and Practical Recommendations (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39160517: Assessment and monitoring of lung disease in patients with severe alpha 1 antitrypsin deficiency: a european delphi consensus of the EARCO group (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39880301: Alpha-1-Antitrypsin Deficiency Targeted Testing and Augmentation Therapy: A Canadian Thoracic Society Meta-Analysis and Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41390004: Multi-Society Expert Panel Consensus Guidance Regarding Clinical Assessment and Clinical Trial Endpoints in Adults With Alpha-1 Antitrypsin Deficiency-Associated Liver Disease (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `alport` (topics 113, 114) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Alport syndrome is managed with genetic diagnosis and RAAS blockade started early to delay kidney failure. The ERKNet/ERA/ESPN 2024 guideline covers diagnosis and management of COL4A3/4/5 variant carriers, including donor assessment.

**Teaching points**
- RAAS blockers delay end-stage kidney disease in Alport syndrome of any genetic type, especially early in disease, with more effective therapies to be added on top of this standard of care. [PMID 37218713]
- The 2024 ERKNet/ERA/ESPN guideline covers diagnosis and management of individuals with pathogenic COL4A3/4/5 variants, and heterozygous carriers should be considered for living kidney donation only as a last resort. [PMID 39673454]
- A molecular diagnostics consensus notes heterozygous COL4A3 and COL4A4 variants are common incidental findings, and interpreting hypomorphic variants remains challenging. [PMID 33854215]
- Expert consensus guidelines on genetic diagnosis note genetic and non-genetic factors contribute to phenotypic variability. [PMID 29987460]
- Ocular manifestations of Alport syndrome mean ophthalmologists can aid diagnosis, and early diagnosis lessens blindness and retinal detachment risk. [PMID 36119140]
- A Chinese expert consensus updated in 2023 covers genetic testing, variant interpretation and treatment follow-up. [PMID 37246000]
- A review of potential renal damage biomarkers in Alport syndrome highlights markers of kidney disease progression risk. [PMID 35806283]

**Uncertainties**
- Management strategies specific to male patients need more data.

**MCQ angles**
- RAAS blockade to delay ESKD.
- COL4A3/4/5 genetics and donors.

**Case hooks**
- Boy with haematuria, deafness and a family history of kidney failure.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Inheritance patterns, hearing and eye surveillance, transplant outcomes and SGLT2 or novel therapies are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29987460: Expert consensus guidelines for the genetic diagnosis of Alport syndrome (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 33854215: Consensus statement on standards and guidelines for the molecular diagnostics of Alport syndrome: refining the ACMG criteria (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35806283: Potential Renal Damage Biomarkers in Alport Syndrome-A Review of the Literature (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 36119140: Ocular Manifestations and Potential Treatments of Alport Syndrome: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 37218713: Effectiveness of renin-angiotensin-aldosterone system blockers in patients with Alport syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37246000: [Expert consensus on the diagnosis and treatment of Alport syndrome (version 2023)] (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39673454: Diagnosis, management and treatment of the Alport syndrome - 2024 guideline on behalf of ERKNet, ERA and ESPN (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `antiamyloid` (topics 116, 117) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anti-amyloid antibodies slow decline in early Alzheimer disease but their effects are small, below the minimal clinically important difference in one analysis, and they carry meaningful harms including ARIA. MRI protocols to detect ARIA are recommended.

**Teaching points**
- FDA-approved anti-amyloid monoclonal antibodies slow disease progression on CDR-SB and other scales, at the cost of increased side effects. [PMID 38017568]
- Monoclonal antibodies targeting amyloid give small benefits on cognitive and functional scales that are far below the minimal clinically important difference, with clinically meaningful harms. [PMID 38253509]
- Aducanumab, lecanemab and donanemab do not appear to differ in effect on cognitive function, and low-dose lithium may be safer. [PMID 38253184]
- A critical assessment suggests benefits of the newest antibodies could be cumulative over time, leading to larger clinical effects in subsequent years. [PMID 39468148]
- Imaging recommendations for anti-amyloid therapies include implementing MRI protocols to detect ARIA in diverse practice settings. [PMID 39179297]
- A meta-analysis of phase III trials found anti-amyloid antibodies associated with improved ADAS-Cog and CDR-SB. [PMID 37423541]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term clinical benefit and cost-effectiveness.

**MCQ angles**
- ARIA and MRI monitoring.
- Magnitude of clinical benefit.

**Case hooks**
- Early Alzheimer's disease with amyloid-positive PET considering lecanemab.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Eligibility criteria, APOE e4 homozygote risk, infusion schedules and ARIA management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37423541: Efficacy and safety of anti-amyloid-β monoclonal antibodies in current Alzheimer's disease phase III clinical trials: A systematic review and interactive web app-based meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38017568: The FDA-approved anti-amyloid-β monoclonal antibodies for the treatment of Alzheimer's disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38253184: Comparative efficacy, tolerability and acceptability of donanemab, lecanemab, aducanumab and lithium on cognitive function in mild cognitive impairment and Alzheimer's disease: A systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38253509: Clinically Important Benefits and Harms of Monoclonal Antibodies Targeting Amyloid for the Treatment of Alzheimer Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39179297: Alzheimer Disease Anti-Amyloid Immunotherapies: Imaging Recommendations and Practice Considerations for Monitoring of Amyloid-Related Imaging Abnormalities (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 39468148: Critical assessment of anti-amyloid-β monoclonal antibodies effects in Alzheimer's disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis highlighting target engagement and clinical meaningfulness (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `amegakaryocytic` (topics 118) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Congenital amegakaryocytic thrombocytopenia is a heterogeneous group, mainly due to MPL mutations, that can evolve to marrow failure. Management ranges from supportive care to transplantation, and acquired forms can respond to immunosuppression.

**Teaching points**
- Congenital amegakaryocytic thrombocytopenia is not a single disease, with the main focus on the form caused by mutations in the MPL gene. [PMID 34404532]
- Reviews summarise the diagnostic features and clinical implications of inherited bone marrow failure syndromes including congenital amegakaryocytic thrombocytopenia. [PMID 30573048]
- Radioulnar synostosis with amegakaryocytic thrombocytopenia type 2 is reviewed with management considerations including transplantation. [PMID 41635268]
- Thrombopoietin-receptor agonists are used selectively in rare inherited thrombocytopenias. [PMID 38925157]
- Anti-thymocyte globulin-based treatment is used for acquired bone marrow failure in adults. [PMID 34831130]
- Amegakaryocytic thrombocytopenia with later aplastic anaemia has been associated with Epstein-Barr virus infection, and recovery may reflect natural history. [PMID 29301129]
- CRISPR/Cas9 gene editing is a promising strategy in haematological disorders, with delivery into stem cells a bottleneck. [PMID 36610813]

**Uncertainties**
- Natural history and optimal timing of transplantation.

**MCQ angles**
- MPL mutations in CAMT.
- Progression to aplastic anaemia.

**Case hooks**
- Neonate with petechiae, isolated severe thrombocytopenia and absent megakaryocytes.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, transplant indications and platelet transfusion thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29301129: Amegakaryocytic Thrombocytopenia and Subsequent Aplastic Anemia Associated with Apparent Epstein-Barr Virus Infection (review, 2018)
- PMID 30573048: Acquired and germline predisposition to bone marrow failure: Diagnostic features and clinical implications (review, 2019)
- PMID 34404532: Congenital amegakaryocytic thrombocytopenia - Not a single disease (review, 2021)
- PMID 34831130: Anti Thymocyte Globulin-Based Treatment for Acquired Bone Marrow Failure in Adults (review, 2021)
- PMID 36610813: CRISPR/Cas9-mediated gene editing. A promising strategy in hematological disorders (review, 2023)
- PMID 38925157: Update on the Use of Thrombopoietin-Receptor Agonists in Pediatrics (review, 2024)
- PMID 41635268: Syndrome of the Month: Radioulnar Synostosis With Amegakaryocytic Thrombocytopenia Type 2 (review, 2026)

## Cluster `aml_mpn` (topics 119) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** AML evolving from MPN has a poor outlook. Combination of hypomethylating agents with venetoclax or ruxolitinib improved outcomes over HMA alone in accelerated or blast phase, and allogeneic transplant is a potential option for selected therapy-related myeloid neoplasms.

**Teaching points**
- Combination therapy with hypomethylating agents plus venetoclax or ruxolitinib was associated with improved outcomes over HMAs alone in MPN accelerated or blast phase, with randomised trials needed. [PMID 36644935]
- Allogeneic transplant remains a potential treatment for carefully selected patients with therapy-related myeloid neoplasms, with relapse the commonest cause of death. [PMID 39890517]
- TP53 mutations strongly predict poorer survival in adult AML. [PMID 42199193]
- CPX-351 showed superior efficacy and comparable safety to standard chemotherapy across diverse AML populations in a systematic review. [PMID 40348597]
- Post-chemotherapy G-CSF may increase relapse risk in paediatric and secondary AML (RR 1.26 and 1.12 in subgroups). [PMID 38922312]
- Cutaneous involvement in Ph-negative MPN has prognostic relevance and histological heterogeneity. [PMID 37649236]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal therapy for blast-phase MPN.

**MCQ angles**
- HMA plus venetoclax in blast-phase MPN.
- Transplant for secondary AML.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with long-standing myelofibrosis presenting with rising blasts.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Definitions of blast-phase MPN, ruxolitinib bridging and survival figures are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36644935: Efficacy and safety of combination therapies vs monotherapy of hypomethylating agents in accelerated or blast phase of Philadelphia negative myeloproliferative neoplasms: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37649236: Cutaneous involvement in Ph-negative myeloproliferative neoplasms: from extramedullary hematopoiesis to myeloid metastasis with histiocytic differentiation. A systematic review of the literature (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 38922312: The Impact of Prophylactic Post-Chemotherapy G-CSF on the Relapse Rates in Patients with Acute Myeloid Leukemia: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39890517: Outcomes with Allogeneic Hematopoietic Stem Cell Transplantation in Therapy Related Myeloid Neoplasms: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40348597: The Clinical Safety and Efficacy of Cytarabine and Daunorubicin Liposome (CPX-351) in Acute Myeloid Leukemia Patients: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2025)
- PMID 42199193: Prognosis of Acute Myeloid Leukemia Based on TP53 Mutation Among Adult Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `als` (topics 120, 121) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ALS care is multidisciplinary, including respiratory support and nutrition. Tofersen slows progression and lowers NfL in SOD1-ALS; respiratory muscle training may help function, and biomarker diagnosis alone risks misclassification.

**Teaching points**
- Tofersen reduces SOD1 and NfL levels and slows disease progression in SOD1 ALS. [PMID 39820998]
- Respiratory muscle training may improve pulmonary function, particularly respiratory muscle strength, in people with ALS, with limited evidence of benefit on quality of life and survival. [PMID 40488544]
- The Japanese ALS guideline covers respiration, rehabilitation, nutrition, metabolism and care support. [PMID 38522911]
- Blood and CSF biomarkers used alone for ALS diagnosis carry a considerable risk of misclassification and should be integrated into multimodal frameworks. [PMID 41140053]
- No association was found between viral infections and ALS risk, unlike Alzheimer's and Parkinson's disease. [PMID 41073371]
- Genetic mutations in ALS and FTD genes have distinct effects on behavioural and cognitive change, suggesting tailored care. [PMID 40649976]
- A US expert consensus sets a standard for ALS home health and durable medical equipment. [PMID 42316902]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for respiratory muscle training is limited.

**MCQ angles**
- Tofersen and SOD1.
- Respiratory care in ALS.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with progressive limb weakness and declining FVC.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** El Escorial and Gold Coast criteria, riluzole and edaravone, NIV and gastrostomy timing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38522911: The clinical practice guideline for the management of amyotrophic lateral sclerosis in Japan-update 2023 (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39820998: Tofersen for SOD1 amyotrophic lateral sclerosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40488544: Efficacy of respiratory muscle training in improving pulmonary function and survival in patients with amyotrophic lateral sclerosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40649976: The Differential Effects of Genetic Mutations in ALS and FTD Genes on Behavioural and Cognitive Changes: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41073371: Viral infections and the risk of neurodegenerative diseases: a comprehensive meta-analysis and systematic review (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41140053: Diagnostic and Prognostic Value of Blood and Cerebrospinal Fluid Biomarkers in Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 42316902: The ALS Home Health and Durable Medical Equipment Medical Standard Expert Consensus Guideline (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `anaemia` (topics 122, 123) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anaemia is defined using WHO haemoglobin cutoffs. In CKD, UK and KDIGO guidelines address iron, ESA and HIF-PHI therapy; IV iron needs adverse event planning; and routine iron supplementation in pregnancy lacks sufficient evidence for the USPSTF to judge.

**Teaching points**
- The WHO guideline sets haemoglobin cutoffs to define anaemia in individuals and populations for clinicians, programme managers and policy-makers. [PMID 38530913]
- The USPSTF found current evidence insufficient to assess benefits and harms of routine iron supplementation for iron deficiency and iron deficiency anaemia in pregnancy (I statement). [PMID 39163015]
- The UK Kidney Association guideline update on anaemia of CKD covers iron deficiency without anaemia, anaemia in transplant recipients and use of HIF-PHI drugs. [PMID 40240983]
- A European commentary on KDIGO 2026 anaemia in CKD supports most recommendations while advising local context on HIF-PHI approvals. [PMID 41604211]
- A systematic review and consensus gives recommendations on intravenous iron administration and management of adverse events, including formulation selection. [PMID 42379899]
- The BSH guideline covers conception and pregnancy in thalassaemia syndromes. [PMID 38715390]

**Uncertainties**
- Anaemia of chronic disease specifics are not in the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- IV iron adverse event mitigation.
- HIF-PHI use in CKD.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with CKD stage 4, Hb 9.5 g/dL and low ferritin.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Classification by MCV, ferritin thresholds, anaemia of chronic disease pathophysiology (hepcidin) and transfusion thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38530913: Guideline on haemoglobin cutoffs to define anaemia in individuals and populations (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38715390: Guideline for the management of conception and pregnancy in thalassaemia syndromes: A British Society for Haematology Guideline (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39163015: Screening and Supplementation for Iron Deficiency and Iron Deficiency Anemia During Pregnancy: US Preventive Services Task Force Recommendation Statement (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40240983: UK kidney association clinical practice guideline: update of anaemia of chronic kidney disease (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41604211: KDIGO 2026 Clinical Practice Guideline for Anemia in Chronic Kidney Disease (CKD): a commentary from the European Renal Best Practice (ERBP) (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `anal_cancer` (topics 124, 125) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anal squamous cell carcinoma is treated with chemoradiotherapy using risk-adapted target volumes; ASTRO guidance covers dose. Risk-targeted screening in high-risk groups (people living with HIV) uses cytology and HPV testing.

**Teaching points**
- The ASTRO guideline on radiation therapy for anal squamous cell carcinoma addresses optimal dose, alternative systemic agents including immunotherapy, adaptive RT and minimising long-term toxicity. [PMID 40023252]
- ECOG-ACRIN and Nordic consensus guidelines give contouring instructions, with a risk-adapted delineation of the elective clinical target volume. [PMID 35717050, 37504978]
- The International Anal Neoplasia Society consensus guidelines support risk-targeted screening for anal cancer prevention. [PMID 38297406]
- Screening biomarkers suggested higher specificity but lower sensitivity than anal cytology and HPV testing. [PMID 35793241]
- Recent guidelines on anal cancer screening differ in target groups, methods, treatment and follow-up, and include smoking cessation and HPV vaccination. [PMID 40019005]
- Cancer risk is raised in people living with HIV and transplant recipients, patterns reflecting immune impairment and exposure to carcinogenic viruses. [PMID 38936380]
- The SITC guideline covers immunotherapy for gastrointestinal cancers including biomarker testing and therapy selection. [PMID 37286304]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal radiotherapy dose.

**MCQ angles**
- Chemoradiation as primary treatment.
- Screening in people living with HIV.

**Case hooks**
- Man who has sex with men and HIV with anal bleeding.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Primary chemoradiation regimen (5-FU/mitomycin), staging and salvage surgery are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35717050: ECOG-ACRIN Guideline for Contouring and Treatment of Early Stage Anal Cancer Using IMRT/IGRT (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 35793241: A systematic review and meta-analysis of cytology and HPV-related biomarkers for anal cancer screening among different risk groups (meta-analysis, 2022)

## Cluster `anaphylaxis` (topics 127) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2024 GA2LEN consensus provides a new definition and clinical support tool to improve anaphylaxis recognition across settings. Related hypersensitivity topics include contrast media reactions and direct penicillin challenges, which are low-risk.

**Teaching points**
- The 2024 consensus report provides an anaphylaxis definition, overview and clinical support tool intended to replace previous definitions and criteria and standardise research outcomes. [PMID 39880313]
- ERC 2025 first aid guidelines include procedures for life-threatening conditions that could progress to cardiac arrest. [PMID 41117568]
- Consensus statements address management and prevention of hypersensitivity reactions to radiocontrast media, including skin testing and contrast switching. [PMID 40326871, 39797723]
- Direct penicillin challenges have infrequent reaction rates comparable to indirect challenges after testing, and are safe across ages and settings. [PMID 39283610]
- A guideline on immunotherapy for inhalant allergy assesses effects on preventing new sensitisations and altering disease history. [PMID 38408152]
- A BSACI guideline addresses management of egg allergy including epidemiology, diagnosis and treatment. [PMID 34586690]

**Uncertainties**
- Adrenaline dosing and observation duration are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Updated anaphylaxis definition.
- Direct penicillin challenge safety.

**Case hooks**
- Urticaria, wheeze and hypotension minutes after contrast injection.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Intramuscular adrenaline dose and site, biphasic reaction, tryptase and discharge planning are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

- PMID 38408152: Clinical Practice Guideline: Immunotherapy for Inhalant Allergy (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39283610: Reaction Risk to Direct Penicillin Challenges: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `atc` (topics 128) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anaplastic thyroid cancer progresses rapidly and needs urgent multimodal care guided by ATA 2021. BRAF V600E-mutated disease has new evidence-based targeted recommendations, and TP53 mutations are frequent.

**Teaching points**
- The 2021 ATA guidelines give evidence-based recommendations for anaplastic thyroid cancer, with all care individualised. [PMID 33728999]
- The FAST multidisciplinary consensus gives recommendations for initial management of BRAF V600E-variant anaplastic thyroid cancer, reflecting remarkable treatment progress in the past decade. [PMID 38990526]
- The ENDOCAN TUTHYREF network summarises consensus recommendations for anaplastic thyroid cancer. [PMID 40350149]
- TP53 mutations are frequent in the pathogenesis of anaplastic thyroid cancer. [PMID 39940804]
- A Spanish consensus notes that although aggressive radiotherapy reduced locoregional recurrence, median overall survival has not improved in more than 50 years. [PMID 27048161]
- Surgical recommendations based on the S3 guideline conferences cover diagnosis and treatment of ATC. [PMID 40131404]

**Uncertainties**
- Better combinations are still needed.

**MCQ angles**
- BRAF V600E testing in ATC.
- Rapid progression requires urgent decisions.

**Case hooks**
- Rapidly enlarging neck mass with hoarseness in an older adult.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Dabrafenib-trametinib dosing, airway management, staging (all stage IV) and prognosis figures are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27048161: Spanish consensus for the management of patients with anaplastic cell thyroid carcinoma (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 33728999: 2021 American Thyroid Association Guidelines for Management of Patients with Anaplastic Thyroid Cancer (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 38990526: Initial Management of BRAF V600E-Variant Anaplastic Thyroid Cancer: The FAST Multidisciplinary Group Consensus Statement (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `anca` (topics 129, 130, 131) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Recent guidance (ACR/VF 2021, EULAR 2022, KDIGO 2024, BSR 2025) favours reduced-dose glucocorticoid induction; reduced-dose regimens probably reduce serious infections without increasing ESKD. Rituximab maintenance lowers relapse risk compared with azathioprine in Japanese review evidence.

**Teaching points**
- Reduced-dose glucocorticoid regimens probably reduce serious infections by about 5.9% to 12.8% and probably do not increase ESKD, according to a practice guideline on plasma exchange and glucocorticoid dosing. [PMID 35217581]
- The KDIGO 2024 executive summary notes the case for lower-dose glucocorticoid induction or complete replacement has strengthened. [PMID 38388147]
- The 2021 ACR/Vasculitis Foundation guideline provides the first endorsed recommendations for ANCA-associated vasculitis management. [PMID 34235894, 34235880]
- The 2025 BSR recommendations include research and audit recommendations to support equitable access. [PMID 40499922]
- A systematic review for the 2023 Japanese guidelines compared rituximab and azathioprine maintenance for relapse risk. [PMID 36112482]
- A review summarises recent guidelines including KDIGO glomerular diseases and EULAR 2022 for AAV management. [PMID 37483479]
- An evidence-based guideline addresses diagnosis and management of eosinophilic granulomatosis with polyangiitis. [PMID 37161084]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal role of plasma exchange.

**MCQ angles**
- Reduced-dose steroid induction.
- Maintenance with rituximab.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with haemoptysis, glomerulonephritis and positive PR3-ANCA.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Induction agent choice, avacopan, maintenance duration and ANCA classification criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `sah` (topics 132) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2023 AHA/ASA guideline updates aneurysmal SAH care. Lumbar drainage was associated with less vasospasm, infarction and mortality in meta-analysis, and risk factors for delayed cerebral ischaemia help target ICU monitoring.

**Teaching points**
- The 2023 AHA/ASA guideline updates many earlier aneurysmal SAH recommendations and adds new ones, including patient and family priorities. [PMID 37212182]
- Lumbar CSF drainage in aneurysmal SAH was associated with benefits in vasospasm, secondary cerebral infarction and mortality (RR 0.75 for poor outcome at 6 months) without more adverse events. [PMID 38246528]
- Risk factors for delayed cerebral ischaemia include pre-existing hypertension, vasospasm, high admission white cell count, intracranial infection and female sex, so such patients need careful ICU monitoring. [PMID 39343384]
- Multimodality neuromonitoring practice standards were developed by Delphi consensus, covering technologies, implementation and training. [PMID 37607072]
- A multidisciplinary practical consensus statement offers a protocol for acute care of aneurysmal SAH, not evidence-based guidelines. [PMID 40739674]
- Machine learning shows potential to forecast delayed cerebral ischaemia and poor outcomes but methodology needs standardising. [PMID 39775123, 39667223]
- A Chinese guideline covers evaluation, treatment selection, anaesthesia and perioperative care of ruptured intracranial aneurysms. [PMID 38825939]

**Uncertainties**
- Standardised prediction tools.

**MCQ angles**
- Delayed cerebral ischaemia risk factors.
- Lumbar drainage benefit.

**Case hooks**
- Thunderclap headache with SAH on CT.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Securing the aneurysm timing, nimodipine, blood pressure targets, hydrocephalus and vasospasm management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37212182: 2023 Guideline for the Management of Patients With Aneurysmal Subarachnoid Hemorrhage: A Guideline From the American Heart Association/American Stroke Association (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37607072: Practice Standards for the Use of Multimodality Neuromonitoring: A Delphi Consensus Process (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38246528: Effectiveness of Cerebrospinal Fluid Lumbar Drainage Among Patients with Aneurysmal Subarachnoid Hemorrhage: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38825939: [Chinese guideline for the clinical management of patients with ruptured intracranial aneurysms (2024)] (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39343384: Risk Factors for the Development of Delayed Cerebral Ischemia After Aneurysmal Subarachnoid Hemorrhage: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39667223: Machine learning for predicting poor outcomes in aneurysmal subarachnoid hemorrhage: A systematic review and meta-analysis involving 8445 participants (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39775123: Leveraging machine learning algorithms to forecast delayed cerebral ischemia following subarachnoid hemorrhage: a systematic review and meta-analysis of 5,115 participants (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `angioedema` (topics 133) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The WAO/EAACI guideline sets global standards for hereditary angioedema, with separate paediatric, normal-C1-inhibitor and resource-limited guidance. Urticaria-associated angioedema is covered by the international urticaria guideline.

**Teaching points**
- The international WAO/EAACI hereditary angioedema guideline (2021 revision and update) aims to establish global standards, including disease activity, impact and control. [PMID 35006617, 35497649]
- A 2026 international guideline addresses diagnosis and management of paediatric hereditary angioedema and promotes HAE centres and registries. [PMID 41618059]
- Hereditary angioedema with normal C1 inhibitor recommendations are all based on expert opinion. [PMID 40053270]
- The International/Canadian HAE guideline targets all specialists who may encounter patients with HAE. [PMID 41606636]
- Indian consensus recognises that resource-constrained settings often cannot follow international evaluation algorithms or access recommended therapy. [PMID 37388810]
- The 2026 international urticaria guideline covers definition, classification, diagnosis and treatment of urticaria subtypes. [PMID 41649409]
- On-demand HAE treatment guidelines evolved with trial and real-world data, changing trial designs. [PMID 39724968]

**Uncertainties**
- Diagnosis and therapy of HAE-nC1INH rely on expert opinion.

**MCQ angles**
- HAE needs different treatment than allergic angioedema.
- Paediatric HAE management.

**Case hooks**
- Recurrent facial swelling without urticaria and family history.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** C4 and C1-INH testing, on-demand and prophylactic drugs (icatibant, lanadelumab), ACE inhibitor angioedema and airway management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `angiotensin2` (topics 134, 135) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Angiotensin II raises blood pressure and spares catecholamines in vasodilatory shock without clearly lowering mortality. It is a rescue (third-line) vasopressor, and patients with high renin may respond best.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of distributive shock found angiotensin II did not reduce mortality but allowed significant adjunctive vasopressor reduction at 3 hours without more reported adverse events. [PMID 38888542]
- In vasoplegic shock, angiotensin II showed comparable in-hospital mortality to standard therapy but produced a significant mean arterial pressure rise. [PMID 39337869]
- A systematic review suggests angiotensin II may be a third-line vasopressor if the target MAP is not reached after noradrenaline above 200 ng/kg/min for more than 6 hours. [PMID 36636460]
- Expert consensus recognises angiotensin II as useful to reduce catecholamine requirements in vasopressor-resistant septic shock, while stressing the need for more trials. [PMID 39152516]
- An exploratory analysis found a greater response in patients with elevated baseline plasma renin and a reduction in renal replacement therapy initiation. [PMID 40548153]
- Safety concerns include thromboembolism with angiotensin II, and in cardiogenic shock reported adverse events included hepatic injury, digital ischaemia and thrombotic events. [PMID 30563433, 36753927]
- An overview of reviews found angiotensin II consistently improved MAP by more than 10 mmHg, with gaps in the definition of catecholamine-resistant shock, initiation thresholds and biomarker-guided treatment. [PMID 41942842]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal initiation threshold and renin-guided selection.
- Effect on hard outcomes beyond MAP and catecholamine sparing.

**MCQ angles**
- Angiotensin II as rescue vasopressor.
- Thromboembolic risk as a key adverse effect.

**Case hooks**
- Septic shock on noradrenaline 0.5 microgram/kg/min with MAP still 55.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** ATHOS-3 trial detail, dosing and titration, and thromboprophylaxis requirements are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30563433: Novel Vasopressors in the Treatment of Vasodilatory Shock: A Systematic Review of Angiotensin II, Selepressin, and Terlipressin (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 36636460: How Effective is Angiotensin II in Decreasing Mortality of Vasodilatory Shock? A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 36753927: Efficacy and safety of angiotensin II in cardiogenic shock: A systematic review (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 38888542: ANGIOTENSIN II IN THE TREATMENT OF DISTRIBUTIVE SHOCK: A SYSTEMATIC-REVIEW AND META-ANALYSIS (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39152516: The use of angiotensin II for the management of distributive shock: expert consensus statements (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39337869: Clinical Outcomes of Angiotensin II Therapy in Vasoplegic Shock: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40548153: Angiotensin II in Catecholamine-Refractory Shock: A Systematic Review and Exploratory Analysis of the Angiotensin II for the Treatment of High-Output Shock (ATHOS-3) Trial (systematic review, 2025)
- PMID 41942842: An overview of reviews of Angiotensin II in distributive shock (systematic review, 2026)

## Cluster `axspa` (topics 136, 137, 223, 224) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Axial spondyloarthritis is increasingly treated as a single entity across radiographic and non-radiographic disease. Upadacitinib improves disease activity and function, and tapering (not withdrawing) biologics preserves remission in more patients.

**Teaching points**
- SPARTAN members strongly support treating axSpA as a single entity in the update of the ACR/SAA/SPARTAN guideline. [PMID 39680252]
- A meta-analysis supports upadacitinib as a new treatment for axSpA, improving disease activity, physical function and wellbeing. [PMID 38874670]
- Tapering biologic DMARDs does not significantly compromise remission or low disease activity, whereas complete withdrawal reduces the likelihood of maintaining inactive or low disease activity. [PMID 41001393]
- A guideline on biomarkers in axSpA addresses their clinical significance and composes a clinical toolkit for healthcare professionals. [PMID 39539543]
- A Chinese 2024 guideline covers diagnosis and treatment of non-radiographic axSpA with 37 clinical questions including drug tapering. [PMID 40123389]
- The ESPOGUIA guideline aims to reduce unjustified variability in managing axial spondyloarthritis and psoriatic arthritis. [PMID 40764228]
- A meta-analysis found gut microbiota dysbiosis in ankylosing spondylitis, suggesting a possible therapeutic target. [PMID 39421642]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal tapering strategy.

**MCQ angles**
- Single-entity approach to axSpA.
- Taper rather than stop biologics.

**Case hooks**
- Young man with inflammatory back pain and sacroiliitis on MRI.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** ASAS criteria, NSAID first-line therapy, TNF and IL-17 inhibitor choice and extra-articular disease are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38874670: Upadacitinib for axial spondyloarthritis: a meta-analysis of efficacy and safety (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39421642: Gut microbiota dysbiosis in ankylosing spondylitis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39539543: A guideline on biomarkers in the diagnosis and evaluation in axial spondyloarthritis (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39680252: Updating the 2019 ACR/SAA/SPARTAN Recommendations for the Treatment of Ankylosing Spondylitis and Nonradiographic Axial Spondyloarthritis (SPARTAN 2024 Annual Meeting Proceedings) (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `anorectal` (topics 138) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The ACG and ACPGBI guidelines cover benign anorectal disorders and anal fissure. Faecal incontinence affects about one in twelve adults, and obstetric anal sphincter injury guidance covers prevention through long-term care.

**Teaching points**
- The ACG guideline on benign anorectal disorders uses GRADE and expert consensus key-concept statements. [PMID 34618700]
- The ACPGBI guideline summarises evidence for management of anal fissure. [PMID 37926920]
- Approximately 1 in 12 adults worldwide have faecal incontinence, more among women and older people. [PMID 37734583]
- Canadian guidelines cover prevention, recognition and immediate management of obstetrical anal sphincter injuries, and their long-term management and counselling. [PMID 39581327, 41308919]
- Best clinical practice recommendations exist for laser haemorrhoidoplasty for symptomatic haemorrhoids, covering perioperative strategies and postoperative care. [PMID 39579281]
- Functional defecation disorders are common in European children. [PMID 39775925]

**Uncertainties**
- First-line medical therapy details are not in the excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Prevalence of faecal incontinence.
- OASIS prevention and counselling.

**Case hooks**
- Painful defecation with a posterior midline fissure.

## Cluster `mediastinal_mass` (topics 139) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anterior mediastinal masses are mostly thymic epithelial tumours, managed by surgery with video-assisted or robotic approaches for suitable cases. Anaesthesia needs care because of airway and vascular compression risk.

**Teaching points**
- A China Anti-Cancer Association guideline provides integrative management for thymic epithelial tumours including systemic therapy for advanced disease and follow-up after resection. [PMID 38881812]
- Robotic-assisted, video-assisted and open thymectomy for thymoma were compared, with robotic use constrained by cost, surgeon training and a lack of high-quality prospective data. [PMID 40656279]
- Thoracoscopic and robotic mediastinal tumour resection show insignificant differences in tumour size and operation time, with robotic surgery increasing hospitalisation costs. [PMID 38413953]
- In children, video-assisted resection of mediastinal tumours can be safe with low length of stay, while more complex tumours still need open surgery. [PMID 40086163]
- Anaesthetic management of patients with an anterior mediastinal mass has evolved, with new approaches for safe induction and maintenance of general anaesthesia. [PMID 37261097]
- AI models for diagnosis and prognosis of thymic epithelial tumours are not yet ready for clinical implementation. [PMID 41098391]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term oncologic adequacy of robotic resection in children.

**MCQ angles**
- Thymoma is the commonest anterior mass.
- Airway risk during anaesthesia.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with myasthenia gravis and an anterior mediastinal mass.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Differential diagnosis (the four Ts), CT features, tumour markers and Masaoka staging are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37261097: The evolution of anesthesia management of patients with anterior mediastinal mass (review, 2023)
- PMID 38413953: Meta-analysis of clinical efficacy of thoracoscopy and robotic surgery in the treatment of mediastinal tumors (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38881812: China Anti-Cancer Association Guidelines for the diagnosis, treatment, and follow-up of thymic epithelial tumors (2023) (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40086163: Comparison of Video-assisted Surgery and Open Surgery for Mediastinal Tumor Resection in Pediatric Population: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40656279: Comparative Effectiveness of Robotic-Assisted, Video-Assisted, and Open Thymectomy for Thymoma: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41098391: Artificial intelligence for diagnosis and prognosis of thymic epithelial tumors: a systematic review (systematic review, 2025)

## Cluster `ie_prophylaxis` (topics 141) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Antibiotic prophylaxis before invasive dental procedures lowers infective endocarditis incidence in high-risk individuals, supporting AHA and ESC recommendations, with no proven association in low or unknown risk groups. S. aureus is now the most common causative organism.

**Teaching points**
- Antibiotic prophylaxis before invasive dental procedures was associated with lower infective endocarditis incidence in high-risk individuals, with no association shown for low or unknown risk, supporting current AHA and ESC recommendations. [PMID 38581643]
- S. aureus is now the most common cause of infective endocarditis, with in-hospital mortality of 14% in the cited review. [PMID 40015544]
- Transitioning from intravenous to oral therapy for S. aureus bacteraemia or endocarditis is likely effective in a carefully selected subgroup but needs randomised trials. [PMID 39290168]
- The 2023 ESC endocarditis guideline added a class I recommendation for surgery in early prosthetic valve endocarditis (under 6 months). [PMID 41410976, 41410977]
- Percutaneous mechanical aspiration is the subject of an advisory for right-sided infective endocarditis, including patient selection and procedural planning. [PMID 42095279]
- In low-risk patients, TAVR gives long-term outcomes similar to SAVR, with more permanent pacemaker implantation. [PMID 39173988]

**Uncertainties**
- Oral step-down evidence is limited.

**MCQ angles**
- Who needs dental prophylaxis (high-risk only).
- Early surgery for prosthetic valve endocarditis.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with a prosthetic valve before tooth extraction.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** The list of high-risk conditions, antibiotic and dose, and Duke criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38581643: Antibiotic Prophylaxis and Infective Endocarditis Incidence Following Invasive Dental Procedures: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39173988: Long-Term Results Following Transcatheter Versus Surgical Aortic Valve Replacement in Low-Risk Patients With Severe Aortic Stenosis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized Trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39290168: Oral Versus Intravenous Antibiotic Therapy for Staphylococcus aureus Bacteremia or Endocarditis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized, Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40015544: Epidemiological Profile and Mortality of Infective Endocarditis Over the Past Decade: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of 133 Studies (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41410976: 2023 European Society of Cardiology guidelines for the management of infective endocarditis : Statement of endorsement by the NVVC Full version (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41410977: 2023 European Society of Cardiology guidelines for the management of infective endocarditis : Statement of endorsement by the NVVC Short version (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42095279: Percutaneous Mechanical Aspiration in Patients With Right-Sided Infective Endocarditis: A Science Advisory From the American Heart Association (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `stewardship` (topics 142, 143, 144, 163, 164) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Stewardship guidance spans cUTI, pneumonia, outpatient parenteral therapy and antibiotic allergy evaluation. Recommendations for empiric antibiotics value stewardship and reduced treatment burden; OPAT is a safe, cost-effective alternative to inpatient care with ID and stewardship expertise.

**Teaching points**
- IDSA's 2025 cUTI guideline gives recommendations for empiric antibiotic choice at the point of care, placing high value on stewardship. [PMID 41419213, 41419448]
- The German OPAT guideline concludes OPAT is a safe, cost-effective alternative to inpatient care requiring ID expertise and antimicrobial stewardship. [PMID 40844698]
- The ESCMID guideline on reported antibiotic allergy supports allergy evaluation including controlled drug challenge to promote prudent prescribing and stewardship. [PMID 41707748]
- The JRS 2024 pneumonia guideline highlights advance care planning and interdisciplinary collaboration. [PMID 40614556]
- Pediatric and adult UTI guidelines note data gaps and call for evidence-based rather than eminence-based practice. [PMID 39495518]
- An international guideline discusses antimicrobial stewardship and microbial-binding dressings to limit antimicrobial requirements in wound care. [PMID 42261894]
- Surgical Infection Society guidelines address stewardship and infection control in paediatric intra-abdominal infection. [PMID 41083248]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for several UTI strategies is weak.

**MCQ angles**
- Allergy delabelling supports stewardship.
- OPAT requires ID oversight.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with cellulitis eligible for outpatient parenteral therapy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** De-escalation, IV-to-oral switch criteria, duration and 48-72 hour review rules are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `adc` (topics 145) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Antibody-drug conjugate toxicity depends on linker cleavability and payload release. Trastuzumab deruxtecan can cause interstitial lung disease, sacituzumab govitecan causes neutropenia and diarrhoea, and HER2-low reporting needs structured pathology.

**Teaching points**
- ADCs with cleavable linkers cause premature payload release, raising systemic free payload and associated toxicities. [PMID 39704752]
- A meta-analysis of ADC toxicity found more all-grade treatment-related adverse events, with no increase in high-grade events. [PMID 37756687]
- Trastuzumab deruxtecan adverse events mostly affect blood, lymphatic and gastrointestinal systems, with interstitial lung disease a notable and potentially serious event. [PMID 37775347]
- Sacituzumab govitecan toxicity includes neutropenia, nausea, diarrhoea and anaemia, supporting prophylactic protocols. [PMID 39616817]
- Caution is advised when irradiating intracranial sites concurrently with trastuzumab emtansine, and international guidance on combining T-DM1 with radiotherapy is needed. [PMID 37437610]
- A US Delphi consensus guides prevention and management of stomatitis with datopotamab deruxtecan. [PMID 40762824]
- International consensus recommends structured HER2 reporting that distinguishes IHC 0 (null), 0+ (ultralow) and HER2-low. [PMID 41167529]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal toxicity prophylaxis for each ADC.

**MCQ angles**
- Interstitial lung disease with trastuzumab deruxtecan.
- HER2-low definition.

**Case hooks**
- Patient on trastuzumab deruxtecan developing new cough and hypoxia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Approved indications, mechanism details and monitoring schedules are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `anti_cd20` (topics 146) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anti-CD20 therapy (rituximab) is supported by EULAR for lupus nephritis, in RA-ILD it is a safe option, and B-cell targeting is being reviewed across immune-mediated kidney disease. Cytokine release and infection risk require monitoring.

**Teaching points**
- EULAR 2025 recommendations for SLE with kidney involvement give consensus management adjusted for severity, long-term efficacy, safety, cost and drug availability. [PMID 41107121]
- An updated meta-analysis for RA-ILD supports the safety of methotrexate and particularly supports abatacept, rituximab and nintedanib as promising options. [PMID 40876558]
- A KDIGO controversies conference on targeting B cells in immune-mediated kidney diseases notes risks of cytokine release syndrome and the need for biomarkers and optimal treatment duration. [PMID 42034308]
- Rituximab for myasthenia gravis has been tested in two trials with differing populations and dosing, leading to mixed conclusions. [PMID 40607605]
- NMOSD consensus recommendations cover diagnosis, treatment and pregnancy planning after 12 months of stability. [PMID 41591715]
- Chinese guidance for off-label drug use in Sjogren's disease gives 21 recommendations for glandular, systemic and organ-specific manifestations. [PMID 41148515]

**Uncertainties**
- Quality of evidence outside lupus nephritis and ANCA vasculitis.

**MCQ angles**
- Rituximab in RA-ILD.
- B-cell depletion in kidney disease.

**Case hooks**
- Refractory lupus nephritis considered for rituximab.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Dosing regimens, hypogammaglobulinaemia, hepatitis B reactivation and vaccination response are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40607605: Rituximab for myasthenia gravis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40876558: Updated systematic literature review and meta-analysis to inform the Italian Society of Rheumatology Recommendations on the treatment of rheumatoid arthritis-associated interstitial lung disease (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41107121: EULAR recommendations for the management of systemic lupus erythematosus with kidney involvement: 2025 update (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `af_anticoag` (topics 147, 149, 175, 176, 177, 204, 205, 206, 208, 495) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** DOACs are as effective as and often safer than warfarin in AF, including CKD (CrCl 15 and above); evidence is weaker in dialysis. DOACs should be standard in low-risk AF needing anticoagulation, with correct dosing, and device-detected AF also benefits modestly.

**Teaching points**
- DOACs may increase efficacy and improve safety versus VKAs in AF with CKD (CrCl 15-90), with at least similar results in kidney failure. [PMID 38679736]
- In stage 5 CKD, DOACs showed a significant reduction in stroke and a non-significant trend to less major bleeding than VKAs. [PMID 38281231]
- In haemodialysis patients with AF, DOACs versus warfarin comparisons need validation by further prospective studies given thrombotic and bleeding risks. [PMID 35757350]
- DOACs should be standard of care in low-risk AF patients needing anticoagulation, with dabigatran showing the best balance of stroke prevention and bleeding reduction in one analysis. [PMID 37573616]
- A network meta-analysis found no difference in major bleeding, thromboembolism or mortality between DOACs and VKAs, and no DOAC clearly more favourable than others. [PMID 37703679]
- Anticoagulation with edoxaban or apixaban reduces stroke in device-detected AF and increases major bleeding. [PMID 37952187]
- Off-label underdosed DOACs, particularly rivaroxaban, were associated with increased ischaemic stroke without reduced bleeding, so on-label dosing should be emphasised. [PMID 39214955]
- DOACs may be a feasible option in AF with cirrhosis, pending validation. [PMID 37639201]

**Uncertainties**
- Best strategy in dialysis patients.

**MCQ angles**
- DOAC dosing adherence.
- Anticoagulation in CKD and cirrhosis.

**Case hooks**
- AF patient with CKD stage 4 on warfarin considering a DOAC.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** CHA2DS2-VASc and HAS-BLED scoring, apixaban versus rivaroxaban head-to-head data and dose reduction criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35757350: Direct Oral Anticoagulants vs. Warfarin in Hemodialysis Patients With Atrial Fibrillation: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37573616: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Direct Oral Anticoagulants Versus Warfarin in Atrial Fibrillation With Low Stroke Risk (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37639201: Direct Oral Anticoagulants versus Vitamin K Antagonists in Cirrhotic Patients with Atrial Fibrillation: Update of Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37703679: An Evidence-Based Approach to Anticoagulation Therapy Comparing Direct Oral Anticoagulants and Vitamin K Antagonists in Patients With Atrial Fibrillation and Bioprosthetic Valves: A Systematic Review, Meta-Analysis, and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37952187: Direct Oral Anticoagulants for Stroke Prevention in Patients With Device-Detected Atrial Fibrillation: A Study-Level Meta-Analysis of the NOAH-AFNET 6 and ARTESiA Trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38281231: Direct oral anticoagulants versus vitamin K antagonists in patients with atrial fibrillation and stage 5 chronic kidney disease under dialysis: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38679736: Efficacy and safety of direct oral anticoagulants in patients with atrial fibrillation combined with chronic kidney disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39214955: Comparative Effectiveness and Safety of Off-Label Underdosed Direct Oral Anticoagulants in Asian Patients with Atrial Fibrillation: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
