## Cluster `penicillin_allergy` (topics 248) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Most patients labelled penicillin allergic can safely receive cefazolin, and delabelling by direct challenge or testing, including by non-allergists, is supported for stewardship. Inpatient testing improves outcomes and preoperative assessment can be done with appropriate training.

**Teaching points**
- Most patients with a penicillin allergy history may safely receive cefazolin, except those with confirmed penicillin allergy where additional care is warranted. [PMID 33729459]
- Patients with a documented penicillin allergy who need penicillin should be tested during hospitalisation given the benefit to outcomes and antibiotic stewardship. [PMID 28370003]
- BSACI guidance for penicillin allergy delabelling services by non-allergists includes provocation testing and separate sections for adults and paediatrics. [PMID 36128691]
- Hong Kong consensus supports penicillin allergy testing by non-allergists, with low-risk cases managed at spoke clinics supported by an allergist-led hub. [PMID 36133403]
- Consensus recommendations for the surgical patient with a penicillin allergy label describe assessment, testing strategies and minimum safe standards. [PMID 30916014]
- The drug allergy practice parameter update covers delayed hypersensitivity, delayed intradermal and patch testing and reactions to chemotherapeutics and biologics. [PMID 36563781]
- The ACOG bulletin addresses antibiotic selection and dosing for women with a penicillin allergy in labour and delivery. [PMID 30134425]

**Uncertainties**
- Cross-reactivity with other cephalosporins is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Cefazolin in penicillin allergy label.
- Delabelling by direct oral challenge.

**Case hooks**
- Preoperative patient with a childhood rash label to penicillin.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Risk stratification tools (PEN-FAST), skin testing sensitivity and oral challenge protocols are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28370003: Clinical outcomes following inpatient penicillin allergy testing: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 30134425: ACOG Practice Bulletin No. 199: Use of Prophylactic Antibiotics in Labor and Delivery (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 30916014: Management of a surgical patient with a label of penicillin allergy: narrative review and consensus recommendations (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 33729459: Assessment of the Frequency of Dual Allergy to Penicillins and Cefazolin: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 36128691: BSACI guideline for the set-up of penicillin allergy de-labelling services by non-allergists working in a hospital setting (guideline, 2022)

## Cluster `biliary_stricture` (topics 249) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ACG and ESGE guidelines direct work-up of biliary strictures, with cholangioscopy-guided biopsy added to standard ERCP sampling. EUS-guided biliary drainage has similar efficacy to ERCP drainage with fewer reinterventions and less post-procedure pancreatitis in malignant obstruction.

**Teaching points**
- The ACG guideline provides evidence-based guidance on extrahepatic and perihilar biliary strictures, focusing on diagnosis and drainage. [PMID 36863037]
- The ESGE guideline suggests cholangioscopy-guided biopsies in addition to standard ERCP diagnostic modalities, with intraductal imaging used selectively. [PMID 39689874]
- A guideline gives a conditional recommendation for single-operator cholangioscopy in indeterminate biliary strictures. [PMID 38148404]
- EUS-guided biliary drainage and ERCP-guided drainage had similar efficacy and safety for malignant biliary obstruction, with EUS-BD having lower reintervention, post-procedure pancreatitis and tumour ingrowth. [PMID 38648989]
- Partially covered self-expandable metal stents may be a standard comparator for palliation of distal malignant biliary obstruction. [PMID 37813199]
- EUS-guided hepaticogastrostomy is an effective and safe procedure for malignant biliary obstruction drainage. [PMID 38447958]
- ASGE guidance addresses the role of endoscopy in diagnosis and management of other biliary and pancreatic conditions including celiac plexus neurolysis for pain. [PMID 39387777]

**Uncertainties**
- Patient selection for EUS-guided drainage.

**MCQ angles**
- Cholangioscopy-guided biopsy.
- EUS versus ERCP drainage.

**Case hooks**
- Jaundiced patient with a hilar stricture on MRCP.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Benign versus malignant differentiation, IgG4 stricture, brush cytology sensitivity and stent selection are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 36863037: ACG Clinical Guideline: Diagnosis and Management of Biliary Strictures (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37813199: Fully covered versus partially covered self-expandable metal stents for palliation of distal malignant biliary obstruction: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38148404: Clinical practice guideline on the use of single-operator cholangioscopy in the diagnosis of indeterminate biliary stricture and the treatment of difficult biliary stones (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38447958: Efficacy and safety of endoscopic ultrasound-guided hepaticogastrostomy: a meta-regression analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38648989: EUS- versus ERCP-guided biliary drainage for malignant biliary obstruction: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39387777: American Society for Gastrointestinal Endoscopy guideline on the role of endoscopy in the diagnosis and management of solid pancreatic masses: summary and recommendations (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39689874: Diagnostic work-up of bile duct strictures: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `btc` (topics 250, 367, 368) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Conversion surgery after downstaging is justified in initially unresectable biliary tract cancer with favourable survival. Minimally invasive liver surgery has similar safety to open surgery for perihilar and intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma, and prognostic scores such as the Glasgow score help risk stratification.

**Teaching points**
- Conversion surgery in initially unresectable biliary tract cancer is justified with favourable long-term survival (hazard ratio 0.39 versus non-surgical treatment). [PMID 39918658]
- Minimally invasive liver surgery for perihilar and intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma has a similar safety profile with benefits in recovery and oncological outcomes compared with open surgery. [PMID 40931102]
- The preoperative Glasgow prognostic score is an effective prognostic indicator in biliary tract cancer and may aid risk stratification. [PMID 40264786]
- A 2026 Chinese guideline covers laparoscopic radical resection of intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma, including conversion and downstaging surgery. [PMID 42669434, 42669443]
- Radiomics and machine learning models may diagnose microvascular invasion in cholangiocarcinoma but need multicentre validation. [PMID 40088548]
- Clinical practice guidelines for peroral cholangioscopy give a practical reference for biliary endoscopy. [PMID 41400491]
- Time-of-day immunotherapy administration was associated with improved outcomes in advanced cancers, warranting prospective evaluation. [PMID 42084869]

**Uncertainties**
- Systemic therapy sequencing is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Conversion surgery for unresectable disease.
- Prognostic scoring.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma downstaged by chemotherapy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Gemcitabine-cisplatin with durvalumab, targeted therapy (FGFR2, IDH1), staging and adjuvant capecitabine are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39918658: Survival benefit of conversion surgery for initially unresectable biliary tract cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40088548: Radiomics and machine learning models for diagnosing microvascular invasion in cholangiocarcinoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis of diagnostic test accuracy studies (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40264786: Preoperative glasgow prognostic score was an effective prognostic indicator in patients with biliary tract cancer (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40931102: Minimally invasive liver surgery for perihilar and intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma: systematic review and meta-analysis of comparative studies (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41400491: Clinical Practice Guidelines for Peroral Cholangioscopy (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42084869: Time-of-Day Immunotherapy Administration and Outcomes in Advanced Cancers: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 42669434: [Clinical practice guideline for laparoscopic radical resection of intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma(2026)] (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `biofilm` (topics 251) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Biofilms are highly prevalent in chronic wounds and form on implants and tubing; the 2025 International Consensus on musculoskeletal infection sets research priorities. Standardised biofilm detection and anti-biofilm therapies remain unmet needs.

**Teaching points**
- Biofilms have a high prevalence in chronic wounds, despite methodological heterogeneity of studies. [PMID 40931368]
- The 2025 International Consensus Meeting on Musculoskeletal Infection sets research priorities to prevent, diagnose and treat biofilm-associated infection, including clinically relevant animal models and multicentre data collection. [PMID 41506787, 41885903]
- Biofilm-forming Enterococcus faecalis is globally prevalent in clinical isolates, supporting standardised biofilm detection protocols, infection control, stewardship and anti-biofilm therapies. [PMID 40764534]
- Biofilm forms on endotracheal and tracheostomy tubing, and culture-based approaches can identify its constituents without tube removal. [PMID 40619937]
- A CIED infection guideline offers practical prevention guidance addressing gaps in existing guidelines. [PMID 41149277]
- Denture wearing alters the oral microbiome, and frequent professional cleaning with individual hygiene strategies helps prevent denture-associated infection. [PMID 40442493]

**Uncertainties**
- Treatment of established biofilm infection is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Biofilm in chronic wounds.
- Device-associated infection.

**Case hooks**
- Chronic wound failing to heal despite repeated antibiotics.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Mechanisms of antibiotic tolerance, wound debridement, device removal and prosthetic joint management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40442493: Dentures and the oral microbiome: Unraveling the hidden impact on edentulous and partially edentulous patients - a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40619937: Biofilm Formation on Endotracheal and Tracheostomy Tubing: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Culture Data and Sampling Method (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40764534: The global prevalence of biofilm-forming Enterococcus faecalis in clinical isolates: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40931368: Prevalence of biofilm in chronic wounds: systematic review with meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41149277: Addressing Complications in Cardiac Implantable Electronic Devices: A Guideline to Prevention of CIED Infection (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41506787: 2025 International Consensus Meeting on Musculoskeletal Infection: Summary From the Biofilm Workgroup on Biofilm Formation, Persistence, and Host-Environment Interactions (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41885903: The 2025 International Consensus Meeting on Musculoskeletal Infection: Research Priorities and Future Directions (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `biologics_malignancy` (topics 253) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Anti-TNF therapy is associated with lymphoma risk in IBD, especially combined with thiopurines, but in rheumatoid arthritis anti-TNFs do not significantly raise cancer risk. Tofacitinib carries a slightly higher cancer risk than anti-TNF, and hepatosplenic T-cell lymphoma is rare, mostly with thiopurine exposure.

**Teaching points**
- There is an increased risk of lymphoma in IBD patients treated with anti-TNF agents, either alone or combined with thiopurines. [PMID 32840893]
- In rheumatoid arthritis, anti-TNFs decrease mortality and cardiovascular events without significantly increasing cancer risk compared with conventional DMARDs. [PMID 27341745]
- Anti-TNF therapy in rheumatoid arthritis was not associated with increased lymphoma risk versus conventional therapies, though more research is needed. [PMID 39064585]
- Tofacitinib cancer risk was similar to placebo or biologics, with a slightly higher risk than anti-TNF agents. [PMID 37190126]
- In hepatosplenic T-cell lymphoma on biologics for IBD, most cases had azathioprine or mercaptopurine exposure and all biologic cases had anti-TNF exposure. [PMID 31990422]
- A review of non-biologic immunosuppressants gives evidence-based malignancy risk estimates influenced by duration and dose. [PMID 35852916]
- Mayo Clinic guidelines summarise diagnosis, risk stratification and management of CLL, including Richter transformation. [PMID 42246910]

**Uncertainties**
- Cancer risk of JAK inhibitors versus TNF inhibitors.

**MCQ angles**
- Combination anti-TNF plus thiopurine and lymphoma.
- Hepatosplenic T-cell lymphoma.

**Case hooks**
- Young man with Crohn's on infliximab and azathioprine.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Solid tumour risk, skin cancer, screening before biologics and management of prior malignancy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27341745: Safety of biologic DMARDs in RA patients in real life: A systematic literature review and meta-analyses of biologic registers (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31990422: Systematic review: hepatosplenic T-cell lymphoma on biologic therapy for inflammatory bowel disease, including data from the Food and Drug Administration Adverse Event Reporting System (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 32840893: Systematic review with meta-analysis: comparative risk of lymphoma with anti-tumour necrosis factor agents and/or thiopurines in patients with inflammatory bowel disease (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 35852916: Malignancy risk of non-biologic immunosuppressive therapies: A review of the literature with evidence-based treatment recommendations (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37190126: Cancer Risk in Patients Treated with the JAK Inhibitor Tofacitinib: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39064585: Anti-TNF Alpha and Risk of Lymphoma in Rheumatoid Arthritis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 42246910: Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma: Mayo Clinic Systematic Management and Risk-Adapted Therapy for Lymphoid Malignancies (lySMART) Consensus Guidelines 2026 (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `ra_biologic_switch` (topics 254) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** EULAR points to consider for difficult-to-treat RA promote personalised strategies, and evidence is scarce. Switching to a drug with a new mechanism of action may be somewhat more effective, tocilizumab and rituximab perform well in difficult-to-treat RA, and tofacitinib has similar efficacy to biologics after TNF failure.

**Teaching points**
- EULAR points to consider for difficult-to-treat RA encourage holistic management and personalised pharmacological and non-pharmacological strategies, with high-quality evidence scarce. [PMID 34407926]
- In difficult-to-treat RA, switching to a drug with a previously untargeted mechanism of action was somewhat more effective, and non-pharmacological interventions helped non-inflammatory complaints and self-management. [PMID 33419871]
- Tocilizumab and rituximab have better efficacy and safety in difficult-to-treat RA, with tocilizumab 8 mg every 4 weeks possibly the first choice for remission. [PMID 39198829]
- Ultrasound may add value to assess inflammatory activity in difficult-to-treat RA, including those with obesity or fibromyalgia. [PMID 33514671]
- Tofacitinib had similar efficacy and adverse-event discontinuation rates to biologic DMARDs in RA with inadequate response to TNF inhibitors. [PMID 28377787, 27889300]
- Secukinumab 150 mg showed better efficacy in RA with inadequate response to TNF inhibitors without increased serious adverse events. [PMID 31087226]
- Single switching between reference TNF inhibitors and biosimilars is efficacious and safe for rheumatoid arthritis. [PMID 37607959]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal sequencing after TNF failure.

**MCQ angles**
- Switching mechanism of action after TNF failure.
- Biosimilar switching safety.

**Case hooks**
- RA patient failing two TNF inhibitors.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Cycling within class versus switching class, definitions of D2T-RA and JAK inhibitor safety are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27889300: Tofacitinib Versus Biologic Treatments in Patients With Active Rheumatoid Arthritis Who Have Had an Inadequate Response to Tumor Necrosis Factor Inhibitors: Results From a Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 28377787: Tofacitinib versus Biologic Treatments in Moderate-to-Severe Rheumatoid Arthritis Patients Who Have Had an Inadequate Response to Nonbiologic DMARDs: Systematic Literature Review and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31087226: Efficacy and safety of secukinumab in active rheumatoid arthritis with an inadequate response to tumor necrosis factor inhibitors: a meta-analysis of phase III randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 33419871: Pharmacological and non-pharmacological therapeutic strategies in difficult-to-treat rheumatoid arthritis: a systematic literature review informing the EULAR recommendations for the management of difficult-to-treat rheumatoid arthritis (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 33514671: Diagnostic issues in difficult-to-treat rheumatoid arthritis: a systematic literature review informing the EULAR recommendations for the management of difficult-to-treat rheumatoid arthritis (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34407926: EULAR points to consider for the management of difficult-to-treat rheumatoid arthritis (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37607959: Impact of switching between reference biologics and biosimilars of tumour necrosis factor inhibitors for rheumatoid arthritis: a systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39198829: Efficacy and safety of current therapies for difficult-to-treat rheumatoid arthritis: a systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `biologic_screening` (topics 256, 257) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Before biologics, screening for latent TB and hepatitis B is advised: USPSTF recommends LTBI screening in at-risk groups, society guidelines continue to recommend TB screening even for drugs without known TB risk, and HBV reactivation requires monitoring and antivirals if it occurs.

**Teaching points**
- The USPSTF recommends screening for latent tuberculosis infection in populations at increased risk (B recommendation). [PMID 37129649]
- IGRA tests are moderately sensitive and highly specific, and LTBI treatment reduces progression to active TB, with isoniazid showing more hepatotoxicity than rifampin. [PMID 37129650]
- Multiple society guidelines continue to recommend TB screening for patients on biologics even for drugs with no known TB reactivation risk, and practice varies. [PMID 39021381]
- Recommendations for psoriasis biologic and targeted therapies in patients with latent TB call for more differentiated updates to existing guidelines. [PMID 39149807]
- ASCO advises monitoring for HBV reactivation during cancer therapy with HBsAg and ALT, starting antivirals if reactivation occurs. [PMID 32716741]
- Young age is related to indeterminate IGRA results. [PMID 37256138]
- European guidance highlights disparities in TB screening of migrants and the need for standardisation. [PMID 40171660]

**Uncertainties**
- Screening need for each biologic class.

**MCQ angles**
- IGRA characteristics.
- HBV reactivation monitoring.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with psoriasis starting a biologic who emigrated from a high-TB country.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Hepatitis C and HIV screening, vaccination status and prophylaxis regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32716741: Hepatitis B Virus Screening and Management for Patients With Cancer Prior to Therapy: ASCO Provisional Clinical Opinion Update (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 37129649: Screening for Latent Tuberculosis Infection in Adults: US Preventive Services Task Force Recommendation Statement (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37129650: Screening for Latent Tuberculosis Infection in Adults: Updated Evidence Report and Systematic Review for the US Preventive Services Task Force (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37256138: Indeterminate results of interferon gamma release assays in the screening of latent tuberculosis infection: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39021381: Tuberculosis screening for patients on biologic Medications: A Single-Center experience and Society guideline Review, Monroe County, New York, 2018-2021 (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39149807: Treatment of psoriasis with biologic and non-biologic targeted therapies in patients with latent tuberculosis infection or at risk for tuberculosis disease progression: Recommendations from a SPIN-FRT expert consensus (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40171660: European guidelines for the management of tuberculosis screening procedures in migrants: A systematic review (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `bipolar` (topics 258, 259, 260) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Lithium has beneficial effects on suicide attempts and completed suicide that align with observational data, though statistical significance may be limited. Postpartum psychosis is classified within the bipolar spectrum, HLA testing guides carbamazepine safety, and objective sleep markers help distinguish bipolar from unipolar depression.

**Teaching points**
- Lithium's beneficial effects on suicide attempts and completed suicide align with observational studies, though trials may be underpowered. [PMID 40441661]
- A consensus statement classifies postpartum psychosis as a distinct illness within the bipolar spectrum, to improve detection and treatment. [PMID 41135771]
- A UK guideline covers HLA genotype testing for carbamazepine, oxcarbazepine and eslicarbazepine, requiring individual risk-benefit assessment. [PMID 42002301]
- Objective sleep markers may help differentiate unipolar and bipolar depression. [PMID 39978428]
- Mood monitoring and ambulatory assessment do not produce large placebo effects or negatively affect mood. [PMID 41499681]
- Patients with schizophrenia or bipolar disorder have shorter telomeres, unaffected by predominant lithium or antipsychotic treatment. [PMID 40209537]
- Divers with bipolar spectrum disorders need an individualised approach incorporating medication effects, comorbidity and relapse-prevention planning. [PMID 42290577]

**Uncertainties**
- Pharmacotherapy for acute mania and bipolar depression is not in the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Lithium and suicide risk.
- HLA-B*1502 testing before carbamazepine (not in excerpts).

**Case hooks**
- Woman with bipolar disorder planning pregnancy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Acute mania drugs, bipolar depression options (quetiapine, lurasidone), lithium monitoring and valproate teratogenicity are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39978428: Objective sleep markers to differentiate unipolar and bipolar depression: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40209537: Pharmacological interventions and telomere length in patients with schizophrenia and bipolar disorder: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40441661: The efficacy of lithium in the treatment of suicidal ideation, behavior and suicide: An updated systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41135771: Postpartum Psychosis and Bipolar Disorder: Review of Neurobiology and Expert Consensus Statement on Classification (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41499681: Mood Monitoring, Mood Tracking, and Ambulatory Assessment Interventions in Depression and Bipolar Disorder: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 42002301: HLA genotype testing for carbamazepine, oxcarbazepine and eslicarbazepine: A guideline developed by the UK Centre of Excellence in Regulatory Science and Innovation in Pharmacogenomics (CERSI-PGx) (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42290577: Bipolar spectrum disorders in divers: risks, recognition, and recommendations (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `bisphosphonates` (topics 261) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The living ACP guideline suggests individualised bisphosphonate use in women over 65 with osteopenia. Antiresorptives do not raise dental implant failure, teriparatide and denosumab increase BMD more than bisphosphonates, and romosozumab cardiovascular concerns appear smaller than feared.

**Teaching points**
- The living ACP guideline suggests an individualised decision about starting a bisphosphonate in females over 65 with osteopenia to reduce fractures (conditional recommendation, low-certainty evidence). [PMID 36592456]
- Teriparatide and denosumab were more efficacious than bisphosphonates in increasing BMD at the femoral neck, total hip and lumbar spine. [PMID 39312040]
- Romosozumab cardiovascular concerns seem less significant than previously suggested, though real-world data are required. [PMID 39227560]
- Implants may be placed safely with concomitant bisphosphonate or denosumab use in osteoporosis, without evidence of higher implant failure. [PMID 40335186]
- Adjuvant bisphosphonates in early breast cancer did not consistently reduce recurrence in any subset, per the ASCO-OH guideline. [PMID 35041467]
- The Endocrine Society guideline gives a framework for managing hypercalcaemia of malignancy. [PMID 36545746, 36545700]
- Updated myeloma bone disease guidance suggests biomarkers for guiding antiresorptive therapy and strategies to enhance osteoblastic activity. [PMID 41082262]

**Uncertainties**
- Treatment duration and drug holidays are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Individualised bisphosphonate in osteopenia.
- Denosumab versus bisphosphonate BMD.

**Case hooks**
- Postmenopausal woman with T-score -2.0 and a previous wrist fracture.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Alendronate and zoledronate regimens, atypical femur fracture, osteonecrosis of the jaw and renal dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35041467: Use of Adjuvant Bisphosphonates and Other Bone-Modifying Agents in Breast Cancer: ASCO-OH (CCO) Guideline Update (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36545700: A Systematic Review Supporting the Endocrine Society Clinical Practice Guideline on the Treatment of Hypercalcemia of Malignancy in Adults (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 36545746: Treatment of Hypercalcemia of Malignancy in Adults: An Endocrine Society Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 36592456: Pharmacologic Treatment of Primary Osteoporosis or Low Bone Mass to Prevent Fractures in Adults: A Living Clinical Guideline From the American College of Physicians (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39227560: Cardiovascular Safety of Romosozumab Compared to Commonly Used Anti-osteoporosis Medications in Postmenopausal Osteoporosis: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39312040: Efficacy and safety of teriparatide vs. bisphosphonates and denosumab vs. bisphosphonates in osteoporosis not previously treated with bisphosphonates: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40335186: Antiresorptive Therapy to Reduce Fracture Risk and Effects on Dental Implant Outcomes in Patients With Osteoporosis: A Systematic Review and Osteonecrosis of the Jaw Taskforce Consensus Statement (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41082262: Updated guidelines in the treatment of myeloma bone disease in 2025: consensus statement by the Medical and Scientific Advisory Group of Australia (MSAG) to Myeloma Australia (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `bk_virus` (topics 262, 263) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The second international BK polyomavirus consensus guides kidney transplant management; leflunomide, cidofovir, quinolones and IVIG are not supported. Screening practice varies widely, and consensus definitions of BKPyV nephropathy separate proven and probable disease for trials.

**Teaching points**
- The second international consensus on BK polyomavirus in kidney transplantation states studies do not support leflunomide, cidofovir, quinolones or IVIG, and experimental treatments should be given within clinical trials. [PMID 38605438]
- Consensus definitions of BK polyomavirus nephropathy refine proven disease and introduce a probable category for clinical trials. [PMID 35100619]
- A regional survey found large variation in BK screening and management protocols across centres, leading participating centres to implement uniform screening. [PMID 30175491]
- A systematic review of risk factors for BK viraemia and nephropathy after kidney transplant calls for full data publication and prospective multicentre studies. [PMID 30343190]
- ECIL guidelines state BKPyV haemorrhagic cystitis in HSCT remains an unmet need requiring novel approaches in trials. [PMID 29190347]
- An Italian paediatric expert consensus covers prevention, diagnosis and management of haemorrhagic cystitis after HCT. [PMID 38909124]
- BK polyomavirus-related infection and lymphopenia are risk factors for Pneumocystis pneumonia in solid organ transplant recipients. [PMID 33323766]

**Uncertainties**
- Effective antiviral therapy is lacking.

**MCQ angles**
- Reduce immunosuppression as primary management (not in excerpts).
- Screening variability.

**Case hooks**
- Kidney transplant recipient with rising creatinine and BK viraemia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Immunosuppression reduction thresholds, biopsy diagnosis and screening schedule are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29190347: ECIL guidelines for the prevention, diagnosis and treatment of BK polyomavirus-associated haemorrhagic cystitis in haematopoietic stem cell transplant recipients (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 30175491: New England BK consortium: Regional survey of BK screening and management protocols in comparison to published consensus guidelines (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 30343190: Risk factors for BK virus viremia and nephropathy after kidney transplantation: A systematic review (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 33323766: A Comprehensive Evaluation of Risk Factors for Pneumocystis jirovecii Pneumonia in Adult Solid Organ Transplant Recipients: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35100619: Consensus Definitions of BK Polyomavirus Nephropathy in Renal Transplant Recipients for Clinical Trials (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 38605438: The Second International Consensus Guidelines on the Management of BK Polyomavirus in Kidney Transplantation (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38909124: An expert consensus on prevention, diagnosis, and management of hemorrhagic cystitis in pediatric hematopoietic cell transplantation, on behalf of the Infectious Disease and Hematopoietic Cell Transplant Working groups of Italian Pediatric Hematology Oncology Association (AIEOP) (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `bladder_cancer` (topics 264) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Non-muscle-invasive bladder cancer risk stratification includes T1 substaging and NLR, with active surveillance proposed for low-grade disease and immune checkpoint inhibitors under study for BCG-unresponsive disease. AFU, EAU and ASTRO guidance cover perioperative chemo-immunotherapy and radiotherapy.

**Teaching points**
- An international Delphi gives the first expert-driven framework for active surveillance in low-grade NMIBC, including exit criteria. [PMID 41922791]
- T1 substaging systems prognosticate recurrence and progression in NMIBC, with Rete Oncologica Lombarda substaging showing promise. [PMID 41241655]
- Neutrophil-to-lymphocyte ratio is associated with recurrence and progression in NMIBC after BCG. [PMID 39507536]
- Immune checkpoint inhibitors show therapeutic response in BCG-unresponsive NMIBC, though variability and adverse events call for randomised trials. [PMID 41139553]
- Real-world data challenge management guidance for very high-risk NMIBC, so treatment decisions should be individualised. [PMID 40374480]
- French AFU recommendations update NMIBC induction and maintenance regimens and cystectomy indications, and the 2025 update includes perioperative chemotherapy with immunotherapy. [PMID 39581667, 41110705]
- The ASTRO guideline gives evidence-based recommendations on radiation therapy for bladder cancer for local control or palliation. [PMID 42744092]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal management of BCG-unresponsive disease.

**MCQ angles**
- Active surveillance in low-grade NMIBC.
- T1 substaging.

**Case hooks**
- High-grade T1 bladder tumour after TURBT.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** TURBT quality, BCG schedules, cystectomy indications and muscle-invasive disease management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39507536: Prognostic value of neutrophil-to-lymphocyte ratio in patients with non-muscle-invasive bladder cancer with intravesical Bacillus Calmette-Guérin immunotherapy: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39581667: French AFU Cancer Committee Guidelines - Update 2024-2026: Non-muscle invasive bladder cancer (NMIBC) (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40374480: Management Dilemma for Very High-risk Non-muscle-invasive Bladder Cancer: Real-World Data Challenge the Guideline Recommendation for Upfront Radical Cystectomy (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41110705: French recommendations of the AFU Oncology Committee for Bladder Cancer: Update 2025 (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `blood_culture` (topics 265) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ASM evidence-based practice guidelines show interventions reduce blood culture contamination by 40-60%, but devices alone are not enough without education. Systematic approaches reduce unnecessary cultures, while delays to sepsis bundle elements worsen outcomes.

**Teaching points**
- Evidence-based interventions can reduce blood culture contamination by approximately 40-60%, but devices alone without an educational component are less effective. [PMID 39495314]
- A systematic approach to blood cultures in critically ill children decreased total cultures and central venous catheter cultures without more mortality, readmission or suspected septic shock. [PMID 27942705]
- Each delay in lactate, blood cultures, crystalloids and antibiotics within the 3-hour sepsis bundle adversely affects outcomes. [PMID 29298189]
- The EFLM supports a correct order of draw for venous blood collection. [PMID 27444170]
- Early-onset neonatal sepsis management should stop antibiotics within 24 hours when cultures are negative, with streamlining when proven. [PMID 39417838]
- A COVID-19 guideline recommends against unnecessary antibiotic use given low prevalence of bacterial co-infection. [PMID 33010444]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal blood volume and number of sets are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Contamination reduction strategies.
- Order of draw.

**Case hooks**
- Single positive blood culture for coagulase-negative staphylococci.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Number of sets, volume per bottle, timing relative to antibiotics, skin antisepsis and interpretation of contaminants are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

**Sources**
- PMID 27444170: Order of blood draw: Opinion Paper by the European Federation for Clinical Chemistry and Laboratory Medicine (EFLM) Working Group for the Preanalytical Phase (WG-PRE) (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 27942705: Association of a Clinical Practice Guideline With Blood Culture Use in Critically Ill Children (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 29298189: Delay Within the 3-Hour Surviving Sepsis Campaign Guideline on Mortality for Patients With Severe Sepsis and Septic Shock (guideline, 2018)

- PMID 39495314: American Society for Microbiology evidence-based laboratory medicine practice guidelines to reduce blood culture contamination rates: a systematic review and meta-analysis (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `bone_joint_oral` (topics 266) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Shorter intravenous courses and early oral switch are non-inferior in prosthetic joint infection, and oral relay therapy with rifampicin companions is feasible, but vertebral osteomyelitis and diabetic foot osteomyelitis lack high-quality proof for early oral switch.

**Teaching points**
- A shorter intravenous antibiotic duration for prosthetic joint infection was non-inferior to a longer course, with improved odds of treatment success (OR 2.45). [PMID 36267262]
- For oral relay therapy in acute staphylococcal prosthetic joint infection, five companions to rifampicin are identified, but evidence is insufficient to compare them or to rationalise alternatives if rifampicin cannot be used. [PMID 39383095]
- Evidence is insufficient to conclude failure or relapse differs between early oral switch and standard therapy for native vertebral osteomyelitis, with high-quality studies needed before routine use. [PMID 36532294]
- Oral antibiotics may favour outcomes as monotherapy or combined with intravenous therapy in selected diabetic foot osteomyelitis. [PMID 40982761]
- Surgical debridement followed by long-term oral amoxicillin-clavulanate can be an effective empirical option in osteomyelitis. [PMID 41569452]
- Oral flucloxacillin is widely used for osteomyelitis, and prospective trials are called for. [PMID 32117685]
- The optimal duration of intravenous antibiotics after single-stage revision hip arthroplasty is undecided. [PMID 32459114]

**Uncertainties**
- Vertebral osteomyelitis oral switch.

**MCQ angles**
- Rifampicin combination in staphylococcal PJI.
- Shorter IV courses.

**Case hooks**
- Acute staphylococcal prosthetic knee infection treated with debridement and retention.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** OVIVA trial results, bioavailability of oral agents and total duration are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32117685: Oral Flucloxacillin for Treating Osteomyelitis: A Narrative Review of Clinical Practice (review, 2020)
- PMID 32459114: What is the optimal duration of intravenous antibiotics following single-stage revision total hip arthroplasty for prosthetic joint infection? A systematic review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 36267262: Intravenous antibiotic duration in the treatment of prosthetic joint infection: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36532294: Early switch to oral antibiotic therapy for the treatment of patients with bacterial native vertebral osteomyelitis: a quaternary center experience, systematic review, and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 39383095: Systematic review on oral antibacterial relay therapy for acute staphylococcal prosthetic joint infections treated with debridement, antibiotics and implant retention (DAIR) (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40982761: Use of Oral Antibiotics for Diabetic Foot Osteomyelitis: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2025)
- PMID 41569452: The role of oral amoxicillin/clavulanic acid in the treatment of osteomyelitis: a systematic review (systematic review, 2026)

## Cluster `bone_marrow` (topics 267, 268) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Bone marrow biopsy remains a component of diagnostic work-up in AL amyloidosis (with fat-pad sampling), mastocytosis and haemophagocytic syndromes, and its role in staging is being displaced by PET in lymphoma and Ewing sarcoma. Retrieved excerpts do not cover technique or fibrosis grading.

**Teaching points**
- ASH 2026 guidelines say AL amyloidosis can be diagnosed through surrogate biopsies, requiring both bone marrow biopsy and fat-pad sampling, with target organ biopsy favoured in some situations. [PMID 41592868]
- FDG-PET/CT was more valuable than bone marrow biopsy for evaluating marrow involvement in newly diagnosed paediatric lymphoma. [PMID 34372894]
- In Ewing sarcoma, bone marrow biopsy may be omitted in otherwise localised disease after FDG-PET staging. [PMID 33219750]
- The amount of haemophagocytosis on marrow samples correlates with HLH-2004 criteria, with marked interobserver variability addressed by reporting recommendations. [PMID 34711415]
- The diagnostic workup for systemic mastocytosis differs from consensus recommendations across regions, and consistent application of WHO criteria is advised. [PMID 38020286]
- Indian paediatric consensus recommends horse ATG and cyclosporine for immunosuppressive therapy in aplastic anaemia, with 3-6 months before response assessment. [PMID 35105820]

**Uncertainties**
- Fibrosis grading is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- PET versus marrow biopsy for lymphoma staging.
- Marrow in AL amyloidosis.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with suspected AL amyloidosis needing tissue diagnosis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Indications for aspirate versus trephine, technique and complications, and marrow fibrosis grading (MF-0 to MF-3) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

**Sources**
- PMID 33219750: Role of bone marrow biopsy for staging new patients with Ewing sarcoma: A systematic review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 34372894: FDG-PET/CT versus bone marrow biopsy in bone marrow involvement in newly diagnosed paediatric lymphoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)

## Cluster `bone_mets` (topics 269, 270) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ASTRO recommends external beam radiotherapy for symptomatic bone metastases with shared decision-making, and ISRS consensus supports SBRT for non-spine bone metastases as efficacious with low adverse events. Antiresorptives are used in myeloma bone disease and osteoporosis guidance.

**Teaching points**
- The ASTRO guideline informs best practice for external beam radiotherapy palliation of symptomatic bone metastases and recommends shared decision-making about toxicity and quality of life. [PMID 38788923]
- ISRS-endorsed consensus recommendations on SBRT for non-spine bone metastases found it efficacious with low rates of adverse events. [PMID 39862925]
- An international consensus defines palliative radiotherapy endpoints for future bone metastasis trials to improve comparability. [PMID 41285182]
- Updated myeloma bone disease guidance suggests markers to guide antiresorptive therapy and strategies to enhance osteoblastic activity. [PMID 41082262]
- The RACGP and Healthy Bones Australia osteoporosis guideline covers risk status and early use of osteoanabolic therapies. [PMID 40134107]
- A meta-analysis reports the sites, rates and survival of lung cancer metastasis with risk factors. [PMID 40650339]
- Implants may be placed safely with bisphosphonates or denosumab in osteoporosis. [PMID 40335186]

**Uncertainties**
- Denosumab versus zoledronic acid in bone metastases is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Radiotherapy for painful bone metastases.
- SBRT for non-spine bone disease.

**Case hooks**
- Prostate cancer patient with painful femoral metastasis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Single-fraction versus multi-fraction dosing, cord compression, pathological fracture fixation and bone-modifying agent dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `bpd` (topics 271) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Borderline personality disorder carries high suicide risk, and dialectical behaviour therapy variants, including DBT-PTSD and DBT-PE, reduce PTSD and depressive symptoms. Digital interventions can help suicidal ideation and paranoia, and personality disorders relate to violence risk.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis reports prevalence of suicide ideation, attempt and suicide in borderline personality disorder, influenced by age and clinical setting, with the impact of comorbid depression unclear. [PMID 40279864]
- DBT variants (DBT-PTSD and DBT-PE) were effective in reducing PTSD symptom severity and comorbid depressive symptoms. [PMID 39351658]
- Digital interventions for BPD symptoms support transdiagnostic digital interventions for paranoia and suicidal ideation. [PMID 39612494]
- A consensus guideline lists recommended psychotherapies for adults with psychiatric disorders, summarising indications and therapeutic tasks. [PMID 38487817]
- Personality disorders are associated with violence and antisocial behaviour, so improving identification and treatment of comorbid substance misuse may reduce adverse outcomes. [PMID 39659141]
- SSRIs, psychotherapy and combinations were compared for non-suicidal self-injury, with depressive symptoms important to address in the first 3 months. [PMID 40181383]
- Guidelines for complex PTSD psychotherapies rate certainty of evidence as low. [PMID 40234083]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term DBT outcomes for BPD are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- DBT variants for comorbid PTSD.
- Suicide risk in BPD.

**Case hooks**
- Young woman with self-harm, unstable relationships and impulsivity.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Core DBT components, mentalisation-based therapy, medication role and crisis management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `botulism` (topics 272) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Early botulinum antitoxin reduces mortality in foodborne botulism, though respiratory failure can still occur and intensive care is essential. CDC clinical guidelines cover foodborne, wound and inhalational botulism, with infant botulism treated by human botulism immune globulin.

**Teaching points**
- Early antitoxin administration reduces mortality in foodborne botulism, although some patients still develop respiratory failure, so prompt antitoxin and meticulous intensive care are essential. [PMID 29293927]
- CDC clinical guidelines recommend best practices for diagnosing, monitoring and treating foodborne, wound and inhalational botulism. [PMID 33956777]
- Human botulism immune globulin (BIG-IV) shortened hospital stay by about 6 weeks in infant botulism with no difference in adverse events or complications. [PMID 30993666]
- Anaphylactic reactions occur in 1-2% of botulinum antitoxin recipients and require epinephrine and antihistamine treatment. [PMID 29293931]
- In children, antitoxin recipients had better survival and serious adverse events were rare, and most deaths occurred early. [PMID 29293924]
- In pregnancy and postpartum, botulism needs a high level of suspicion, with preparation for possible intubation. [PMID 29293925]
- Heptavalent botulism antitoxin registry data specifically solicited bradycardia, hypersensitivity and serum sickness events. [PMID 35050996]

**Uncertainties**
- Antitoxin evidence is largely observational.

**MCQ angles**
- Antitoxin for foodborne botulism.
- BIG-IV for infants.

**Case hooks**
- Descending paralysis with dilated pupils after home-canned food.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Clinical features (descending flaccid paralysis, cranial neuropathies), differentiation from Guillain-Barre syndrome and wound botulism debridement are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29293924: Pediatric Botulism and Use of Equine Botulinum Antitoxin in Children: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2017)
- PMID 29293925: Botulism During Pregnancy and the Postpartum Period: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2017)
- PMID 29293927: Efficacy of Antitoxin Therapy in Treating Patients With Foodborne Botulism: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis of Cases, 1923-2016 (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29293931: Workgroup Report by the Joint Task Force Involving American Academy of Allergy, Asthma & Immunology (AAAAI); Food Allergy, Anaphylaxis, Dermatology and Drug Allergy (FADDA) (Adverse Reactions to Foods Committee and Adverse Reactions to Drugs, Biologicals, and Latex Committee); and the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention Botulism Clinical Treatment Guidelines Workgroup-Allergic Reactions to Botulinum Antitoxin: A Systematic Review (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 30993666: Medical treatment for botulism (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 33956777: Clinical Guidelines for Diagnosis and Treatment of Botulism, 2021 (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35050996: Use of Botulism Antitoxin Heptavalent (A, B, C, D, E, F, G)-(Equine) (BAT<sup>®</sup>) in Clinical Study Subjects and Patients: A 15-Year Systematic Safety Review (systematic review, 2021)

## Cluster `crc` (topics 273, 420, 421, 423) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** JSCCR 2024 and ACS 2026 guidelines frame treatment and screening of colorectal cancer. Circulating tumour DNA is prognostic in metastatic disease, low skeletal muscle index predicts worse survival in non-metastatic disease, and UGT1A1 genotyping informs irinotecan use.

**Teaching points**
- The 2024 JSCCR treatment guidelines present recommendations with an evidence classification and recommendation category based on committee consensus. [PMID 41186794]
- Circulating tumour DNA is a prognostic marker in metastatic colorectal cancer under systemic therapy and could be incorporated into future trials. [PMID 40743933]
- Pretreatment skeletal muscle index from CT is associated with survival outcomes in non-metastatic colorectal cancer, though thresholds need standardising. [PMID 40576903]
- A UK guideline covers UGT1A1 genotype testing for irinotecan, requiring individual risk-benefit assessment. [PMID 42389808]
- The 2026 ACS screening update says a positive non-colonoscopy screening test requires timely follow-up colonoscopy, preferably within 6 months. [PMID 42200680]
- The US Multi-Society Task Force consensus says first-degree relatives of patients with serrated polyposis syndrome are at heightened CRC risk and should undergo colonoscopic screening. [PMID 42814066]
- Non-adherence to colorectal cancer guidelines varies across populations and systems, affecting survival. [PMID 42684532]

**Uncertainties**
- Adjuvant chemotherapy details are not in the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- ctDNA as prognostic marker.
- Colonoscopy after positive stool test.

**Case hooks**
- Stage III colon cancer after resection.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Staging, adjuvant chemotherapy by stage and MMR/MSI status, and metastatic regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40576903: Pretreatment Skeletal Muscle Index and Survival Outcomes in Non-Metastatic Colorectal Cancer: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40743933: Circulating tumor DNA as prognostic marker in patients with metastatic colorectal cancer undergoing systemic therapy: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41186794: Japanese Society for Cancer of the Colon and Rectum (JSCCR) guidelines 2024 for the treatment of colorectal cancer (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `bowel_obs` (topics 274) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** For malignant large bowel obstruction, stenting as a bridge to surgery has short-term advantages over emergency surgery, with stoma an alternative if stenting is unavailable. MASCC recommends octreotide in inoperable malignant bowel obstruction.

**Teaching points**
- A bridge-to-surgery stent for left-sided malignant colonic obstruction reduces adverse events and stoma formation in the short term, depending on local expertise and clinical status. [PMID 28392363]
- ESGE suggests a decompressing stoma as bridge to elective surgery is valid when stenting is not suitable or expertise unavailable (weak recommendation, low-quality evidence). [PMID 32259849]
- International guidelines lack high-quality evidence on whether emergency surgery or stenting as a bridge is optimal for left-sided malignant obstruction. [PMID 31139245]
- The 2021 MASCC guideline recommends octreotide in non-operable malignant bowel obstruction and notes few randomised antiemetic trials. [PMID 34390398]
- MASCC multidisciplinary recommendations also provide guidance on advance care planning and psychosocial support in malignant bowel obstruction. [PMID 35274188]
- In small bowel obstruction, early diagnosis with CT is key, with rehydration, antibiotics and nil per os and surgery for failed medical therapy; mortality rises to about 30% with necrosis or perforation. [PMID 28439845]
- WSES guidelines address colon and rectal cancer emergencies of obstruction and perforation, and ASGE guidance covers endoscopic management of sigmoid volvulus. [PMID 30123315, 31791596]

**Uncertainties**
- Oncological outcome of stenting.

**MCQ angles**
- Stent as bridge to surgery.
- Octreotide in inoperable malignant obstruction.

**Case hooks**
- Elderly patient with left-sided obstructing colon cancer.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Causes in general, imaging, nasogastric decompression and surgical decision-making in adhesive obstruction are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28392363: Stent as bridge to surgery for left-sided malignant colonic obstruction reduces adverse events and stoma rate compared with emergency surgery: results of a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 28439845: A Systematic Review of the Clinical Presentation, Diagnosis, and Treatment of Small Bowel Obstruction (systematic review, 2017)
- PMID 30123315: 2017 WSES guidelines on colon and rectal cancer emergencies: obstruction and perforation (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 31139245: Optimal management of malignant left-sided large bowel obstruction: do international guidelines agree? (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 31791596: American Society for Gastrointestinal Endoscopy guideline on the role of endoscopy in the management of acute colonic pseudo-obstruction and colonic volvulus (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 32259849: Self-expandable metal stents for obstructing colonic and extracolonic cancer: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline - Update 2020 (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 34390398: Medical management of malignant bowel obstruction in patients with advanced cancer: 2021 MASCC guideline update (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35274188: MASCC multidisciplinary evidence-based recommendations for the management of malignant bowel obstruction in advanced cancer (guideline, 2022)

## Cluster `brain_mets` (topics 275, 276) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Radiosurgery guidance from ISRS and AAPM supports stereotactic approaches for brain tumours; ISRS recommends 12-16 Gy single fraction for cavernous sinus haemangioma and offers practice guidelines for recurrent glioma and paediatric cranial SRS. Retrieved evidence on brain metastases specifically is limited.

**Teaching points**
- The AAPM-RSS practice guideline provides minimum practice standards for SRS and SBRT services as they spread to community practice. [PMID 40071780]
- ISRS practice guidelines found radiosurgery with 24-25 Gy in 3-5 fractions safe and apparently effective for recurrent high-grade glioma, with limited prospective data. [PMID 41143546]
- ISRS guidelines recommend 12-16 Gy in one fraction for tumour control in cavernous sinus haemangioma. [PMID 40819808]
- ISRS consensus guidelines for paediatric cranial SRS note acceptable local control rates though data are limited. [PMID 39390948]
- Large vestibular schwannoma can be treated with planned subtotal microsurgery followed by radiosurgery, preserving facial and cochlear function. [PMID 40172792]
- A meta-analysis of lung cancer metastasis describes metastatic rates, risk factors and survival, including by number of brain metastases and radiotherapy. [PMID 40650339]
- Radiomics AI models in brain tumours face limited validation and inconsistent reporting. [PMID 42043503]

**Uncertainties**
- SRS versus whole-brain radiotherapy for brain metastases is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

- Radiosurgery dose by lesion type.
- Lung cancer brain metastasis survival factors.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with 3 brain metastases from lung cancer.

## Cluster `bronchial_provocation` (topics 280) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Methacholine and mannitol challenges assess airway hyperresponsiveness, and national guidelines standardise testing and safety. Exercise-induced bronchoconstriction has a joint task force update, and preterm-born subjects with chronic lung disease show more hyperresponsiveness.

**Teaching points**
- Methacholine provocation testing guidelines provide judgment criteria, management of deep inspirations and the role of the challenge in asthma, exercise-induced and occupational asthma. [PMID 30097294]
- A Chinese consensus standardises bronchial provocation testing, covering reasons for false positive and false negative results, safety precautions and emergency measures. [PMID 38858213]
- The mannitol test is a novel and reproducible test for airway hyperresponsiveness in intervention trials. [PMID 34743708]
- The 2016 joint task force update provides an evidence- and consensus-based document on exercise-induced bronchoconstriction. [PMID 27665489]
- Preterm-born subjects, especially with chronic lung disease, have increased bronchial hyperresponsiveness to methacholine and exercise. [PMID 30014518]
- A Chinese consensus proposes diagnosis and treatment flowcharts for chest tightness variant asthma. [PMID 37475566]
- Chronic cough guidelines prioritise symptom-based clinical evaluation and do not routinely recommend chest imaging. [PMID 39756065]

**Uncertainties**
- Test choice by clinical context.

**MCQ angles**
- Direct versus indirect challenges.
- Safety precautions.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with cough variant asthma and normal spirometry.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Contraindications, PC20 thresholds, drug preparation and interpretation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `bronchiectasis` (topics 281, 282) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 ERS guideline provides an evidence-based framework for adult bronchiectasis, with inhaled antibiotics giving a slight symptom and quality-of-life gain. NTM prevalence is rising, and immune deficiency such as APDS and AATD can underlie bronchiectasis.

**Teaching points**
- The ERS clinical practice guideline gives an evidence-based framework for managing adult bronchiectasis, including managing the deteriorating patient. [PMID 41016738]
- Inhaled antibiotics for adults with bronchiectasis likely slightly improve symptoms and quality of life. [PMID 38309462]
- The prevalence of non-tuberculous mycobacteria in non-CF bronchiectasis has been rising, and the most common subspecies changed greatly in recent years. [PMID 35914904]
- A core outcome set for bronchiectasis in children and adolescents was developed internationally for research. [PMID 38070531]
- A Japanese clinical practice guideline covers activated PI3K-delta syndrome, with a diagnostic flowchart and treatment options. [PMID 37178059]
- The Canadian Thoracic Society statement defines targeted testing and augmentation therapy for alpha-1 antitrypsin deficiency with emphysema. [PMID 39880301]
- The ATS document updates the interstitial lung abnormality definition and gives evaluation suggestions. [PMID 40387336]

**Uncertainties**
- Airway clearance evidence is not in the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Inhaled antibiotics benefit.
- NTM in bronchiectasis.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with daily purulent sputum and recurrent chest infections.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Airway clearance, long-term macrolides, aetiology work-up and Pseudomonas eradication are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `bronchiolitis` (topics 283, 284, 285) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 Australasian Bronchiolitis Guideline updates investigations, management and RSV prevention for the commonest infant admission. High-flow nasal cannula may shorten oxygen duration, and rhinovirus-induced bronchiolitis carries the strongest association with later wheeze and asthma.

**Teaching points**
- The 2025 Australasian Bronchiolitis Guideline provides updated evidence-based guidance on investigations, management and RSV prevention for the commonest condition in infancy requiring hospital admission. [PMID 40685806, 40673466]
- High-flow nasal cannula therapy for infants with bronchiolitis may reduce the duration of oxygen therapy (low-certainty evidence). [PMID 38506440]
- Rhinovirus-induced bronchiolitis was more strongly associated with developing wheeze and childhood asthma than RSV. [PMID 35338734]
- A bronchiolitis core outcome set was developed so future trials use relevant, comparable outcomes. [PMID 35264343]
- The AARC paediatric oxygen guideline recommends establishing an oxygen target on admission, with no specific target recommended for respiratory diseases other than bronchiolitis. [PMID 33790048]
- Finnish guidelines for paediatric lower respiratory tract infections aim for appropriate antibiotics, inhaled drugs, corticosteroids, radiology and laboratory testing. [PMID 39487609]
- Bronchiolitis obliterans syndrome detection after paediatric HSCT has evidence-based recommendations. [PMID 38889365]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of HFNC across settings.

**MCQ angles**
- Rhinovirus bronchiolitis and later asthma.
- Supportive care as mainstay.

**Case hooks**
- Six-month-old with coryza, tachypnoea and wheeze.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Risk stratification for admission, feeding support, saturation thresholds and what not to give (bronchodilators, steroids, antibiotics) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `blvr` (topics 286) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Endobronchial valves improve outcomes in heterogeneous emphysema without collateral ventilation, with pneumothorax the main risk. Lung volume reduction in severe emphysema gives clinically meaningful benefit, and coils are no longer produced.

**Teaching points**
- Endobronchial valves have favourable effects in heterogeneous emphysema, particularly without collateral ventilation, without increasing fatal outcomes. [PMID 35698281]
- Zephyr valves provided significant and clinically meaningful short-term improvements in homogeneous or heterogeneous emphysema without collateral ventilation, with more adverse events. [PMID 31117102]
- A network meta-analysis found EBV and EBC effective but increasing pneumothorax, with EBV slightly increasing mortality and long-term survival data needed. [PMID 39762564]
- Lung volume reduction in severe emphysema on maximal medical treatment has clinically meaningful benefits to be weighed against adverse events. [PMID 30744937]
- A 2026 consensus statement gives practical guidance on patient selection and periprocedural management for valve therapy. [PMID 40952939]
- An expert statement updates prevention and management of pneumothorax associated with one-way valve therapy. [PMID 34062550]
- Coil treatment for severe emphysema improves exercise capacity, but the owner has stopped production. [PMID 35405678]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term survival data.

**MCQ angles**
- Absent collateral ventilation for valves.
- Pneumothorax after valve placement.

**Case hooks**
- Severe heterogeneous emphysema with hyperinflation despite optimal therapy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Chartis assessment, target lobe selection and hospital observation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30744937: Surgical and endoscopic interventions that reduce lung volume for emphysema: a systemic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 31117102: Bronchoscopic Lung Volume Reduction with Endobronchial Zephyr Valves for Severe Emphysema: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 34062550: Expert Statement: Pneumothorax Associated with One-Way Valve Therapy for Emphysema: 2020 Update (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35405678: Bronchoscopic Lung Volume Reduction Coil Treatment for Severe Emphysema: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Participant Data (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35698281: Meta-analysis and Systematic Review of Bronchoscopic Lung Volume Reduction Through Endobronchial Valves in Severe Emphysema (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 39762564: Surgical and Bronchoscopic Lung Volume Reduction for Severe Emphysema: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40952939: Bronchoscopic Lung Volume Reduction with Endobronchial Valves: A Consensus Statement on Practical Aspects of Patient Selection and Periprocedural Management (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `brucellosis` (topics 287) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Triple antibiotic therapy is more effective than dual therapy for human brucellosis without added side effects. Diagnosis relies on serology and other tests needing standardisation, and neurobrucellosis has dedicated consensus guidance.

**Teaching points**
- Triple antibiotic therapy has better efficacy than dual therapy for human brucellosis without increasing side effects. [PMID 37708094]
- A diagnostic systematic review highlights a need for standardisation, improved reporting and advancing test development for human brucellosis. [PMID 38452046]
- Pooled brucellosis prevalence was estimated at 15.53%, with more studies needed in developing and low-income countries. [PMID 36619963]
- A Chinese expert consensus on neurobrucellosis covers assessment, diagnosis, differential diagnosis, therapeutic strategies and prognosis. [PMID 39865018]
- Livestock vaccination with S19 and RB51 prevents abortion and infection at suitable doses. [PMID 34328699]
- Domestic animal contact is associated with higher seroprevalence in wildlife, supporting buffalo as a reservoir species. [PMID 33727580]
- Economic assessments of livestock brucellosis control rarely explain socio-economic consequences. [PMID 36857972]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal regimen duration is not in the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Triple versus dual therapy.
- Diagnostic testing variation.

**Case hooks**
- Farm worker with undulant fever and sacroiliitis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Doxycycline-rifampicin versus doxycycline-aminoglycoside dosing, relapse and focal complications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33727580: Brucellosis in wildlife in Africa: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34328699: Efficacy of Brucella abortus S19 and RB51 vaccine strains: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)

## Cluster `burkitt` (topics 288) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ERN-EuroBloodNet clinical practice guidelines cover diagnosis and treatment of adult Burkitt lymphoma. EBV is found in over half of Burkitt cases, HBV is linked to non-Hodgkin lymphoma, and diagnosis in large B-cell lymphomas follows WHO 5th and ICC classifications.

**Teaching points**
- The ERN-EuroBloodNet guidelines for adult Burkitt lymphoma cover prognostic assessment to guide treatment and highlight unresolved questions on genetics, imaging and second-line therapies. [PMID 39909657]
- EBV was found in more than 50% of Burkitt lymphoma patients, supporting EBV testing for prediction and disease status assessment. [PMID 37370963]
- A diagnostic approach in large B-cell lymphomas contrasts WHO 5th and ICC classifications for Burkitt lymphoma and HGBCL with 11q aberration. [PMID 39684922]
- Hepatitis B infection is positively linked to non-Hodgkin lymphoma development, including Burkitt lymphoma. [PMID 29532605]
- A practice guideline recommends rituximab for specific lymphoma indications and in addition to fludarabine-based chemotherapy or chlorambucil for CLL. [PMID 27746042]
- NCCN haematology guidelines are largely developed from lower levels of evidence but with uniform expert opinion. [PMID 34270186]
- CD19 CAR-T therapy offers short-term effectiveness and manageable toxicity in solid organ transplant recipients with relapsed PTLD. [PMID 36575360]

**Uncertainties**
- Second-line therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- EBV association with Burkitt.
- Rituximab-chemotherapy for aggressive B-cell lymphoma.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with a rapidly growing abdominal mass and high LDH.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Intensive regimens, CNS prophylaxis, tumour lysis prevention and cytogenetics (MYC) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27746042: Rituximab in Lymphoma and Chronic Lymphocytic Leukaemia: A Practice Guideline (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 29532605: Hepatitis B virus and risk of non-Hodgkin lymphoma: An updated meta-analysis of 58 studies (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 34270186: Level of Scientific Evidence Underlying the National Comprehensive Cancer Network Clinical Practice Guidelines for Hematologic Malignancies: Are We Moving Forward? (guideline, 2021)

## Cluster `c3g` (topics 289) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Complement-targeted agents such as iptacopan and pegcetacoplan are now indicated in C3 glomerulopathy to reduce proteinuria or clear C3 deposits, and proteinuria is an acceptable marker of treatment effect. Classification relies on immunofluorescence and electron microscopy.

**Teaching points**
- Complement-targeted therapies may specifically address unmet needs in primary and secondary glomerular diseases. [PMID 40731834]
- A systematic review of C3G and primary IC-MPGN emphasises the need for new targeted treatments, and proteinuria is an acceptable marker for assessing treatment efficacy on long-term kidney outcomes. [PMID 40971335]
- A narrative review of complement-targeted therapies for C3G and atypical HUS notes outcomes should be priorities and percentages are not pooled estimates. [PMID 42621905]
- Iptacopan is indicated in adults with C3 glomerulopathy to reduce proteinuria, with unknown benefit in rapidly progressive disease or severe chronic kidney injury. [PMID 42485481]
- Pegcetacoplan response includes clear removal of C3 build-up on follow-up kidney biopsy, with reassessment at least yearly and budget impact considerations. [PMID 42479859]
- The Mayo/Renal Pathology Society report gives a pathogenesis-based classification of glomerulonephritis and standardised reporting. [PMID 26567243]
- A review updates understanding of C3G as a complement-mediated disease. [PMID 32369815]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term outcomes of complement inhibitors.

**MCQ angles**
- Complement inhibition in C3G.
- Proteinuria as treatment marker.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with nephrotic-range proteinuria and low C3 on biopsy showing C3-dominant deposits.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, immunosuppression (MMF, steroids), eculizumab and transplant recurrence are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 26567243: Mayo Clinic/Renal Pathology Society Consensus Report on Pathologic Classification, Diagnosis, and Reporting of GN (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 32369815: Update on C3 Glomerulopathy: A Complement-Mediated Disease (review, 2020)

## Cluster `cabg_pci` (topics 290, 462) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Meta-analyses suggest CABG gives sustained lower cumulative all-cause mortality than PCI in multivessel disease without left main involvement, particularly in diabetes and complex anatomy, with lower MI and repeat revascularisation but upfront stroke risk. Left main outcomes are comparable.

**Teaching points**
- An individual patient data meta-analysis showed sustained lower cumulative all-cause mortality with CABG than PCI for multivessel disease without left main involvement. [PMID 35402572]
- Mortality benefit of CABG over PCI was seen in diabetes and higher coronary complexity, with no benefit for CABG in left main disease. [PMID 29478841]
- CABG is associated with a significant benefit regarding future myocardial infarction versus PCI in multivessel or left main CAD. [PMID 31311660]
- In chronic total occlusion eligible for both strategies, CABG is associated with superior survival, fewer MI and fewer repeat revascularisations. [PMID 38759955]
- With left ventricular dysfunction, CABG has long-term benefits for mortality and revascularisation but higher upfront stroke rate. [PMID 32902738, 27069109]
- In ACS with unprotected left main disease, CABG reduced repeat revascularisation with a trend to more stroke. [PMID 41379597]
- Alternative approaches to CABG such as hybrid revascularisation merit exploration for combined benefits. [PMID 36688825]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence on LV dysfunction is predominantly observational.

**MCQ angles**
- CABG for diabetic multivessel disease.
- Stroke risk with CABG.

**Case hooks**
- Diabetic with three-vessel disease and SYNTAX score 33.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** SYNTAX score thresholds, FREEDOM and SYNTAX trial details, and heart team decision-making are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27069109: Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting Versus Percutaneous Coronary Intervention in Patients With Left Ventricular Systolic Dysfunction: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29478841: Mortality after coronary artery bypass grafting versus percutaneous coronary intervention with stenting for coronary artery disease: a pooled analysis of individual patient data (systematic review, 2018)
- PMID 31311660: Meta-Analysis Comparing the Risk of Myocardial Infarction Following Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting Versus Percutaneous Coronary Intervention in Patients With Multivessel or Left Main Coronary Artery Disease (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 32902738: Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting Versus Percutaneous Coronary Intervention in Patients with Left Ventricular Systolic Dysfunction (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35402572: Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting Versus Percutaneous Coronary Intervention for Multivessel Coronary Artery Disease: A One-Stage Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36688825: Alternative Approaches to Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting Versus Percutaneous Coronary Intervention, How Do They Compare?: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38759955: Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting versus Percutaneous Coronary Intervention in Patients with Chronic Total Occlusion (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `cadasil` (topics 291) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CADASIL is caused by NOTCH3 mutations (common arginine variants), with MRI changes and white matter alterations linked to cognition. In CADASIL migraine, beta-blockers may be relatively contraindicated, and cholinesterase inhibitors help vascular cognitive impairment generally.

**Teaching points**
- The most common NOTCH3 mutations causing CADASIL include p.R75P, p.R141C, p.R182C and p.R544C, with possible founder effects. [PMID 38966425]
- Cysteine-sparing NOTCH3 missense mutations can present with clinical CADASIL and typical MRI findings, with less anterior temporal lobe involvement. [PMID 28902129]
- In CADASIL migraine, antimigraine prophylaxis with beta-blockers may be contraindicated relative to other therapies, and controlled studies are needed. [PMID 33520412]
- White matter alterations relate to domain-specific cognitive impairment in cerebral small vessel disease. [PMID 36483114]
- The parahippocampal gyrus may be a reliably affected brain region in CADASIL. [PMID 36376927]
- Impaired neurovascular coupling is associated with cerebral small vessel disease including CAA and CADASIL. [PMID 37697725]
- Donepezil 5 mg improves cognition slightly in vascular cognitive impairment according to network meta-analysis. [PMID 33704781]

**Uncertainties**
- No disease-modifying therapy is covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- NOTCH3 mutation spectrum.
- Migraine treatment caution.

**Case hooks**
- Middle-aged patient with migraine with aura, strokes and anterior temporal white matter lesions.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Inheritance, skin biopsy and genetic testing, anterior temporal lesion MRI sign and stroke prevention are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28902129: Systematic Review of Cysteine-Sparing NOTCH3 Missense Mutations in Patients with Clinical Suspicion of CADASIL (systematic review, 2017)
- PMID 33520412: Treatment of migraine in patients with CADASIL: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 33704781: Cholinesterase inhibitors for vascular dementia and other vascular cognitive impairments: a network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 36376927: Structural and functional alterations in cerebral small vessel disease: an ALE-based meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 36483114: Association between white matter alterations and domain-specific cognitive impairment in cerebral small vessel disease: A meta-analysis of diffusion tensor imaging (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37697725: Associations between neurovascular coupling and cerebral small vessel disease: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38966425: Most common NOTCH3 mutations causing CADASIL or CADASIL-like cerebral small vessel disease: A systematic review (systematic review, 2024)
