## Cluster `cppd` (topics 293, 294) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CPPD diagnosis uses conventional radiography and ultrasound as complementary tests, with international consensus definitions of imaging features and a G-CAN nomenclature clarifying chondrocalcinosis. Core outcome domains for chronic and recurrent CPPD have been agreed.

**Teaching points**
- Conventional radiography and ultrasonography showed great diagnostic accuracy for CPPD and should be regarded as complementary in the work-up. [PMID 33577959]
- An international group defined imaging features characteristic of CPPD on radiography, CT and dual-energy CT. [PMID 35439343]
- The 2026 G-CAN consensus resolves ambiguities, particularly about chondrocalcinosis, giving consistent research definitions. [PMID 42264993]
- A core domain set for chronic and recurrent CPPD supports joint pain, tenderness, swelling, acute CPP crystal arthritis flares, overall function and global disease activity as core domains. [PMID 40086154]
- A systematic review found wide variation in labels for CPPD condition concepts, providing a rationale for agreed nomenclature. [PMID 39884730]
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria address imaging of chronic extremity joint pain with suspected inflammatory or crystalline arthritis. [PMID 37236743, 28473097]

**Uncertainties**
- Treatment of chronic CPP arthritis is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Chondrocalcinosis on radiography.
- Complementary role of ultrasound.

**Case hooks**
- Older patient with an acute knee effusion and chondrocalcinosis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Joint aspiration findings (rhomboid positively birefringent crystals), acute attack treatment and associated metabolic conditions are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `sglt2_ckd` (topics 295, 296, 383) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** SGLT2 inhibitors reduce kidney outcomes across the range of GFR and albuminuria, including stage 4 CKD and in non-diabetic CKD, and reduce cardiovascular events and mortality. Adding an MRA lowers albuminuria and blood pressure further, and GLP-1 agonist benefits are independent of SGLT2 inhibitor use.

**Teaching points**
- SGLT2 inhibitors improve kidney outcomes across the full spectrum of GFR and albuminuria, including stage 4 CKD or minimal albuminuria, among patients with type 2 diabetes, CKD or heart failure. [PMID 41203232]
- SGLT2 inhibitors significantly reduced the primary renal outcome in CKD patients, with differences by patient features and underlying disease. [PMID 34790672]
- SGLT2 inhibitors help delay progression of non-diabetic kidney disease. [PMID 37046125]
- Empagliflozin kidney benefits were evident regardless of diabetes status, heart failure status, cause of kidney disease or the size of the initial eGFR dip. [PMID 41082889]
- SGLT2 inhibitors in CKD reduce cardiovascular disease, heart-failure hospitalisation and all-cause death without increasing serious adverse events or UTIs but with more respiratory tract infections. [PMID 38047108]
- An MRA combined with an SGLT2 inhibitor gives greater reductions in albuminuria and blood pressure than an SGLT2 inhibitor alone, pending outcome trials. [PMID 39510041]
- The cardiovascular and kidney benefits of GLP-1 receptor agonists are consistent regardless of SGLT2 inhibitor use in type 2 diabetes. [PMID 39210781]
- Use of gliflozins in elderly patients with type 2 diabetes and CKD remains suboptimal compared with guidelines. [PMID 40573204]

**Uncertainties**
- Benefit in kidney transplant recipients needs randomised trials.

**MCQ angles**
- SGLT2 inhibitors in non-diabetic CKD.
- eGFR dip does not predict loss of benefit.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with diabetes and eGFR 28 with albuminuria considering SGLT2 inhibitor.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** CREDENCE results, starting eGFR thresholds, sick-day rules and genital infection risk are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `canakinumab` (topics 297) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Canakinumab is supported for adult-onset Still's disease and, with anakinra, in autoinflammatory conditions, while anti-inflammatory therapy in established atherosclerosis is addressed by an EAPCI consensus on patient selection. Retrieved evidence on CANTOS itself is limited.

**Teaching points**
- A clinical consensus statement discusses criteria for patient selection and decision-making regarding introduction of anti-inflammatory therapy in individuals with established atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease. [PMID 42536391]
- A Cochrane analysis of interleukin-receptor antagonists and TNF inhibitors for cardiovascular prevention reported possible increases in adverse events (RR 1.13) and no clear increase in serious infection. [PMID 39297531]
- Evidence supports tocilizumab, anakinra and canakinumab for adult-onset Still's disease, with uncertain comparative effectiveness. [PMID 38302170]
- Anti-IL-1 agents were effective with a low adverse event profile in paediatric and adult familial Mediterranean fever patients. [PMID 37769252]
- Injection site reactions vary across biologics, with some IL-17 and IL-23 agents showing the lowest prevalence. [PMID 37533141]
- A bibliometric analysis maps immunotherapy trends for atherosclerosis. [PMID 39628489]

**Uncertainties**
- Clinical role of canakinumab in atherosclerosis outside trials.

**MCQ angles**
- Anti-inflammatory therapy for residual risk.
- Canakinumab in Still's disease.

**Case hooks**
- Post-MI patient with persistently raised CRP.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** CANTOS trial design and outcomes, fatal infection signal and cost are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts. Page is thin.

**Sources**
- PMID 37533141: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Injection Site Reactions in Randomized-Controlled Trials of Biologic Injections (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37769252: Efficacy and safety of anti-interleukin-1 treatment in familial Mediterranean fever patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `cachexia` (topics 299) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ASCO cachexia guidance notes limited drug options: progesterone analogues and short-term corticosteroids may improve appetite and weight. Diagnostic criteria differ by region, food intake predicts mortality in older adults with cancer, and IL-6 pathway inhibition may influence weight.

**Teaching points**
- When trialling a drug to improve appetite or weight gain in cancer cachexia, options include progesterone analogues and short-term corticosteroids. [PMID 32432946]
- The ASCO cachexia guideline was given a rapid recommendation update in 2023. [PMID 37467399]
- Asian Working Group for Cachexia criteria are more effective in diagnosing cancer cachexia in the Asian population and stratify postoperative risk. [PMID 38744096]
- In older adults with cancer, measures of food intake predicted mortality, and a screening tool distinguishing malnutrition, cachexia and sarcopenia is needed. [PMID 32366995]
- IL-6 signalling pathway inhibition affects weight and BMI, a potential future adjunct for cachexia and anorexia nervosa. [PMID 32878032]
- Cytokines relate to cachexia in incurable cancer, though a causal relationship is unproven. [PMID 35080147]
- Guideline recommendations on supportive nutrition and exercise therapy for cancer patients need health policy support to be implemented. [PMID 37513591]

**Uncertainties**
- No approved drug reliably reverses cachexia.

**MCQ angles**
- Corticosteroids and progestins for appetite.
- Food intake as prognostic marker.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with pancreatic cancer losing weight despite nutritional support.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Definition (weight loss over 5%), anamorelin, nutrition counselling and exercise are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32366995: Relationship between markers of malnutrition and clinical outcomes in older adults with cancer: systematic review, narrative synthesis and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 32432946: Management of Cancer Cachexia: ASCO Guideline (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 32878032: Effects of IL-6 Signaling Pathway Inhibition on Weight and BMI: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 35080147: A systematic review examining the relationship between cytokines and cachexia in incurable cancer (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 37467399: Cancer Cachexia: ASCO Guideline Rapid Recommendation Update (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37513591: Are Guideline Recommendations on Supportive Nutrition and Exercise Therapy for Cancer Patients Implemented in Clinical Routine? A National Survey with Real-Life Data (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38744096: Characteristics and prognostic impact of cancer cachexia defined by the Asian Working Group for Cachexia consensus in patients with curable gastric cancer (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `hereditary_cancer` (topics 300) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ASCO guidelines support germline panel testing in patients with cancer and offer germline testing when tumour testing finds a pathogenic variant in listed genes. PARP inhibitors benefit mCRPC with BRCA alterations most, and FAP and Lynch syndrome have JSCCR guidance.

**Teaching points**
- ASCO advises that patients with a pathogenic variant on tumour testing in a listed gene should be offered germline testing. [PMID 38759122]
- Germline and somatic genomic testing guidance for metastatic prostate cancer says sequencing findings with prognostic only value should not guide treatment outside trials. [PMID 39787437]
- PARP inhibitor benefit in metastatic castration-resistant prostate cancer is greatest with BRCA alterations, with a strong signal for PALB2 or CDK12 and no benefit for ATM or CHEK2. [PMID 39848867]
- JSCCR guidelines summarise hereditary colorectal cancer with clinical recommendations for FAP and Lynch syndrome. [PMID 41214301]
- Early-onset colorectal cancer is associated with aggressive histological subtypes and TP53 and PTEN mutations. [PMID 38737895]
- European consensus recommends molecular predictive biomarker testing in advanced thyroid cancer, with DNA and RNA NGS for BRAF-negative tumours. [PMID 40540622]
- The ASCO pleural mesothelioma guideline update includes germline testing among its recommendations. [PMID 39778125]

**Uncertainties**
- Surveillance for carriers depends on gene.

**MCQ angles**
- Germline testing triggered by tumour findings.
- PARP inhibitors by gene.

**Case hooks**
- Young patient with colorectal cancer and MSH2 loss.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Genetic counselling, cascade testing, surveillance and risk-reducing surgery are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38737895: The histological and molecular characteristics of early-onset colorectal cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38759122: Selection of Germline Genetic Testing Panels in Patients With Cancer: ASCO Guideline (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39778125: Treatment of Pleural Mesothelioma: ASCO Guideline Update (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 39787437: Germline and Somatic Genomic Testing for Metastatic Prostate Cancer: ASCO Guideline (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 39848867: Heterogeneity of the Treatment Effect with PARP Inhibitors in Metastatic Castration-resistant Prostate Cancer: A Living Interactive Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40540622: Molecular predictive biomarker testing in advanced thyroid cancer - a European consensus (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41214301: Japanese society for cancer of the colon and rectum (JSCCR) guidelines 2024 for the clinical practice of hereditary colorectal cancer (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `candidaemia` (topics 301, 302, 303) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The ECMM global guideline updates diagnosis and management of candidiasis, and multimodal bundles including central line management and ID consultation improve outcomes. FUNDICU consensus definitions standardise invasive fungal disease in ICU and rapid diagnosis relies on antigen, serology and PCR.

**Teaching points**
- The ECMM global guideline provides updated recommendations for managing Candida infections, covering emerging pathogens and new treatment options. [PMID 39956121]
- Consensus guidelines for invasive candidiasis in haematology, oncology and ICU emphasise central venous catheter management, infectious diseases consultation and care bundles. [PMID 34937142]
- FUNDICU consensus definitions of invasive fungal diseases in ICU patients aim to speed up the design of comparative studies. [PMID 38512399]
- Rapid, accurate diagnosis of fungal infections relies on appropriate use of antigen testing, serology and PCR-based assays. [PMID 31469325, 31219341]
- ECIL-6 guidelines advise against routine first-line combination antifungal therapy for invasive aspergillosis, with few changes for mucormycosis. [PMID 28011902]
- Spanish guidelines address preventive and empirical therapy for invasive candidiasis based largely on low-quality evidence. [PMID 39556361]
- The British Society for Medical Mycology gives best-practice diagnostic recommendations for serious fungal disease. [PMID 41232547]

**Uncertainties**
- Empirical therapy criteria in non-neutropenic ICU patients.

**MCQ angles**
- Central line removal in candidaemia (bundle).
- Biomarker diagnosis.

**Case hooks**
- ICU patient on parenteral nutrition with persistent fever and a central line.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** First-line agent (echinocandin), echocardiography, ophthalmology review and treatment duration are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28011902: ECIL-6 guidelines for the treatment of invasive candidiasis, aspergillosis and mucormycosis in leukemia and hematopoietic stem cell transplant patients (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 31219341: Diagnosis of Fungal Infections. A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis Supporting American Thoracic Society Practice Guideline (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 31469325: Microbiological Laboratory Testing in the Diagnosis of Fungal Infections in Pulmonary and Critical Care Practice. An Official American Thoracic Society Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2019)

## Cluster `capacity` (topics 304) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Decision-making capacity fluctuates and varies with admission stage, legal status and information provided; capacity is impaired in mild cognitive impairment compared with controls, though less than Alzheimer's disease. Advance care planning covers all stages of capacity.

**Teaching points**
- Decision-making capacity in psychiatric inpatients varies with admission stage, formal (involuntary or voluntary) status and information provided, and relates to severity of psychopathology. [PMID 38433596]
- Patients with mild cognitive impairment have reduced capacity to consent to treatment and research compared with healthy controls, though better than in Alzheimer's disease, so capacity should be carefully evaluated. [PMID 33583459]
- A 33-country Delphi defines advance care planning in dementia, covering all stages of capacity and involving family caregivers, with fluctuating capacity visualised. [PMID 37985444]
- Effective and acceptable psychological interventions to support treatment decision-making capacity are lacking. [PMID 28882828]
- Advance care planning interventions in older adults are well received and generally improve outcomes, though economic impact trials are needed. [PMID 27451328]
- Structural and legal barriers to ECT for people lacking decision-making capacity should be critically reviewed. [PMID 40042615]

**Uncertainties**
- Assessment tools are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Capacity fluctuates and is decision-specific.
- Advance care planning in dementia.

**Case hooks**
- Older patient with delirium refusing treatment.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** The Mental Capacity Act four-stage test, best-interests decisions and documentation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27451328: Advance care planning: A systematic review of randomised controlled trials conducted with older adults (systematic review, 2016)
- PMID 28882828: Systematic review and meta-analysis of factors that help or hinder treatment decision-making capacity in psychosis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 33583459: Decisional capacity to consent to treatment and research in patients affected by Mild Cognitive Impairment. A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37985444: Consensus definition of advance care planning in dementia: A 33-country Delphi study (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38433596: Decision making capacity for treatment in psychiatric inpatients: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40042615: [Access to electroconvulsive therapy for people lacking decision making capacity and as nonvoluntary treatment : Expert consensus and statement of the German Association for Psychiatry, Psychotherapy and Psychosomatics (DGPPN)] (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `capsule_endoscopy` (topics 305) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Capsule endoscopy and device-assisted enteroscopy are first-line small-bowel tools: early evaluation of obscure GI bleeding affects diagnosis, treatment and prognosis. AI-assisted reading is accurate, fecal calprotectin of 100 micrograms/g screens for small-bowel Crohn's, and colon capsule is as tolerable as colonoscopy.

**Teaching points**
- Small-bowel studies in the early evaluation of obscure GI bleeding impact diagnosis, therapeutic intervention and prognosis, though optimal timing needs comparative studies. [PMID 34952093]
- ESGE guidance covers small-bowel capsule endoscopy and device-assisted enteroscopy, including DAE-ERCP as first-line for pancreaticobiliary disease in surgically altered anatomy except Billroth II. [PMID 36423618]
- AI-assisted capsule endoscopy has high sensitivity and diagnostic accuracy with lower pooled specificity than conventional reading. [PMID 40083189]
- A fecal calprotectin cutoff of 100 micrograms/g can screen for small bowel Crohn's disease. [PMID 33361549]
- Convolutional neural networks perform highly for computer-aided diagnosis of GI ulcers and haemorrhage on capsule images. [PMID 32721487]
- Patient tolerability of colon capsule endoscopy was higher or equal to colonoscopy, with rare adverse events. [PMID 34574071]
- ESGE/ESGAR guidance on imaging alternatives to colonoscopy covers CT colonography and colon capsule and referral for polypectomy. [PMID 33104846, 33105507]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal timing of capsule endoscopy in obscure bleeding.

**MCQ angles**
- Capsule for obscure GI bleeding.
- Retention contraindication (not in excerpts).

**Case hooks**
- Iron deficiency with negative gastroscopy and colonoscopy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Capsule retention risk, patency capsule use and bowel preparation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32721487: High pooled performance of convolutional neural networks in computer-aided diagnosis of GI ulcers and/or hemorrhage on wireless capsule endoscopy images: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 33104846: Imaging alternatives to colonoscopy: CT colonography and colon capsule. European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) and European Society of Gastrointestinal and Abdominal Radiology (ESGAR) Guideline - Update 2020 (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 33105507: Imaging alternatives to colonoscopy: CT colonography and colon capsule. European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) and European Society of Gastrointestinal and Abdominal Radiology (ESGAR) Guideline - Update 2020 (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 33361549: Diagnostic Accuracy of Fecal Calprotectin for the Detection of Small Bowel Crohn's Disease through Capsule Endoscopy: An Updated Meta-Analysis and Systematic Review (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34574071: Patient-Reported Outcomes and Preferences for Colon Capsule Endoscopy and Colonoscopy: A Systematic Review with Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34952093: Diagnostic and therapeutic yields of early capsule endoscopy and device-assisted enteroscopy in the setting of overt GI bleeding: a systematic review with meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36423618: Small-bowel capsule endoscopy and device-assisted enteroscopy for diagnosis and treatment of small-bowel disorders: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Guideline - Update 2022 (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 40083189: Artificial Intelligence-Assisted Capsule Endoscopy Versus Conventional Capsule Endoscopy for Detection of Small Bowel Lesions: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `cre` (topics 306) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Ceftazidime-avibactam is associated with lower 30-day mortality than other regimens and colistin-based regimens for CRE bloodstream infection, and superior efficacy versus polymyxins. Resistance to ceftazidime-avibactam is rising, and MDR/CRE colonisation raises mortality in transplant recipients.

**Teaching points**
- Ceftazidime-avibactam was associated with significantly lower 30-day mortality than other regimens, including colistin-based regimens, for carbapenem-resistant Enterobacterales bloodstream infections. [PMID 35377233]
- Ceftazidime-avibactam showed superior clinical efficacy to polymyxins for CRE infections (relative risk about 0.44 for mortality). [PMID 37878197]
- Ceftazidime-avibactam compared with other antimicrobials favoured it for carbapenem-resistant Klebsiella pneumoniae infections. [PMID 37004964]
- Rising ceftazidime-avibactam resistance among Gram-negative bacteria warrants continuous monitoring. [PMID 39934901]
- A Korean guideline covers antibacterial treatment of CRE infections, including current and upcoming agents. [PMID 39231504]
- Multidrug-resistant colonisation, particularly CRE, in solid organ transplant recipients is associated with increased mortality. [PMID 38608872]
- A practice guideline gives pathogen- and site-specific recommendations for multidrug-resistant Gram-negative infections. [PMID 41130038]

**Uncertainties**
- Combination therapy for CRE is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Ceftazidime-avibactam over colistin.
- Resistance surveillance.

**Case hooks**
- Transplant recipient with CRE bloodstream infection.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Carbapenemase class-specific choices (KPC, OXA-48, MBL), aztreonam-avibactam and meropenem-vaborbactam are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35377233: Efficacy and Safety of Ceftazidime-Avibactam for the Treatment of Carbapenem-Resistant <i>Enterobacterales</i> Bloodstream Infection: a Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37004964: Efficacy and safety of ceftazidime-avibactam compared to other antimicrobials for the treatment of infections caused by carbapenem-resistant Klebsiella pneumoniae strains, a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37878197: Ceftazidime-avibactam versus polymyxins in treating patients with carbapenem-resistant Enterobacteriaceae infections: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38608872: The impact of colonization by multidrug resistant bacteria on graft survival, risk of infection, and mortality in recipients of solid organ transplant: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39231504: Guidelines for Antibacterial Treatment of Carbapenem-Resistant Enterobacterales Infections (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39934901: Global trends of ceftazidime-avibactam resistance in gram-negative bacteria: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41130038: Guideline for antimicrobial treatment of multidrug-resistant Gram-negative infections: practice recommendations of the Brazilian Society of Infectious Diseases (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `cardiac_amyloid` (topics 307) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Diagnosing ATTR cardiac amyloidosis by bone scintigraphy requires excluding light chain disease with a monoclonal protein screen. Management relies on expert opinion in the absence of trials: SGLT2 inhibitors may be associated with better prognosis, heart failure drugs behave differently, and transplant has Mayo consensus.

**Teaching points**
- The ACC/AHA quality measures update stresses a monoclonal protein screen to exclude light chain amyloidosis when interpreting bone scintigraphy for transthyretin cardiac amyloidosis. [PMID 39116212, 39127953]
- SGLT2 inhibitors may be associated with more favourable prognosis in ATTR cardiomyopathy, with observational evidence needing validation in RCTs. [PMID 39868862]
- A consensus on non-amyloid-specific treatment of ATTR cardiac amyloidosis relies on expert opinion and retrospective studies given the lack of randomised trials. [PMID 41055898]
- Guideline-directed heart failure drugs based on LVEF should be reconsidered in the context of ATTR cardiomyopathy. [PMID 39963776]
- CT-derived myocardial extracellular volume has high diagnostic accuracy and is useful for detecting amyloidosis in preoperative TAVR CT. [PMID 37999657]
- A Mayo Clinic consensus gives recommendations for pretransplant evaluation and post-transplant management in cardiac amyloidosis. [PMID 40767789]
- An Italian consensus gives diagnostic-therapeutic pathways for suspected or established cardiac amyloidosis. [PMID 39588628]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for heart failure drugs in ATTR-CM.

**MCQ angles**
- Exclude AL before bone scan diagnosis.
- Treatment relies on expert opinion.

**Case hooks**
- Older man with HFpEF, carpal tunnel syndrome and low-voltage ECG.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Tafamidis and acoramidis evidence, light chain work-up and red flags are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37999657: Clinical Utility of Computed Tomography-Derived Myocardial Extracellular Volume Fraction: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39116212: 2024 Update to the 2020 ACC/AHA Clinical Performance and Quality Measures for Adults With Heart Failure: A Report of the American Heart Association/American College of Cardiology Joint Committee on Performance Measures (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39127953: 2024 Update to the 2020 ACC/AHA Clinical Performance and Quality Measures for Adults With Heart Failure: A Report of the American Heart Association/American College of Cardiology Joint Committee on Performance Measures (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39588628: [Diagnostic-therapeutic care pathways for patients with cardiac amyloidosis - SIC/ANMCO Consensus document. Edited by the Italian Cardiac Amyloidosis Network (RIAC)] (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39868862: Sodium-glucose cotransporter 2 inhibitors and outcomes in transthyretin amyloid cardiomyopathy: Systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

- PMID 40767789: Heart Transplantation for Cardiac Amyloidosis: Mayo Clinic Consensus Statement (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `arrest_special` (topics 309) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ECPR reduces in-hospital mortality and improves neurological outcomes and survival compared with conventional CPR, with a newly significant benefit in out-of-hospital arrest. Consensus statements standardise ECPR cannulation and selection, and ILCOR identifies knowledge gaps.

**Teaching points**
- ECPR reduced in-hospital mortality and improved long-term neurological outcomes and post-arrest survival compared with conventional CPR, particularly in in-hospital arrest. [PMID 37230097]
- An updated meta-analysis found ECPR reduces in-hospital mortality, improves neurological outcome and 30-day survival, with newly significant benefit in out-of-hospital arrest. [PMID 38383506]
- An international consensus identifies evidence gaps and inequities in OHCA care that must be addressed before ECPR trials. [PMID 40040619]
- The ELSO interim guideline consensus on adult ECPR provides guidance where high-quality evidence is scarce. [PMID 33627592]
- A German consensus gives a standardised treatment algorithm for ECPR based on expert opinion. [PMID 30361819]
- A modified Delphi consensus on percutaneous cannulation for ECPR gives a template for new programmes. [PMID 35817270]
- ILCOR 2023 consensus statements include priority knowledge gaps and justifications for cardiac arrest recommendations. [PMID 37942682, 37937881]

**Uncertainties**
- Patient selection criteria for ECPR.

**MCQ angles**
- ECPR benefit in refractory arrest.
- Cannulation standards.

**Case hooks**
- Witnessed refractory VF arrest in a hospital with an ECMO team.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Cardiac arrest in pregnancy, drowning, hypothermia and anaphylaxis modifications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30361819: Recommendations for extracorporeal cardiopulmonary resuscitation (eCPR): consensus statement of DGIIN, DGK, DGTHG, DGfK, DGNI, DGAI, DIVI and GRC (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 33627592: Extracorporeal Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation in Adults. Interim Guideline Consensus Statement From the Extracorporeal Life Support Organization (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35817270: ECPR<sup>2</sup>: Expert Consensus on PeRcutaneous Cannulation for Extracorporeal CardioPulmonary Resuscitation (guideline, 2022)

- PMID 37937881: 2023 International Consensus on Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiovascular Care Science With Treatment Recommendations: Summary From the Basic Life Support; Advanced Life Support; Pediatric Life Support; Neonatal Life Support; Education, Implementation, and Teams; and First Aid Task Forces (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 37942682: 2023 International Consensus on Cardiopulmonary Resuscitation and Emergency Cardiovascular Care Science With Treatment Recommendations: Summary From the Basic Life Support; Advanced Life Support; Pediatric Life Support; Neonatal Life Support; Education, Implementation, and Teams; and First Aid Task Forces (guideline, 2023)

## Cluster `arrhythmias` (topics 310) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence centres on ventricular and supraventricular tachyarrhythmias: AHA 2025 advanced life support guidance stresses identifying causative versus secondary arrhythmias, catheter ablation beats antiarrhythmic drugs for VT in ischaemic heart disease, and troponin in SVT has low prognostic value.

**Teaching points**
- The 2025 AHA advanced life support guidelines underscore identifying causative versus secondary arrhythmias to inform timing of therapeutic strategies. [PMID 41122884]
- In ischaemic heart disease with VT, catheter ablation compared with antiarrhythmic drugs reduced appropriate ICD therapy, cardiovascular rehospitalisation and adverse events, with benefit most evident versus sotalol. [PMID 41316690]
- Stereotactic arrhythmia radioablation for refractory VT reduces VT episodes and ICD shocks, but recurrence is high and severe adverse events occur. [PMID 40633941]
- Non-sustained VT was modestly associated with appropriate ICD therapy (HR 1.82) but not with VF or mortality, suggesting reconsideration of its clinical role. [PMID 41967966]
- More than one-third of ICD participants had rapid-rate NSVT despite HFrEF therapy compliance, and sustained VT was less frequent with guideline-directed therapy compliance. [PMID 40772237]
- Troponin is often elevated in ED patients with SVT but has low prognostic value for MACE. [PMID 39242347]
- A gap exists between guideline indications for wearable cardioverter-defibrillators and real-world prescribing. [PMID 41655635]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of NSVT in risk stratification.

**MCQ angles**
- Ablation versus drugs for VT.
- Troponin in SVT.

**Case hooks**
- Ischaemic cardiomyopathy patient with recurrent ICD shocks.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Atrial fibrillation, flutter, bradyarrhythmias and acute management algorithms are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39242347: The Role of Troponin Testing in Patients with Supraventricular Tachycardia, Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40633941: Stereotactic arrhythmia radioablation in patients with refractory ventricular tachycardia: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `cied` (topics 311, 312) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2026 HRS-led consensus statement covers cardiovascular implantable electronic device infection and lead management, and PET-CT is useful for diagnosing infection. Non-laser lead extraction is safer than laser, with timing of reimplantation affecting reinfection.

**Teaching points**
- The 2026 multisociety expert consensus statement update covers CIED lead management, tricuspid valve replacement considerations and standardised transvenous lead extraction approach and facilities. [PMID 42034327]
- 18F-FDG PET-CT and leukocyte scintigraphy have high sensitivity and specificity for CIED infection, with PET-CT preferred when available. [PMID 28377468]
- Non-laser transvenous lead extraction has a better safety and efficacy profile than laser methods, with greater risk of SVC injury with laser sheaths. [PMID 37882609]
- Excimer laser sheath-assisted lead extraction has high success and low procedural mortality rates for leads needing advanced extraction. [PMID 37757839]
- Laser sheaths were associated with lower complete lead removal rate and a 9.3-fold higher risk of death compared with rotating sheaths in one meta-analysis. [PMID 34839431]
- Time to reimplantation may affect reinfection rates after CIED infection, though findings are exploratory. [PMID 31481556]
- European physicians show knowledge gaps and lack of confidence across stages of CIED care, requiring education and streamlined referral. [PMID 33175984]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal timing of reimplantation.

**MCQ angles**
- PET-CT in device infection.
- Lead extraction method safety.

**Case hooks**
- Pacemaker pocket infection with positive blood cultures.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Indications for complete device removal, antibiotic duration and device malfunction troubleshooting are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28377468: Positron Emission Tomography and Single-Photon Emission Computed Tomography Imaging in the Diagnosis of Cardiac Implantable Electronic Device Infection: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31481556: Timing of device reimplantation and reinfection rates following cardiac implantable electronic device infection: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 33175984: Knowledge gaps, lack of confidence, and system barriers to guideline implementation among European physicians managing patients with CIED lead or infection complications: a European Heart Rhythm Association/European Society of Cardiology educational needs assessment survey (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 34839431: Efficacy and mortality of rotating sheaths versus laser sheaths for transvenous lead extraction: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37757839: Safety and success of transvenous lead extraction using excimer laser sheaths: a meta-analysis of over 1700 patients (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37882609: Comparison of non-laser and laser transvenous lead extraction: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 42034327: 2026 HRS/AHA/APHRS/EHRA/IDSA/LAHRS/PACES/STS expert consensus statement update on cardiovascular implantable electronic device lead management and extraction (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `myxoma` (topics 313) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cardiac myxoma has low surgical mortality (4.7 per 1000 person-years) and low recurrence, and timely excision is warranted. Embolism risk factors include an irregular tumour surface and narrow base, and cerebral aneurysm is a rare complication.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of cardiac myxoma found late mortality of 4.7 per 1000 person-years and recurrence of 0.5 per 1000 person-years, with timely excision warranted once diagnosed. [PMID 38219702]
- Risk factors for embolism in cardiac myxoma include irregular tumour surface, atypical location, narrow base and raised fibrinogen, so early surgery may benefit such patients. [PMID 32711463]
- Cerebral aneurysms from atrial myxoma are rare but have serious long-term consequences, so patients should be monitored. [PMID 36675669]
- Acute arterial occlusion from tumour embolism is most likely lung cancer or atrial myxoma, and early revascularisation in selected cases can prevent severe complications. [PMID 36389271]
- Surgical approach choice depends on surgeon expertise, with routine postoperative echocardiography essential for residual or recurrent tumour. [PMID 39797207]
- Pulmonary valve myxoma needs early diagnosis and complete resection before heart failure symptoms. [PMID 36941612]
- Cardiac myxoma in Carney complex shows a role for key genes dysregulated in cardiac cells. [PMID 38110859]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term efficacy of minimally invasive and robotic approaches.

**MCQ angles**
- Embolism risk factors.
- Postoperative echo surveillance.

**Case hooks**
- Young woman with a left atrial mass, syncope and embolic stroke.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Clinical triad (obstruction, embolism, constitutional symptoms), tumour plop and differential diagnosis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32711463: Risk factors of embolism for the cardiac myxoma patients: a systematic review and metanalysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 36389271: Tumor embolism and acute arterial occlusion: A systematic review (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 36675669: Cerebral Aneurysms Caused by Atrial Myxoma-A Systematic Review of the Literature (systematic review, 2022)
- PMID 36941612: Clinical characteristics, management, and outcomes of pulmonary valve myxoma: systematic review of published case reports (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 38110859: The benign nature and rare occurrence of cardiac myxoma as a possible consequence of the limited cardiac proliferative/ regenerative potential: a systematic review (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 38219702: Clinical characteristics and surgical outcomes of cardiac myxoma: A meta-analysis of worldwide experience (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39797207: The Therapeutic Potential of Different Surgical Approaches in the Management of Cardiac Myxoma: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2024)

## Cluster `cardiac_rehab` (topics 314) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cardiac rehabilitation referral and patient education are core quality measures after coronary events. Guideline gaps include delivery in low-resource and culturally diverse settings and nutrition, psychosocial and education recommendations; home-based mHealth rehabilitation shows promise but needs comparative evidence.

**Teaching points**
- A review of cardiac rehabilitation guidelines identified gaps in delivery in low-resource and culturally diverse populations and few recommendations on nutrition, psychosocial counselling and patient education. [PMID 38376955]
- The 2025 AHA/ACC performance measures for chronic coronary disease emphasise cardiac rehabilitation referral and patient education, including symptom management and lifestyle modification. [PMID 40305583, 40310322]
- Home-based cardiac rehabilitation delivered by mHealth needs robust evidence of clinical and cost-effectiveness compared with centre-based rehabilitation. [PMID 40023729]
- Portuguese post-MI pathways include smoking cessation, nutrition, psychology and physical therapy, with a cardiac rehabilitation programme key. [PMID 40484125]
- The Australian ACS guideline supports tailored secondary prevention with stricter LDL-C targets and PCSK9 inhibitors. [PMID 41693087]
- Consensus recommendations on SCAD care show regional variation, particularly in fibromuscular dysplasia screening. [PMID 38056913]

**Uncertainties**
- Comparative effectiveness of home-based rehabilitation.

**MCQ angles**
- Referral to cardiac rehabilitation after MI.
- Access inequities.

**Case hooks**
- Patient discharged after STEMI with no rehabilitation referral.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Programme components, exercise prescription, mortality benefit figures and eligibility are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38056913: Regional and temporal variations of spontaneous coronary artery dissection care according to consensus recommendations: a systematic review and meta-analysis (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38376955: A Systematic Review of Clinical Practice Guidelines and Consensus Statements for Cardiac Rehabilitation Delivery: Consensus, Divergence, and Important Knowledge Gaps (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40023729: Effectiveness of home-based cardiac rehabilitation interventions delivered via mHealth technologies: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40305583: 2025 AHA/ACC Clinical Performance and Quality Measures for Patients With Chronic Coronary Disease: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Performance Measures (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40310322: 2025 AHA/ACC Clinical Performance and Quality Measures for Patients With Chronic Coronary Disease: A Report of the American College of Cardiology/American Heart Association Joint Committee on Performance Measures (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40484125: Post-myocardial infarction patient pathways in Portugal (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41693087: National Heart Foundation of Australia and Cardiac Society of Australia and New Zealand: Australian Clinical Guideline for Diagnosing and Managing Acute Coronary Syndromes 2025 (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `crt` (topics 315, 316, 317) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cardiac physiologic pacing and CRT are guided by the 2023 HRS/APHRS/LAHRS guideline. CRT enables guideline-directed therapy and reduces loop diuretic need in HFrEF, though CRT-D may not add benefit in non-ischaemic or older patients, and avoiding isolated trans-tricuspid RV leads limits valvular regurgitation.

**Teaching points**
- The 2023 HRS/APHRS/LAHRS guideline on cardiac physiologic pacing covers evaluation, implant, follow-up and optimisation of response, including paediatric use, and identifies knowledge gaps. [PMID 37283271]
- In HFrEF, CRT implantation was associated with enabled use and dose of heart failure therapy and decreased loop diuretic need. [PMID 40470816]
- The treatment effect of CRT-D on mortality improved from early to late studies, with CRT-D not superior in groups such as non-ischaemic and older patients. [PMID 37766466]
- Pacing strategies avoiding isolated trans-tricuspid RV leads may prevent incident or worsening valvular regurgitation and reduce mortality. [PMID 38812433]
- A systematic review of HFrEF guidelines found common comorbidities such as iron deficiency, AF, obesity, sleep-disordered breathing and frailty were addressed, and calls for harmonisation. [PMID 41778370]
- The Canadian CCS/CHRS guidelines support selection of patients for CIED implantation. [PMID 41539743]

**Uncertainties**
- Selection of CRT-D versus CRT-P.

**MCQ angles**
- QRS morphology and duration for CRT selection (not in excerpts).
- Tricuspid regurgitation after leads.

**Case hooks**
- HFrEF patient with LBBB and QRS 160 ms on optimal therapy.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Class I indications (LVEF 35% or less, LBBB, QRS 150 ms), COMPANION and CARE-HF results and responder definition are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37283271: 2023 HRS/APHRS/LAHRS guideline on cardiac physiologic pacing for the avoidance and mitigation of heart failure (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37766466: Time-trend treatment effect of cardiac resynchronization therapy with or without defibrillator on mortality: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38812433: Evolution and prognosis of tricuspid and mitral regurgitation following cardiac implantable electronic devices: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40470816: Cardiac resynchronization therapy for enabling guideline-directed medical therapy optimization in heart failure (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41539743: The Canadian Cardiovascular Society/Canadian Heart Rhythm Society Comprehensive Guidelines for the Selection, Implantation, and Management of Patients With Cardiac Implantable Electronic Devices (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41778370: Heart failure with reduced ejection fraction: a systematic review of clinical practice guidelines and recommendations (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `tamponade` (topics 318) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence is limited. Most malignant pericardial effusions present with tamponade, mortality is high and treatment options are limited. Simpson's volumetric apical 4-chamber estimation is more accurate than linear grading for pericardial fluid volume.

**Teaching points**
- Malignant pericardial effusions are often severe, with most patients presenting with cardiac tamponade, limited treatment options and high mortality. [PMID 39211754]
- A simple apical 4-chamber Simpson's volumetric approach estimates pericardial fluid more accurately than guideline-based linear grading and better supports categorical classification and serial follow-up. [PMID 42242567]
- Brazilian oncology surgery recommendations address management of neoplastic pericardial effusion. [PMID 39737532]
- The first combined ESC guidelines on myocarditis and pericarditis cover pericardial disease management. [PMID 41511532]
- Left atrial appendage closure carries higher pericardial effusion risk with Amulet than Watchman. [PMID 41274294]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal drainage strategy in malignant effusion.

**MCQ angles**
- Volumetric effusion estimation.
- Tamponade in malignancy.

**Case hooks**
- Cancer patient with dyspnoea, hypotension and large pericardial effusion.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Beck's triad, echocardiographic signs of tamponade, pericardiocentesis technique and surgical drainage are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts; the ESC pericardial disease guideline text is needed.

**Sources**
- PMID 39211754: Malignant Pericardial Effusion: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2024)
- PMID 39737532: Recommendations of the Brazilian Society of Surgical Oncology for the Treatment of Neoplastic Pericardial Effusion (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41274294: Comparing Effectiveness and Safety of Left Atrial Appendage Closure Devices: A Network Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41511532: [ESC guidelines on myocarditis and pericarditis : First combined European guidelines for myocarditis and pericarditis] (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42242567: Quantitation of Pericardial Fluid Volume: Volumetric Estimation Compared with Guideline-Based Linear Grading (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `cardiogenic_shock` (topics 319, 320, 321, 322) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Mechanical circulatory support in cardiogenic shock is complex: in a network meta-analysis ECMO-IABP may reduce mortality while other devices did not, and women receive less mechanical support than men. Prognostic factors aid risk assessment and complication management is crucial after MI.

**Teaching points**
- In a network meta-analysis of randomised trials, ECMO with IABP may reduce mortality in cardiogenic shock while other mechanical circulatory support devices did not, with limited certainty. [PMID 38206381]
- Women with cardiogenic shock are less likely to be treated with mechanical circulatory support than men, so prompt diagnosis and aggressive treatment are needed. [PMID 38845019]
- A SCAI/EAPCI/ACVC consensus statement guides diagnosis and treatment of cardiogenic shock in women, noting limited sex-based data. [PMID 40387531]
- A meta-analysis identified prognostic factors associated with mortality in cardiogenic shock to aid risk assessment and risk tools. [PMID 39437131]
- AATS consensus notes mechanical complications and malignant arrhythmias after MI have high mortality and need prompt management with consideration of mechanical circulatory support. [PMID 40320004]
- An EACTS consensus covers pre-emptive use of temporary mechanical circulatory support in protected cardiac surgery. [PMID 41063414]
- An ISHLT consensus covers primary graft dysfunction mitigation and treatment. [PMID 41823895]

**Uncertainties**
- Device selection and timing.

**MCQ angles**
- MCS in cardiogenic shock.
- Sex differences in treatment.

**Case hooks**
- Anterior STEMI with hypotension and cool peripheries.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** SCAI stages, vasopressor and inotrope choice, revascularisation strategy (CULPRIT-SHOCK) and IABP-SHOCK II results are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38206381: Mechanical circulatory support for cardiogenic shock: a network meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials and propensity score-matched studies (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38845019: Sex differences in treatments and outcomes of patients with cardiogenic shock: a systematic review and epidemiological meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39437131: Prognostic Factors Associated with Mortality in Cardiogenic Shock - A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40320004: 2025 American Association for Thoracic Surgery (AATS) Expert Consensus Document: Surgical management of acute myocardial infarction and associated complications (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40387531: SCAI/EAPCI/ACVC Expert Consensus Statement on Cardiogenic Shock in Women (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41063414: EACTS Expert Consensus Document on protected cardiac surgery: pre-emptive use of temporary mechanical circulatory support in adult cardiac surgery patients at high risk for perioperative low cardiac output syndrome (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41823895: Summary of the International Society for Heart and Lung Transplantation (ISHLT) Consensus Conference on Graft Dysfunction within the First 72 hours after Heart Transplantation: A 10-year Update (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `cardio_oncology` (topics 323) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cardio-oncology guidance covers baseline risk assessment, transthoracic echocardiographic definitions of cancer therapy-related dysfunction, cardiac CT, radiation-related disease and quality indicators; paediatric patients have tailored approaches.

**Teaching points**
- BSE and BCOS guidelines describe echocardiographic acquisition methodology and definitions of cancer therapeutics-related cardiac dysfunction, including during trastuzumab. [PMID 34396303, 34106116]
- ESC quality indicators aim to quantify and improve adherence to guideline-recommended practice in preventing and managing cancer therapy-related cardiovascular toxicity. [PMID 36316010]
- Cardiac CT has defined applications in cardio-oncology, with an expert consensus giving practice recommendations. [PMID 36216699]
- A multidisciplinary consensus statement addresses cardiovascular manifestations from therapeutic radiation, including management considerations. [PMID 34604797]
- An Australian and New Zealand Delphi gives risk, baseline assessment, screening and follow-up recommendations for paediatric oncology patients. [PMID 38939459]
- Expert panel discussion highlights evidence gaps and drug-drug interactions in cardiovascular care during cancer therapy. [PMID 39801647]
- Digital solutions may optimise guideline-directed therapy prescription in heart failure. [PMID 39563907]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence gaps in cardioprotection.

**MCQ angles**
- Echo definitions of cardiotoxicity.
- Radiation heart disease.

**Case hooks**
- Breast cancer patient receiving trastuzumab with a falling ejection fraction.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Immune checkpoint inhibitor myocarditis, QT prolongation and surveillance schedules are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `cpet` (topics 324) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cardiopulmonary exercise testing measures cardiorespiratory fitness, which predicts all-cause and cardiovascular mortality. It identifies exertional intolerance mechanisms in long COVID beyond deconditioning and predicts postoperative complications.

**Teaching points**
- Better cardiorespiratory fitness measured by CPET was associated with lower all-cause mortality and cardiovascular disease risk. [PMID 34198003]
- In long COVID, CPET shows exertional intolerance with mechanisms other than deconditioning, including altered autonomic function, dysfunctional breathing, endothelial dysfunction and muscle or mitochondrial pathology. [PMID 36223120]
- CPET variables are predictive of postoperative complications and hospital length of stay in colorectal surgery, whereas field walk tests lack enough data. [PMID 29377872]
- Maximal and submaximal CPET measures are prognostic of major adverse cardiac events in congenital heart disease. [PMID 34405863]
- Preoperative assessment tool choice for ERAS in liver transplantation depends on setting and resources. [PMID 35293025]
- The overlap of HFpEF diagnosis between ESC, HFA-PEFF and H2FPEF algorithms was only 3.5%. [PMID 40595806]

**Uncertainties**
- Standardised thresholds across populations.

**MCQ angles**
- Peak VO2 as prognostic marker.
- CPET in unexplained dyspnoea.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with unexplained dyspnoea and normal echocardiogram and spirometry.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Test protocol, VO2 thresholds, ventilatory thresholds and interpretation of limiting mechanism are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29377872: Systematic Review and Meta-analysis of Objective Assessment of Physical Fitness in Patients Undergoing Colorectal Cancer Surgery (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 34198003: Cardiorespiratory fitness measured with cardiopulmonary exercise testing and mortality in patients with cardiovascular disease: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34405863: The role of cardiopulmonary exercise testing in predicting mortality and morbidity in people with congenital heart disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35293025: Which preoperative assessment modalities best identify patients who are suitable for enhanced recovery after liver transplantation? A systematic review of the literature and expert panel recommendations (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36223120: Use of Cardiopulmonary Exercise Testing to Evaluate Long COVID-19 Symptoms in Adults: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 40595806: Comparison of the 2021 ESC guideline with the HFA-PEFF and H&lt;sub&gt;2&lt;/sub&gt;FPEF scores in diagnosing heart failure with preserved ejection fraction (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `post_arrest` (topics 325, 457) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Post-arrest care covers temperature control, haemodynamic and ventilatory goals, prognostication, and organ donation. AHA 2025 and ILCOR 2022 consensus guidance sets recommendations and knowledge gaps, with neurocritical care guidance on targeted temperature management.

**Teaching points**
- The AHA 2025 post-cardiac arrest care guideline expands recommendations on optimising survivorship for patients, caregivers and rescuers and reviews the role of organ donation. [PMID 41122894]
- ILCOR 2022 consensus statements give treatment recommendations with justifications and priority knowledge gaps for resuscitation science. [PMID 36325905, 36336195, 36325925]
- A neurocritical care guideline on targeted temperature management gives strong recommendations in some areas and many conditional recommendations to contextualise to patient and system. [PMID 29038971]
- Targeted temperature management in intracerebral haemorrhage, subarachnoid haemorrhage and ischaemic stroke needs further research to improve guidelines. [PMID 37225535]
- A scientific statement covers epidemiology, pathophysiology, management and prognostication after paediatric cardiac arrest. [PMID 31242751]
- A consensus guideline covers determination of brain death or death by neurologic criteria in children and adults. [PMID 37821233]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal temperature target is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Prognostication timing.
- Organ donation after arrest.

**Case hooks**
- Comatose patient after out-of-hospital VF arrest.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Immediate coronary angiography after arrest, temperature targets, blood pressure goals and prognostic tests are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `cardiorenal` (topics 326, 327) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** About a quarter of patients with cardiorenal syndrome type 1 develop AKI and roughly 3% need RRT, with the greatest mortality impact in cardiogenic shock. Optimising cardiorenal guideline-directed therapy and early CKD detection slow progression, and simultaneous heart-kidney transplantation has acceptable survival.

**Teaching points**
- Among patients with cardiorenal syndrome type 1, almost one quarter developed AKI and about 3% needed RRT, with highest occurrence in acute heart failure and greatest mortality impact in cardiogenic shock. [PMID 26989397]
- A 2023 Chinese guideline standardises diagnosis, prevention, treatment and management of cardiorenal syndrome. [PMID 38092552]
- Optimising cardiorenal guideline-directed medical therapy and early CKD detection can slow CKD progression. [PMID 40869709]
- Simultaneous heart and kidney transplantation has acceptable rejection and survival rates, with 71% survival at 5 years in the pooled analysis. [PMID 38881801]
- A consensus conference addresses heart-kidney transplantation selection and the ethical problems of multi-organ transplantation. [PMID 33527725]
- Dapagliflozin in acute heart failure needs more data to establish safety and efficacy. [PMID 39731023]
- A Canadian focused update stresses a personalised approach by heart failure phenotype with careful drug titration and consideration of cardiac rehabilitation. [PMID 37169222]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of ultrafiltration is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Cardiorenal syndrome type 1.
- Dual organ transplant.

**Case hooks**
- Acute heart failure with rising creatinine on diuretics.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Classification types 1-5, diuretic resistance and ultrafiltration trial results are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 26989397: Acute Kidney Injury in Cardiorenal Syndrome Type 1 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 33527725: Consensus conference on heart-kidney transplantation (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 37169222: Canadian Cardiovascular Society-Canadian Heart Failure Society Focused Clinical Practice Update of Patients With Differing Heart Failure Phenotypes (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38092552: [Clinical practice guideline for the diagnosis and treatment of cardiorenal syndrome (2023 Edition)] (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38881801: Simultaneous Heart and Kidney Transplantation: A Systematic Review and Proportional Meta-Analysis of Its Characteristics and Long-Term Variables (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39731023: Dapagliflozin in acute heart failure management: a systematic review and meta-analysis of safety and effectiveness (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40869709: Integrating and Simplifying Evidence to Optimize Cardiorenal Guideline-Directed Therapies (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `cmr` (topics 328) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** SCMR consensus supports CMR in patients with cardiac implants, and quantitative perfusion CMR is being standardised. Late iodine enhancement on cardiac CT is a reliable alternative to LGE when CMR is unavailable, and guidelines for dilated and hypertrophic cardiomyopathy use LGE for risk stratification.

**Teaching points**
- The SCMR consensus informs risk-benefit decisions for CMR in patients with cardiac implantable electronic devices. [PMID 38219955]
- An expert consensus guides quantitative myocardial perfusion CMR, setting research goals for data reliability, validity and multi-vendor use. [PMID 40784605]
- Late iodine enhancement on cardiac CT provides a reliable alternative to LGE on CMR, especially when CMR is unavailable, infeasible or contraindicated. [PMID 39702631]
- Hypertrophic cardiomyopathy guidelines differ in diagnostic criteria and the utility of late gadolinium enhancement for risk stratification. [PMID 39749832]
- A review of dilated cardiomyopathy guidelines highlights gaps such as screening relatives of genotype-positive probands and aetiology-oriented therapies. [PMID 39674807]
- A consensus on cardiac imaging in athletes supports harmonised, judicious multimodality imaging to avoid unnecessary sport restriction. [PMID 42332979]
- International experts identified consensus areas for cardiac imaging in suspected and established cardiac sarcoidosis. [PMID 40467149]

**Uncertainties**
- Standardisation of CMR protocols.

**MCQ angles**
- LGE for prognosis.
- CMR safety with devices.

**Case hooks**
- Young athlete with chest pain and raised troponin.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Viability thresholds, Lake Louise criteria, T1/T2 mapping and contraindications (gadolinium in advanced CKD) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `cv_diabetes` (topics 329) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** GLP-1 receptor agonists reduce MACE and mortality in diabetes with peripheral artery disease without clear limb benefit, may lower the combined mortality-hospitalisation endpoint in HFpEF, and tirzepatide gives cardiovascular benefit comparable to GLP-1RAs. Early SGLT2 inhibitor use after MI reduces MACE, and combining the classes needs randomised trials.

**Teaching points**
- GLP-1 receptor agonists lower MACE and all-cause mortality in type 2 diabetes with peripheral artery disease but do not clearly benefit limb-specific outcomes. [PMID 41022246]
- GLP-1 receptor agonists may significantly lower the combined risk of mortality and hospitalisation in HFpEF, without a significant effect on all-cause mortality alone (HR 0.81). [PMID 40637782]
- Tirzepatide provides cardiovascular benefit at least comparable to established GLP-1RAs in type 2 diabetes. [PMID 41761267]
- Early initiation of SGLT2 inhibitors in acute MI significantly reduced MACE, with uncertainty on heart failure hospitalisation and no effect on all-cause mortality or non-fatal MI. [PMID 40684113]
- Combining SGLT2 inhibitors and GLP-1 receptor agonists is supported by observational data, with randomised trials of combined versus monotherapy needed. [PMID 41117973]
- GLP-1 receptor agonists exert anti-inflammatory effects in type 2 diabetes. [PMID 40230207]
- GLP-1 receptor agonists are a potential option for obstructive sleep apnoea in obesity and type 2 diabetes if adverse events are tolerable. [PMID 40181368]

**Uncertainties**
- Effect on muscle mass and functional skeletal muscle needs analysis.

**MCQ angles**
- GLP-1 in diabetes with PAD.
- SGLT2 inhibitor in acute MI.

**Case hooks**
- Diabetic patient with prior MI choosing between SGLT2i and GLP-1RA.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Pivotal trial names (EMPA-REG, LEADER, SUSTAIN-6) and heart failure hospitalisation numbers are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 40181368: Glucagon-like Peptide-1 receptor agonists for obstructive sleep apnea in patients with obesity and type 2 diabetes mellitus: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40230207: The effect of GLP-1 receptor agonists on circulating inflammatory markers in type 2 diabetes patients: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40637782: Impact of GLP-1 receptor agonists on cardiovascular outcomes in heart failure with preserved ejection fraction (HFpEF): systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 40684113: Early initiation of SGLT2 inhibitors in acute myocardial infarction and cardiovascular outcomes, an updated systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41022246: GLP-1 Receptor Agonists and Cardiovascular Outcomes in Adults With Diabetes and Peripheral Artery Disease: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41117973: Effectiveness and safety of combining SGLT2 inhibitors and GLP-1 receptor agonists in individuals with type 2 diabetes: a systematic review and meta-analysis of cohort studies (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41761267: Comparative efficacy of tirzepatide and glucagon-like peptide-1 receptor agonists on cardiovascular outcomes in patients with type 2 diabetes: a systematic review and network meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `cv_risk_mod` (topics 330) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Adherence to lipid-lowering therapy is associated with reduced cardiovascular events in ASCVD, and each 1 mmol/L LDL-C reduction lowers MACE by 30% in primary prevention. Combined clinician-support and patient-access interventions best improve secondary prevention prescribing.

**Teaching points**
- Adherence to lipid-lowering therapy was associated with reduced risk of cardiovascular events in patients with clinical ASCVD. [PMID 40879042]
- Interventions targeting both clinician support and patient access were most effective at improving medication prescription and lowering lipids in peripheral artery disease. [PMID 41260242]
- The International Lipid Expert Panel consensus gives lipid management guidance for primary and secondary stroke prevention. [PMID 41249078]
- Positive outcome lipid trials driven by reduced non-fatal MI more often received strong guideline recommendations, prompting scrutiny of whether recommendations match clinical significance. [PMID 40254096]
- Adding PCSK9 inhibitors to lipid-lowering interventions improves arterial stiffness beyond LDL-C reduction. [PMID 39031778]
- Oral PCSK9 inhibitors show lipid-lowering efficacy and safety comparable to injectable PCSK9 therapies, though evidence is limited to early-phase studies. [PMID 41288928]
- Tenofovir alafenamide raises lipid levels in chronic hepatitis B, so lipid profiles should be monitored in high-risk patients. [PMID 40195938]

**Uncertainties**
- Cardiovascular risk of TAF-related lipid changes.

**MCQ angles**
- LDL-C reduction and risk.
- Adherence to statins.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with ASCVD stopping statin because of muscle symptoms.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Risk calculators, blood pressure and smoking interventions, and statin eligibility thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39031778: Effect of adding PCSK9 inhibitors to lipid-lowering interventions on arterial stiffness: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40195938: Impact of Tenofovir Alafenamide on Lipid Profiles in Chronic Hepatitis B Patients: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40254096: Positive outcome trials driven by reduction in nonfatal myocardial infarction: A systematic review and relevant guideline recommendations (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40879042: Adherence to Lipid-Lowering Therapies and Cardiovascular Outcomes in Patients With Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41249078: Lipid management for primary and secondary stroke prevention consensus paper of the International Lipid Expert Panel (ILEP) (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41260242: Editor's Choice - A Systematic Review of Interventions to Improve Guideline Directed Secondary Cardiovascular Risk Reduction in Patients with Peripheral Arterial Disease (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41288928: Lipid-Lowering Efficacy and Safety of Oral Proprotein Convertase Subtilisin/Kexin Type 9 Inhibitors: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)

## Cluster `cad_dissection` (topics 331) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Individual patient data meta-analysis found no significant difference between anticoagulants and antiplatelets in preventing early recurrent events after cervical artery dissection. Recurrence is low, thrombolysis should not be denied, and stenting is second-line.

**Teaching points**
- An IPD meta-analysis of two randomised trials found no significant difference between anticoagulants and antiplatelets in preventing early recurrent events in cervical artery dissection. [PMID 38739383]
- A systematic review calls for large randomised trials of anticoagulation versus antiplatelets in spontaneous cervical artery dissection, weighing stroke reduction and bleeding. [PMID 38847098]
- Recurrence of cervical artery dissection is low, and recurrence with ischaemia is even lower. [PMID 37661311]
- Patients with dissection-related ischaemic stroke should not be denied thrombolysis, pending large randomised studies. [PMID 27199235]
- A history of migraine is associated with an increased risk of cervical artery dissection. [PMID 37555306]
- Carotid stenting for cervical dissection has acceptable mortality and complication rates as a second-line treatment, with low-quality evidence. [PMID 35961627]
- Fibromuscular dysplasia has neurologic manifestations including dissection. [PMID 30285053]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal antithrombotic strategy.

**MCQ angles**
- Anticoagulant versus antiplatelet equivalence.
- Thrombolysis allowed.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with neck pain and Horner syndrome.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Imaging diagnosis, treatment duration and trauma versus spontaneous mechanisms are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27199235: Safety and Efficacy of Thrombolysis in Cervical Artery Dissection-Related Ischemic Stroke: A Meta-Analysis of Observational Studies (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 30285053: Fibromuscular Dysplasia and Its Neurologic Manifestations: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2019)
- PMID 35961627: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Carotid Artery Stenting for the Treatment of Cervical Carotid Artery Dissection (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37555306: Migraine and the risk of cervical artery dissection: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `carpal_tunnel` (topics 332, 333) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** AAOS/ASSH guidance covers carpal tunnel management, and D5W injection parallels corticosteroid injection with fewer adverse effects in mild-to-moderate disease. Neuromuscular ultrasound and electrodiagnostic studies are both used, with the choice shared with patients.

**Teaching points**
- The AAOS/ASSH guideline summary gives practical recommendations for carpal tunnel syndrome management and highlights gaps in the literature. [PMID 39637428]
- D5W injection parallels corticosteroids in pain, symptom severity, electrodiagnostic measures and nerve area, with lower adverse effects, recommended as a preferred option for mild to moderate carpal tunnel syndrome. [PMID 39032850]
- Choice between neuromuscular ultrasound and electrodiagnostic studies should involve shared decision-making weighing accuracy, availability, cost and tolerability. [PMID 39324685]
- An expert consensus gives the role of neuromuscular ultrasound alongside electrodiagnostic tests in polyneuropathies and carpal tunnel, with future work to define when ultrasound can stand alone. [PMID 40411981]
- The global prevalence of carpal tunnel syndrome is high, highlighting the need for effective management strategies. [PMID 39672798]

**Uncertainties**
- Surgical versus non-surgical selection is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Ultrasound versus nerve conduction.
- Injection options.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with nocturnal hand paraesthesia and positive Phalen test.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Splinting, steroid injection benefit duration, surgical release indications and associated conditions (pregnancy, diabetes, hypothyroidism) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39032850: Comparative Efficacy of 5% Dextrose and Corticosteroid Injections in Carpal Tunnel Syndrome: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `car_t` (topics 334, 335, 336, 337) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CAR T-cell therapy is part of relapsed/refractory large B-cell lymphoma care in the BSH guideline, with attention to bridging, infection and toxicity. Non-relapse mortality is about 11.5%, driven by infections, and BCMA CAR-T carries rare non-ICANS neurotoxicity.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of non-relapse mortality after CAR T-cell therapy found cumulative non-relapse deaths of 11.5%, emphasising infectious complications. [PMID 38977912]
- The BSH guideline for relapsed or refractory large B-cell lymphoma recommends antibody therapy in eligible patients and addresses bendamustine use and bridging to CAR T-cell therapy. [PMID 40384597]
- Understanding toxicity profiles across CAR T products in non-Hodgkin lymphoma may allow tailored therapy and early anticipation of toxicities. [PMID 38582666]
- A modified Delphi gave 33 consensus recommendation statements on CAR T-cell administration in adult B-ALL. [PMID 42154904]
- Consensus on bridging therapy before anti-BCMA CAR-T in myeloma aims to standardise care. [PMID 42129310]
- Non-ICANS neurologic toxicity after BCMA CAR-T is rare but important, particularly with cilta-cel. [PMID 41886632]
- HHV-6B infection after HCT and CAR-T therapy has ASTCT consensus guidance on risk factors, diagnosis, prophylaxis and treatment. [PMID 40409689]
- A guideline addresses anti-infective vaccination strategies in patients with haematological malignancies, including those on CAR T cells. [PMID 41926972]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal bridging therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Infectious deaths after CAR-T.
- Neurotoxicity types.

**Case hooks**
- Relapsed DLBCL after R-CHOP being considered for CAR-T.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Cytokine release syndrome and ICANS grading and tocilizumab or steroid treatment, and ZUMA-7 and TRANSFORM results are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38582666: Safety and Toxicity Profiles of CAR T Cell Therapy in Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38977912: A systematic review and meta-analysis of nonrelapse mortality after CAR T cell therapy (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40384597: Management of relapsed or refractory large B-cell lymphoma: A British Society for Haematology Guideline (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `crpc` (topics 338) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The ASCO living guideline for mCRPC systemic therapy recommends radium-223 for symptomatic bone-only disease and notes optimal sequencing is not established. PARP inhibitors benefit mostly BRCA-altered disease, and lutetium-PSMA-617 is covered in a systemic therapy update.

**Teaching points**
- The ASCO mCRPC guideline update recommends radium-223 for symptomatic bone-only disease and notes evidence for optimal sequencing of regimens is lacking. [PMID 40315400, 41557978]
- PARP inhibitor benefit in mCRPC is greatest with BRCA alterations, strong with PALB2 or CDK12, and absent with ATM or CHEK2. [PMID 39848867]
- The efficacy of PARP inhibitors is not uniform across mCRPC patients with DNA damage repair gene alterations, making patient selection challenging, with no unexpected safety signals. [PMID 37716332]
- ASCO germline and somatic testing guidance says sequencing findings with only prognostic value should not guide treatment outside a trial. [PMID 39787437]
- Improving patient profiling with genomic, molecular and clinical indicators is fundamental to optimise sequential therapy in mCRPC. [PMID 39306478]
- Primary prophylactic G-CSF may be beneficial during cabazitaxel for mCRPC (weak recommendation to perform). [PMID 38538963]
- ASCO provides a systemic therapy update on 177Lu-PSMA-617 for mCRPC. [PMID 37931186]

**Uncertainties**
- Sequencing of systemic therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Radium-223 for symptomatic bone-only disease.
- PARP inhibitor and BRCA.

**Case hooks**
- Progression on abiraterone with painful bone metastases.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Docetaxel, androgen receptor pathway inhibitors, cabazitaxel and PSMA PET selection criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37716332: Efficacy and safety of PARP inhibitors in metastatic castration-resistant prostate cancer: A systematic review and meta-analysis of clinical trials (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37931186: Systemic Therapy Update on <sup>177</sup>Lutetium-PSMA-617 for Metastatic Castration-Resistant Prostate Cancer: ASCO Guideline Rapid Recommendation Update (guideline, 2024)
