## Cluster `catatonia` (topics 339) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Catatonia guidelines (BAP and others) rest on low-quality evidence from small observational studies, case series and case reports, with multidisciplinary psychiatry-physician work. Intravenous haloperidol should be ECG-monitored at high doses, and ECT is an option in treatment-resistant illness including catatonia.

**Teaching points**
- British Association for Psychopharmacology catatonia recommendations are informed by small observational studies, case series and reports, highlighting need for randomised trials. [PMID 37039129]
- Catatonia guidelines stress multidisciplinary working between psychiatrists and physicians and acknowledge low-quality underlying evidence. [PMID 37236789]
- ECG monitoring is recommended only when using intravenous haloperidol above 5 mg, with telemetry for high-risk patients receiving cumulative doses of at least 100 mg or with corrected QTc above 500 ms. [PMID 32979582]
- ECT is used in children and adolescents for severe treatment-resistant mood disorders, catatonia and schizophrenia, especially older adolescents, with high-quality studies warranted. [PMID 34424874]
- Catatonia prevalence in clinical samples is not decreasing over time, with high heterogeneity. [PMID 29140521]
- Catatonia occurs in autism spectrum disorders, with longitudinal studies required. [PMID 34906264]
- A Delphi consensus offers a paediatric catatonia clinical pathway for acute medical care settings. [PMID 41865798]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for benzodiazepine dosing and ECT is low quality.

**MCQ angles**
- Low-quality evidence base.
- Haloperidol cardiac monitoring.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with mutism, posturing and refusal to eat.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Lorazepam challenge, Bush-Francis scale, dosing and ECT indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29140521: Prevalence of Catatonia and Its Moderators in Clinical Samples: Results from a Meta-analysis and Meta-regression Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 32979582: Intravenous haloperidol: A systematic review of side effects and recommendations for clinical use (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 34424874: Electroconvulsive Therapy in Children and Adolescents: A Systematic Review of Current Literature and Guidelines (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34906264: Catatonia in autism spectrum disorders: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 37039129: Evidence-based consensus guidelines for the management of catatonia: Recommendations from the British Association for Psychopharmacology (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37236789: The diagnosis and treatment of catatonia (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 41865798: Recommendations for a Pediatric Catatonia Clinical Pathway in Acute Medical Care Settings: A Delphi Consensus Study (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `cauda_equina` (topics 341) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cauda equina syndrome guidelines agree that suspected cases need urgent MRI, though red flags are inconsistent and sensitive early features matter more than late severe signs. ACR criteria and a core outcome set support imaging and research.

**Teaching points**
- Systematic review of international guidelines found consensus that MRI should be carried out urgently in suspected cauda equina syndrome. [PMID 40000448]
- A wide variety of red flags in low back pain guidelines lack consensus and evidence on accuracy. [PMID 27376890]
- Guidelines should emphasise early, incomplete cauda equina syndrome rather than late severe untreatable features, and emergency MRI demand will rise, being part of triage. [PMID 28637110]
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria address imaging for low back pain. [PMID 34794594, 27496288]
- A core outcome set for cauda equina syndrome was developed with patients and professionals for research. [PMID 31923259]
- WFNS guidance supports conservative treatment first for lumbar disc herniation, with NSAIDs improving acute pain, and fusion not routinely recommended for first-time herniation. [PMID 38389961, 38496347]

**Uncertainties**
- Timing of surgery relative to outcome is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Urgent MRI for suspected CES.
- Red flag inconsistency.

**Case hooks**
- Back pain with urinary retention and saddle numbness.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Red flag list (saddle anaesthesia, retention, bowel dysfunction), surgical timing and post-void bladder scan are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27376890: Red flags presented in current low back pain guidelines: a review (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 27496288: ACR Appropriateness Criteria Low Back Pain (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 28637110: Guidelines for cauda equina syndrome. Red flags and white flags. Systematic review and implications for triage (guideline, 2017)

## Cluster `coeliac` (topics 342, 416) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 ESsCD guidelines update adult coeliac diagnosis and management: selected adults with IgA-tTG at least 10 times the upper limit of normal and moderate-to-high pretest probability can be diagnosed without biopsy. Screening guidance for children with thyroid disease varies and HLA testing needs standardised reporting.

**Teaching points**
- Selected adult patients with IgA-tTG of at least 10 times ULN and moderate to high pretest probability of coeliac disease could be diagnosed without endoscopy and duodenal biopsy. [PMID 38176661]
- The 2025 ESsCD guidelines update diagnosis and management, integrating evolving diagnostic strategies, minimising over-testing and promoting patient-centred care. [PMID 40999951, 41831197]
- UK NEQAS and BSHI guidance standardises laboratory HLA genotyping and reporting for coeliac disease. [PMID 38153308]
- An Academy of Nutrition and Dietetics guideline supports medical nutrition therapy for people with coeliac disease. [PMID 37499866]
- International guidelines lack consensus on screening for coeliac disease in children with thyroid disease and in asymptomatic at-risk children, including HLA testing and follow-up. [PMID 40230804]
- A European consensus on malabsorption covers definitions, diagnostic testing, high-risk groups and nutritional assessment. [PMID 40129317]

**Uncertainties**
- Screening of asymptomatic at-risk individuals.

**MCQ angles**
- No-biopsy diagnosis in adults.
- Role of HLA typing.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with iron deficiency and tTG ten times ULN.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Serology on a gluten-containing diet, endomysial antibodies, follow-up and complications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `cellulitis` (topics 343, 344, 345) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Clinical reassessment is best done 2-4 days after starting antibiotics in uncomplicated cellulitis, and CT cannot reliably distinguish abscess from cellulitis by morphology. Differential diagnoses of leg redness are many, and stewardship projects show guideline non-adherence and reduced broad-spectrum use after implementing guidelines.

**Teaching points**
- The best available data suggest optimal time to clinical reassessment of uncomplicated cellulitis is between 2 and 4 days after starting antibiotics. [PMID 35593975]
- The German S1 guideline on redness of the lower legs lists seven key diagnoses and over 40 further differential diagnoses. [PMID 35758562]
- No morphological CT criteria are highly accurate for separating neck abscess from cellulitis. [PMID 36288374]
- After implementing a paediatric preseptal cellulitis guideline, broad-spectrum use fell from 100% to 66%. [PMID 39246158]
- Antibiotic guideline adherence in a tertiary centre shows need for tailored interventions in prescribing. [PMID 38660495]
- Outpatient antibiotic prescribing rose in the COVID-19 era with discrepancies in guideline concordance. [PMID 40765632]
- Consensus recommendations address risk reduction practices in people at risk of or with breast cancer-related lymphoedema. [PMID 39438352]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal antibiotic duration is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Reassess at 48-72 hours.
- Mimics of cellulitis.

**Case hooks**
- Bilateral red swollen legs thought to be cellulitis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** First-line antibiotics (flucloxacillin), MRSA, erysipelas and necrotising infection red flags are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `clabsi` (topics 346, 347, 348) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CLABSI prevention uses site selection, chlorhexidine-impregnated dressings and securement, with PICCs inserted by best practice having low DVT risk. Short time to positivity of blood cultures predicts mortality and septic shock, and a home-infusion surveillance definition exists.

**Teaching points**
- Chlorhexidine-impregnated dressing is beneficial in preventing central venous catheter-related complications, including catheter-related bloodstream infection. [PMID 31096918]
- Dressings and securement devices reduce central venous catheter complications, though heterogeneity requires caution. [PMID 37879273]
- PICCs inserted with current best practices have low deep vein thrombosis rates, so use should reflect therapeutic need rather than fear of complications. [PMID 32356479]
- Evidence on femoral site catheter-related bloodstream infection risk compared with other sites is inconclusive. [PMID 27632678]
- Short time to positivity of blood culture predicts mortality and septic shock in bacteraemic patients. [PMID 35144551]
- A Chinese 2025 practice guideline covers prevention, diagnosis and treatment of CLABSI in ICU adults. [PMID 40201989]
- A home infusion CLABSI surveillance definition was developed by consensus. [PMID 31982215]
- In paediatric cancer, evidence is insufficient to guide device selection between PICCs and totally implanted devices. [PMID 39283363]

**Uncertainties**
- Femoral versus other site risk.

**MCQ angles**
- Chlorhexidine dressings.
- Time to positivity as prognostic marker.

**Case hooks**
- ICU patient with fever and positive blood cultures from a central line.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Insertion bundle, line removal criteria, antibiotic lock therapy and treatment duration are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27632678: Cumulative Evidence of Randomized Controlled and Observational Studies on Catheter-Related Infection Risk of Central Venous Catheter Insertion Site in ICU Patients: A Pairwise and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31096918: Chlorhexidine-impregnated dressing for the prophylaxis of central venous catheter-related complications: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 31982215: Reaching consensus on a home infusion central line-associated bloodstream infection surveillance definition via a modified Delphi approach (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 32356479: Peripherally inserted central catheters inserted with current best practices have low deep vein thrombosis and central line-associated bloodstream infection risk compared with centrally inserted central catheters: A contemporary meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35144551: Short time to positivity of blood culture predicts mortality and septic shock in bacteremic patients: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37879273: The effectiveness of dressings and securement devices to prevent central venous catheter-associated complications: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39283363: Central venous access device adverse events in pediatric patients with cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `pacns` (topics 349) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2023 ESO guideline is the first comprehensive European GRADE guideline on PACNS. Angiography and biopsy-defined disease differ, diagnostic tests often disagree, and adult PACNS has frequent relapses and high mortality.

**Teaching points**
- The ESO guideline is the first comprehensive European guideline on primary angiitis of the CNS using GRADE methodology, to assist clinicians in choosing management strategies. [PMID 37903069]
- Where both biopsy and angiography were performed in primary CNS vasculitis, disagreement between them was more than five times more likely than agreement, so diagnostic criteria may need revision. [PMID 30107009]
- There are distinct clinical features between biopsy-defined and angiography-defined primary CNS vasculitis, which may aid selection of invasive tests. [PMID 33197577]
- Adult PACNS is associated with frequent relapses and high mortality, with symptomatic epilepsy, prolonged biopsy time window and CD20 expression linked to recurrence. [PMID 37953861]
- A systematic review of childhood PACNS recommends induction including trimethoprim/sulfamethoxazole and maintenance with mycophenolate, though no grade-A evidence exists. [PMID 31355303]
- Immune checkpoint inhibitor-related CNS vasculitis is rare and needs early immunosuppression. [PMID 39984148]
- Tumefactive PACNS can mimic glioblastoma. [PMID 41112399]

**Uncertainties**
- Revision of diagnostic criteria.

**MCQ angles**
- Biopsy versus angiography discordance.
- Relapse risk.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with headaches, multifocal strokes and lymphocytic CSF.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Induction regimens in adults (steroids with cyclophosphamide), imaging features and differentials (RCVS) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30107009: Diagnostic test results in primary CNS vasculitis: A systematic review of published cases (systematic review, 2017)
- PMID 31355303: Strategies for treatment of childhood primary angiitis of the central nervous system (review, 2019)
- PMID 33197577: Primary CNS vasculitis: A systematic review on clinical characteristics associated with abnormal biopsy and angiography (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 37903069: European Stroke Organisation (ESO) guidelines on Primary Angiitis of the Central Nervous System (PACNS) (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37953861: Primary Angiitis of the Central Nervous System in Adults: A Comprehensive Review of 76 Biopsy-Proven Case Reports (review, 2023)
- PMID 39984148: Immune checkpoint inhibitor-related CNS vasculitis - A systematic review and report of 6 cases (systematic review, 2025)
- PMID 41112399: Tumefactive primary angiitis of the central nervous system mimicking glioblastoma: A case-based systematic review (systematic review, 2025)

## Cluster `central_sleep_apnoea` (topics 350) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 AASM guideline covers treatment of central sleep apnoea by aetiology (primary, heart failure, medication, treatment-emergent, medical condition), with mostly conditional, very-low-certainty recommendations. PAP does not prevent cardiovascular events or death in sleep apnoea, and adaptive servo-ventilation is restricted to defined populations.

**Teaching points**
- The 2025 AASM guideline gives treatment recommendations for central sleep apnoea by aetiology: primary, heart failure, medication or substance, treatment-emergent, and medical condition; certainty is very low in places. [PMID 40820608]
- Updated adaptive servo-ventilation recommendations apply only to the target populations, with LVEF above 45% or mild CHF-related central sleep apnoea syndrome addressed explicitly. [PMID 27092695]
- A meta-analysis found PAP therapy for sleep apnoea does not support treatment aimed at preventing cardiovascular events or death, though PAP has other benefits. [PMID 28697252]
- Acetazolamide improved both obstructive and central sleep apnoea in a meta-analysis, though long-term data are needed. [PMID 32768459]
- Sleep-related breathing disorders are common in dementia, particularly Alzheimer's and Parkinson's dementia. [PMID 36758347]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence certainty for most treatments is low.

**MCQ angles**
- Adaptive servo-ventilation limits.
- PAP and cardiovascular outcomes.

**Case hooks**
- Heart failure patient with Cheyne-Stokes breathing at night.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria, the SERVE-HF mortality signal and opioid-related CSA management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27092695: Updated Adaptive Servo-Ventilation Recommendations for the 2012 AASM Guideline: "The Treatment of Central Sleep Apnea Syndromes in Adults: Practice Parameters with an Evidence-Based Literature Review and Meta-Analyses" (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 28697252: Association of Positive Airway Pressure With Cardiovascular Events and Death in Adults With Sleep Apnea: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 32768459: Acetazolamide for OSA and Central Sleep Apnea: A Comprehensive Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 36758347: Prevalence of sleep-related breathing disorders and associated risk factors among people with dementia: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 40820608: Treatment of central sleep apnea in adults: an American Academy of Sleep Medicine clinical practice guideline (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `caa` (topics 351) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cerebral amyloid angiopathy is found in a substantial share of lobar intracerebral haemorrhage. Microbleed burden and distribution, and disseminated cortical superficial siderosis, identify survivors at higher risk of recurrent haemorrhage, and microbleeds raise haemorrhage risk after thrombolysis.

**Teaching points**
- CAA prevalence is 50-57% in patients with lobar intracerebral haemorrhage, with large heterogeneity across neuropathology and MRI protocols. [PMID 34057813]
- Microbleed burden and distribution on MRI identify ICH survivors with higher recurrence risk. [PMID 28747441]
- Disseminated cortical superficial siderosis (more than 3 sulci) increases future ICH risk in symptomatic CAA survivors (Class III evidence). [PMID 31732564]
- Increasing microbleed burden is associated with higher risk of ICH and poor 3-6 month functional outcome after intravenous thrombolysis. [PMID 28720659]
- CSF patterns of Aβ42, Aβ40, tau and p-tau differ in CAA versus Alzheimer's disease and may serve as biomarkers. [PMID 29386280]
- In CAA-related inflammation, MRI findings are most prevalent and about 34% carry homozygous ApoE ε4/ε4. [PMID 36453271]

**Uncertainties**
- Biomarker validation in larger cohorts.

**MCQ angles**
- Superficial siderosis and bleeding risk.
- Microbleeds and thrombolysis.

**Case hooks**
- Elderly patient with lobar haemorrhage and multiple cortical microbleeds.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Boston criteria, antithrombotic decisions and management of CAA-related inflammation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28720659: Microbleeds, Cerebral Hemorrhage, and Functional Outcome After Stroke Thrombolysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 28747441: Brain hemorrhage recurrence, small vessel disease type, and cerebral microbleeds: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29386280: Core cerebrospinal fluid biomarker profile in cerebral amyloid angiopathy: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 31732564: Cortical superficial siderosis and bleeding risk in cerebral amyloid angiopathy: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 34057813: Prevalence of cerebral amyloid angiopathy: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36453271: Clinical, Neuroimaging, and Genetic Markers in Cerebral Amyloid Angiopathy-Related Inflammation: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `cvst` (topics 352, 353) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cerebral venous thrombosis is managed per the 2024 Canadian Stroke Best Practice module and Chinese Stroke Association guideline. Meta-analyses suggest DOACs have similar efficacy and safety to vitamin K antagonists, with caution pending randomised confirmation.

**Teaching points**
- The Canadian Stroke Best Practice Recommendations 7th edition include a cerebral venous thrombosis module with implementation resources and performance measures. [PMID 38826076]
- The Chinese Stroke Association guideline summarises evidence for CVST diagnosis, treatment and secondary prevention. [PMID 32409571]
- A meta-analysis found DOACs in CVT have similar efficacy and safety to VKAs with better recanalisation rate. [PMID 34287841]
- Another meta-analysis of DOACs versus VKAs in CVT urges caution because of limited randomised data, small sample and rare events. [PMID 35938419]
- COVID-19-associated CVT is a diagnostic challenge needing high suspicion; evidence on pathophysiology and prevention is scarce. [PMID 33426733]
- Population-based CVT incidence is heterogeneous across studies, with Canadian incidence lower than elsewhere. [PMID 36337058]
- The ACR Appropriateness Criteria address imaging in stroke and related conditions. [PMID 38823945]

**Uncertainties**
- DOAC versus VKA awaiting more randomised data.

**MCQ angles**
- DOAC use in CVT.
- COVID-associated CVT.

**Case hooks**
- Young woman on oral contraception with headache and venous infarct.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Heparin in acute phase, treatment duration, MR venography and risk-factor workup are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32409571: Chinese Stroke Association guidelines for clinical management of cerebrovascular disorders: executive summary and 2019 update of clinical management of cerebral venous sinus thrombosis (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 33426733: Cerebral venous thrombosis and severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus-2 infection: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)

## Cluster `cervical_cancer` (topics 354) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** NCCN guidelines (version 2.2026) cover diagnosis, staging, surgery and systemic therapy for cervical cancer, and the French society updates indications for external beam radiotherapy and brachytherapy. Prevention relies on HPV testing, with self-collected vaginal specimens expanding screening access and a 3-year interval, and the WHO guideline for pre-cancer screening and treatment.

**Teaching points**
- NCCN guideline version 2.2026 covers diagnosis and workup, staging and surgery principles, primary treatment for early-stage and advanced disease, and systemic therapy for cervical cancer. [PMID 41671440]
- The French radiation oncology society recommends indications and techniques for external beam radiotherapy and brachytherapy in cervical cancer. [PMID 41086563]
- Self-collected vaginal specimens for HPV testing expand screening options and access for under-screened people, requiring laboratory and clinical workflow changes. [PMID 39982254]
- When self-collected vaginal specimens are used for HPV testing, a 3-year interval should be followed, with screening-exit stipulations for higher-risk women. [PMID 41342729]
- The WHO guideline supports screening and treatment of cervical pre-cancer lesions as part of the global strategy to eliminate cervical cancer. [PMID 38976622]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal screening-exit criteria for higher-risk women.

**MCQ angles**
- Self-collected HPV testing.
- Brachytherapy role.

**Case hooks**
- Woman with post-coital bleeding and visible cervical lesion.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** FIGO staging, chemoradiation regimen, radical hysterectomy criteria and immunotherapy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `cervical_myelopathy` (topics 355, 498) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 AO Spine guideline states surgery is valid for mild degenerative cervical myelopathy and anterior or posterior approaches give similar clinical benefit with different complication profiles. Clinical signs should prompt imaging, and laminoplasty versus fusion trade lordosis preservation against complications.

**Teaching points**
- The AO Spine guideline notes surgical treatment is effective and a valid option for mild DCM, with anterior and posterior approaches giving similar benefit but different complications. [PMID 40257837]
- Clinical signs suggesting cord compression should prompt neuroimaging to confirm or refute DCM. [PMID 37903098]
- Anterior decompression with fusion preserved cervical lordosis better than laminoplasty, with more surgical complications, and similar neurological recovery. [PMID 32205020]
- Laminoplasty versus laminectomy shows better C5 radiculopathy and superficial infection results with laminoplasty. [PMID 35111804]
- Predictors of kyphotic deformity after laminoplasty are reviewed, with a preoperative risk score proposed as future work. [PMID 36057129]
- Gait speed is the only spatio-temporal parameter that improved significantly after decompression in DCM. [PMID 37866484]
- WFNS and Italian guidance lists contemporary recommendations for cervical spondylotic myelopathy. [PMID 37401060]
- Understanding the natural history of DCM is a research priority, with MRI-related pathologies common in healthy people. [PMID 35255357]

**Uncertainties**
- Natural history of asymptomatic cord compression.

**MCQ angles**
- Surgery for mild DCM.
- Laminoplasty versus fusion.

**Case hooks**
- Older patient with clumsy hands, gait disturbance and hyperreflexia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Hoffmann sign, mJOA severity grading and indications for conservative care are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32205020: A systematic review and meta-analysis comparing anterior decompression with fusion and posterior laminoplasty for cervical spondylotic myelopathy (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35111804: Comparison of Laminoplasty vs. Laminectomy for Cervical Spondylotic Myelopathy: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35255357: The Prevalence of Degenerative Cervical Myelopathy-Related Pathologies on Magnetic Resonance Imaging in Healthy/Asymptomatic Individuals: A Meta-Analysis of Published Studies and Comparison to a Symptomatic Cohort (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36057129: Predictors for cervical kyphotic deformity following laminoplasty: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `cmt` (topics 356) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 clinical practice guidelines for Charcot-Marie-Tooth disease cover diagnosis and management, with exercise, orthotic adaptation and tendon transfer, and state follow-up is clinical without routine ancillary testing. Paediatric guidance and a French gene-list consensus support multidisciplinary care and molecular diagnosis.

**Teaching points**
- CMT guidelines address exercise, orthotic adaptation and conservative surgeries such as tendon transfer, with clinical-only follow-up and no routine ancillary testing. [PMID 38431252]
- A paediatric CMT guideline advocates improved access to multidisciplinary care. [PMID 35140138]
- A consensus statement on CMT surgery gives an approach to cavovarus deformity at Level V evidence. [PMID 32478578]
- A French consensus gene list for inherited neuropathies has 93 definitive and 34 limited genes. [PMID 35205364]
- Neurofilament light chain is a blood-based marker of neuronal injury in peripheral neuropathies. [PMID 36574244]
- CMT prevalence studies are heterogeneous, with higher rates in males and Northern Europe, and need clearer diagnostic criteria. [PMID 36631678]

**Uncertainties**
- Epidemiology varies by method and country.

**MCQ angles**
- Genetic testing panels.
- Orthotics and exercise.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with pes cavus, foot drop and slow nerve conduction.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** CMT1A PMP22 duplication, nerve conduction thresholds and inheritance are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `ici_myocarditis` (topics 357) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ICI cardiotoxicity has high mortality, and early recognition, ICI cessation and prompt corticosteroids are recommended though randomised evidence is absent. Systematic reviews report systematic cardiology screening before treatment and feasible re-challenge after recovery.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found cardiovascular adverse event mortality with ICIs remains high, with early recognition, ICI cessation and prompt corticosteroids advised in absence of randomised evidence. [PMID 39172480]
- Systematic cardiological screening is advocated before PD-1, PD-L1 and CTLA-4 inhibitor therapy. [PMID 39667715]
- Analysis of case reports suggests glucocorticoids can be reduced or stopped after recovery and ICI re-challenge may be feasible after resolution. [PMID 37876928]
- A review compared high-dose with low-dose steroids for ICI-associated myocarditis, suggesting therapy duration be tailored and combination therapy considered in steroid resistance. [PMID 40013153]
- Thymic alterations are associated with incidence and seriousness of ICI myotoxicities. [PMID 37884625]
- TripleM syndrome (myocarditis, myositis, myasthenia) management has limited evidence and needs consensus definitions. [PMID 40647363]
- In lung cancer, ICI plus chemotherapy raised cardiotoxicity risk versus single ICI, while dual immunotherapy did not. [PMID 38886852]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal steroid dose and second-line agents.

**MCQ angles**
- Overlap with myositis and myasthenia.
- Re-challenge after recovery.

**Case hooks**
- Melanoma patient on nivolumab with troponin rise and conduction block.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria (biopsy, cardiac MRI), troponin surveillance and abatacept or other second-line therapy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37876928: Immune checkpoint inhibitor-associated myocarditis: a systematic analysis of case reports (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 37884625: Thymus alterations and susceptibility to immune checkpoint inhibitor myocarditis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38886852: Immune checkpoint inhibitor-induced cardiotoxicity in patients with lung cancer: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39172480: Immune Checkpoint Inhibitor-Induced Cardiotoxicity: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `extravasation` (topics 358) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** A 2024 multidisciplinary Japanese guideline updates prevention and treatment of cancer-drug extravasation, classifying drugs by tissue injury. Neonatal and contrast extravasation guidance emphasises clinical diagnosis, and surgical referral by clinical severity rather than extravasated volume.

**Teaching points**
- A multidisciplinary guideline from the Japanese Society of Clinical Oncology updates prevention and treatment of extravasation of cancer drug therapy and classifies drugs by tissue injury. [PMID 39389005]
- A neonatal extravasation guideline with an educational programme was tested in a before-and-after study, and needs periodic education incorporated into nurse induction. [PMID 32967637]
- For contrast medium extravasation, diagnosis is mostly clinical, outcomes mostly favourable, and surgical referral should rest on clinical severity not volume. [PMID 35175378]
- Peripheral IV catheter non-infectious complications, including infiltration, are a common cause of catheter failure in adults and children. [PMID 33016412, 31862530]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for antidotes is not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

- Drug classification by tissue injury.
- Contrast extravasation referral.

- Painful swollen cannula site during anthracycline infusion.

## Cluster `cinv` (topics 359, 360, 361) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CINV prevention is guided by SEOM guidance covering multiday regimens, oral treatments and radiation-induced emesis. Adding olanzapine to a triplet regimen helps in highly emetogenic chemotherapy, and paediatric guidelines address breakthrough and anticipatory CINV.

**Teaching points**
- Adding olanzapine to 5-HT3 antagonist, NK1 antagonist and dexamethasone triplet therapy is useful in preventing nausea and vomiting from highly emetogenic chemotherapy with minimal adverse effects. [PMID 39570460]
- The SEOM emesis guideline covers antiemetic prophylaxis options including oral treatments, multiday regimens and radiation-induced emesis. [PMID 35347571]
- The ASCO cannabis guideline notes uncertainty about whether cannabinoids improve supportive care outcomes in adults with cancer. [PMID 38478773]
- A paediatric guideline recommends options for acute and delayed CINV, including patients unable to receive dexamethasone or an NK1 antagonist, and identifies evidence gaps. [PMID 36221901]
- Paediatric breakthrough CINV guidance recommends using antiemetics that controlled breakthrough to prevent refractory CINV. [PMID 37178438]
- Hypnosis, systematic desensitisation, relaxation and lorazepam are conditionally recommended for secondary prevention of anticipatory CINV in children. [PMID 33686754]
- Systematic desensitisation may reduce anticipatory CINV in adults, pending further research. [PMID 38722486]
- Acupuncture may improve acute and delayed vomiting control but certainty of evidence is very low. [PMID 37226372]

**Uncertainties**
- Non-drug measures have very low certainty.

**MCQ angles**
- Olanzapine in HEC.
- Anticipatory CINV management.

**Case hooks**
- Patient on cisplatin with delayed nausea despite ondansetron.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Emetogenic risk classification, specific NK1 antagonist doses and the MASCC/ASCO regimen by risk level are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33686754: Prevention and treatment of anticipatory chemotherapy-induced nausea and vomiting in pediatric cancer patients and hematopoietic stem cell recipients: Clinical practice guideline update (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35347571: SEOM clinical guideline emesis (2021) (guideline, 2022)

## Cluster `chest_pain_ed` (topics 362, 363) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2021 AHA/ACC multi-society chest pain guideline gives an evidence-based approach to risk stratification and diagnostic workup, incorporating cost-value and shared decision-making. Sex differences, nonobstructive CAD and imaging in pregnancy are addressed in a women-focused summary, and coronary CTA has practice guidance for acute chest pain.

**Teaching points**
- The 2021 AHA/ACC/ASE/CHEST/SAEM/SCCT/SCMR chest pain guideline provides an evidence-based approach to risk stratification and diagnostic workup, with cost-value considerations and shared decision-making. [PMID 34756653, 34709928, 34709879, 34756652, 34955448]
- A summary of the 2021 guideline for women covers sex differences in presentation, nonobstructive CAD, ischaemia with no obstructive coronary arteries, and cardiac imaging in pregnancy. [PMID 36921653]
- A 2022 practice guideline addresses coronary CT angiography for patients presenting to the emergency department with acute chest pain. [PMID 36253281]
- Canadian imaging referral guidelines cover acute and chronic chest pain. [PMID 38733286]

**Uncertainties**
- Cost-value of testing strategies.

**MCQ angles**
- Chest pain in women.
- Evidence-based risk stratification and shared decision-making.

**Case hooks**
- ED patient with intermittent chest pain and normal ECG.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** High-sensitivity troponin pathways, timing of stress testing and disposition thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `chikungunya` (topics 364) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Chikungunya has an acute phase and a potentially long chronic phase with arthritis. Rheumatoid arthritis incidence is significant after infection, mortality risk is higher in elderly men with chronic disease, and methotrexate for chronic arthritis lacks randomised evidence.

**Teaching points**
- Chikungunya clinical outcomes include an acute phase and a potentially long-lasting chronic phase. [PMID 38848443]
- RA incidence after chikungunya infection is significant, motivating long-term rheumatological follow-up and biomarker research. [PMID 39998058]
- Methotrexate treatment of chronic chikungunya arthritis is supported only by heterogeneous studies, calling for randomised placebo-controlled trials. [PMID 29361202]
- Elderly men with chronic diseases have a higher risk of death from chikungunya and deserve more careful evaluation. [PMID 39894663]
- Chronic disability prevalence after chikungunya infection and force of infection differ between epidemic and endemic settings. [PMID 38342105]
- Chikungunya is associated with neurological disorders in meta-analysis. [PMID 38885813]
- Clinical manifestations of dengue, Zika and chikungunya in the Pacific Islands were pooled to assist clinicians in outbreaks. [PMID 38340071]

**Uncertainties**
- Treatment of chronic arthritis.

**MCQ angles**
- Chronic arthritis after chikungunya.
- Mortality risk factors.

**Case hooks**
- Traveller with persistent joint pain months after fever and rash.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Acute diagnosis (PCR, IgM), analgesic management and vaccination are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29361202: Treatment of Chronic Chikungunya Arthritis With Methotrexate: A Systematic Review (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 38340071: Clinical manifestations of dengue, Zika and chikungunya in the Pacific Islands: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38342105: Chikungunya seroprevalence, force of infection, and prevalence of chronic disability after infection in endemic and epidemic settings: a systematic review, meta-analysis, and modelling study (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38848443: Clinical outcomes of chikungunya: A systematic literature review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38885813: A meta-analysis of Chikungunya virus in neurological disorders (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39894663: Risk factors for mortality in patients with chikungunya: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39998058: Chikungunya Fever and Rheumatoid Arthritis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `childhood_asthma` (topics 365, 366, 376) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence addresses risk factors rather than treatment: rhinovirus bronchiolitis is more strongly associated than RSV with later recurrent wheeze and asthma, and particulate pollution and Mycoplasma infection are associated with childhood asthma. Mediterranean diet shows a low-certainty inverse association.

**Teaching points**
- Rhinovirus-induced bronchiolitis was more strongly associated than RSV bronchiolitis with recurrent wheeze and childhood asthma. [PMID 35338734]
- Childhood asthma prevalence is highest in Oceania and lower in Eurasia, with modifiable risk factors identified. [PMID 39833735]
- Exposure to particulate matter is associated with developing asthma and wheeze in children, supporting measures to reduce particle pollution. [PMID 40165124]
- Black and organic carbon and ammonium in PM2.5 mainly contributed to increased childhood asthma risk. [PMID 40478447]
- Mycoplasma pneumoniae infection is positively associated with childhood asthma, though causal mechanisms are unclear. [PMID 33932544]
- Mediterranean diet adherence shows a trend to inverse association with childhood asthma with low certainty. [PMID 37933707]

**Uncertainties**
- Causality of infection and diet associations.

**MCQ angles**
- Viral bronchiolitis and later asthma.
- Air pollution and asthma risk.

**Case hooks**
- Toddler with recurrent wheeze after rhinovirus bronchiolitis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnosis, inhaled corticosteroid stepwise therapy, GINA and BTS guidance and wheeze phenotypes are not covered by the retrieved excerpts; a guideline source is needed.

**Sources**
- PMID 33932544: Mycoplasma pneumoniae infection and risk of childhood asthma: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35338734: The role of respiratory syncytial virus- and rhinovirus-induced bronchiolitis in recurrent wheeze and asthma-A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 37933707: Greater fruit and vegetables consumption, and adherence to a Mediterranean type of diet reduces the risk for asthma in children; a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39833735: Prevalence and risk factors for childhood asthma: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40165124: The odds of developing asthma and wheeze among children and adolescents exposed to particulate matter: asystematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40478447: Chemical constituents of PM&lt;sub&gt;2.5&lt;/sub&gt; and their association with childhood asthma and lung function: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `gallstones` (topics 369, 370, 371) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** For suspected common bile duct stones, diagnostic guidelines have limited specificity and cannot reliably select patients for upfront ERCP. EUS before ERCP can spare about half of patients with bilirubin above 4 mg/dL and dilated duct a diagnostic ERCP, and EAES provides a rapid guideline on CBD stone management.

**Teaching points**
- The EAES rapid guideline updates management of common bile duct stones with network meta-analysis and GRADE. [PMID 36229556]
- Existing diagnostic guidelines for choledocholithiasis in acute biliary presentations have limited specificity for high-risk CBD stones and cannot reliably select patients for upfront ERCP. [PMID 40569779]
- Using endoscopic ultrasound to confirm CBD stones before ERCP can help almost half of patients with bilirubin above 4 mg/dL and a dilated CBD avoid diagnostic ERCP. [PMID 39845698]
- A surgery-first paediatric choledocholithiasis guideline was safe, feasible and effective using structured validated criteria. [PMID 40633501]
- Predictors of post-ERCP pancreatitis were pooled to support risk prediction, informed consent and prevention. [PMID 39694210]
- A clinical practice guideline covers acute and chronic pancreatitis, with long-term care for uncomplicated disease with primary care and gastroenterology. [PMID 35945698]

**Uncertainties**
- Pre-test risk stratification of CBD stones.

**MCQ angles**
- EUS before ERCP.
- Post-ERCP pancreatitis predictors.

**Case hooks**
- Jaundiced patient with dilated duct and bilirubin 5 mg/dL.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Cholecystectomy timing for biliary colic, cholangitis antibiotics and Tokyo grading are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35945698: Clinical Practice Guideline—Acute and Chronic Pancreatitis (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36229556: EAES rapid guideline: updated systematic review, network meta-analysis, CINeMA and GRADE assessment, and evidence-informed European recommendations on the management of common bile duct stones (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 39694210: Predictors of Post-endoscopic Retrograde Cholangiopancreatography Pancreatitis: A Comprehensive Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39845698: The use of endoscopic ultrasound in tandem with endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography in the 2019 American Society for Gastrointestinal Endoscopy guideline for patients at high risk of choledocholithiasis can help to avoid diagnostic endoscopic retrograde cholangiopancreatography in individuals without ascending cholangitis (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40569779: Performance of Diagnostic Guidelines in the Evaluation of Choledocholithiasis in Patients With Acute Biliary Presentation: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40633501: Shifting the pediatric choledocholithiasis paradigm: Development of a surgery-first clinical practice guideline at a tertiary children's hospital (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `cholera` (topics 372) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Oral killed cholera vaccines give high-certainty protection with about 40% vaccine efficacy at two years. Paediatric diarrhoea guidance restricts antibiotics to dysentery-like symptoms, suspected cholera with severe dehydration or comorbidities, and antimicrobial resistance, including tetracycline, is rising in V. cholerae.

**Teaching points**
- Oral killed cholera vaccines reduce cholera, with overall vaccine efficacy about 40% at both one- and two-year follow-up in a trial of 204,700 participants (high-certainty evidence). [PMID 38197546]
- A Chinese paediatric diarrhoea guideline recommends zinc for children over 6 months in deficient regions and restricts antibiotics to dysenteric symptoms, suspected cholera with severe dehydration, or comorbidities. [PMID 40437180]
- Antimicrobial resistance is rising in V. cholerae, especially in low-income and endemic areas, so surveillance and appropriate susceptibility testing are crucial. [PMID 35468830]
- Tetracycline resistance among clinical V. cholerae isolates requires monitoring when choosing treatment. [PMID 34362438]
- Ciprofloxacin, azithromycin and other agents showed the best effectiveness against V. cholerae isolates in a resistance meta-analysis. [PMID 35790112]
- Non-cholera Vibrio infections carry a high burden in South Asia, with antibiotic-resistant and ESBL strains reported. [PMID 38311027]

**Uncertainties**
- Emerging resistance patterns.

**MCQ angles**
- Vaccine efficacy.
- When to give antibiotics.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with profuse watery stool in an outbreak area.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Oral rehydration and intravenous fluid regimens (the core of therapy) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 34362438: Global status of tetracycline resistance among clinical isolates of Vibrio cholerae: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35468830: Global status of antimicrobial resistance among environmental isolates of Vibrio cholerae O1/O139: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35790112: Antimicrobial resistance in <i>Vibrio cholerae</i> O1/O139 clinical isolates: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 38197546: Oral killed cholera vaccines for preventing cholera (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38311027: Vibriosis in South Asia: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40437180: Clinical practice guidelines for acute infectious diarrhea in children in China (2024) (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `chronic_abd_pain` (topics 373) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence is mainly paediatric: functional abdominal pain disorders affect over 1 in 9 children, with CBT and hypnotherapy the best-supported psychosocial treatments and major evidence gaps for drug treatment. Adult chronic abdominal pain guidance is not covered.

**Teaching points**
- Functional abdominal pain disorders affect over 1 in 9 children worldwide, more in girls and those with psychological stressors. [PMID 39761807]
- CBT and hypnotherapy may be considered for childhood functional abdominal pain disorders. [PMID 35404394]
- ESPGHAN/NASPGHAN guidelines for IBS and functional abdominal pain identified major evidence gaps and incentivise large paediatric trials. [PMID 40444524]
- A multidisciplinary expert consensus guides primary care providers in diagnosing and managing paediatric functional abdominal pain disorders. [PMID 40433476]
- A probiotics meta-analysis in children with functional abdominal pain disorders found similar adverse event withdrawals between probiotic and placebo. [PMID 36799531]
- Vitamin D supplementation may help IBS with potential efficacy and good safety, pending further trials. [PMID 35396764]
- Medical cannabis recommendations place it as third- or fourth-line therapy in chronic noncancer pain after detailed discussion of benefits and harms. [PMID 33613794]
- Video capsule endoscopy is a complementary test for GI bleeding, Crohn's or coeliac disease with negative or inconclusive prior studies. [PMID 28063287]

**Uncertainties**
- Drug treatments for paediatric functional pain lack trials.

**MCQ angles**
- CBT for functional abdominal pain.
- Role of capsule endoscopy.

**Case hooks**
- Child with recurrent abdominal pain and normal examination.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Adult chronic abdominal pain assessment, alarm features, neuromodulators and abdominal wall pain are not covered in the retrieved excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28063287: Clinical Practice Guidelines for the Use of Video Capsule Endoscopy (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 33613794: Medical Cannabis for Chronic Noncancer Pain: A Systematic Review of Health Care Recommendations (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35396764: Vitamin D supplementation for irritable bowel syndrome: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35404394: Psychosocial Interventions for the Treatment of Functional Abdominal Pain Disorders in Children: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36799531: Probiotics for management of functional abdominal pain disorders in children (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39761807: Epidemiology of Pediatric Functional Abdominal Pain Disorders: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40433476: Understanding functional abdominal pain disorders among children: a multidisciplinary expert consensus statement (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40444524: ESPGHAN/NASPGHAN guidelines for treatment of irritable bowel syndrome and functional abdominal pain-not otherwise specified in children aged 4-18 years (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `chronic_allograft` (topics 374) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence spans organs. In kidney transplant, donor-derived cell-free DNA is a valuable marker for antibody-mediated rejection though less accurate for acute rejection, and imlifidase enables transplantation in highly sensitised patients. Antibody-mediated rejection is a critical unmet treatment need across heart and lung allografts.

**Teaching points**
- Plasma donor-derived cell-free DNA accuracy for acute rejection in kidney allografts is limited by heterogeneity, but it is a valuable biomarker for antibody-mediated rejection. [PMID 38386614]
- A risk-adapted approach to HLA delisting and imlifidase-enabled deceased-donor kidney transplantation addresses highly sensitised patients after kidney-preserving pathways are exhausted. [PMID 42344929]
- A JACC position statement highlights contemporary diagnosis and therapy of antibody-mediated rejection after heart transplantation, including complement-mediated injury and cytokine pathways. [PMID 40848703]
- The ISHLT scientific statement on pulmonary antibody-mediated rejection stresses better treatments as a critical unmet need and proposes a more precise definition. [PMID 42274447]
- An ISHLT consensus conference prepared a platform for further studies of antibodies and AMR in transplant patients. [PMID 40498433]
- A clinical consensus statement on heart transplantation covers cardiac allograft vasculopathy, infections and malignancies. [PMID 42085519]
- AASLD/AST guidance on liver transplant graft-related complications highlights the need to optimise management including recurrence prevention of alcohol-associated and metabolic liver disease. [PMID 40844852]

**Uncertainties**
- Treatment of antibody-mediated rejection.

**MCQ angles**
- dd-cfDNA use.
- Imlifidase.

**Case hooks**
- Kidney transplant recipient with rising creatinine and donor-specific antibodies.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Kidney-specific chronic allograft nephropathy, interstitial fibrosis and tubular atrophy, calcineurin inhibitor toxicity and Banff classification are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38386614: Donor-derived cell-free DNA as a diagnostic marker for kidney-allograft rejection: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40498433: Summary of the International Society for Heart and Lung Transplantation consensus conference on emerging understanding of antibodies and antibody-mediated rejection in heart transplantation (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40844852: AASLD AST Practice Guideline on adult liver transplantation: Diagnosis and management of graft-related complications (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 40848703: Contemporary Antibody-Mediated Rejection in Heart Transplantation: JACC: Heart Failure Position Statement (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42085519: Update on heart transplantation: a clinical consensus statement of the Advanced Heart Failure Committee of the Heart Failure Association of the European Society of Cardiology (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42274447: International Society for Heart and Lung Transplantation Scientific Statement on pulmonary antibody-mediated rejection and proposed graft, antibody, and pathology (GAP) definition (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42344929: Risk-adapted HLA delisting and imlifidase-enabled deceased-donor kidney transplantation in highly sensitized kidney transplant candidates: a German expert consensus report (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `chronic_ar` (topics 375) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** In chronic aortic regurgitation, vena contracta width and LV volumes reliably identify significant disease, while CMR and global longitudinal strain add prognostic information in asymptomatic patients. Reviews suggest surgery timed earlier than current guidelines may give survival benefit, but randomised trials are lacking.

**Teaching points**
- A simplified echo approach using vena contracta width and LV volumes can reliably identify significant AR when compared with CMR. [PMID 39545892]
- Global longitudinal strain is a prognostic biomarker in asymptomatic moderate-to-severe AR with preserved LVEF and may help timing of surgery, though thresholds need validation. [PMID 41010738]
- Multi-parameter CMR predicts aortic valve surgery as an endpoint in chronic AR. [PMID 39077090]
- Myocardial remodelling assessment beyond volumes and function may identify irreversible myocardial injury earlier. [PMID 40911421]
- Studies of AR, AS and mixed disease found long-term survival benefit from surgery earlier than current guideline timing, but RCTs are required. [PMID 32825345]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal timing of surgery in asymptomatic patients.

**MCQ angles**
- Echo grading of AR.
- Strain in asymptomatic AR.

**Case hooks**
- Asymptomatic young adult with severe AR and LVEF 55%.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Guideline surgery thresholds (LVEF, LVESD), surveillance intervals and vasodilator therapy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32825345: Early Valve Replacement for Severe Aortic Valve Disease: Effect on Mortality and Clinical Ramifications (review, 2020)
- PMID 39077090: Prognostic Value of Cardiac Magnetic Resonance Imaging in Chronic Aortic Regurgitation: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39545892: Echocardiographic Evaluation of Chronic Aortic Regurgitation: Comparison With Cardiac Magnetic Resonance and Implications for Guideline Recommendations (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40911421: Myocardial remodelling in aortic regurgitation: time to think beyond volumes and function? (review, 2025)
- PMID 41010738: Global Longitudinal Strain as a Prognostic Biomarker for Asymptomatic Moderate to Severe Aortic Regurgitation with Preserved Ejection Fraction: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `ccs` (topics 377) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2024 ESC chronic coronary syndrome guideline sets antithrombotic regimens, and a systematic review of CCS guidelines calls for higher-quality evidence-based guidance. A meta-analysis found revascularisation improved outcomes over optimal medical treatment, with greater benefit at longer follow-up.

**Teaching points**
- A review of the 2024 ESC guidelines covers oral antithrombotic regimens in chronic coronary syndrome and compares them with a European expert consensus. [PMID 39657008]
- A systematic review of CCS clinical practice guidelines calls for stronger multidisciplinary efforts toward higher-quality evidence-based guidelines. [PMID 38146345]
- Revascularisation was associated with better clinical outcomes than optimal medical treatment in CCS, with larger benefit at longer follow-up and a role for personalised choice. [PMID 37949356]
- Atherogenic index of plasma is linked to CAD risk, severity and prognosis, with optimal cut-offs unclear. [PMID 39844262]
- Triglyceride-glucose index is associated with in-stent restenosis after PCI. [PMID 38702615]

**Uncertainties**
- Benefit of revascularisation relative to modern medical therapy.

**MCQ angles**
- Antithrombotic therapy in CCS.
- Revascularisation versus medical therapy.

**Case hooks**
- Stable angina on beta-blocker with positive stress test.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Pre-test probability, stress imaging, CT coronary angiography and anti-anginal drug sequencing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37949356: Clinical impact of coronary revascularization over medical treatment in chronic coronary syndromes: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38146345: Diagnosis, Treatment, and Management for Chronic Coronary Syndrome: A Systematic Review of Clinical Practice Guidelines and Consensus Statements (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38702615: The association between the triglyceride-glucose index and in-stent restenosis in patients undergoing percutaneous coronary intervention: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39657008: Antithrombotic therapy in patients with chronic coronary syndrome: a focus on the 2024 European Society of Cardiology guidelines (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 39844262: Atherogenic index of plasma and coronary artery disease: a systematic review and meta-analysis of observational studies (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `chronic_cough` (topics 378) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CHEST guidelines treat chronic cough by cause and population: extraesophageal reflux symptoms improving on a PPI should not be taken as proof of GERD, children should not receive GERD treatment without GERD features, and ILD-associated cough has limited evidence.

**Teaching points**
- AGA best practice advice says symptom improvement on PPI in extraesophageal reflux may arise from non-acid mechanisms and does not confirm GERD. [PMID 37061897]
- In children with chronic cough, GERD treatment should not be used without clinical features of GERD, and paediatric GERD guidelines should guide investigation. [PMID 31002783]
- The CHEST paediatric chronic cough guideline now has high-quality evidence for some management aspects, with further primary care studies needed. [PMID 32179109]
- Aetiologies of chronic cough in children differ from adults and depend on age and setting. [PMID 28645463]
- The CHEST guideline on cough due to asthma and non-asthmatic eosinophilic bronchitis addresses non-invasive airway inflammation measurement and treatment. [PMID 31972181]
- Evidence for managing chronic cough in interstitial lung disease is limited. [PMID 30036496]
- The Chinese national cough guideline covers evaluation, diagnosis, and empirical and symptomatic treatment of chronic cough. [PMID 35000304]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for ILD cough therapy is limited.

**MCQ angles**
- PPI response not proving GERD.
- Paediatric versus adult cough causes.

**Case hooks**
- Non-smoker with chronic dry cough and normal chest radiograph.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Adult upper airway cough syndrome, ACE inhibitor cough and refractory cough neuromodulators are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28645463: Etiologies of Chronic Cough in Pediatric Cohorts: CHEST Guideline and Expert Panel Report (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 30036496: Treatment of Interstitial Lung Disease Associated Cough: CHEST Guideline and Expert Panel Report (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 31002783: Chronic Cough and Gastroesophageal Reflux in Children: CHEST Guideline and Expert Panel Report (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 31972181: Managing Chronic Cough Due to Asthma and NAEB in Adults and Adolescents: CHEST Guideline and Expert Panel Report (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 32179109: Managing Chronic Cough as a Symptom in Children and Management Algorithms: CHEST Guideline and Expert Panel Report (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 35000304: [Chinese national guideline on diagnosis and management of cough(2021)] (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37061897: AGA Clinical Practice Update on the Diagnosis and Management of Extraesophageal Gastroesophageal Reflux Disease: Expert Review (guideline, 2023)

## Cluster `hbv` (topics 379) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2026 AASLD/IDSA guideline updates chronic hepatitis B treatment, urging caution when stopping antivirals in virologically suppressed patients without HBsAg loss and tailored HCC surveillance. Antiviral treatment in the indeterminate phase may reduce HCC risk in high-risk subgroups, and non-invasive tests have defined false-positive and false-negative rates.

**Teaching points**
- The AASLD/IDSA practice guideline provides updated evidence-based recommendations to optimise care of persons with chronic hepatitis B. [PMID 41186418]
- Supporting systematic review highlights caution in discontinuing antiviral therapy in virologically suppressed individuals without HBsAg loss, and tailored HCC surveillance for co-infection or cirrhosis. [PMID 41186417]
- Antiviral treatment in the indeterminate phase may reduce hepatocellular carcinoma risk in high-risk subgroups. [PMID 39577468]
- Non-invasive tests for fibrosis and cirrhosis in chronic HBV carry false positives and negatives depending on cutoff, e.g. APRI low cutoff for cirrhosis gave 24.7% false positives at 5% prevalence. [PMID 39983746]
- Expert consensus updates treatment navigation for functional cure of chronic hepatitis B using antivirals and immunomodulators. [PMID 39838828, 41167769]
- Australian guidelines on HCC surveillance set recommendations for people at high risk. [PMID 40976940]
- AGA advice notes that among patients without cirrhosis only a subset with chronic hepatitis B need HCC surveillance. [PMID 42017860]

**Uncertainties**
- Stopping antivirals and functional cure strategies.

**MCQ angles**
- Caution stopping antivirals.
- Surveillance in non-cirrhotic HBV.

**Case hooks**
- HBeAg-negative patient with intermediate ALT and DNA levels.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Drug choice (tenofovir, entecavir), phase definitions and treatment thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `cidp` (topics 380, 381) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** IVIG is supported as first- or second-line therapy in autoimmune neuropathies with efficacy similar to plasmapheresis, and subcutaneous immunoglobulin has similar efficacy to IVIG in CIDP with fewer systemic adverse effects. Many patients are in remission, so ongoing immunomodulation should be reconsidered.

**Teaching points**
- IVIG efficacy in autoimmune neurological diseases was comparable to plasmapheresis (OR 0.83), supporting use as first- or second-line or adjuvant therapy. [PMID 34920107]
- Subcutaneous immunoglobulin has similar efficacy to IVIG for CIDP and multifocal motor neuropathy with fewer moderate or systemic adverse effects. [PMID 27649063]
- An outcomes meta-analysis cautions against continuing immunomodulating therapy in the many CIDP patients in remission. [PMID 36928889]
- An updated IVIG consensus lists neuromuscular indications with evidence, including chronic autoimmune neuropathy. [PMID 37432872]
- CIDP carries a high physical and psychosocial burden. [PMID 32583051]
- Anti-TNF-associated neuropathies frequently cause chronic impairment despite withdrawal, and rechallenge needs caution. [PMID 40447953]
- In children, acute-onset CIDP is suspected when Guillain-Barré syndrome follows a prolonged course. [PMID 31941581]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term immunotherapy needs after remission.

**MCQ angles**
- SCIG versus IVIG.
- Withdrawal in remission.

**Case hooks**
- Progressive symmetric weakness over more than 8 weeks.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Electrodiagnostic criteria, corticosteroid regimens and EFNS/PNS guideline thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27649063: Subcutaneous versus intravenous immunoglobulin for chronic autoimmune neuropathies: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31941581: Diagnosis and treatment of Guillain-Barré Syndrome in childhood and adolescence: An evidence- and consensus-based guideline (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 32583051: Systematic literature review of burden of illness in chronic inflammatory demyelinating polyneuropathy (CIDP) (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 34920107: Efficacy of intravenous immunoglobulin in autoimmune neurological diseases. Literature systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36928889: Outcome in chronic inflammatory demyelinating polyneuropathy: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37432872: Updated consensus statement: Intravenous immunoglobulin in the treatment of neuromuscular disorders report of the AANEM ad hoc committee (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 40447953: Peripheral neuropathies associated with anti-tnf-α treatments: a systematic review and proposed recommendations (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `ckd` (topics 382, 403, 442) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2026 AHA/ACC/ADA/ASN guideline frames cardiovascular-kidney-metabolic syndrome across cardiology, endocrinology and nephrology. A living guideline covers cardiovascular, kidney and weight effects of type 2 diabetes drugs, and KDIGO 2026 covers CKD anaemia. Frailty and renal dose adjustment are recurring management problems.

**Teaching points**
- The 2026 AHA/ACC/ADA/ASN guideline addresses prevention, detection, evaluation and management of cardiovascular-kidney-metabolic syndrome for cardiologists, endocrinologists, nephrologists and primary care. [PMID 42265997, 42263157]
- A living guideline on type 2 diabetes therapeutics covers cardiovascular, kidney-related and weight loss effects, and notes cost and availability limit implementation. [PMID 40813129]
- The KDIGO 2026 guideline gives recommendations for management of anaemia in chronic kidney disease. [PMID 41485807]
- A consensus report on frailty in CKD links serial frailty measurement to decisions on assisted home dialysis and transplant eligibility. [PMID 42791366]
- Renal dose adjustment guideline adherence is inconsistent, and non-adherence to dosing recommendations was associated with adverse consequences and higher healthcare use. [PMID 42590882]
- An Indian consensus aims to standardise online haemodiafiltration practice in kidney replacement therapy. [PMID 42054263]

**Uncertainties**
- Strategies to improve prescribing safety.

**MCQ angles**
- CKM syndrome concept.
- Renal dose adjustment.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with diabetes, CKD stage 3 and rising cardiovascular risk.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Staging by eGFR and albuminuria, SGLT2 inhibitor and finerenone recommendations and referral thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `cll` (topics 384, 385, 386) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CLL treatment selection uses frontline guidance including targeted agents, with zanubrutinib possibly preferable to acalabrutinib as monotherapy, though randomised data are needed. BTK inhibitors need baseline and 3-monthly ECG and blood pressure monitoring for AF and hypertension, and pneumococcal vaccination is recommended in haematological malignancy.

**Teaching points**
- The Canadian evidence-based guideline gives consensus recommendations on first-line CLL treatment. [PMID 36634577]
- A 2024 workshop consensus complements existing guidelines with US real-world expert approaches to CLL/SLL treatment selection. [PMID 39561376]
- For BTK inhibitor cardiovascular adverse events, blood pressure monitoring and ECG at baseline and every 3 months are recommended, with close haematologist-cardiologist collaboration. [PMID 40684756]
- In a meta-analysis of newer BTK inhibitors, zanubrutinib may be preferred over acalabrutinib monotherapy, but randomised trials are needed. [PMID 37843620]
- A venetoclax meta-analysis found no specific infection that would benefit from routine antimicrobial prophylaxis or pre-emptive testing, with higher neutropenia risk in AML. [PMID 38154071]
- Pneumococcal vaccination is recommended for all patients with lymphoma, AML and adults with ALL. [PMID 40376577]
- An international consensus addresses diagnosis, evaluation and research for Richter transformation. [PMID 40239121]
- Saudi guidelines cover CLL diagnosis, staging and first-line and relapsed/refractory management. [PMID 41613320]

**Uncertainties**
- Direct randomised comparison of BTK inhibitors.

**MCQ angles**
- BTKi cardiovascular monitoring.
- Vaccination in haematological malignancy.

**Case hooks**
- CLL patient on ibrutinib who develops atrial fibrillation.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Treatment indications, TP53 testing, venetoclax-obinutuzumab versus BTKi choice and tumour lysis prophylaxis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

- PMID 37843620: Efficacy and safety of new-generation Bruton tyrosine kinase inhibitors in chronic lymphocytic leukemia/small lymphocytic lymphoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `cml` (topics 387, 388, 389) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 Chinese CML guideline favours treatment minimising toxicity while ensuring efficacy, with allogeneic transplant for multidrug TKI resistance or intolerance and advanced phases. An expert consensus defines TKI intolerance in chronic phase, TKI haematological adverse event rates differ by drug, and ponatinib needs cardiovascular risk management.

**Teaching points**
- The Chinese CML guideline emphasises minimising toxicity and improving quality of life while ensuring efficacy, with allogeneic transplant valuable for multidrug TKI resistance or intolerance and advanced phases. [PMID 41486652]
- An expert consensus defines treatment intolerance of TKIs in chronic-phase CML to standardise care and shared decision-making. [PMID 41054963]
- Haematological adverse event prevalence differs between TKIs, in an order of ponatinib above bosutinib, imatinib and nilotinib in pooled analysis. [PMID 37686630]
- French recommendations propose practical measures to minimise cardiovascular events in ponatinib-treated patients. [PMID 35725593]
- Imatinib is associated with reduced height growth in paediatric CML, independent of pubertal status. [PMID 37027248]
- ABCG2 c.421C>A is associated with imatinib trough plasma levels in GIST and CML. [PMID 36834713]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal TKI choice given toxicity profiles.

**MCQ angles**
- Ponatinib cardiovascular risk.
- Growth effect of imatinib in children.

**Case hooks**
- Chronic-phase CML patient intolerant of imatinib.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Response milestones, BCR::ABL1 monitoring, treatment-free remission and blast crisis management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35725593: [French Chronic Myeloid Leukemia Intergroup 2022 recommendations for managing the risk of cardiovascular events on ponatinib in chronic myeloid leukemia] (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36834713: Impact of <i>ABCG2</i> and <i>ABCB1</i> Polymorphisms on Imatinib Plasmatic Exposure: An Original Work and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37027248: Effect of Imatinib Mesylate on Growth in Pediatric Chronic Myeloid Leukemia: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37686630: Hematological Adverse Events with Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors for Chronic Myeloid Leukemia: A Systematic Review with Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 41054963: Expert consensus definition of treatment intolerance in chronic myeloid leukemia in chronic phase (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41486652: [Guideline for the diagnosis and treatment of chronic myeloid leukemia in China (2025)] (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `cmml` (topics 390, 391) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** European recommendations standardise CMML diagnosis, prognostication and treatment. For transplant candidates, EBMT advises upfront transplantation without prior disease-modifying therapy where possible, and hypomethylating agents decitabine and azacitidine appear comparably effective. ASXL1 mutation predicts poorer prognosis.

**Teaching points**
- International expert guidelines set standardised diagnostic and prognostic procedures and therapeutic choices for adult CMML. [PMID 31723789]
- EBMT recommends upfront transplantation without prior disease-modifying treatment, whenever possible, in CMML transplant candidates regardless of blast count. [PMID 38493484]
- Decitabine and azacitidine appear comparably effective and safe in CMML, but direct comparison needs a randomised trial. [PMID 33706667]
- In CMML, TET2 mutated with ASXL1 wild-type genotype was most favourable for survival; ASXL1 mutation raised hazard. [PMID 35306971]
- ASXL1 mutations are associated with poor prognosis in MDS and aid risk stratification. [PMID 27077763]
- SETBP1 mutation predicts poor prognosis in MDS and CMML but not CNL. [PMID 28158286]
- SRSF2 mutation predicted shorter survival in MDS but showed no prognostic effect in CMML. [PMID 29757120]

**Uncertainties**
- Hypomethylating agent comparative efficacy.

**MCQ angles**
- Upfront transplant in CMML.
- Prognostic mutations.

**Case hooks**
- Older patient with persistent monocytosis and cytopenias.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria (monocyte count thresholds), CPSS-Mol scoring and ruxolitinib or other therapies are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27077763: Prognostic significance of ASXL1 mutations in myelodysplastic syndromes and chronic myelomonocytic leukemia: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 28158286: Prognostic significance of SETBP1 mutations in myelodysplastic syndromes, chronic myelomonocytic leukemia, and chronic neutrophilic leukemia: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29757120: Prognostic significance of SRSF2 mutations in myelodysplastic syndromes and chronic myelomonocytic leukemia: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 31723789: Diagnosis and Treatment of Chronic Myelomonocytic Leukemias in Adults: Recommendations From the European Hematology Association and the European LeukemiaNet (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 33706667: Hypomethylating agents in the treatment of chronic myelomonocytic leukemia: a meta-analysis and systematic review (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35306971: The prognostic value of the interaction between ASXL1 and TET2 gene mutations in patients with chronic myelomonocytic leukemia: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 38493484: Management of adult patients with CMML undergoing allo-HCT: recommendations from the EBMT PH&G Committee (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `chronic_pancreatitis` (topics 392, 393) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 European guideline covers diagnosis and treatment of pancreatic exocrine insufficiency, and ASGE suggests endoscopic therapy over surgery for specified chronic pancreatitis complications. ESPEN advises screening and supplementing at-risk patients for nutrient deficiencies and acknowledging osteoporosis, and an international consensus standardises pain assessment.

**Teaching points**
- European guidelines summarise prevalence, clinical impact and diagnostic and therapeutic approaches for pancreatic exocrine insufficiency across clinical conditions. [PMID 39639485]
- ESPEN advises patients with chronic pancreatitis be considered at risk, screened and supplemented for deficiencies, with osteoporosis and fracture risk acknowledged and preventive measures considered. [PMID 38169174]
- ASGE suggests endoscopic therapy over surgery for the chronic pancreatitis indications addressed in its guideline. [PMID 39115496]
- An international consensus recommends standardised pain assessment in chronic pancreatitis together with quality of life and psychiatric comorbidities. [PMID 34391675]
- A clinical practice guideline covers acute and chronic pancreatitis, with uncomplicated long-term care by primary care and gastroenterology. [PMID 35945698]
- Gut microbiota in chronic pancreatitis and pancreatic cancer show fewer beneficial and more pathogenic species, with therapeutic implications unproven. [PMID 38847785]
- A consensus report defines fibrosis-related conditions in the pancreas, aiming to close knowledge gaps. [PMID 41689768]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal pain management strategies.

**MCQ angles**
- Endoscopic versus surgical therapy.
- Osteoporosis risk in chronic pancreatitis.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with chronic pain, steatorrhoea and pancreatic calcification.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Pancreatic enzyme dosing, diabetes management and ductal stone therapy details are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `csu` (topics 394) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The international EAACI/GA2LEN/EDF/WAO guideline (2018, updated 2022 and 2026) defines, classifies and manages urticaria. Higher pretreatment total IgE is associated with better omalizumab response, WAO defines CSU clinical remission as at least 6 months symptom-free without drugs, and head-to-head trials of H1-refractory therapies are lacking.

**Teaching points**
- The international urticaria guideline defines, classifies and sets evidence-based diagnostic and therapeutic approaches for urticaria subtypes, and has been updated in 2022 and 2026. [PMID 29336054, 36481045, 41649409]
- Pretreatment total serum IgE in CSU is associated with clinical response to omalizumab. [PMID 37263348]
- World Allergy Organization defines clinical remission of CSU as total resolution of signs and symptoms without pharmacotherapy for at least 6 months, a definition needing validation. [PMID 39732405]
- For H1 antihistamine-refractory CSU, high-quality head-to-head trials with harmonised outcomes are needed to inform guidelines. [PMID 34431983]
- Dupilumab has reports of effectiveness in several inflammatory skin diseases including chronic spontaneous urticaria. [PMID 37189381]
- A critical appraisal of urticaria guidelines found quality gains from rigorous GRADE rating, methodologist involvement and external review. [PMID 37451615]

**Uncertainties**
- Comparative efficacy of refractory CSU therapies.

**MCQ angles**
- IgE and omalizumab response.
- Definition of remission.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with daily wheals for 8 weeks despite standard antihistamine.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Antihistamine up-dosing, ciclosporin and weaning schedule are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29336054: The EAACI/GA²LEN/EDF/WAO guideline for the definition, classification, diagnosis and management of urticaria (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 34431983: Evaluation of Pharmacologic Treatments for H1 Antihistamine-Refractory Chronic Spontaneous Urticaria: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 36481045: Chronic spontaneous urticaria guidelines: What is new? (guideline, 2022)
