- PMID 37451615: Systematic Review and Critical Appraisal of Urticaria Clinical Practice Guidelines: A Global Guidelines in Dermatology Mapping Project (GUIDEMAP) (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39732405: Chronic spontaneous urticaria remission definition and therapy stepping down: World Allergy Organization position paper (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41649409: The International Guideline for the Definition, Classification, Diagnosis and Management of Urticaria (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `cteph` (topics 395) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CTEPH should be managed in an experienced multidisciplinary centre combining pulmonary endarterectomy, balloon pulmonary angioplasty and drugs. BPA is safe and effective in selected patients, with mostly mild complications, and appears to improve exercise capacity earlier than PEA while PEA may achieve better haemodynamics at 12 months.

**Teaching points**
- CTEPH patients should be treated in a multidisciplinary centre experienced in pulmonary endarterectomy, balloon pulmonary angioplasty and pharmacological therapy. [PMID 38531395]
- Meta-analysis shows BPA is a safe and effective option in select CTEPH patients with improved haemodynamics and exercise tolerance and low major complication rates. [PMID 36474104]
- BPA complications occurred in 14% of procedures and were mostly mild, with more thoracic complications when mean PAP exceeded 45 mmHg. [PMID 39124580]
- In comparison, BPA may give greater early exercise capacity improvement than PEA, while PEA may be superior for haemodynamics at 12 months. [PMID 33544306]
- A clinical consensus statement on BPA companions the ESC/ERS guidelines and standardises complication definitions. [PMID 37470202]
- Cologne consensus update reserves PEA and BPA for specialised centres, with inoperable patients candidates for targeted drug therapy and BPA. [PMID 30195840]
- After venous thromboembolism therapy, patients should be reassessed for symptoms of post-thrombotic syndrome or CTEPH. [PMID 38412982]
- A CTEPH consensus statement is a literature review with expert opinion because comparative trials are limited. [PMID 34420851]

**Uncertainties**
- Head-to-head BPA versus PEA trials are limited.

**MCQ angles**
- BPA versus PEA.
- Follow-up after PE.

**Case hooks**
- Persistent dyspnoea six months after pulmonary embolism.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** V/Q scanning for diagnosis, riociguat and anticoagulation duration are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30195840: Chronic thromboembolic pulmonary hypertension (CTEPH): Updated Recommendations from the Cologne Consensus Conference 2018 (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 33544306: Balloon pulmonary angioplasty vs. pulmonary endarterectomy in patients with chronic thromboembolic pulmonary hypertension: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34420851: Evaluation and management of patients with chronic thromboembolic pulmonary hypertension - consensus statement from the ISHLT (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 36474104: Balloon Pulmonary Angioplasty for Chronic Thromboembolic Pulmonary Hypertension: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37470202: Balloon pulmonary angioplasty for chronic thromboembolic pulmonary hypertension: a clinical consensus statement of the ESC working group on pulmonary circulation and right ventricular function (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38412982: [Venous thromboembolism - was is new in the revised AWMF guideline?] (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38531395: Post-Pulmonary Embolism Syndrome: An Update Based on the Revised AWMF-S2k Guideline (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39124580: Complications of Balloon Pulmonary Angioplasty: A Comprehensive Analysis Based on the Latest ESC Consensus Statement (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `tophaceous_gout` (topics 396) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2020 ACR guideline uses GRADE and patient preferences, and ACP and EULAR recommend discussing urate-lowering therapy with a predefined urate target in recurrent gout. Guideline inconsistencies exist across guidelines, and gout in CKD has important knowledge gaps.

**Teaching points**
- The 2020 ACR gout guideline uses GRADE methodology and patient preferences to direct clinicians on gout management. [PMID 32391934, 32390306]
- ACP strongly recommends discussing benefits, harms, costs and preferences before starting urate-lowering therapy with concomitant prophylaxis in recurrent attacks (moderate-quality evidence). [PMID 27802508]
- EULAR recommendations aim to reach a predefined urate target using non-pharmacological and pharmacological treatment. [PMID 27457514]
- A systematic review of guidelines found recommendation inconsistencies in hyperuricaemia and gout, and calls for standard methods and higher-quality evidence. [PMID 31446403]
- A G-CAN statement identifies research gaps in the safety and efficacy of gout drugs in CKD. [PMID 34331037]
- The Asia-Pacific League guideline formulates recommendations for clinical scenarios including renal impairment and non-pharmacological interventions. [PMID 34931463]
- A Taiwan multidisciplinary consensus manages gout and hyperuricaemia according to local epidemiology. [PMID 29363262]

**Uncertainties**
- Gout management in CKD.

**MCQ angles**
- Treat-to-target urate.
- Prophylaxis when starting urate-lowering therapy.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with multiple tophi and frequent flares.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific urate targets, allopurinol HLA-B*5801 testing, pegloticase and flare management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `cvi` (topics 397) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Venous guidelines weigh ablation techniques against compression, and compliance with graduated compression is low because of discomfort, cost and psychosocial issues. Emerging pharmacological options have inconsistent trial quality, and an international consensus standardises compression terminology.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review supporting SVS/AVF/AVLS guidelines compares ablation techniques and notes recommendations must consider patient values, anatomy and surgeon expertise. [PMID 34450355]
- Low compliance with graduated compression for CVI is driven by discomfort, financial and psychosocial issues, and better compliance could reduce disease burden. [PMID 38522455]
- Emerging pharmacological interventions for CVI are limited by methodological inconsistencies and need standardised trials. [PMID 39861707]
- A VIVA consensus covers patient selection, imaging, stent technique and aftercare for nonthrombotic iliac vein lesions. [PMID 39034930]
- An international consensus defines compression therapy terminology for precise classification of materials and devices. [PMID 39905644]
- Intermittent pneumatic compression is safe and effective when correctly indicated and used, including as add-on therapy for edema. [PMID 29951853]
- A ceramide-containing moisturiser improved CVI-related leg xerosis and dermatitis alongside compression. [PMID 38306139]

**Uncertainties**
- Drug therapy evidence.

**MCQ angles**
- Compression adherence.
- Iliac vein stenting.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with varicose veins, oedema and skin changes.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Compression strength, indications for venous ulcer healing and duplex criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29951853: [S1 guideline on intermittent pneumatic compression (IPC)] (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 34450355: A systematic review supporting the Society for Vascular Surgery, the American Venous Forum, and the American Vein and Lymphatic Society guidelines on the management of varicose veins (guideline, 2022)

## Cluster `cirrhosis` (topics 398) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Carvedilol reduced hepatic venous pressure gradient more than propranolol and lowered variceal bleeding risk in a systematic review. Ascites has substantial burden with NSBBs, diuretics and procedures offering benefit, and elastography accurately stages fibrosis. Other retrieved papers were off topic.

**Teaching points**
- Carvedilol reduced hepatic venous pressure gradient more than propranolol and decreased variceal bleeding risk in cirrhosis (seven studies, 533 patients). [PMID 37577269]
- Ascites carries substantial burden, and non-selective beta-blockers, diuretics and advanced procedures help control it and prevent decompensation. [PMID 40387194]
- 2-D shear wave elastography and point shear wave elastography have high sensitivity and specificity for fibrosis stages, with 2-D SWE more sensitive for significant and advanced fibrosis. [PMID 33342618]
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria address radiologic management of portal hypertension. [PMID 33958110]
- Cardiovascular assessment and monitoring around liver transplant reduce CVD complications. [PMID 40523024]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal beta-blocker choice by outcome.

**MCQ angles**
- Carvedilol versus propranolol.
- Elastography.

**Case hooks**
- Cirrhotic with large varices and ascites.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Variceal screening, TIPS, spontaneous bacterial peritonitis, hepatorenal syndrome and hepatic encephalopathy are not covered by the retrieved excerpts; a guideline source (AASLD, Baveno) is needed.

**Sources**
- PMID 33342618: Comparison of 2-D Shear Wave Elastography and Point Shear Wave Elastography for Assessing Liver Fibrosis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 33958110: ACR Appropriateness Criteria® Radiologic Management of Portal Hypertension (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 37577269: The Efficacy of Carvedilol in Comparison to Propranolol in Reducing the Hepatic Venous Pressure Gradient and Decreasing the Risk of Variceal Bleeding in Adult Cirrhotic Patients: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 40387194: The burden of ascites in cirrhosis (review, 2025)
- PMID 40523024: Perioperative cardiovascular assessment and management of liver transplant patients (review, 2025)

## Cluster `ckd_cv` (topics 400) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** KDIGO 2021 BP guidance recommends standardised measurement and a target systolic below 120 mm Hg (differing from some guidelines), while KDOQI notes a weak evidence base for diabetes and advanced CKD. KDIGO 2024 CKD guidance includes SGLT2 inhibitors for some patients and statins for adults over 50 with CKD, with UK lipid and diabetic kidney disease guidance.

**Teaching points**
- KDIGO 2021 BP guidance for CKD recommends standardised BP measurement and a target systolic BP below 120 mm Hg, differing from some other guidelines. [PMID 34152826, 33637203]
- KDOQI agreed with most KDIGO BP recommendations but highlighted the weak evidence base, especially for diabetes and advanced CKD. [PMID 35063302]
- The KDIGO CKD guideline synopsis recommends SGLT2 inhibitors for some patients with CKD with and without diabetes, and statins for adults older than 50 with CKD. [PMID 40063957]
- The KDIGO 2022 diabetes in CKD guideline covers GLP-1 receptor agonists, nonsteroidal MRAs and control of BP, glycaemia and lipids. [PMID 36623286]
- A UK guideline covers management of hypertension and RAAS blockade in diabetic kidney disease across CKD stages. [PMID 34979961]
- A UK practical guideline provides lipid management recommendations for adults with diabetic kidney disease. [PMID 38971750]
- A living guideline covers cardiovascular, kidney and weight effects of type 2 diabetes therapeutics, noting cost and availability limits. [PMID 40813129]

**Uncertainties**
- Intensive BP targets in diabetes and advanced CKD.

**MCQ angles**
- KDIGO systolic target.
- Statins in CKD over 50.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with CKD stage 3 and diabetes and BP 140/85.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Anaemia, vascular calcification and dialysis-specific cardiovascular management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33637203: Executive summary of the KDIGO 2021 Clinical Practice Guideline for the Management of Blood Pressure in Chronic Kidney Disease (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34152826: Management of Blood Pressure in Patients With Chronic Kidney Disease Not Receiving Dialysis: Synopsis of the 2021 KDIGO Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34979961: Management of hypertension and renin-angiotensin-aldosterone system blockade in adults with diabetic kidney disease: Association of British Clinical Diabetologists and the Renal Association UK guideline update 2021 (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 35063302: KDOQI US Commentary on the 2021 KDIGO Clinical Practice Guideline for the Management of Blood Pressure in CKD (guideline, 2022)

## Cluster `ckd_mbd` (topics 401, 402) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** KDIGO 2017 bases CKD-MBD treatment on trends in laboratory values rather than single abnormal results and urges caution to avoid hypercalcaemia when treating secondary hyperparathyroidism. A 2025 KDIGO controversies conference stresses personalised care given bone and cardiovascular manifestations, and vitamin D evidence is inconsistent.

**Teaching points**
- The KDIGO CKD-MBD update (G3a to G5 and dialysis) bases treatment on trends in laboratory values rather than a single result and cautions against hypercalcaemia when treating secondary hyperparathyroidism. [PMID 29459980, 28646995]
- A 2025 KDIGO controversies conference concluded that complex bone and cardiovascular manifestations, including valvular calcification and LV hypertrophy, require personalised management. [PMID 39864017]
- KDOQI commentary on the 2017 KDIGO update discusses CKD-MBD diagnosis, prevention, and treatment of abnormal PTH for the US nephrology community. [PMID 28941764]
- Vitamin D supplementation effect in CKD is inconsistent across studies; calcifediol and analogues suppress PTH, but FGF23 rise with calcitriol analogues warrants caution. [PMID 33895867]
- A consensus on nutritional vitamin D in CKD-MBD advises avoiding excess levels to reduce toxicity risk and calls for outcome trials. [PMID 39875204]
- A European consensus recommends calcium intake kept within the age-appropriate normal range in adults and children with CKD, based on limited evidence. [PMID 37697718]

**Uncertainties**
- Patient-relevant outcomes of vitamin D supplementation.

**MCQ angles**
- Trend-based treatment decisions.
- Caution with calcitriol analogues.

**Case hooks**
- Dialysis patient with rising PTH and high phosphate.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Phosphate binders, calcimimetics, target ranges and bone biopsy use are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28646995: Executive summary of the 2017 KDIGO Chronic Kidney Disease-Mineral and Bone Disorder (CKD-MBD) Guideline Update: what's changed and why it matters (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 28941764: KDOQI US Commentary on the 2017 KDIGO Clinical Practice Guideline Update for the Diagnosis, Evaluation, Prevention, and Treatment of Chronic Kidney Disease-Mineral and Bone Disorder (CKD-MBD) (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 29459980: Diagnosis, Evaluation, Prevention, and Treatment of Chronic Kidney Disease-Mineral and Bone Disorder: Synopsis of the Kidney Disease: Improving Global Outcomes 2017 Clinical Practice Guideline Update (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 33895867: Vitamin D Supplementation for Patients with Chronic Kidney Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-analyses of Trials Investigating the Response to Supplementation and an Overview of Guidelines (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 37697718: Recommended calcium intake in adults and children with chronic kidney disease-a European consensus statement (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39864017: Chronic kidney disease-mineral and bone disorder: conclusions from a Kidney Disease: Improving Global Outcomes (KDIGO) Controversies Conference (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 39875204: The role of nutritional vitamin D in chronic kidney disease-mineral and bone disorder in children and adults with chronic kidney disease, on dialysis, and after kidney transplantation-a European consensus statement (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `class1` (topics 404) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence on class I antiarrhythmics is limited. A Cochrane review found all analysed drugs maintained sinus rhythm after cardioversion but increased withdrawals for adverse effects, flecainide may beat digoxin as first-line fetal SVT therapy, and guideline-concordant antiarrhythmic use in AF is high but variable. The ESC 2024 AF guideline provides current recommendations.

**Teaching points**
- A Cochrane review of antiarrhythmics for maintaining sinus rhythm after AF cardioversion found all analysed drugs increased withdrawals due to adverse effects compared with placebo or no treatment. [PMID 31483500, 25820938]
- Flecainide may be more effective than digoxin as first-line transplacental treatment of fetal supraventricular tachycardia, with no difference in fetal demise. [PMID 29246961]
- Guideline-concordant antiarrhythmic drug use in the Get With The Guidelines-AF registry was high but varied by drug, hospital and region. [PMID 33419385]
- The 2024 ESC atrial fibrillation guidelines further develop earlier guidance with practical, detailed recommendations. [PMID 39604644]
- In LQT3 with an SCN5A variant disrupting the lidocaine/mexiletine binding site, phenytoin may be an alternative therapeutic. [PMID 36731785]
- An expert consensus on J-wave syndromes elevates substrate ablation as a therapeutic advance. [PMID 41738055]

**Uncertainties**
- Comparative efficacy of individual class I drugs.

**MCQ angles**
- Adverse-effect withdrawals.
- Fetal SVT therapy.

**Case hooks**
- Young patient with paroxysmal AF and structurally normal heart.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Contraindications in structural or ischaemic heart disease (CAST), pill-in-the-pocket use and QRS monitoring are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 25820938: Antiarrhythmics for maintaining sinus rhythm after cardioversion of atrial fibrillation (meta-analysis, 2015)
- PMID 29246961: First-Line Antiarrhythmic Transplacental Treatment for Fetal Tachyarrhythmia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31483500: Antiarrhythmics for maintaining sinus rhythm after cardioversion of atrial fibrillation (systematic review, 2019)
- PMID 33419385: Guideline-Concordant Antiarrhythmic Drug Use in the Get With The Guidelines-Atrial Fibrillation Registry (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 36731785: Functional characterization and identification of a therapeutic for a novel SCN5A-F1760C variant causing type 3 long QT syndrome refractory to all guideline-directed therapies (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39604644: [European Society of Cardiology (ESC) guidelines on atrial fibrillation 2024 : What is new and what is important?] (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41738055: Contemporary Perspectives on J-Wave Syndromes: An Expert Consensus Statement (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `claudication` (topics 405) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 SVS guideline on intermittent claudication stresses patient-centred assessment of risk, benefit, impairment and durability. Structured home exercise programmes are recommended when supervised exercise is unavailable, paclitaxel-coated balloons may raise amputation risk, and symptom presentation differs between sexes.

**Teaching points**
- The SVS clinical practice guideline on intermittent claudication calls for patient-centred assessment of risk, benefit, degree of impairment and treatment durability. [PMID 40316185]
- When supervised exercise is unavailable, structured home-based exercise programmes incorporating behaviour change elements are recommended. [PMID 34087396]
- Meta-analysis suggests heightened risk of major amputation after paclitaxel-coated balloons in peripheral arteries, though certainty is low to moderate. [PMID 34326002]
- Cilostazol, a phosphodiesterase-3 inhibitor, has implications for atherosclerosis and vascular biology in high-risk patients. [PMID 38473840]
- PAD symptom presentation differs between women and men, with women more often having atypical leg symptoms. [PMID 35248439]

**Uncertainties**
- Paclitaxel device safety.

**MCQ angles**
- Home exercise when SET is unavailable.
- Atypical PAD presentation in women.

**Case hooks**
- Calf pain on walking relieved by rest.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** ABI diagnosis, antiplatelet and statin therapy, and revascularisation indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 34087396: An updated systematic review and meta-analysis of home-based exercise programs for individuals with intermittent claudication (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34326002: Editor's Choice - Risk of Major Amputation Following Application of Paclitaxel Coated Balloons in the Lower Limb Arteries: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomised Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35248439: Differences in Symptom Presentation in Women and Men with Confirmed Lower Limb Peripheral Artery Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 38473840: The Role of Cilostazol, a Phosphodiesterase-3 Inhibitor, in the Development of Atherosclerosis and Vascular Biology: A Review with Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `minor_stroke_dapt` (topics 406) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Short-term dual antiplatelet therapy started within 24 hours of high-risk TIA or minor stroke reduces recurrent stroke compared with aspirin alone, at the cost of more major bleeding. Long-term combination offers no extra safety, and CYP2C19 loss-of-function carriers do worse on clopidogrel.

**Teaching points**
- Aspirin plus clopidogrel started within 24 hours of high-risk TIA or mild-moderate ischaemic stroke reduces recurrent stroke compared with aspirin alone, at the expense of higher major bleeding risk. [PMID 33902301]
- For patients not receiving intravenous alteplase, DAPT started within 24 hours and continued up to 21 days, followed by antiplatelet monotherapy, reduces recurrent ischaemic stroke for up to 90 days. [PMID 36313610]
- Short-term aspirin plus clopidogrel is more effective and equally safe compared with aspirin alone, while excess all-cause mortality was limited to long-term combination therapy. [PMID 30852971, 30511260]
- A 2024 meta-analysis found aspirin plus clopidogrel reduces stroke recurrence and MACE but increases moderate or severe bleeding. [PMID 39164077]
- Ticagrelor or clopidogrel combined with aspirin was superior to aspirin alone in minor stroke, with no statistically significant difference between the two regimens. [PMID 34870698]
- Carriers of CYP2C19 loss-of-function alleles have higher risk of stroke and composite vascular events on clopidogrel, with an association seen in non-East Asian patients. [PMID 27806998, 38038819]

**Uncertainties**
- Genotype-guided therapy outside East Asian populations.

**MCQ angles**
- Duration of DAPT after minor stroke.
- CYP2C19 and clopidogrel.

**Case hooks**
- NIHSS 2 stroke presenting within 12 hours.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** CHANCE and POINT trial specifics, ABCD2 criteria and loading doses are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27806998: Genetic Polymorphisms and Clopidogrel Efficacy for Acute Ischemic Stroke or Transient Ischemic Attack: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 30511260: Clopidogrel and aspirin after ischemic stroke or transient ischemic attack: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized clinical trials (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 30852971: Optimal Duration of Aspirin Plus Clopidogrel After Ischemic Stroke or Transient Ischemic Attack (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 33902301: Dual Antiplatelet Therapy Versus Aspirin in Patients With Stroke or Transient Ischemic Attack: Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34870698: Comparison of Ticagrelor vs Clopidogrel in Addition to Aspirin in Patients With Minor Ischemic Stroke and Transient Ischemic Attack: A Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36313610: Dual Antiplatelet Therapy in the Management of Acute Minor Ischemic Stroke and High-Risk Transient Ischemic Attack: An Expert Consensus Statement From Taiwan Stroke Society and Taiwan Society of Emergency Medicine (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 38038819: Impact of CYP2C19 Genotype on Efficacy and Safety of Clopidogrel-based Antiplatelet Therapy in Stroke or Transient Ischemic Attack Patients: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-analysis of Non-East Asian Studies (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39164077: Efficacy and safety of aspirin plus clopidogrel versus aspirin alone in ischemic stroke or high-risk transient ischemic attack: A meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `cdiff` (topics 407, 408) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** IDSA/SHEA guidance updates adult and paediatric C. difficile management. FMT is recommended as adjuvant therapy for select adults hospitalised with severe or fulminant infection not responding to antibiotics, and FMT in second recurrence is clearer, though timing in severe refractory disease needs study. Special populations have tailored consensus guidance.

**Teaching points**
- The AGA guideline suggests fecal microbiota-based therapy as adjuvant treatment for select adults hospitalised with severe or fulminant C. difficile not responding to standard antibiotics. [PMID 38395525]
- A review notes the role of FMT in second recurrence is clearer, but timing and mode in severe-complicated refractory disease require study. [PMID 36441203]
- IDSA/SHEA clinical practice guideline updates for adults and children use GRADE with online evidence summaries and knowledge gaps. [PMID 34164674, 34492699]
- Guidelines for children with cancer include isolation for CDI and minimising systemic antibacterials where feasible. [PMID 38680517]
- A consensus for haematological malignancy proposes definitions of proven, probable and possible CDI for research. [PMID 40823499]
- ASTCT guidance covers colonisation versus infection, diagnosis, therapy for episodic or recurrent infection and roles of prophylaxis, probiotics and FMT in transplant recipients. [PMID 35202891]
- A systematic review of CDI guidelines found heterogeneity, recommending developers address factors causing inconsistency. [PMID 36111240]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal FMT timing in severe disease.

**MCQ angles**
- FMT indications.
- Special populations.

**Case hooks**
- Second recurrence of C. difficile after vancomycin course.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Severity grading criteria, vancomycin versus fidaxomicin choice and bezlotoxumab are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `clozapine` (topics 409, 410) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Trial of clozapine is supported after two other antipsychotics have failed, and a Delphi guideline supports optimising clozapine monotherapy. Safety monitoring covers neutrophil counts and myocarditis, and pharmacogenetic CYP testing is individual rather than routine.

**Teaching points**
- Response rates in sequential antipsychotic trials support a clozapine trial after two other antipsychotics have been ineffective. [PMID 38493023]
- A Delphi consensus guideline supports clinical decision-making to optimise clozapine monotherapy in treatment-resistant schizophrenia. [PMID 36943247]
- Consensus guidance addresses absolute neutrophil count and adverse reaction monitoring in clozapine treatment, balancing safety with practicality. [PMID 40617246]
- A multidisciplinary consensus covers prevention, screening, monitoring and rechallenge for clozapine-associated myocarditis. [PMID 40452212]
- The Dutch Pharmacogenetics Working Group states CYP2D6 or CYP3A4 genotyping before clozapine or olanzapine should be on an individual patient basis only. [PMID 37002327]
- The VA/DoD suicide risk guideline notes clozapine has a role in managing suicide risk in schizophrenia. [PMID 39903866]
- A network meta-analysis found antipsychotic cognitive effects relate to fewer positive symptoms rather than genuine cognitive improvement. [PMID 39412783]

**Uncertainties**
- Rechallenge after myocarditis.

**MCQ angles**
- Clozapine after two failed antipsychotics.
- Neutrophil monitoring.

**Case hooks**
- Schizophrenia unresponsive to two antipsychotic trials.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Titration schedule, constipation and sialorrhoea management, and the exact myocarditis screening schedule are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

- PMID 37002327: Dutch Pharmacogenetics Working Group (DPWG) guideline for the gene-drug interaction between CYP2D6, CYP3A4 and CYP1A2 and antipsychotics (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `cluster_headache` (topics 411, 412) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** European Academy of Neurology guidelines recommend greater occipital nerve block while electrical GON stimulation is not recommended, and non-invasive vagus nerve stimulation helps episodic but not chronic cluster headache. Verapamil and lithium need monitoring, and occipital nerve stimulation may be the first strategy in refractory chronic disease.

**Teaching points**
- EAN guidelines recommend greater occipital nerve block, recommend against electrical stimulation of the greater occipital nerve because of side effects, and find vagus nerve stimulation efficacious in episodic but not chronic cluster headache. [PMID 37515405]
- The AHS guidelines note lack of Class I evidence and Level A recommendations for many preventive therapies. [PMID 27432623]
- Close follow-up is essential when using verapamil and lithium, and novel targeted preventives are an unmet need. [PMID 37667192]
- A meta-analysis suggests occipital nerve stimulation could be the first therapeutic strategy for refractory chronic cluster headache. [PMID 36310189]
- A genome-wide meta-analysis identified eight loci and indicates smoking is a causal risk factor for cluster headache. [PMID 37486023]
- Cluster headache and migraine share circadian features implicating the hypothalamus. [PMID 36990725]
- Studies on alcohol and primary headaches are low quality, and alcohol-headache myths should be avoided. [PMID 37612595]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence for common preventives is weak.

**MCQ angles**
- GON block.
- Verapamil monitoring.

**Case hooks**
- Severe unilateral periorbital pain with autonomic features at night.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** High-flow oxygen and triptan acute treatment details, verapamil ECG monitoring thresholds and prednisolone bridging are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27432623: Treatment of Cluster Headache: The American Headache Society Evidence-Based Guidelines (guideline, 2016)
- PMID 36310189: Preventive treatment of refractory chronic cluster headache: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 36990725: Circadian Features of Cluster Headache and Migraine: A Systematic Review, Meta-analysis, and Genetic Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37486023: Cluster Headache Genomewide Association Study and Meta-Analysis Identifies Eight Loci and Implicates Smoking as Causal Risk Factor (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37515405: European Academy of Neurology guidelines on the treatment of cluster headache (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 37612595: Relationship between alcohol and primary headaches: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37667192: Current treatment options for cluster headache: limitations and the unmet need for better and specific treatments-a consensus article (guideline, 2023)

## Cluster `cmv_disease` (topics 413, 494) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 ASTCT and ECIL-10 guidelines update CMV management, including CAR T-cell and bispecific antibody recipients, and maribavir is second-line therapy after first-line failure. In solid organ transplant, valganciclovir prevents CMV disease and is comparable to oral ganciclovir.

**Teaching points**
- ASTCT updated CMV guidelines cover new antiviral treatments, expanded prevention indications and special populations such as CAR T-cell and paediatric recipients. [PMID 40581305]
- ECIL-10 recommendations add categories for patients treated with CAR T cells or bispecific T-cell-engaging antibodies. [PMID 40188837]
- Oral maribavir is recommended as second-line antiviral therapy for refractory CMV after first-line therapy in allogeneic HSCT and solid organ recipients. [PMID 40709451]
- Antiviral prophylaxis prevents CMV disease in solid organ transplant recipients, with valganciclovir or IV ganciclovir showing little difference from oral ganciclovir. [PMID 38700045]
- Korean guidelines on CMV prevention in solid organ transplant note insufficient evidence to recommend CMV-specific cell-mediated immunity or viral load kinetics monitoring. [PMID 38527780]
- A Taiwan consensus covers prevention and management of CMV in kidney transplant, including CMV immunoglobulin for refractory disease. [PMID 38777672]
- International consensus guidelines cover diagnosis and management of CMV retinitis, with novel antivirals and immunotherapy as second-line options. [PMID 41038280]
- Lung transplant follow-up care is built on tacrolimus-based triple immunosuppression, infection prophylaxis, CLAD prevention and bone protection. [PMID 42301957]

**Uncertainties**
- Immune monitoring to guide prophylaxis.

**MCQ angles**
- Maribavir.
- Prophylaxis in SOT.

**Case hooks**
- Transplant recipient with fever, leukopenia and rising CMV viral load.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Letermovir indications, valganciclovir dosing with renal function and CMV colitis or pneumonitis diagnosis are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38527780: Prevention of Cytomegalovirus Infection in Solid Organ Transplant Recipients: Guidelines by the Korean Society of Infectious Diseases and the Korean Society for Transplantation (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38700045: Antiviral medications for preventing cytomegalovirus disease in solid organ transplant recipients (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38777672: Prevention and management of cytomegalovirus infection and disease in kidney transplant: A consensus statement of the Transplantation Society of Taiwan (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40188837: Recommendations from the 10th European Conference on Infections in Leukaemia for the management of cytomegalovirus in patients after allogeneic haematopoietic cell transplantation and other T-cell-engaging therapies (guideline, 2025)

- PMID 40709451: Management of Cytomegalovirus Infection in Allogeneic Hematopoietic Stem Cell and in Solid Organ Transplantation: Updated Recommendations by the GITMO, SITO, SIMIT, and AMCLI Italian Societies (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `coag_disorders` (topics 414) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The ISTH guideline covers treatment of congenital haemophilia with gene therapy and non-factor therapies planned for update. Consensus supports prophylaxis in non-severe haemophilia with a severe bleeding phenotype, acquired von Willebrand syndrome occurs on ECMO, and aortic valve replacement resolves bleeding in Heyde syndrome.

**Teaching points**
- The ISTH guideline on congenital haemophilia treatment will be updated to include new FVIII and FIX concentrates, nonfactor therapies and gene therapy. [PMID 39043543]
- A Delphi consensus defines a severe bleeding phenotype and recommends initiation of prophylaxis in non-severe haemophilia with risk factors for severe bleeding or arthropathy. [PMID 41879621]
- A comprehensive care pathway for haemophilia gene therapy defines contact moments and responsibilities across treatment. [PMID 41161417]
- Acquired von Willebrand syndrome occurs during ECMO, but its contribution to bleeding is uncertain and evidence for VWF concentrates is limited. [PMID 38925492]
- Optimal monitoring and replacement parameters for antithrombin, fibrinogen and von Willebrand factor in paediatric ECMO are largely unknown. [PMID 38959358]
- Aortic valve replacement is associated with rapid recovery of the bleeding diathesis and GI bleeding cessation in Heyde syndrome, with residual valve disease compromising benefit. [PMID 37555393]
- ASH/ISTH/NBDF/WFH monitor the 2021 VWD guideline for new evidence warranting revision. [PMID 40273329]
- A survey shows variation worldwide in recognition and management of bleeding disorders of unknown cause, calling for consensus pathways. [PMID 38866246]

**Uncertainties**
- Bleeding disorders of unknown cause.

**MCQ angles**
- Heyde syndrome treatment.
- Prophylaxis in non-severe haemophilia.

**Case hooks**
- Elderly patient with aortic stenosis and recurrent GI bleeding.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Screening tests (PT, aPTT), differentiating factor deficiency from inhibitors, DIC and thrombotic disorders are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37555393: Effectiveness of aortic valve replacement in Heyde syndrome: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38866246: Examining variability in the diagnosis and management of people with bleeding disorders of unknown cause: communication from the ISTH SSC Subcommittee on von Willebrand Factor (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38925492: Acquired von Willebrand syndrome during extracorporeal membrane oxygenation support: a comprehensive review of current evidence: communication from the ISTH SSC on perioperative and critical care thrombosis and hemostasis (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38959358: Recommendations on Monitoring and Replacement of Antithrombin, Fibrinogen, and Von Willebrand Factor in Pediatric Patients on Extracorporeal Membrane Oxygenation: The Pediatric Extracorporeal Membrane Oxygenation Anticoagulation CollaborativE Consensus Conference (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39043543: International Society on Thrombosis and Haemostasis clinical practice guideline for treatment of congenital hemophilia A and B based on the Grading of Recommendations Assessment, Development, and Evaluation methodology (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40273329: 2025 ASH ISTH NBDF WFH monitoring report on the 2021 clinical guidelines on the diagnosis and management of von Willebrand disease (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41161417: A comprehensive care pathway of gene therapy for hemophilia based on current guideline documents and summary of product characteristics: communication from the ISTH SSC working group on gene therapy (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41879621: Delphi Consensus Recommendations for the Definition of a Severe Bleeding Phenotype and Initiation of Prophylaxis in Patients With Non-Severe Haemophilia (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `cwp` (topics 415) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence is epidemiological: global CWP prevalence has trended down over 60 years after a 1990s rebound, and prevention and dust control matter most in developing countries. Silica exposure raises tuberculosis risk, so exposed workers merit both silicosis and TB screening.

**Teaching points**
- Global prevalence of coal worker's pneumoconiosis rebounded in the 1990s but the overall trend is downward over 60 years, with prevention emphasised in developing countries. [PMID 35836046]
- Studies of CWP prevalence, mortality and survival are mostly from the United States, limiting understanding elsewhere, and support surveillance and dust control. [PMID 34343220]
- Silicosis and silica exposure raise tuberculosis risk, so silica-exposed workers such as miners should be screened for both. [PMID 37349198]
- Coal mine workers have increased mortality from non-malignant respiratory disease but decreased prostate cancer and cardiovascular mortality, requiring long follow-up. [PMID 34782367]
- Occupational pneumoconiosis incidence was 0.093 in a pooled analysis with seven associated factors. [PMID 36858466]
- A review pooled prevalence and levels of occupational exposure to silica, asbestos and coal dusts across regions and sectors. [PMID 37487377]

**Uncertainties**
- Burden outside the United States.

**MCQ angles**
- Silica and tuberculosis.
- Dust control prevention.

**Case hooks**
- Retired coal miner with progressive dyspnoea and nodular chest radiograph.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Radiographic ILO classification, progressive massive fibrosis, black lung compensation and management are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 34343220: A systematic review and meta-analysis on international studies of prevalence, mortality and survival due to coal mine dust lung disease (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34782367: Cancer and mortality in coal mine workers: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35836046: Prevalence of coal worker's pneumoconiosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36858466: Incidence and influencing factors of occupational pneumoconiosis: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37349198: Silicosis and tuberculosis: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 37487377: The prevalences and levels of occupational exposure to dusts and/or fibres (silica, asbestos and coal): A systematic review and meta-analysis from the WHO/ILO Joint Estimates of the Work-related Burden of Disease and Injury (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `syncope` (topics 417, 418) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Vasovagal syncope is highly prevalent. ED risk stratification tools have low-quality evidence, with only SFSR, CSRS and OESIL validated in more than two studies. Cardioneuroablation has a scientific statement and meta-analysis, SSRIs may help prevent vasovagal syncope, and dedicated syncope units operate to guideline-based standards.

**Teaching points**
- Clinical decision rules for ED adult syncope have low-quality evidence for routine use, and only the SFSR, CSRS and OESIL have more than two validating studies. [PMID 39496561]
- Vasovagal syncope has a high global prevalence requiring preventive, diagnostic and therapeutic measures. [PMID 38283939]
- A scientific statement on cardioneuroablation for reflex syncope stresses careful patient selection and technique for safety and effectiveness. [PMID 39082698]
- Cardioneuroablation meta-analysis found efficacy and safety against recurrence in vasovagal syncope, with some mapping methods and right atrial approach linked to higher recurrence. [PMID 39067734]
- Serotonin-specific reuptake inhibitors may prevent tilt-induced and community syncope in randomised, double-blind trials. [PMID 37971641]
- Paediatric guidelines address diagnosis and non-pharmacological therapy for neurally mediated syncope in children and adolescents. [PMID 39110332]
- Syncope units in the Netherlands largely met the guideline-based SU-19 score, with neurology and cardiology most involved and non-physicians important. [PMID 38190741]
- Canadian guidelines assist decisions on cardiac implantable electronic device implantation. [PMID 41539743]

**Uncertainties**
- Cardioneuroablation technique standardisation.

**MCQ angles**
- Validated syncope decision rules.
- SSRIs and vasovagal syncope.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult fainting on standing from a hot bath.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Red flags for cardiac syncope, orthostatic hypotension management and tilt testing indications are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37971641: Serotonin reuptake inhibition for the prevention of vasovagal syncope: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38190741: A cross-sectional nationwide survey of guideline based syncope units in the Netherlands: the SU-19 score-a novel validation for best practices (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38283939: Global prevalence of vasovagal syncope: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39067734: Cardioneuroablation in patients with vasovagal syncope: An updated systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 39082698: Cardioneuroablation for the treatment of reflex syncope and functional bradyarrhythmias: A Scientific Statement of the European Heart Rhythm Association (EHRA) of the ESC, the Heart Rhythm Society (HRS), the Asia Pacific Heart Rhythm Society (APHRS) and the Latin American Heart Rhythm Society (LAHRS) (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39110332: Guidelines for the diagnosis and treatment of neurally mediated syncope in children and adolescents (revised 2024) (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39496561: Risk-stratification tools for emergency department patients with syncope: A systematic review and meta-analysis of direct evidence for SAEM GRACE (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41539743: The Canadian Cardiovascular Society/Canadian Heart Rhythm Society Comprehensive Guidelines for the Selection, Implantation, and Management of Patients With Cardiac Implantable Electronic Devices (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `volvulus` (topics 419) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** WSES consensus guidelines and ASGE guidance cover sigmoid volvulus, with endoscopic decompression and then resection. For gangrenous sigmoid volvulus there is no significant mortality difference between resection with primary anastomosis and Hartmann's procedure, so choice is individualised. PEC suits frail elderly patients, and Hirschsprung disease should be excluded in younger patients.

**Teaching points**
- The WSES consensus guidelines propose management of sigmoid volvulus, including endoscopic decompression and upfront colectomy options. [PMID 37189134]
- ASGE guidance updates evaluation and endoscopic management of sigmoid volvulus and acute colonic pseudo-obstruction. [PMID 31791596]
- For gangrenous sigmoid volvulus, resection with primary anastomosis and Hartmann's procedure showed no significant mortality or morbidity difference, so choice is individualised. [PMID 38784481]
- Percutaneous endoscopic colostomy has acceptable mortality compared with 6.6-44% mortality with operative intervention, supporting use in frail elderly patients. [PMID 27556086]
- Best PEC outcomes were seen with two tubes left in situ indefinitely, and further safety study is needed. [PMID 32777932]
- Hirschsprung disease should be excluded with rectal biopsy when sigmoid volvulus occurs under age 40 with constipation since childhood. [PMID 32856883]
- Transverse colon volvulus is rare, usually diagnosed at laparotomy, and has high mortality compared with sigmoid and caecal volvulus. [PMID 34461758]
- Colonic complications of systemic sclerosis are under-reported and need diagnostic suspicion. [PMID 31805016]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal definitive surgery in gangrenous disease.

**MCQ angles**
- PEC in frail patients.
- Hirschsprung exclusion in young patients.

**Case hooks**
- Elderly institutionalised patient with abdominal distension and coffee-bean sign.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Radiographic signs, endoscopic detorsion success rates and recurrence after decompression are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27556086: Use of percutaneous endoscopic colostomy (PEC) to treat sigmoid volvulus: a systematic review (systematic review, 2016)
- PMID 31791596: American Society for Gastrointestinal Endoscopy guideline on the role of endoscopy in the management of acute colonic pseudo-obstruction and colonic volvulus (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 31805016: Colonic Manifestations and Complications Are Relatively Under-Reported in Systemic Sclerosis: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2019)
- PMID 32777932: Management of sigmoid volvulus using percutaneous endoscopic colostomy (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 32856883: Hirschsprung's Disease Complicated by Sigmoid Volvulus: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 34461758: Volvulus of the Transverse Colon (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 37189134: WSES consensus guidelines on sigmoid volvulus management (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38784481: Comparing Resection and Primary Anastomosis versus Hartmann's Stoma on the Mortality and Morbidity of Gangrenous Sigmoid Volvulus: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `crc_screening` (topics 422) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Colorectal cancer screening guidelines (ACS 2026, Austria, others) cover average-risk persons, requiring colonoscopy after a positive non-colonoscopy test within 6 months. Computer-assisted detection adds small absolute benefit with overdiagnosis burden.

**Teaching points**
- A positive result on any non-colonoscopy screening test needs timely follow-up colonoscopy, preferably within 6 months, to complete screening. [PMID 42200680]
- The Austrian recommendation applies only to asymptomatic, average-risk persons and requires a piloted programme with information campaign, quality assurance and data collection. [PMID 37256423]
- Computer-assisted detection colonoscopy gives small absolute benefits for CRC incidence and mortality, with more polyp overdiagnosis and surveillance colonoscopy burden. [PMID 40148135]
- Advances in screening include technological innovations and guideline discrepancies, moving towards precision-based individualised prevention. [PMID 41395603]
- In inflammatory bowel disease surveillance, low-certainty evidence suggests chromoendoscopy-type techniques detect more dysplastic lesions than high-definition white-light endoscopy. [PMID 39675405]
- Rural-urban disparities exist in CRC screening, diagnosis, treatment and survivorship care. [PMID 38243853]
- Population-based CRC screening in Latin America needs development, with a knowledge gap in lower middle-income countries. [PMID 38300621]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal screening age and interval are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**MCQ angles**
- Follow-up colonoscopy after positive FIT.
- Value of CADe.

**Case hooks**
- Asymptomatic 50-year-old with a positive FIT result.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** FIT threshold and interval, screening start age and surveillance after polypectomy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37256423: Screening for colorectal cancer : A recommendation statement of the Austrian National Committee for Cancer Screening (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38243853: Rural-Urban Disparities in Colorectal Cancer Screening, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Survivorship Care: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38300621: Colorectal Cancer Screening Programs in Latin America: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 39675405: Comparative Efficacy and Safety of Endoscopic Modalities for Colorectal Cancer Screening in Inflammatory Bowel Disease: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40148135: Use of computer-assisted detection (CADe) colonoscopy in colorectal cancer screening and surveillance: European Society of Gastrointestinal Endoscopy (ESGE) Position Statement (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41395603: Advances in colorectal cancer screening: technological innovations, guideline discrepancies, and individualized strategies (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42200680: Colorectal cancer screening: An update to the American Cancer Society guideline, 2026 (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `cvid` (topics 424) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CVID presents with infections plus immune dysregulation features including lymphoproliferation, GI disease and autoimmunity, which can be the first manifestation. Malignancy, mainly lymphoma, and bronchiectasis are important complications, and BSI/UKPIN consensus statements guide management including granulomatous-lymphocytic lung disease.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review shows CVID presents with infections plus immune dysregulation (lymphoproliferative, gastrointestinal, autoimmune), and screening for antibody deficiency in those with these manifestations may improve early detection. [PMID 33833753]
- Autoimmunity may be the first or only manifestation of CVID, so the co-occurrence can be a misleading clue. [PMID 33203275]
- Malignancy prevalence has risen in CVID with improved survival, lymphoma being most common, supporting malignancy screening. [PMID 31452405]
- Bronchiectasis prevalence in CVID is high and should be managed by controlling recurrent and severe pneumonia, being associated with poor prognosis. [PMID 31833673]
- BSI/UKPIN consensus on CVID management produced 22 recommendations and three research areas. [PMID 30724343]
- BLF/UKPIN consensus on granulomatous-lymphocytic interstitial lung disease supports corticosteroids alone as first-line treatment when required after optimising immunoglobulin. [PMID 28351785]
- Cancer incidence in inborn errors of immunity depends on molecular and pathway defect, supporting individualised screening. [PMID 39466473]

**Uncertainties**
- Lymphoid neoplasia predisposition mechanisms.

**MCQ angles**
- Autoimmunity as first presentation.
- Malignancy screening.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with recurrent sinopulmonary infections and ITP.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Diagnostic criteria (low IgG with poor vaccine response), immunoglobulin replacement dosing and trough targets are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28351785: British Lung Foundation/United Kingdom Primary Immunodeficiency Network Consensus Statement on the Definition, Diagnosis, and Management of Granulomatous-Lymphocytic Interstitial Lung Disease in Common Variable Immunodeficiency Disorders (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 30724343: British Society for Immunology/United Kingdom Primary Immunodeficiency Network consensus statement on managing non-infectious complications of common variable immunodeficiency disorders (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 31452405: Malignancy in common variable immunodeficiency: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 31833673: Bronchiectasis in common variable immunodeficiency: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 33203275: Autoimmunity in common variable immunodeficiency: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 33833753: Lessons Learned From the Clinical Presentation of Common Variable Immunodeficiency Disorders: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 39466473: Cancer Trends in Inborn Errors of Immunity: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `cap` (topics 425, 426, 427, 428, 429, 430, 431, 432) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The ATS 2026 guideline covers diagnosis and treatment of adult CAP including antibiotic duration and systemic corticosteroids. Meta-analyses suggest corticosteroids probably reduce short-term mortality and need for mechanical ventilation in hospitalised CAP, especially severe, and guideline-concordant therapy is linked to lower mortality.

**Teaching points**
- The ATS guideline gives recommendations on selected diagnostic and treatment strategies for adult CAP, including antibiotic duration and systemic corticosteroids. [PMID 40679934]
- Corticosteroids probably reduce short-term mortality and need for invasive ventilation in hospitalised patients with non-viral CAP. [PMID 40323455]
- Adjunctive corticosteroids decrease mechanical ventilation, shock, and hospital and ICU stay, especially in severe CAP. [PMID 38128217]
- Guideline-concordant therapy for hospitalised CAP is associated with lower mortality than nonconcordant therapy, with limited evidence for other outcomes. [PMID 38966853]
- An Italian paediatric guideline addresses antibiotic use and duration for mild to moderate CAP in previously healthy children, noting research needs. [PMID 39427174]
- An IDSA/PIDS guideline using GRADE addresses paediatric community-acquired pneumonia. [PMID 41839546]
- A guideline on Chinese patent medicines for CAP notes more high-quality studies are needed. [PMID 37722517]

**Uncertainties**
- Selection of patients for corticosteroids.

**MCQ angles**
- Corticosteroids in severe CAP.
- Guideline-concordant antibiotics.

**Case hooks**
- Hospitalised patient with severe CAP and rising oxygen requirement.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Severity scores (CURB-65), empirical regimens and duration of therapy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 37722517: Guideline on treating community-acquired pneumonia with Chinese patent medicines (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38128217: Efficacy and safety of corticosteroids for the treatment of community-acquired pneumonia: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 38966853: Guideline-Concordant Therapy for Community-Acquired Pneumonia in the Hospitalized Population: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39427174: Treatment of mild to moderate community-acquired pneumonia in previously healthy children: an Italian intersociety consensus (SIPPS-SIP-SITIP-FIMP-SIAIP-SIMRI-FIMMG-SIMG) (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40323455: Corticosteroids for adult patients hospitalised with non-viral community-acquired pneumonia: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40679934: Diagnosis and Management of Community-acquired Pneumonia: An Official American Thoracic Society Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 41839546: Clinical Practice Guideline by the Infectious Diseases Society of America and the Pediatric Infectious Diseases Society: 2026 Guideline Update on The Management of Community-Acquired Pneumonia in Infants and Children Older than 3 Months of Age (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `compartment_syndrome` (topics 433) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Clinical findings for compartment syndrome have limited and heterogeneous accuracy, and regional analgesia after lower leg trauma has a guideline from European anaesthesia and trauma societies. For rhabdomyolysis, EAST conditionally recommends aggressive fluid resuscitation and against bicarbonate or mannitol, and gluteal compartment syndrome in neurologically intact patients may be observed.

**Teaching points**
- A diagnostic meta-analysis of compartment syndrome finds clinical findings need revisiting with new prospective studies to characterise statistical value (Level III). [PMID 37053115]
- A guideline on regional analgesia for lower leg trauma addresses risk of acute compartment syndrome, noting patients' right to refuse treatment and neither surgeon nor anaesthetist having veto. [PMID 34096035]
- EAST conditionally recommends aggressive intravenous fluid resuscitation in rhabdomyolysis to improve acute renal failure outcomes and lessen dialysis, and conditionally recommends against bicarbonate or mannitol. [PMID 34836603]
- In gluteal compartment syndrome, neurologically intact patients may be managed medically with neurological surveillance, and fasciotomy does not affect mortality. [PMID 34602242]
- Prehospital tourniquet use for civilian extremity vascular trauma decreased mortality without significantly increasing amputation or compartment syndrome. [PMID 38504263]
- Consensus recommendations aim to shorten recognition-to-action time in acute distal limb ischaemia after large-bore arterial access. [PMID 42019653]

**Uncertainties**
- Diagnostic accuracy of individual clinical signs.

**MCQ angles**
- Fluids in rhabdomyolysis.
- Tourniquet and compartment syndrome.

**Case hooks**
- Tibial fracture patient with escalating analgesic need.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Pressure thresholds, fasciotomy technique and the pain-on-passive-stretch sign are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 34096035: Regional analgesia for lower leg trauma and the risk of acute compartment syndrome: Guideline from the Association of Anaesthetists (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34602242: Gluteal compartment syndrome: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 34836603: Management of rhabdomyolysis: A practice management guideline from the Eastern Association for the Surgery of Trauma (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37053115: Diagnosis Accuracy for Compartment Syndrome: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38504263: Effectiveness and safety of tourniquet utilization for civilian vascular extremity trauma in the pre-hospital settings: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 42019653: Acute Distal Limb Ischemia After Large-Bore Arterial Access: An Expert Review and Consensus Recommendations (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `complement_def` (topics 434) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Complement deficiency predisposes to meningococcal and encapsulated infections, and management centres on patient education, preventive immunisation and prompt medical attention. Complement deficiencies are probably under-recognised, and new complement-targeted drugs add therapeutic blockade infection risk needing vaccination and prophylaxis.

**Teaching points**
- In complement deficiency and asplenia, key management is educating patients about infection predisposition, preventive immunisation and seeking prompt medical attention. [PMID 20930072]
- A 2026 Australian consensus gives recommendations for managing increased meningococcal infection risk, including alternative prevention where vaccination is problematic. [PMID 42604968]
- A review emphasises anticipatory care, vaccination and prophylaxis as complement-targeted drugs emerge, covering infection risk with therapeutic blockade. [PMID 41421099]
- Complement deficiencies may not be rare, and recognising warning signs should prompt step-wise functional, protein and molecular complement analysis. [PMID 25037634]
- A review links complement deficiency states to clinically relevant infections and their prevention and therapy. [PMID 21624663]
- Molecular characterisation of complement deficiencies or dysregulation is a prerequisite for informed therapeutic management. [PMID 31421540]
- Meningococcal capsule and lipooligosaccharide composition play critical roles in complement evasion. [PMID 24104403]

**Uncertainties**
- Immunogenicity assay choice for meningococcal vaccines.

**MCQ angles**
- Recurrent meningococcal disease.
- Complement inhibitor infection risk.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with second episode of meningococcal meningitis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Which complement components are associated with which infections, CH50 testing and antibiotic prophylaxis regimens are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 20930072: Infections of people with complement deficiencies and patients who have undergone splenectomy (review, 2010)
- PMID 21624663: Complement deficiency states and associated infections (review, 2011)
- PMID 24104403: Meningococcal disease and the complement system (review, 2014)
- PMID 25037634: Are complement deficiencies really rare? Overview on prevalence, clinical importance and modern diagnostic approach (review, 2014)
- PMID 31421540: Complement deficiencies and dysregulation: Pathophysiological consequences, modern analysis, and clinical management (review, 2019)
- PMID 41421099: Complement deficiencies and infections (review, 2026)
- PMID 42604968: Australian consensus recommendations for the management of increased meningococcal infection risk in adults with neurological diseases treated with complement inhibitors (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `crps` (topics 435) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Parenteral bisphosphonates are supported as first-line pharmacological treatment for CRPS-I in a meta-analysis, while rehabilitation interventions lack high-quality evidence over conventional therapy. Sensory profiling shows secondary hyperalgesia and pain facilitation in adults, milder in children.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis of randomised trials supports parenteral bisphosphonates as first-line pharmacological treatment of CRPS-I. [PMID 35104332]
- Rehabilitation interventions for CRPS show no clear advantage over conventional physical therapy or sham, and high-quality studies are needed. [PMID 36650605]
- Quantitative sensory testing shows secondary hyperalgesia, enhanced pain facilitation and elevated pain ratings dominate adult CRPS, while children and adolescents have milder sensory abnormalities. [PMID 36593515]
- Methods for assessing body perception disturbances in CRPS and their psychometric properties were systematically reviewed. [PMID 36065635]
- Pain symptoms are common in functional neurological disorder and often do not improve even when other symptoms improve. [PMID 38383157]
- Canadian imaging referral guidelines cover musculoskeletal imaging including complex regional pain syndrome. [PMID 37635274]
- A neuropathic pain meta-analysis reports numbers needed to treat and harm for pharmacotherapies including capsaicin 8% patches and opioids. [PMID 40252663]

**Uncertainties**
- Rehabilitation approach efficacy.

**MCQ angles**
- Bisphosphonates for CRPS-I.
- Sensory findings.

**Case hooks**
- Swollen painful limb weeks after wrist fracture.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Budapest criteria, vitamin C prophylaxis, and nerve block or neuromodulation roles are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35104332: Pharmacological treatment in adult patients with CRPS-I: a systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36065635: Assessment of body perception disturbances in complex regional pain syndrome: A systematic review using the COSMIN guideline (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36593515: Pain mechanisms in complex regional pain syndrome: a systematic review and meta-analysis of quantitative sensory testing outcomes (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 36650605: The Effectiveness of Rehabilitation Interventions on Pain and Disability for Complex Regional Pain Syndrome: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37635274: Canadian Association of Radiologists Musculoskeletal System Diagnostic Imaging Referral Guideline (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38383157: Pain and functional neurological disorder: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 40252663: Pharmacotherapy and non-invasive neuromodulation for neuropathic pain: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `ciai` (topics 436, 437) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Evidence on antimicrobials for complicated intra-abdominal infection mostly concerns lower-risk patients, and novel beta-lactam/beta-lactamase inhibitors are non-inferior to alternatives. Untargeted antifungal therapy has low to very low quality evidence with no firm benefit or harm, and source control is key in paediatric LMIC burden.

**Teaching points**
- Data on antimicrobial efficacy, safety and tolerability in complicated intra-abdominal infection pertain mostly to lower-risk patients. [PMID 37085768]
- Novel beta-lactam/beta-lactamase inhibitors are not inferior to other antibiotics for cIAI, and are active against E. coli and K. pneumoniae. [PMID 29343141]
- Non-polymyxin antibiotics options for Gram-negative complicated infections should be chosen with carbapenem-sparing regimens in mind. [PMID 37328062]
- Evidence supporting untargeted antifungal treatment in adult cIAI is low to very low quality with no firm evidence of benefit or harm. [PMID 29124727]
- EPICO 3.0 Delphi recommendations address identifying and managing invasive candidiasis in cIAI. [PMID 27422492]
- An Indonesian guideline summarises definition, classification, risk factors and management of cIAI adapted to local characteristics. [PMID 30427771]
- In children in LMICs, cIAI burden is high and source control should be a research focus to reduce complications and mortality. [PMID 32453672]

**Uncertainties**
- Empirical antifungal therapy benefit.

**MCQ angles**
- Antifungal therapy evidence.
- Carbapenem-sparing.

**Case hooks**
- Postoperative peritonitis with persistent fever.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Source control timing, antibiotic duration (STOP-IT) and empirical regimen choice are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27422492: EPICO 3.0. Recommendations on invasive candidiasis in patients with complicated intra-abdominal infection and surgical patients with ICU extended stay (guideline, 2016)

## Cluster `cuti` (topics 438) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 IDSA guideline on complicated UTI emphasises stewardship, shorter durations and reducing avoidable IV catheter harms. A consensus position questions cUTI as a meaningful entity for study design, and novel antibiotics including cefiderocol show efficacy similar to comparators.

**Teaching points**
- IDSA 2025 cUTI guidelines provide empiric antibiotic recommendations for the point of care, valuing stewardship and reducing patient antibiotic burden. [PMID 41419213, 41419448]
- The IDSA guideline favours shorter therapy duration, valuing reduction in avoidable intravenous catheter-related adverse events and costs. [PMID 41414903]
- A consensus statement questions the concept of complicated UTI as an entity with its own study guidance because of heterogeneous patient, test and pathogen characteristics. [PMID 40378860]
- Novel antibiotics may be more effective than conventional antibiotics in cUTI, though evidence on clinical cure rate is inconclusive. [PMID 37100354]
- Cefiderocol achieved similar clinical and microbiological responses to comparators in serious bacterial infections, with similar safety. [PMID 33596476]
- Cefiderocol is promising against multidrug-resistant Gram-negatives, though resistance during treatment has been reported. [PMID 35496290]
- Symptom-based international consensus guidelines exist for cUTI in spinal cord injury. [PMID 40862211]

**Uncertainties**
- Definition of complicated UTI.

**MCQ angles**
- Shorter duration therapy.
- Resistance to cefiderocol.

**Case hooks**
- Catheter-associated UTI with fever and bacteraemia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Specific empirical agents, oral step-down choices and exact durations are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33596476: Clinical efficacy and safety of cefiderocol in the treatment of acute bacterial infections: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35496290: Cefiderocol for the Treatment of Multidrug-Resistant Gram-Negative Bacteria: A Systematic Review of Currently Available Evidence (systematic review, 2022)

## Cluster `cah` (topics 439) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The Endocrine Society guideline covers 21-hydroxylase deficiency management. Non-classic CAH can need different strategies over time and genetic counselling, CAH carries cardiovascular and metabolic risk based on surrogate outcomes, and adrenal imaging may be considered, especially with abdominal pain, given adrenal tumours and myelolipomas.

**Teaching points**
- The Endocrine Society guideline gives best-practice recommendations for 21-hydroxylase deficiency CAH, considering patient safety, quality of life, cost and utilisation. [PMID 30272171]
- Non-classic CAH may need different therapeutic strategies at different follow-up stages, and genetic counselling can prevent CAH in offspring. [PMID 28582566]
- Individuals with CAH have a high prevalence of cardiovascular and metabolic risk factors, with evidence based on surrogate outcomes. [PMID 30272185]
- Adrenal tumours, particularly myelolipomas, are prevalent in CAH, associated with late diagnosis or poor control, so imaging may be considered especially with abdominal pain. [PMID 33471666]
- After genital reconstructive surgery in females with CAH, most were sexually active and satisfied but some reported impaired sexual experience; evidence certainty is very low. [PMID 30272250]
- Differences of sex development warrant multidisciplinary care and long-term psychosexual support, with variable gender identity outcomes. [PMID 33246831]
- French consensus on adrenal insufficiency notes DHEA androgen replacement may be offered and monitoring relies on signs of under- and over-dosage. [PMID 29338844]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term cardiovascular outcomes.

**MCQ angles**
- Adrenal imaging in CAH.
- Genetic counselling in non-classic CAH.

**Case hooks**
- Newborn girl with virilised genitalia and salt loss.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Newborn screening, 17-hydroxyprogesterone thresholds, glucocorticoid and fludrocortisone dosing and stress dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28582566: Non-classic congenital adrenal hyperplasia due to 21-hydroxylase deficiency revisited: an update with a special focus on adolescent and adult women (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29338844: SFE/SFEDP adrenal insufficiency French consensus: Introduction and handbook (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 30272171: Congenital Adrenal Hyperplasia Due to Steroid 21-Hydroxylase Deficiency: An Endocrine Society Clinical Practice Guideline (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 30272185: Cardiovascular and Metabolic Outcomes in Congenital Adrenal Hyperplasia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 30272250: Genital Reconstructive Surgery in Females With Congenital Adrenal Hyperplasia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 33246831: Gender identity disorder (GID) in adolescents and adults with differences of sex development (DSD): A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 33471666: Prevalence and Characteristics of Adrenal Tumors and Myelolipomas in Congenital Adrenal Hyperplasia: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)

## Cluster `ctd_ild` (topics 440, 441) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence concerns systemic sclerosis ILD. FVC is the preferred lung physiology measure for trials, DLCO did not meet OMERACT criteria, though DLCO and TLC should not yet be discounted. CT honeycombing predicts respiratory mortality, and the role of biopsy and lavage is uncertain.

**Teaching points**
- FVC is recommended as the lung physiology instrument for SSc-ILD trials, while DLCO did not meet OMERACT criteria. [PMID 34493396]
- Evidence suggests DLCO and TLC should not yet be discounted as surrogate markers of SSc-ILD progression despite FVC preference. [PMID 29769294]
- CT honeycombing extent predicts respiratory mortality in SSc-ILD, and CT predictors of progression need further study. [PMID 35155484]
- Lung biopsy for SSc-ILD is poorly studied, and its value for refining prognosis and therapy is unknown. [PMID 38521926]
- Bronchoalveolar lavage findings relate to severe pulmonary disease and worse prognosis in SSc, though added value over non-invasive tests is unknown. [PMID 33185168]
- Global SSc epidemiology shows ILD in 35-55% and pulmonary arterial hypertension in 6-12%, with need for early detection in expert centres. [PMID 42185138]
- MUC5B rs35705950 predicts susceptibility to idiopathic interstitial pneumonia and familial IIP but showed no significant relation with SSc-ILD. [PMID 34178730]

**Uncertainties**
- Use of DLCO as outcome.

**MCQ angles**
- FVC as trial endpoint.
- CT honeycombing prognosis.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with diffuse systemic sclerosis and falling FVC.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Other CTD-ILD (rheumatoid arthritis, myositis), treatments (mycophenolate, nintedanib, tocilizumab) are not covered by the retrieved excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29769294: Pulmonary function tests as outcomes for systemic sclerosis interstitial lung disease (systematic review, 2018)
- PMID 33185168: Bronchoalveolar Lavage in Systemic Sclerosis Patients: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 34178730: The Association between MUC5B Rs35705950 and Risks of Idiopathic Interstitial Pneumonia, Systemic Sclerosis Interstitial Lung Disease, and Familial Interstitial Pneumonia: A Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 34493396: Outcome measurement instrument selection for lung physiology in systemic sclerosis associated interstitial lung disease: A systematic review using the OMERACT filter 2.1 process (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 35155484: Computed Tomography Predictors of Mortality or Disease Progression in Systemic Sclerosis-Interstitial Lung Disease: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 38521926: The role of lung biopsy for diagnosis and prognosis of interstitial lung disease in systemic sclerosis: a systematic literature review (systematic review, 2024)
- PMID 42185138: Systemic sclerosis: A comprehensive systematic review of global epidemiology, sex and ethnic disparities, disease burden, and organ-specific involvement (systematic review, 2026)
