## Cluster `conservative_oxygen` (topics 443) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Pooled analyses find conservative oxygen therapy does not consistently reduce mortality in ICU, ventilated, acute medical or post-cardiac-arrest patients. An earlier IOTA meta-analysis found liberal oxygen increases mortality, and benefit may be limited to fewer ventilator-free days or organ failure; targets above SpO2 94-96% may be unfavourable.

**Teaching points**
- The IOTA meta-analysis of acutely ill adults found liberal oxygen increases mortality, with supplemental oxygen possibly unfavourable above SpO2 94-96%. [PMID 29726345]
- Conservative oxygen therapy did not reduce all-cause mortality in ICU patients, affecting only mechanical-ventilation-free days. [PMID 38668955]
- A meta-analysis found conservative oxygen did not reduce mortality but decreased ventilation time, new organ failure and renal replacement therapy risk in critical care. [PMID 34957139]
- For ventilated ICU patients, conservative oxygen was not significantly superior to liberal oxygen on clinical outcomes. [PMID 34526060]
- In acutely ill medical patients, conservative oxygen therapy was not associated with decreased mortality. [PMID 33774265]
- After cardiac arrest, conservative oxygen did not decrease mortality, improve neurologic recovery or shorten ICU stay in a meta-analysis of RCTs. [PMID 37484065]
- An individual-level analysis after cardiac arrest found lower mortality with conservative oxygen with low to very low certainty. [PMID 33058991]
- Implementing a conservative oxygenation guideline changed attitudes and practice and improved adherence to targets. [PMID 32209714]

**Uncertainties**
- Effect in specific populations.

**MCQ angles**
- Liberal oxygen harms.
- Post-arrest oxygen targets.

**Case hooks**
- Ventilated ICU patient with SpO2 100%.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Exact target ranges used in trials (e.g. PaO2 or SpO2 thresholds) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29726345: Mortality and morbidity in acutely ill adults treated with liberal versus conservative oxygen therapy (IOTA): a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 32209714: Changes in Attitudes and Actual Practice of Oxygen Therapy in ICUs after Implementation of a Conservative Oxygenation Guideline (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 33058991: Conservative or liberal oxygen therapy in adults after cardiac arrest: An individual-level patient data meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 33774265: Conservative versus liberal oxygen therapy for acutely ill medical patients: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34526060: Liberal or conservative oxygen therapy for ventilated patients in the ICU: a meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34957139: The Effect of Conservative Oxygen Therapy in Reducing Mortality in Critical Care Patients: A Meta-Analysis and Trial Sequential Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 37484065: Oxygen targets following cardiac arrest: A meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38668955: Conservative versus liberal oxygen therapy for intensive care unit patients: meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2024)

## Cluster `constipation` (topics 444) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2023 AGA-ACG guideline covers pharmacological management of chronic idiopathic constipation, with guidance on patient preference and cost. European, Korean and Indian consensus guidelines for functional constipation rest on low-level evidence, and fibre supplementation and dietary management have newer recommendations.

**Teaching points**
- The AGA-ACG clinical practice guideline on chronic idiopathic constipation bases choice on patient preference, cost and availability and highlights evidence gaps. [PMID 37211380, 37204227]
- The European neurogastroenterology society guideline on functional constipation had high expert agreement but low level of evidence for most recommendations. [PMID 31756783]
- The 2022 Seoul consensus gives clinical practice guidance on functional constipation for physicians and patients. [PMID 37417257]
- The British Dietetic Association guideline makes recommendations for dietary supplements, foods and drinks not previously included in guidelines. [PMID 41081513]
- Meta-analysis supports optimal type and regimen of fibre supplementation for adults with chronic constipation. [PMID 35816465]
- A Rome working group sets a standardised definition and therapeutic recommendations for occasional constipation. [PMID 37797905]
- An Indian consensus on chronic constipation supports teaching, practice and research in similar settings. [PMID 30617919]

**Uncertainties**
- Evidence quality for most functional constipation recommendations.

**MCQ angles**
- Fibre supplementation.
- Patient preference in drug choice.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with long-standing infrequent stools and straining.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Pelvic floor disorder diagnosis (anorectal manometry), biofeedback and specific drug classes are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `constrictive_pericarditis` (topics 445) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** BNP and NT-proBNP are lower in constrictive pericarditis than restrictive cardiomyopathy, and Mayo Clinic echocardiographic criteria have good accuracy for differentiating them. Tuberculosis is an important cause, especially with HIV in Africa, and pericardiectomy outcomes are worse in non-idiopathic aetiologies.

**Teaching points**
- BNP and NT-proBNP were significantly lower in constrictive pericarditis than restrictive cardiomyopathy, with BNP showing good pooled diagnostic accuracy. [PMID 34967020]
- All echocardiographic parameters from the Mayo Clinic criteria have good diagnostic accuracy for differentiating constrictive pericarditis from restrictive cardiomyopathy. [PMID 37744658]
- HIV infection complicates a significant portion of constrictive pericarditis in Africa, and early mortality has improved globally but remains high. [PMID 36883817]
- In pericardiectomy for constrictive pericarditis, post-radiation and other non-idiopathic aetiologies carry higher mortality than idiopathic, with heterogeneity and a possible need for randomised trials. [PMID 33539860]
- Pericardiectomy for constrictive tuberculous pericarditis has operative mortality with significant heterogeneity and methodological concerns. [PMID 34722042]
- Tuberculous cardiac involvement is frequent and can lead to heart failure, constrictive pericarditis or death, so early detection of complications is key. [PMID 33733808]
- Cochrane evidence on tuberculous pericarditis interventions shows low-certainty findings, with colchicine and steroid effects uncertain. [PMID 28902412]
- Intrapericardial fibrinolysis to prevent complications of effusion has very low certainty of efficacy and safety. [PMID 29107356]

**Uncertainties**
- Steroid benefit in TB pericarditis.

**MCQ angles**
- CP versus RCM biomarkers.
- TB as aetiology.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with HIV, ascites, raised JVP and pericardial calcification.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Respirophasic septal shift, Kussmaul sign, CT or MRI findings and timing of pericardiectomy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28902412: Interventions for treating tuberculous pericarditis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29107356: A systematic review of the efficacy and safety of intrapericardial fibrinolysis in patients with pericardial effusion (systematic review, 2018)
- PMID 33539860: Meta-Analysis of Population Characteristics and Outcomes of Patients Undergoing Pericardiectomy for Constrictive Pericarditis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 33733808: Tuberculosis and the Heart (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 34722042: Pericardiectomy for Constrictive Tuberculous Pericarditis: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis on the Etiology, Patients' Characteristics, and the Outcomes (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34967020: Natriuretic peptides to differentiate constrictive pericarditis and restrictive cardiomyopathy: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36883817: Updates on the Global Prevalence and Etiology of Constrictive Pericarditis: A Systematic Review (meta-analysis, 2024)
- PMID 37744658: Echocardiographic Criteria to Differentiate Constrictive Pericarditis From Restrictive Cardiomyopathy: A Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `contact_dermatitis` (topics 446) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2026 ESCD guideline sets best practice for diagnostic patch testing, and German S1 and S2k guidelines provide decision aids for contact dermatitis and hand eczema diagnosis, prevention and therapy. Occupational acrylate exposure in beauticians is under-regulated, and metal implant allergy diagnostics remain uncertain.

**Teaching points**

- The German S1 guideline on contact dermatitis provides a decision aid for diagnostics, therapy and prevention in dermatology, allergology and occupational dermatology. [PMID 35527339]
- The S2k guideline on hand eczema covers diagnosis, prevention and therapy, with consented key statements valid until February 2028. [PMID 37700424]
- Occupational allergic contact dermatitis from acrylates in cosmetic glues affects hairdressers and beauticians, and regulations inadequately address the risk. [PMID 35088905]
- Expert consensus on diagnostics in suspected metal implant allergy shows uncertainty around preoperative testing, histology and in vitro testing. [PMID 38606660]
- Drug allergy practice parameters review delayed drug hypersensitivity and the use of delayed intradermal and patch testing for specific phenotypes. [PMID 36563781]

**Uncertainties**
- Preoperative testing for suspected metal allergy.

**MCQ angles**
- Occupational acrylate allergy.
- Patch testing role in delayed drug reactions.

- Hairdresser with hand dermatitis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Irritant versus allergic differentiation, standard series allergens and treatment steps are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `ceeg` (topics 447) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Continuous EEG is advised for patients with altered mental status after convulsive status epilepticus, while diagnostic criteria for nonconvulsive status epilepticus in comatose patients remain unsettled. cEEG also aids neonatal seizure assessment, and neurophysiology contributes to prognostication after hypoxic-ischaemic encephalopathy.

**Teaching points**
- Guidelines and consensus statements recommend continuous EEG monitoring for patients with altered mental status after convulsive status epilepticus. [PMID 32890062]
- The modified Salzburg and updated ACNS criteria for nonconvulsive status epilepticus in comatose patients have unresolved diagnostic accuracy. [PMID 38045184]
- Continuous EEG should be used to diagnose neonatal seizures and assess severity, remission and drug efficacy. [PMID 34867732]
- Very low cortical SSEP amplitudes (approximately 0.5 µV or less) strongly predict poor outcome after hypoxic-ischaemic encephalopathy, though not yet in guidelines. [PMID 42359655]
- Diagnosis and management of adult status epilepticus in resource-limited settings is non-uniform, highlighting the need for appropriate guidelines. [PMID 40168634]
- In COVID-19, EEG commonly shows encephalopathy correlating with severity, with frontal findings proposed as a biomarker. [PMID 33121875]
- A practical approach to ICU encephalopathy notes dexmedetomidine is effective for delirium in ventilated patients, while haloperidol has minimal effect. [PMID 38441156]

**Uncertainties**
- NCSE criteria in coma.

**MCQ angles**
- cEEG after status epilepticus.
- SSEP prognostication.

**Case hooks**
- Patient not waking after convulsive seizure ended.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Recommended duration of monitoring, quantitative EEG and subclinical seizure treatment thresholds are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 32890062: EEG Monitoring After Convulsive Status Epilepticus (review, 2020)
- PMID 33121875: Systematic review of EEG findings in 617 patients diagnosed with COVID-19 (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 34867732: Efficacy and Safety of Levetiracetam vs. Phenobarbital for Neonatal Seizures: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 38045184: EEG criteria for diagnosing nonconvulsive status epilepticus in comatose - An unsolved puzzle: A narrative review (review, 2023)
- PMID 38441156: Acute encephalopathy in the ICU: a practical approach (review, 2024)
- PMID 40168634: Diagnosis and Management of Adult Status Epilepticus in Resource-Limited Settings: A Systematic Review (systematic review, 2025)
- PMID 42359655: Role of Neurophysiologic Testing in Prognostication of Hypoxic-Ischemic Encephalopathy (review, 2026)

## Cluster `cgm` (topics 448, 449) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CGM adds benefit across settings: in hospitalised non-critically ill adults it improves control and reduces hypoglycaemia over point-of-care testing, in pregnancy it lowers HbA1c and possibly large-for-gestational-age risk, and in non-insulin-treated type 2 diabetes it supports lifestyle change. An Italian guideline supports wider T2D use.

**Teaching points**
- In non-critically ill hospitalised adults with diabetes, adding CGM to point-of-care testing for insulin dosing gave superior glycaemic control and less hypoglycaemia. [PMID 39798897]
- CGM in pregnancy reduces HbA1c and possibly large-for-gestational-age risk, with greatest benefit in type 1 diabetes, then gestational diabetes. [PMID 40216177]
- CGM in non-insulin-treated type 2 diabetes improves outcomes and patient experience, reduces resource use, and has acceptable cost-effectiveness. [PMID 40757453]
- CGM guides lifestyle choices and nutrition in type 2 diabetes with benefits for glycaemia and body weight. [PMID 41109846]
- An Italian guideline supports a shift to wider use of CGM in type 2 diabetes, with CGM becoming mandatory for people on insulin therapy and reimbursement needing adaptation. [PMID 42207272]
- Real-world evidence shows commercially available automated insulin delivery systems differ in glycaemic outcomes including time in range, with age influencing outcomes. [PMID 41058520]
- An international guideline defines study design, comparator measurements and minimum accuracy requirements for CGM system assessment. [PMID 41253217]

**Uncertainties**
- Real-world evidence biases for AID systems.

**MCQ angles**
- CGM in hospital.
- CGM in pregnancy.

**Case hooks**
- Pregnant woman with type 1 diabetes and variable glucose.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Time-in-range targets, AGP metrics and indications by diabetes type are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39798897: Use of continuous glucose monitoring and point-of-care glucose testing in hospitalized patients with diabetes mellitus in non-intensive care unit settings: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40216177: Evidence for improved glucose metrics and perinatal outcomes with continuous glucose monitoring compared to self-monitoring in diabetes during pregnancy (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40757453: Continuous glucose monitoring in noninsulin-treated type 2 diabetes: A critical review of reported trials with an updated systematic review and meta-analysis of randomised controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41058520: A systematic literature review and meta-analysis of real-world evidence on commercially available automated insulin delivery systems in people with type 1 diabetes (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41109846: Continuous Glucose Monitoring to Guide Lifestyle Choices With a Focus on Nutrition in the Management of Type 2 Diabetes: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)

## Cluster `osa_cpap` (topics 450) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CPAP lowers blood pressure in OSA, including resistant hypertension, mainly with follow-up of at least 3 months, but evidence that it prevents cardiovascular events is not firm because of trial limitations. CPAP reduces haemoglobin and haematocrit, and paediatric adherence is around 47%.

**Teaching points**
- CPAP reduced blood pressure in patients with OSA and systemic hypertension, except when follow-up was shorter than 3 months. [PMID 35701490]
- CPAP lowers blood pressure in OSA with resistant hypertension. [PMID 38460066]
- In OSA with hypertension, CPAP lowered nighttime blood pressure especially, with no benefit for aortic stiffness. [PMID 33607443]
- A meta-analysis of CPAP for preventing cardiovascular events found limitations in trial populations and adherence that weaken support for benefit in all patients. [PMID 32248026]
- CPAP effects on cardiac events and metabolic components in moderate to severe OSA with coronary artery disease were assessed in a meta-analysis. [PMID 37497624]
- CPAP changes inflammatory markers in OSA, but more RCTs are needed to confirm biomarker usefulness for cardiovascular risk. [PMID 40346316]
- OSA is associated with secondary polycythaemia, more in severe disease, and CPAP reduces haemoglobin and haematocrit. [PMID 35962574]
- CPAP adherence in children with OSA was 46.56%, with young age, low BMI and high AHI associated with acceptable adherence. [PMID 36761690]

**Uncertainties**
- Cardiovascular event prevention.

**MCQ angles**
- BP effect of CPAP.
- Adherence in children.

**Case hooks**
- Resistant hypertension with loud snoring.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Titration methods, mask interface and symptom outcome (sleepiness) results are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `crrt` (topics 451, 452) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** A 2023 guideline provides 16 recommendations on regional citrate anticoagulation for CRRT, and a network meta-analysis compares CRRT anticoagulation options. Adsorptive oXiris filter evidence is uncertain with no 90-day mortality difference, and paediatric consensus addresses extracorporeal blood purification in the PICU.

**Teaching points**
- A guideline on regional citrate anticoagulation for CRRT formed 16 recommendations from expert consensus, balancing generalisability, resource use and advantages and disadvantages. [PMID 37248514]
- A network meta-analysis compared anticoagulation options for CRRT in critically ill patients on mortality, adverse events and kidney recovery. [PMID 37287084]
- Evidence on oXiris adsorptive filters is low or very low quality, with no significant difference in 90-day, ICU or hospital mortality. [PMID 37424026]
- A consensus statement guides extracorporeal blood purification decisions in European paediatric ICUs where evidence is limited. [PMID 39899300]
- Paediatric CRRT working group guidance addresses AKI after haematopoietic cell transplantation and how to optimise therapy. [PMID 35224659]
- A meta-analysis compares mortality by dialysis mode in CKD, limited by heterogeneity. [PMID 38172835]

**Uncertainties**
- Value of adsorptive filters.

**MCQ angles**
- Citrate anticoagulation.
- oXiris filter evidence.

**Case hooks**
- ICU patient with AKI and shock on CRRT.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Modality differences (CVVH, CVVHD, CVVHDF), dose and timing of initiation are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `copd` (topics 455, 456) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Current COPD guidance includes the 2026 JRS guideline, the 2023 Canadian Thoracic Society pharmacotherapy guideline and Chinese guidelines. Emergency department care often fails to deliver guideline-recommended inhaled triple therapy, and the Polish consensus recommends discharge instructions with follow-up and inhaled medication prescriptions after exacerbation.

**Teaching points**
- The JRS 7th edition COPD guideline (2026) uses Minds/GRADE for practical recommendations in the Japanese context. [PMID 42447589]
- Implementation of the 2023 Canadian Thoracic Society guideline aims to help family physicians apply stable COPD pharmacotherapy to relieve symptoms, prevent exacerbations and reduce mortality. [PMID 42875068]
- COPD management in the emergency department falls short of guidelines, with patients not receiving guideline-recommended inhaled triple therapy. [PMID 41985297]
- The Polish Respiratory Society recommends discharge instructions for all patients hospitalised for COPD exacerbation, including follow-up appointments and prescriptions for inhaled medications. [PMID 41562962]
- A Saudi consensus advocates stratified triage and evidence-based aerosol delivery device selection for acute asthma and COPD exacerbations. [PMID 42625150]
- A Chinese practice guideline for COPD diagnosis and treatment aims to standardise care and reduce disease burden. [PMID 42373445]
- Diaphragm ultrasound should be an adjunct, not the sole basis, for weaning decisions in ventilated patients, pending validation. [PMID 42576250]
- Biomarkers for COPD assessment have utilities and limitations supporting personalised strategies. [PMID 41498660]

**Uncertainties**
- Biomarker-guided care.

**MCQ angles**
- Triple therapy gap.
- Discharge planning after exacerbation.

**Case hooks**
- Frequent exacerbator on LAMA/LABA.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** GOLD groups, exacerbation treatment (steroids, antibiotics) and oxygen/NIV criteria are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

- PMID 41562962: Recommendations Following Hospitalization for Acute Exacerbation of COPD-A Consensus Statement of the Polish Respiratory Society (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `cac` (topics 458, 459) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CAC scoring refines risk in selected patients, but one meta-analysis found no evidence that adding CAC to traditional risk scores provides clinical benefit. CAC has prognostic value in symptomatic patients, and global guidelines and SCCT/NASCI consensus link CAC and CCTA findings to statin and aspirin decisions.

**Teaching points**
- A review of major global CAC guidelines aims to help clinicians adjudicate personalised statin and aspirin therapy. [PMID 36599573]
- A meta-analysis found CACS may refine risk in selected patients, but no evidence shows that adding CACS to traditional risk scores provides clinical benefit. [PMID 35467692]
- In stable symptomatic patients with suspected CAD, CAC predicts MACE with risk rising with higher score. [PMID 31229262]
- Rule of thumb for CAC above the 75th percentile: men under 50 and women under 60 with any CAC, or men under 60 and women under 70 with CAC above 100. [PMID 35902315]
- SCCT/NASCI expert consensus recommends rigorous documentation of atherosclerosis on CCTA to guide preventive care. [PMID 33303383]
- Extent of CAD quantified by segment involvement score on CCTA is a strong independent predictor of cardiovascular events. [PMID 28483581]
- The 2023 Australian guideline recategorises high CVD risk at 10% or more over five years with a new calculator. [PMID 38623719, 38737366]

**Uncertainties**
- Which patients benefit from CAC addition.

**MCQ angles**
- CAC percentile rule.
- CAC in symptomatic patients.

**Case hooks**
- Intermediate-risk asymptomatic adult considering statin.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Score thresholds (0, 1-99, 100+) for statin decisions and radiation exposure are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28483581: Prognostic value of segment involvement score compared to other measures of coronary atherosclerosis by computed tomography: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 31229262: Prognostic value of coronary artery calcium score in symptomatic individuals: A meta-analysis of 34,000 subjects (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 33303383: Society of Cardiovascular Computed Tomography / North American Society of Cardiovascular Imaging - Expert Consensus Document on Coronary CT Imaging of Atherosclerotic Plaque (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 35467692: Evaluation of the Incremental Value of a Coronary Artery Calcium Score Beyond Traditional Cardiovascular Risk Assessment: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35902315: A Simple Approach to the Identification of Guideline-Based Coronary Artery Calcium Score Percentiles (From the Multi-Ethnic Study of Atherosclerosis) (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36599573: Major Global Coronary Artery Calcium Guidelines (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38623719: 2023 Australian guideline for assessing and managing cardiovascular disease risk (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38737366: Assessing, communicating and managing cardiovascular disease risk: a practical summary of the 2023 guideline (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `ccta` (topics 460, 461) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CAD-RADS 2.0 (2022) standardises coronary CT angiography reporting and subsequent management. Quantitative CCTA standards define plaque measures, and AI-enabled CCTA recommendations place high plaque burden percentiles in the high-intensity treatment category.

**Teaching points**
- CAD-RADS 2.0 is an expert consensus reporting system giving a standardised framework for education, research, trial design, AI development and quality assurance. [PMID 35864070, 36115815, 36436841]
- The original CAD-RADS (2016) established standardised reporting that links CCTA findings to subsequent patient management. [PMID 27318587, 27609151, 27318576]
- An expert consensus sets definitions and standards for performing and reporting quantitative measures of coronary artery disease by CCTA. [PMID 38849237]
- Recommendations on AI-assisted CCTA propose that a high percentile of total plaque volume warrants high-intensity treatment, laying groundwork for future trials. [PMID 40751112]

**Uncertainties**
- Outcome trials of quantitative plaque-guided treatment.

**MCQ angles**
- CAD-RADS purpose.
- Plaque volume and treatment intensity.

**Case hooks**
- Stable chest pain with CCTA showing moderate stenosis.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** FFR-CT, indications for CCTA in stable chest pain and CAD-RADS category definitions are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `cbd` (topics 463) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** A neurologist-oriented algorithm and PSP/CBS management guidance support diagnosis of atypical parkinsonian disorders, but epidemiology estimates are heterogeneous. NfL is highest in multiple system atrophy among parkinsonian disorders, and CSF p-tau decreases in tauopathies.

**Teaching points**
- Best-practice guidance covers clinical management of progressive supranuclear palsy and corticobasal syndrome. [PMID 34276544]
- A consensus diagnostic algorithm helps general neurologists with atypical parkinsonian disorders, including ambiguous cases. [PMID 39185098]
- PSP and CBS prevalence and incidence estimates are highly heterogeneous, requiring rigorous phenotyping with current criteria. [PMID 37289323]
- Neurofilament light chain differs between parkinsonian syndromes, with MSA showing the highest levels. [PMID 40180649]
- Tauopathies may show an inherent decrease in CSF p-tau, and AD admixture confounds FTD cohorts. [PMID 39200244]
- CNS-enriched extracellular vesicle biomarkers for parkinsonian disorders need larger standardised studies. [PMID 38103086, 37416941]
- Pooled estimates of frontotemporal dementia incidence and prevalence provide a basis for planning. [PMID 40920400]

**Uncertainties**
- Biomarker validation.

**MCQ angles**
- NfL in MSA.
- Diagnostic algorithm.

**Case hooks**
- Asymmetric rigid apraxic limb with cortical sensory loss.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Alien limb phenomenon, diagnostic criteria for CBD versus CBS and treatment are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `covid_mgmt` (topics 466, 467, 471) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** IDSA and WHO living guidelines, and Pan-American guidance, address COVID-19 treatment: strong recommendation for nirmatrelvir/ritonavir in outpatients, no benefit from hydroxychloroquine or ivermectin, and awake proning for hypoxaemic respiratory failure. Standard thromboprophylaxis is advised for critically ill patients, and long-term effects have a NICE rapid guideline.

**Teaching points**
- The Pan-American guideline strongly recommends nirmatrelvir/ritonavir for outpatients and finds no benefit from hydroxychloroquine or ivermectin. [PMID 37550690]
- IDSA 2025 treatment guidance uses GRADE and includes an algorithm for antiviral selection. [PMID 41920902, 40757658]
- The 2024 IDSA update on management includes information on pemivibart under FDA emergency use authorisation. [PMID 39471458]
- Critically ill COVID-19 patients should receive standard thromboprophylaxis, and moderately ill patients with low bleeding risk might benefit from therapeutic heparin, with no role for intermediate dosing. [PMID 35167861]
- A trial of awake prone positioning is recommended in acute hypoxaemic respiratory failure due to COVID-19, with staff able to detect deterioration. [PMID 36691710]
- The WHO living guideline conditionally recommends awake prone positioning for severely ill hospitalised patients requiring supplemental oxygen or non-invasive ventilation. [PMID 35917394]
- A meta-analysis of remdesivir found evidence across severity levels, arguing recommendations should rely on both RCT and real-world data. [PMID 40067859]
- A rapid guideline addresses managing long-term effects of COVID-19. [PMID 33555768]

**Uncertainties**
- Weight of real-world versus trial evidence.

**MCQ angles**
- Outpatient nirmatrelvir/ritonavir.
- Awake proning.

**Case hooks**
- High-risk outpatient with mild COVID-19 within five days of symptom onset.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Eligibility for antivirals, corticosteroid timing and dosing, and vaccination are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33555768: COVID-19 rapid guideline: managing the long-term effects of COVID-19 (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 35167861: Thromboprophylaxis in Patients With COVID-19: A Brief Update to the CHEST Guideline and Expert Panel Report (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 35917394: Clinical management of COVID-19: Living guideline (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36691710: Awake proning in patients with COVID-19-related hypoxemic acute respiratory failure: A rapid practice guideline (guideline, 2023)

- PMID 39471458: 2024 Clinical Practice Guideline Update by the Infectious Diseases Society of America on the Management of COVID-19: Anti-SARS-CoV-2 Neutralizing Antibody Pemivibart for Pre-exposure Prophylaxis (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `covid_critical` (topics 468, 469, 470, 472) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** For COVID-19 critical illness, hospitalised patients on corticosteroids show similar mortality reductions with baricitinib and sarilumab compared with tocilizumab, and JAK inhibitors show no credible subgroup effect. ISTH advises against adding antiplatelet agents in noncritically ill patients, and invasive aspergillosis should be considered in ICU.

**Teaching points**
- Among hospitalised COVID-19 patients on corticosteroids, baricitinib and sarilumab have high probability of similar mortality reductions compared with tocilizumab. [PMID 35863630]
- JAK inhibitors in adults hospitalised with COVID-19 showed no credible subgroup effect on 28-day mortality by ventilation status, comorbidity or timing. [PMID 40378861]
- The 2023 ISTH update recommends against adding an antiplatelet agent in hospitalised noncritically ill COVID-19 patients (COR 3). [PMID 38503600]
- The Pan-American guideline strongly recommends outpatient nirmatrelvir/ritonavir and reports no benefit from hydroxychloroquine or ivermectin. [PMID 37550690]
- Invasive aspergillosis should be considered in critically ill ICU patients, with isavuconazole and voriconazole first-line and liposomal amphotericin B alternative. [PMID 40465080]
- Diabetes is a significant risk factor for invasive aspergillosis in ICU, highest in COVID-associated pulmonary aspergillosis. [PMID 39885384]
- Mucormycosis control in Brazil requires control of hyperglycaemia and immunosuppression, standardised guidance and rapid diagnostics. [PMID 40974632]
- Guidance addresses the oXiris filter for sepsis and hyperinflammatory states, with limited evidence. [PMID 40929000]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal choice among immunomodulators.

**MCQ angles**
- IL-6 versus JAK inhibitors.
- Secondary fungal infection.

**Case hooks**
- Ventilated COVID-19 patient with new infiltrates and diabetes.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Dexamethasone dose, oxygenation targets and ventilation strategy are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 35863630: Effect of tocilizumab, sarilumab, and baricitinib on mortality among patients hospitalized for COVID-19 treated with corticosteroids: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37550690: Pan-American Guidelines for the treatment of SARS-CoV-2/COVID-19: a joint evidence-based guideline of the Brazilian Society of Infectious Diseases (SBI) and the Pan-American Association of Infectious Diseases (API) (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 38503600: 2023 ISTH update of the 2022 ISTH guidelines for antithrombotic treatment in COVID-19 (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `cox2` (topics 473) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Celecoxib showed no excess cardiovascular risk in RA and OA patients independent of dose or duration, though uncertainty remains for patients on aspirin or with established cardiovascular disease. Genetic work points to mechanisms for coxib-associated coronary risk, and concomitant aspirin attenuates chemoprevention benefit.

**Teaching points**
- A meta-analysis found celecoxib cardiovascular safety in RA and OA independent of dose or duration, though results in aspirin-treated and established cardiovascular disease patients remain uncertain. [PMID 34932581]
- A review emphasises recognising high-risk cases, choosing a specific NSAID agent, ulcer prophylaxis and monitoring in patients with hypertension, cardiovascular and renal disease. [PMID 31937550]
- A genomic exploration identified mechanisms that may explain adverse cardiovascular effects of COX-2 inhibitors. [PMID 28860667]
- Coxibs may reduce colorectal neoplasm risk but chemopreventive effects attenuate over time, and are not useful with concomitant low-dose aspirin. [PMID 30656820]
- For concomitant aspirin and NSAID use, decisions remain at patient level, with factors such as body weight possibly modifying aspirin effectiveness. [PMID 30406348]
- NSAID cardiovascular toxicity may be underestimated in haemophilia patients, who share cardiovascular risk factors. [PMID 26899022]
- COX-2 has a role in cardiovascular disease mechanisms, with natural inhibitors such as quercetin-like compounds under study. [PMID 34592918]

**Uncertainties**
- Risk when combined with aspirin.

**MCQ angles**
- Celecoxib safety.
- Aspirin and NSAID interaction.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with osteoarthritis and ischaemic heart disease needing analgesia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Rofecoxib withdrawal, trial data such as PRECISION and renal effects are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 26899022: Is the cardiovascular toxicity of NSAIDS and COX-2 selective inhibitors underestimated in patients with haemophilia? (review, 2016)
- PMID 28860667: A genomic exploration identifies mechanisms that may explain adverse cardiovascular effects of COX-2 inhibitors (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 30406348: A narrative review of the cardiovascular risks associated with concomitant aspirin and NSAID use (review, 2019)
- PMID 30656820: Risks of colorectal neoplasms and cardiovascular thromboembolic events after the combined use of selective COX-2 inhibitors and aspirin with 5-year follow-up: a meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 31937550: Non-steroidal anti-inflammatory drug (NSAID) therapy in patients with hypertension, cardiovascular, renal or gastrointestinal comorbidities: joint APAGE/APLAR/APSDE/APSH/APSN/PoA recommendations (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 34592918: New horizons in the roles and associations of COX-2 and novel natural inhibitors in cardiovascular diseases (review, 2021)
- PMID 34932581: Cardiovascular safety of celecoxib in rheumatoid arthritis and osteoarthritis patients: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)

## Cluster `craniopharyngioma` (topics 474) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** UK paediatric guidelines and RAPNO response criteria standardise care and assessment of craniopharyngioma. Gamma knife radiosurgery gives about 74% tumour control, adult series show gross total resection favours lower recurrence without significant difference, and hypopituitarism is linked to premature mortality.

**Teaching points**
- UK national guidelines for paediatric craniopharyngioma, endorsed by RCPCH using AGREE II methodology, standardise care for children and young people. [PMID 37549682]
- RAPNO guidelines define response assessment for paediatric craniopharyngioma where standardised parameters were lacking. [PMID 36124689]
- Gamma Knife for craniopharyngioma gave overall tumour control of 74% and five-year progression-free survival of 70%. [PMID 34638482]
- In adults, gross total resection favours lower recurrence, though the difference did not reach significance, and outcomes should be stratified by modality. [PMID 29267973]
- Hypopituitarism is associated with premature mortality in adults, higher in women and those diagnosed younger. [PMID 27817141]
- Growth hormone replacement may be considered in GH-deficient adult cancer survivors in remission after individual risk-benefit analysis. [PMID 35319491]
- ISRS consensus guidelines inform judicious paediatric cranial stereotactic radiosurgery, with acceptable local control. [PMID 39390948]
- ESTRO ACROP gives target volume delineation recommendations for skull base tumours. [PMID 33309848]

**Uncertainties**
- Extent of resection versus function preservation.

**MCQ angles**
- RAPNO response assessment.
- Hypopituitarism mortality.

**Case hooks**
- Child with headaches, visual loss and suprasellar calcified cystic mass.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Hypothalamic obesity management, diabetes insipidus care, BRAF-targeted therapy and surgical approach details are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 27817141: Mortality in adults with hypopituitarism: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2017)
- PMID 29267973: Adult Craniopharyngioma: Case Series, Systematic Review, and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 33309848: ESTRO ACROP guideline for target volume delineation of skull base tumors (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 34638482: Gamma Knife Radiosurgery for Pituitary Tumors: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)

## Cluster `cjd` (topics 475) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Sporadic CJD is rare and rapidly progressive, mimicking other rapidly progressive dementias, and recommendations for palliative and supportive care in prion disease were published in 2026. Blood neurofilament light is abnormal in CJD and FTD, and seed amplification assays work well for Lewy body disease, supporting a similar method for prions, though effective therapies are lacking.

**Teaching points**
- A systematic review stresses awareness of CJD, particularly sporadic CJD, which occurs in older people and can resemble rapidly progressive dementia. [PMID 31876504]
- Multidisciplinary recommendations address palliative and supportive care in CJD and related rapidly progressive dementias, with best practice suggestions where evidence is limited. [PMID 42472622]
- A review of sporadic CJD identifies possible therapeutic targets, but evaluating treatments is challenging given low incidence and rapid course. [PMID 39311352]
- Blood neurofilament light is abnormal in AD, MCI, FTD and CJD but not preclinical AD. [PMID 37264656]
- NfL cannot be used for diagnosis without clinical symptoms and other auxiliary examinations. [PMID 31744001]
- RT-QuIC and PMCA show high diagnostic performance for synucleinopathies with Lewy bodies, though results for multiple system atrophy are less robust. [PMID 37205253, 37266333]
- Consensus classification for posterior cortical atrophy aids interpretation across research studies. [PMID 28259709]

**Uncertainties**
- Therapeutic targets for prion disease.

**MCQ angles**
- Rapidly progressive dementia differential.
- NfL in CJD.

**Case hooks**
- Older patient with rapidly progressive dementia and myoclonus.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** CJD diagnostic criteria, 14-3-3 and RT-QuIC performance in prion disease, MRI DWI findings and infection control are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 28259709: Consensus classification of posterior cortical atrophy (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 31744001: Neurofilament Light Chain in Cerebrospinal Fluid and Blood as a Biomarker for Neurodegenerative Diseases: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 31876504: Creutzfeldt-Jakob disease: a systematic review of global incidence, prevalence, infectivity, and incubation (systematic review, 2020)
- PMID 37205253: α-Synuclein Seed Amplification Assays in the Diagnosis of Synucleinopathies Using Cerebrospinal Fluid-A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37264656: Blood Neurofilament Light Chain in Different Types of Dementia (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37266333: Misfolded alpha-synuclein detection by RT-QuIC in dementia with lewy bodies: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 39311352: A Systematic Review of Sporadic Creutzfeldt-Jakob Disease: Pathogenesis, Diagnosis, and Therapeutic Attempts (systematic review, 2024)
- PMID 42472622: Multidisciplinary recommendations for palliative and supportive care in Creutzfeldt-Jakob disease and related disorders (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `crispr_scd` (topics 476) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Gene therapy for sickle cell disease now has ASTCT and national consensus recommendations covering patient selection, psychosocial readiness and lifelong late-effect surveillance. Comparisons of allogeneic transplant with gene therapy lack standardised complication reporting, and HBG1/2 editing may induce fetal haemoglobin more than BCL11A targeting.

**Teaching points**
- ASTCT practice recommendations for sickle cell gene therapy aim to harmonise practice and support shared decision-making, with lifelong surveillance for late effects. [PMID 41905703]
- A National Alliance of Sickle Cell Disease consensus supports safe, consistent, high-quality implementation of gene therapy programmes. [PMID 42896182]
- Psychosocial risk and resilience assessment is advised to support readiness for gene therapy in sickle cell disease. [PMID 39167410]
- A systematic review comparing allogeneic HSCT with gene therapy found lacking reporting of SCD complications and patient-important outcomes, and advocates standardised reporting. [PMID 36728286]
- CRISPR/Cas9 offers a potential one-time functional cure for thalassaemia and SCD in systematic review. [PMID 39794549]
- HBG1/2 had significantly greater impact on HbF induction than BCL11A in a CRISPR editing analysis. [PMID 36250099]
- A CADTH report on exagamglogene autotemcel (Casgevy) for patients 12 and older with severe SCD highlights need for therapies reducing complications and treatment burden. [PMID 39977554]
- Quality of life outcomes after stem cell transplant in SCD and thalassaemia need longitudinal study and reporting from gene therapy trials. [PMID 33496753]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term safety of gene editing.

**MCQ angles**
- HbF induction target.
- Gene therapy readiness.

**Case hooks**
- Adolescent with recurrent vaso-occlusive crises on hydroxyurea.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Eligibility criteria, conditioning toxicity, fertility preservation and cost are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33496753: A systematic review of quality of life in sickle cell disease and thalassemia after stem cell transplant or gene therapy (systematic review, 2021)
- PMID 36250099: Using Clustered Regularly Interspaced Short Palindromic Repeats gene editing to induce permanent expression of fetal hemoglobin in β-thalassemia and sickle cell disease: A comparative meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 36728286: A systematic review comparing allogeneic hematopoietic stem cell transplant to gene therapy in sickle cell disease (systematic review, 2023)
- PMID 39167410: Assessing Psychosocial Risk and Resilience to Support Readiness for Gene Therapy in Sickle Cell Disease: A Consensus Statement (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39794549: CRISPR/Cas9 System as a Promising Therapy in Thalassemia and Sickle Cell Disease: A Systematic Review of Clinical Trials (systematic review, 2026)
- PMID 39977554: Exagamglogene Autotemcel (Casgevy): Indication: For the treatment of patients 12 years of age and older with sickle cell disease with recurrent vaso-occlusive crises: Reimbursement Recommendation (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `cip_cim` (topics 477) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ICU-acquired weakness affects about 48% of critically ill patients, who lose almost 2% of muscle per day in the first week. Early mobilisation appears to reduce weakness and improve function, corticosteroid exposure is associated with weakness, and neuromuscular electrical stimulation may lower its occurrence.

**Teaching points**
- ICU-acquired weakness prevalence is 48%, with critically ill patients losing nearly 2% of skeletal muscle per day in the first ICU week. [PMID 36597123]
- Early mobilisation appears to decrease ICU-acquired weakness incidence, improve functional capacity, increase ventilator-free days and discharge-to-home rates. [PMID 31581205]
- Meta-analysis assesses whether early mobilisation and rehabilitation reduce ICU-AW and improve strength and recovery. [PMID 31219229]
- RCTs are more likely to show benefit of early mobilisation the larger the timing difference between intervention and comparator. [PMID 33407707]
- Corticosteroid use is significantly associated with ICU-acquired weakness, so exposure and duration should be limited. [PMID 30075789]
- Neuromuscular electrical stimulation may result in lower occurrence of ICU-AW in critical illness. [PMID 37232695]
- A consensus of 29 on recovery after critical illness stresses insights into ICU-acquired weakness, family-centred care and a post-ICU research agenda. [PMID 29208005]
- Early mobilisation and rehabilitation is conditionally recommended in critically ill burn patients to reduce delirium. [PMID 35639543]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal timing of mobilisation.

**MCQ angles**
- Corticosteroids and weakness.
- NMES.

**Case hooks**
- Sepsis survivor unable to wean from ventilator with flaccid weakness.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Distinguishing critical illness polyneuropathy from myopathy (nerve conduction, muscle biopsy) and MRC scoring are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29208005: Recovery after critical illness: putting the puzzle together-a consensus of 29 (guideline, 2017)
- PMID 30075789: Corticosteroid use and intensive care unit-acquired weakness: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2018)
- PMID 31219229: The effect of early mobilization in critically ill patients: A meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 31581205: Early mobilization of critically ill patients in the intensive care unit: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 33407707: Systematic early versus late mobilization or standard early mobilization in mechanically ventilated adult ICU patients: systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35639543: Clinical Practice Guideline: Early Mobilization and Rehabilitation of Critically Ill Burn Patients (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 36597123: The rate and assessment of muscle wasting during critical illness: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 37232695: Effect of Neuromuscular Electrical Stimulation in Patients With Critical Illness: An Updated Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized Controlled Trials (meta-analysis, 2023)

## Cluster `crohn` (topics 478, 479, 480) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** ACG 2025 and AGA living guidelines address Crohn's disease management, with induction network meta-analysis showing immunomodulators followed by anti-TNF monotherapy having a large effect size with moderate certainty. Perianal disease has dedicated consensus criteria and practice updates, and partial enteral nutrition evidence is low quality.

**Teaching points**
- The ACG 2025 guideline gives recommendations for management of Crohn's disease in adults, based on data available at publication. [PMID 40701562]
- The AGA living guideline provides an evidence-based, patient-centred approach to pharmacologic management of moderate-to-severe Crohn's disease and identifies knowledge gaps. [PMID 41274746]
- In a network meta-analysis for induction, immunomodulators followed by anti-TNF monotherapy had large effect size with moderate certainty, and novel therapies had similar but less precise effects. [PMID 40973970]
- A systematic review and expert consensus proposes novel diagnostic criteria for isolated perianal Crohn's disease. [PMID 40315877]
- An Indian clinical practice update standardises treatment protocols for perianal fistulizing Crohn's disease. [PMID 41718934]
- Partial enteral nutrition may help Crohn's disease management, but evidence is mostly low quality. [PMID 39565924]
- APAGE guidelines on small molecules highlight screening and monitoring for side effects and herpes zoster vaccination, with S1P modulators efficacious in UC. [PMID 42117726]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal sequencing of advanced therapies.

**MCQ angles**
- Anti-TNF induction.
- Perianal disease definitions.

**Case hooks**
- Young adult with Crohn's ileitis and perianal fistula.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Treat-to-target strategy, surgery indications and postoperative recurrence prevention are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39565924: Partial Enteral Nutrition in the Management of Crohn's Disease: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40315877: Isolated perianal Crohn's disease: a systematic review and expert consensus proposing novel diagnostic criteria and management advice (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40701562: ACG Clinical Guideline: Management of Crohn's Disease in Adults (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40973970: Immunomodulators and Advanced Therapies for Induction of Remission in Crohn's Disease: A Systematic Review and Network Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2026)
- PMID 41274746: AGA Living Clinical Practice Guideline on the Pharmacologic Management of Moderate-to-Severe Crohn's Disease (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `croup` (topics 481) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Cochrane evidence shows glucocorticoids reduce croup scores at two hours, shorten hospital stay and reduce return visits, with little or no difference between dexamethasone and prednisolone in the 2023 update. Guideline plus orderset implementation cut admissions without raising revisits.

**Teaching points**
- Glucocorticoids for croup reduced croup scores at two hours, shortened hospital stays and reduced return visits. [PMID 30133690]
- There was likely little to no difference between prednisolone and dexamethasone for croup score reduction, with high-certainty evidence from one RCT of 1231 children. [PMID 36626194]
- A clinical guideline and orderset significantly decreased hospital admissions for croup with no increase in revisits. [PMID 35970819]
- Reflux medication treatment appears associated with reduced croup symptoms, but studies are retrospective and biased. [PMID 32040207]
- Guideline order bundles in an admission order set may reduce adoption for excluded guidelines, with diagnostic uncertainty an unrecognised barrier. [PMID 36943348]
- Bronchiolitis guideline nonadherence at US children's hospitals is associated with multiple factors. [PMID 34493589]
- A review of coughing children in primary care highlights asthma and pertussis possibility and need for pre-test probability data. [PMID 34088294]

**Uncertainties**
- Reflux association.

**MCQ angles**
- Steroid effect time course.
- Dexamethasone versus prednisolone.

**Case hooks**
- Toddler with barking cough and stridor at rest.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Severity scoring (Westley), nebulised adrenaline, dexamethasone dose and observation period are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**

## Cluster `cryoglobulinaemia` (topics 482) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Direct-acting antivirals gave 100% clinical vasculitis response on intention-to-treat in HCV-associated mixed cryoglobulinaemia, and Italian consensus offers 17 recommendations on rituximab use. Severe life-threatening syndromes warrant plasma exchange and viral eradication when feasible, and SVR reduces extrahepatic HCV manifestations.

**Teaching points**
- A prospective study of guideline-tailored direct-acting antivirals in HCV-associated mixed cryoglobulinaemia reported 100% clinical response of vasculitis on intention-to-treat. [PMID 27483451]
- Seventeen recommendations from the Italian cryoglobulinaemia study group guide rituximab use in cryoglobulinaemic vasculitis. [PMID 36169798]
- Recommendations for severe and life-threatening mixed cryoglobulinaemia include plasma exchange, with HCV eradication using DAAs as soon as the patient's condition allows. [PMID 31181326]
- Antiviral therapy reduces HCV extrahepatic manifestations when sustained virological response is achieved. [PMID 29703790]
- A meta-analysis of extrahepatic manifestations of HCV assesses prevalence, quality of life and economic burden, calling for real-world prospective studies. [PMID 26924097]
- A review of rituximab in immune-mediated disorders found mixed results for several conditions, warranting better trials. [PMID 31555262]
- HCV is epidemiologically linked to non-Hodgkin lymphoma, and antiviral therapy or chemotherapy options are discussed. [PMID 26807206]

**Uncertainties**
- Quality of data on long-term follow-up after SVR.

**MCQ angles**
- DAA in HCV cryoglobulinaemia.
- Rituximab consensus.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with purpura, arthralgia and HCV infection.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Cryoglobulin type classification, complement findings and steroid or cyclophosphamide roles are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 26807206: Hepatitis C virus and non-Hodgkin's lymphomas: Meta-analysis of epidemiology data and therapy options (meta-analysis, 2016)
- PMID 26924097: Extrahepatic Manifestations of Hepatitis C: A Meta-analysis of Prevalence, Quality of Life, and Economic Burden (meta-analysis, 2016)

## Cluster `crypto_meningitis` (topics 484, 485) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The ECMM/ISHAM global guideline sets cryptococcosis diagnosis and management. In HIV, serum CrAg negativity may rule out meningitis, positivity should prompt induction therapy if lumbar puncture is not feasible, and routine CrAg screening with preemptive fluconazole reduces incidence but CrAg-positive patients still have higher mortality.

**Teaching points**
- The ECMM and ISHAM global guideline informs clinicians on diagnosing and managing cryptococcosis and was endorsed internationally. [PMID 38346436]
- In adults with HIV and meningitis symptoms, negative serum CrAg may rule out cryptococcal meningitis, positive serum CrAg should prompt induction therapy if lumbar puncture is not feasible, and CSF CrAg positivity is diagnostic in a first episode. [PMID 32829406]
- Routine CrAg screening with pre-emptive fluconazole reduced cryptococcal meningitis incidence and showed survival benefit, yet mortality in CrAg-positive participants remained 2.2 times higher. [PMID 30020446]
- Failure to initiate antifungals among eligible patients with cryptococcal antigenaemia is a missed opportunity, so CrAg screening needs emphasis. [PMID 39854376]
- HIV-associated meningitis mortality in sub-Saharan Africa is very high under routine care. [PMID 31957332]
- In pregnancy, cryptococcal meningitis treated with amphotericin B without first trimester fluconazole gave good maternal but poor fetal outcomes. [PMID 31689712]
- An Australasian consensus covers cryptococcosis and rare yeast infections in the haematology/oncology setting. [PMID 34937137]
- A Chinese expert consensus emphasises early diagnosis, combined long-term antifungal therapy and control of intracranial hypertension. [PMID 29747285]

**Uncertainties**
- Outcomes after antigenaemia treatment.

**MCQ angles**
- CrAg screening strategy.
- Pregnancy treatment.

**Case hooks**
- Advanced HIV patient with headache and positive serum CrAg.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Induction regimen (amphotericin plus flucytosine), India ink sensitivity and raised ICP therapeutic taps are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 29747285: [Expert consensus on the diagnosis and treatment of cryptococcal meningitis] (guideline, 2018)
- PMID 30020446: Impact of Routine Cryptococcal Antigen Screening and Targeted Preemptive Fluconazole Therapy in Antiretroviral-naive Human Immunodeficiency Virus-infected Adults With CD4 Cell Counts <100/μL: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2019)
- PMID 31689712: Cryptococcosis in pregnancy and the postpartum period: Case series and systematic review with recommendations for management (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 31957332: Mortality from HIV-associated meningitis in sub-Saharan Africa: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 32829406: Cryptococcal Antigen in Serum and Cerebrospinal Fluid for Detecting Cryptococcal Meningitis in Adults Living With Human Immunodeficiency Virus: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Diagnostic Test Accuracy Studies (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34937137: Consensus guidelines for the diagnosis and management of cryptococcosis and rare yeast infections in the haematology/oncology setting, 2021 (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 38346436: Global guideline for the diagnosis and management of cryptococcosis: an initiative of the ECMM and ISHAM in cooperation with the ASM (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39854376: Systematic review on the compliance of WHO guidelines in the management of patients with advanced HIV disease in Africa: The case of cryptococcal antigen screening (guideline, 2025)

## Cluster `ct_head_ctpa` (topics 486) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Retrieved evidence covers CTPA rather than CT head. ACR criteria guide imaging in suspected PE, guideline-discordant CTPA ordering reflects factors outside risk scores, and the ACP guideline can be applied to cancer patients to reduce unnecessary imaging. CT signs such as septal bowing are specific for RV dysfunction.

**Teaching points**
- ACR Appropriateness Criteria give guidance on imaging for suspected pulmonary embolism. [PMID 36436972, 28473076]
- Guideline-discordant CTPA ordering in the ED may reflect factors not in risk scores that shape clinical suspicion. [PMID 31047834]
- The ACP guideline performs well for detecting PE in cancer patients, and compliance could reduce unnecessary imaging. [PMID 31376398]
- D-dimer cut-offs used to exclude PE in guidelines appear applicable to COVID-19 patients. [PMID 33320063]
- CT findings of RV dysfunction, particularly septal bowing, are specific, and multiple findings may improve accuracy. [PMID 34245115]
- V/Q SPECT/CT has high accuracy for acute PE while Q SPECT/CT has high sensitivity but limited specificity. [PMID 36423623]
- CTPA shows good diagnostic performance for chronic thromboembolic pulmonary hypertension, with lower specificity when relying on parenchymal perfusion alone. [PMID 35482124]

**Uncertainties**
- Performance of CTPA for CTEPH in general radiology.

**MCQ angles**
- Septal bowing sign.
- D-dimer in COVID.

**Case hooks**
- Cancer patient with pleuritic pain and suspected PE.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Acute CT head findings (haemorrhage, early infarct signs, mass effect) are not covered by the retrieved excerpts; a CT head source is needed.

**Sources**

- PMID 31047834: An Evaluation of Guideline-Discordant Ordering Behavior for CT Pulmonary Angiography in the Emergency Department (guideline, 2019)

## Cluster `cushing` (topics 487, 488, 489) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2008 Endocrine Society screening recommendations for Cushing syndrome remain valid in 2022. Dynamic testing and bilateral inferior petrosal sinus sampling differentiate ACTH-dependent causes, with desmopressin a safe cheaper alternative to CRH in BIPSS, and endogenous Cushing syndrome still carries increased mortality driven by comorbidities.

**Teaching points**
- The 2008 Endocrine Society guideline defined at-risk populations for Cushing syndrome screening, and its recommendations remain valid in 2022. [PMID 35730067]
- A positive ACTH response after a CRH test makes Cushing disease highly likely in ACTH-dependent Cushing syndrome, and dynamic testing plus imaging needs further study. [PMID 36453141]
- DDAVP stimulation is a safe, effective, cheaper alternative to CRH in bilateral inferior petrosal sinus sampling for all age groups. [PMID 35478451]
- Endogenous Cushing syndrome retains increased mortality, with deaths from comorbidities including cardiovascular disease, infection and suicide, highlighting comorbidity prevention alongside treating hypercortisolism. [PMID 35486378]
- Functioning adrenal incidentalomas are prevalent, with Cushing syndrome prevalence estimates around 3.1%-3.8% in pooled data. [PMID 36718682]
- Safe use of mifepristone needs clinical judgment and close monitoring, per consensus recommendations. [PMID 33292727]
- A French consensus addresses genetic screening for Cushing disease and adrenal Cushing syndrome predisposition. [PMID 38253221]
- A consensus guideline addresses diagnosis and management of paediatric pituitary adenomas, optimising transition and lifelong care. [PMID 38336898]

**Uncertainties**
- Role of dynamic testing relative to imaging.

**MCQ angles**
- DDAVP in BIPSS.
- Residual mortality.

**Case hooks**
- Patient with weight gain, myopathy and hypertension needing screening.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** First-line screening tests (late-night salivary cortisol, 1 mg dexamethasone suppression, urinary free cortisol) and surgical outcomes are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33292727: Clinical management of patients with Cushing syndrome treated with mifepristone: consensus recommendations (guideline, 2020)
- PMID 35478451: Diagnostic accuracy of bilateral inferior petrosal sinus sampling using desmopressin or corticotropic- releasing hormone in ACTH-dependent Cushing's syndrome: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35486378: The Effect of Endogenous Cushing Syndrome on All-cause and Cause-specific Mortality (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 35730067: Whom Should We Screen for Cushing Syndrome? The Endocrine Society Practice Guideline Recommendations 2008 Revisited (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 36453141: Dynamic Testing for Differential Diagnosis of ACTH-Dependent Cushing Syndrome: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 36718682: Prevalence of Functioning Adrenal Incidentalomas: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2023)
- PMID 38253221: Consensus statement by the French Society of Endocrinology (SFE) and French Society of Pediatric Endocrinology &amp; Diabetology (SFEDP) for the diagnosis of Cushing's syndrome: Genetics of Cushing's syndrome (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38336898: Consensus guideline for the diagnosis and management of pituitary adenomas in childhood and adolescence: Part 2, specific diseases (guideline, 2024)

## Cluster `cscc` (topics 490) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The European interdisciplinary guideline on invasive cSCC (parts 1 and 2) covers classification, risk stratification, staging, treatment and follow-up tailored to risk. Poor outcomes link to patient and tumour characteristics, and immune checkpoint inhibitors show higher response probability in cSCC among transplant recipients with concurrent mTOR inhibitors and steroids possibly reducing rejection risk.

**Teaching points**
- Part 1 of the European guideline on invasive cSCC addresses classification, epidemiology, diagnosis, risk stratification, staging and prevention in immunocompetent and immunosuppressed patients. [PMID 37717283]
- Part 2 of the European guideline notes follow-up frequency and investigations depend on risk characteristics, with best supportive care for symptom management. [PMID 37708630]
- The German S3 guideline update covers epidemiology, diagnostics, surgical and systemic treatment, surveillance and prevention of cSCC. [PMID 37840404]
- Patient risk factors, tumour characteristics and treatment modality are associated with poor outcomes in primary cSCC. [PMID 36576732]
- In solid organ transplant recipients with advanced cancers, response to checkpoint inhibitors was more likely in cSCC, and concurrent mTOR inhibitors and steroids may reduce acute allograft rejection risk. [PMID 40545616]
- The European actinic keratosis guideline advises topical treatments and photodynamic therapy, with sun protection, self-examination and repeated field treatment in high-risk patients. [PMID 38451047]
- The European BCC guideline recommends long-term follow-up for high-risk BCC, multiple BCCs and Gorlin syndrome. [PMID 37604067]
- Immune checkpoint inhibitor cardiovascular adverse events carry high mortality, with early recognition and corticosteroids advised. [PMID 39172480]

**Uncertainties**
- Allograft rejection risk with checkpoint inhibitors.

**MCQ angles**
- Risk-based follow-up.
- Checkpoint inhibitors in transplant recipients.

**Case hooks**
- Organ transplant recipient with a rapidly growing keratotic lesion.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** High-risk features (thickness, perineural invasion), Mohs surgery, radiotherapy and cemiplimab dosing are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

## Cluster `ctcl` (topics 491) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2023 EORTC update guides treatment of mycosis fungoides and Sézary syndrome, with ISCL/EORTC guidance for early-stage paediatric MF and ASTCT/USCLC recommendations for allogeneic transplant. UV radiation is implicated in CTCL pathogenesis, chlormethine gel is limited by contact dermatitis but manageable, and dupilumab may unmask or exacerbate CTCL.

**Teaching points**
- EORTC consensus recommendations (2023 update) guide treatment of mycosis fungoides and Sézary syndrome. [PMID 37890355]
- ASTCT and USCLC provide clinical practice recommendations for allogeneic stem cell transplant in MF/SS, supporting multidisciplinary pre- and post-transplant management. [PMID 39222792]
- Dupilumab may unmask or exacerbate CTCL, particularly MF and SS, and use of IL-4/IL-13 targeting biologics is discouraged with insufficient data. [PMID 41821352]
- Chlormethine gel use is often limited by contact dermatitis, but proper regimens and adverse effect management can prevent unnecessary withdrawal. [PMID 35429108]
- UV mutational signature 7 appeared only in CTCL among T-cell lymphomas, implicating UV radiation in pathogenesis. [PMID 33597573]
- ISCL/EORTC-CLTG/USCLC recommendations address diagnosis, staging and treatment of early-stage paediatric MF, which differs from adults. [PMID 40420841]
- Consensus recommendations for paediatric MF stress documentation of extended severity and outcome measures and protocols for advanced-stage disease. [PMID 39181404]
- Japanese guidelines outline management of primary cutaneous lymphomas by a multidisciplinary panel. [PMID 33245165]

**Uncertainties**
- Dupilumab safety in suspected CTCL.

**MCQ angles**
- Dupilumab unmasking CTCL.
- Chlormethine gel adverse effects.

**Case hooks**
- Adult with refractory eczema-like patches not responding to treatment.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Skin-directed therapy choices by stage, bexarotene, mogamulizumab and staging systems are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 33245165: Outlines of the Japanese guidelines for the management of primary cutaneous lymphomas 2020 (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 33597573: Spectrum of mutational signatures in T-cell lymphoma reveals a key role for UV radiation in cutaneous T-cell lymphoma (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 35429108: The optimal use of chlormethine gel for mycosis fungoides: An expert consensus from Germany, Austria and Switzerland (DACH region) (guideline, 2022)
- PMID 37890355: EORTC consensus recommendations for the treatment of mycosis fungoides/Sézary syndrome - Update 2023 (guideline, 2023)
- PMID 39181404: Approach to Mycosis Fungoides in children: Consensus-based recommendations (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 39222792: ASTCT and USCLC Clinical Practice Recommendations for Allogeneic Stem Cell Transplant in Mycosis Fungoides and Sézary Syndrome (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40420841: ISCL/EORTC-CLTG/USCLC recommendations for the diagnosis, staging and treatment of early-stage paediatric mycosis fungoides: a modified Delphi consensus. Part one: diagnosis and staging (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41821352: Cutaneous T-cell lymphomas and dupilumab for atopic dermatitis: A systematic review and expert consensus (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `cf` (topics 492, 493) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** CF newborn screening guidance aims to improve sensitivity, equity and timeliness, and the CF Foundation guideline covers CRMS/CFSPID management. German S3 guidance defines chronic Pseudomonas infection, fecal elastase-1 is sensitive for exocrine insufficiency, and consensus exists for following children exposed to CFTR modulators in utero or via breastfeeding.

**Teaching points**
- A US consensus guideline on CF newborn screening aims for improved sensitivity, equity and timeliness. [PMID 40265445]
- The CF Foundation guideline manages CRMS/CFSPID designation, noting future research needs on medication, antibiotics and chest imaging. [PMID 38577740]
- The German S3 guideline defines chronic Pseudomonas aeruginosa infection and gives evidence-based diagnosis and treatment for CF lung disease. [PMID 38350639]
- Fecal elastase-1 is a sensitive but moderately specific test for pancreatic exocrine insufficiency, suitable for initial screening in high-risk populations. [PMID 40569793]
- International Delphi recommendations support follow-up of children exposed to CFTR modulators in utero or via breastfeeding. [PMID 42049593]
- A focused update of consensus recommendations addresses prolonged-infusion beta-lactam use in CF, suggesting loading doses when initiating continuous infusion. [PMID 42598847]
- Probiotics, prebiotics and synbiotics in CF should be considered experimental adjuncts rather than standard. [PMID 40142300]
- Adult chronic rhinosinusitis guidance lists cystic fibrosis among modifying conditions. [PMID 40742114]

**Uncertainties**
- Long-term effects of in utero modulator exposure.

**MCQ angles**
- Fecal elastase test.
- Modulator exposure in pregnancy.

**Case hooks**
- Infant with positive newborn screen and borderline sweat chloride.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Adult CF care, modulator efficacy (elexacaftor/tezacaftor/ivacaftor) and eligibility are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 38350639: [CF Lung Disease - a German S3 Guideline: Pseudomonas aeruginosa] (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 38577740: Cystic Fibrosis Foundation Evidence-Based Guideline for the Management of CRMS/CFSPID (guideline, 2024)
- PMID 40142300: Effectiveness of Probiotics, Prebiotics, and Symbiotic Supplementation in Cystic Fibrosis Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Clinical Trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40265445: Cystic Fibrosis Newborn Screening: A Systematic Review-Driven Consensus Guideline from the United States Cystic Fibrosis Foundation (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40569793: Diagnostic Accuracy of Fecal Elastase-1 Test for Pancreatic Exocrine Insufficiency: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40742114: Clinical Practice Guideline: Adult Sinusitis Update (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 42049593: International Delphi consensus recommendations for the follow-up of children born to people with CF and exposed to CFTR modulators in utero or through breastfeeding; endorsed by the European Cystic Fibrosis Society (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42598847: Focused Update to International Consensus Recommendations for the Use of Prolonged-Infusion Beta-Lactam Antibiotics Among Severely Ill Adult Patients: Endorsed by the American College of Clinical Pharmacy, British Society for Antimicrobial Chemotherapy, Cystic Fibrosis Foundation, European Society of Clinical Microbiology and Infectious Diseases, Infectious Diseases Society of America, Society of Critical Care Medicine, and Society of Infectious Diseases Pharmacists (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `dbs` (topics 496) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Consensus expert recommendations support timely referral of Parkinson's patients for DBS. Directional electrodes lack clear clinical justification for added complexity and cost, STN-DBS patients may benefit from physical therapy, and metabolic effects like weight gain need monitoring. DBS has emerging evidence in epilepsy, OCD and depression.

**Teaching points**
- Consensus expert recommendations provide a referral framework so appropriate Parkinson's disease candidates have timely access to DBS while reducing misconceptions. [PMID 41484162]
- Directional DBS electrodes in Parkinson's disease are widely accepted, but clinical data justifying added complexity and cost are sparse. [PMID 39304337]
- Physical therapy after subthalamic DBS may maximise clinical improvement, though more research is needed to create guidelines. [PMID 40211348]
- Metabolic effects of DBS in Parkinson's disease warrant continuous monitoring of metabolic parameters and cardiometabolic risk. [PMID 40497379]
- DBS of anterior nucleus and centromedian nucleus shows efficacy in drug-resistant epilepsy, but patient selection and response prediction need research. [PMID 40516441]
- In Lennox-Gastaut syndrome, DBS and responsive neurostimulation may be more effective than vagus nerve stimulation. [PMID 40657941]
- DBS for OCD shows efficacy over sham but evidence quality is low with high heterogeneity. [PMID 40579425]
- More than a dozen preoperative predictors of DBS response in depression await replication. [PMID 40381858]

**Uncertainties**
- Value of directional electrodes.

**MCQ angles**
- Referral framework.
- Metabolic effects of DBS.

**Case hooks**
- Parkinson's patient with motor fluctuations and dyskinesia.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** STN versus GPi target selection and candidate criteria (levodopa responsiveness) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39304337: Directional deep brain stimulation electrodes in Parkinson's disease: meta-analysis and systematic review of the literature (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40211348: Is physical therapy recommended for people with parkinson's disease treated with subthalamic deep brain stimulation? a delphi consensus study (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 40381858: Pre-operative predictors of response to deep brain stimulation in depression: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40497379: Metabolic effects of deep brain stimulation in Parkinson's disease - a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40516441: Deep brain stimulation for epilepsy: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized and non-randomized studies of thalamic targeting (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40579425: Deep brain stimulation for obsessive-compulsive-disorder: a systematic review and meta-analysis of individual participant outcome data from sham-controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40657941: Comparative efficacy of neuromodulation therapies in Lennox-Gastaut syndrome: A systematic review and meta-analysis of vagus nerve stimulation, deep brain stimulation, and responsive neurostimulation (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 41484162: Consensus expert recommendations for referral of Parkinson's disease patients for deep brain stimulation surgery (guideline, 2026)
