## Cluster `dvt` (topics 497) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** The 2025 ESVM guideline covers catheter-based interventional treatment of VTE, and non-instrumental early mobilisation and ankle pump exercises reduce DVT in hospitalised and orthopaedic surgical patients. Prophylactic IVC filters are limited to selected indications in severe trauma, and paediatric thrombolysis evidence is very low certainty.

**Teaching points**
- The 2025 ESVM guideline provides recommendations on indications and management of catheter-based therapy in acute VTE. [PMID 40587333]
- Non-instrumental early mobilisation reduces the incidence of deep vein thrombosis in hospitalised patients (RR 0.55 versus usual care). [PMID 39413511]
- Postoperative ankle pump exercises help prevent DVT and improve venous haemodynamics in lower limb orthopaedic surgery. [PMID 41024203]
- An algorithm for IVC filter placement in severely injured patients restricts prophylactic use to selected indications, retrieval infrastructure and limited duration, with the seven-day contraindication threshold a deliberative prompt. [PMID 42812097]
- Thrombolysis for paediatric VTE is supported only by very-low-certainty evidence. [PMID 40561511]
- A Chinese consensus standardises ultrasound screening and thrombus evaluation for lower extremity DVT. [PMID 42706204]
- An Austrian consensus formulates risk-adapted VTE prevention recommendations in orthopaedics and traumatology. [PMID 42550214]

**Uncertainties**
- Thrombolysis in children.

**MCQ angles**
- Early mobilisation effect.
- IVC filter limits.

**Case hooks**
- Post-operative patient with unilateral leg swelling.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Wells score, D-dimer, anticoagulant choice and duration are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 39413511: Effectiveness of non-instrumental early mobilization to reduce the incidence of deep vein thrombosis in hospitalized patients: A systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40561511: Efficacy and safety of thrombolysis for pediatric venous thromboembolism: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 40587333: 2025 ESVM Guidelines on interventional treatment of venous thromboembolism (guideline, 2025)
- PMID 41024203: Effect of postoperative ankle pump exercises on the prevention of deep vein thrombosis and venous hemodynamics following lower limb orthopedic surgery: a meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials (meta-analysis, 2025)
- PMID 42550214: [Prevention of venous thromboembolism in orthopaedics and traumatology: Austrian interdisciplinary consensus statement 2026] (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42706204: [Expert consensus on standardized ultrasound screening for lower extremity deep vein thrombosis in prevention and management of venous thromboembolism] (guideline, 2026)
- PMID 42812097: An Algorithm for Inferior Vena Cava Filter Placement in Severely Injured Patients: A Systematic Review-Based Consensus and Guidelines by SMA-VS (guideline, 2026)

## Cluster `dhtr` (topics 499) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Delayed haemolytic transfusion reactions in sickle cell disease follow red cell alloimmunisation, and complement activation drives post-transfusion haemolysis and hyperhaemolysis. C5 inhibitors have been used effectively in hyperhaemolysis, and case reports support eculizumab in DHTR, though prospective dosing studies are needed.

**Teaching points**
- Complement is a key disease driver in post-transfusion haemolysis and hyperhaemolysis and a promising therapeutic target. [PMID 31672341]
- C5 inhibitors have been used effectively in hyperhaemolysis and other conditions with bystander haemolysis. [PMID 38708453]
- Eculizumab use in DHTR is supported by a case report, while prospective studies are needed for dosing and sequencing. [PMID 30768787]
- RBC alloimmunisation in sickle cell disease requires multicentre studies to develop prevention strategies for life-threatening haemolytic transfusion reactions. [PMID 30857806]
- Complement plays a role in alloimmunisation and hyperhaemolysis, and complement-modulating drugs may help intervention. [PMID 32889827]
- Complement activation in sickle cell disease occurs in both asymptomatic and severe states, with complement inhibition a new therapeutic perspective. [PMID 33504459, 33790681]
- Therapeutic complement inhibition is advancing in haematological diseases beyond PNH. [PMID 34482398]

**Uncertainties**
- Optimal eculizumab dosing.

**MCQ angles**
- Complement in hyperhaemolysis.
- Alloimmunisation prevention.

**Case hooks**
- Sickle cell patient with falling haemoglobin days after transfusion.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Clinical features and diagnosis, avoiding further transfusion, and steroid or IVIG use are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 30768787: Anti-N and anti-Do<sup>a</sup> immunoglobulin G alloantibody-mediated delayed hemolytic transfusion reaction with hyperhemolysis in sickle cell disease treated with eculizumab and HBOC-201: case report and review of the literature (review, 2019)
- PMID 30857806: Red blood cell alloimmunization and delayed hemolytic transfusion reactions in patients with sickle cell disease (review, 2019)
- PMID 31672341: The role of Complement in Post-Transfusion Hemolysis and Hyperhemolysis Reaction (review, 2019)
- PMID 32889827: Role of complement in alloimmunization and hyperhemolysis (review, 2020)
- PMID 33504459: Complement in sickle cell disease and targeted therapy: I know one thing, that I know nothing (review, 2021)
- PMID 33790681: Complement in Sickle Cell Disease: Are We Ready for Prime Time? (review, 2021)
- PMID 34482398: Advancing therapeutic complement inhibition in hematologic diseases: PNH and beyond (review, 2022)
- PMID 38708453: What is the role of complement in bystander hemolysis? Old concept, new insights (review, 2024)

## Cluster `delayed_puberty` (topics 500) [draft_pending_clinician_review]

**Mentor intro.** Inhibin B differentiates congenital hypogonadotropic hypogonadism from constitutional delay in boys with high accuracy, particularly with testicular volume 3 ml or less. Letrozole is safe and effective for constitutional delay and may be better than testosterone for testicular volume, and FGFR1 and ANOS1 are the most affected genes in absent puberty.

**Teaching points**
- Serum inhibin B differentiates CHH from constitutional delay of growth and puberty with sensitivity 92% and specificity 98%, especially in boys with testicular volume of 3 ml or less. [PMID 33464540]
- Letrozole is safe and effective for improving height and pubertal outcomes in CDGP, better than testosterone for testicular volume and possibly better at delaying bone-age progression. [PMID 34477355]
- In males with absent puberty and complete CHH, FGFR1 and ANOS1 accounted for 53.5% of disease-causing variants. [PMID 40101754]
- A guideline updates referral of short or tall children from preventive child health care to specialists. [PMID 33118654]
- Phthalate exposure is associated with pubertal timing in girls and boys, supporting reduced exposure. [PMID 32091510]
- Patients and caregivers identify barriers to endocrine and bone health recommendations, as in Duchenne muscular dystrophy. [PMID 31429780]

**Uncertainties**
- Outcome data after referral.

**MCQ angles**
- Inhibin B to distinguish CHH from CDGP.
- Letrozole in CDGP.

**Case hooks**
- 15-year-old boy with testicular volume 3 ml and no pubertal progression.

**Gaps for the reviewer.** Gonadotropin stimulation testing, bone age, testosterone priming and female evaluation (Turner syndrome) are not stated in the retrieved abstract excerpts.

**Sources**
- PMID 31429780: Patient and caregiver perspectives on guideline adherence: the case of endocrine and bone health recommendations for Duchenne muscular dystrophy (guideline, 2019)
- PMID 32091510: Association of phthalate exposure with precocious and delayed pubertal timing in girls and boys: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2020)
- PMID 33118654: Guideline for referring short or tall children in preventive child health care (guideline, 2021)
- PMID 33464540: Serum inhibin B for differentiating between congenital hypogonadotropic hypogonadism and constitutional delay of growth and puberty: a systematic review and meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2021)
- PMID 34477355: Efficacy and Safety of Letrozole in the Management of Constitutional Delay in Growth and Puberty: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis (meta-analysis, 2022)
- PMID 40101754: Clinical presentation of congenital hypogonadotropic hypogonadism in males with delayed puberty according to genetic etiology: a systematic review and meta-analysis after reclassification of gene variants (meta-analysis, 2025)
